Chapter 1351: That’s Just How He’s Made. (1)

Hong Dae-Kwang, now ashen, fumbled and continued speaking.

“Um, currently the Four Sects Alliance reports that Jang Ilso has set out with the Red Dogs and other elites. Um, the exact destination isn’t known, but roughly the south…”

“Are you sure?”

Hong Dae-Kwang nodded vigorously without answering. He was sweating profusely; just looking at his face you could feel the urgency and tension.

“We—the Beggars’ Union—have lost most of our informants in the Southern lands, but it’s not to the point that we can’t even watch the Four Sects Alliance’s main force. To be honest, we’re barely watching their main force… Anyway, moving on!”

Hong Dae-Kwang continued speaking.

“Anyway, since the last Hangzhou incident, this is the first time Jang Ilso has personally moved! The Beggars’ Union is in an uproar! And the fact he mobilized the Red Dogs and elites of the Ten Thousand People Clan means they’re planning something big, doesn’t it?”

Hyun Jong’s face remained hardened the whole time as he listened.

The Beggars’ Union still doesn’t know the situation of Chung Myung and the Heavenly Friends Alliance party. So they can only make that interpretation.

But to Hyun Jong, who knew the situation of those who went to Southern Island, Jang Ilso’s objective was clear.

“If the Beggars’ Union learned this news, it means the Nine Great Sects know as well.”

Hong Dae-Kwang fell silent like a mute and nodded.

Of course the Beggars’ Union still belongs to the Nine Great Sects, and it’s natural that the information they obtain is passed on to the Sects. Frankly speaking, Hong Dae-Kwang telling the Heavenly Friends Alliance this news could actually be problematic.

Still, whenever Hong Dae-Kwang said such things in front of Hyun Jong, he couldn’t help but feel as if he were committing a sin.

Hyun Jong, as if understanding that feeling, bowed deeply to Hong Dae-Kwang.

“Thank you. It must have been difficult to bring the news.”

“P-Please don’t do that, Alliance leader!”

Hong Dae-Kwang recoiled and waved his arms.

“You don’t need to thank me. And… thanks are usually for outsiders. Though I’m formally of the Beggars’ Union and my position is awkward, how could I possibly be on equal terms with Mount Hua’s Divine Dragon… no, Mount Hua’s Sword Saint?”

Hyun Jong quietly nodded. Knowing it wasn’t empty praise, he was simply grateful.

But now there was no time to leisurely express gratitude.

His gaze turned to the Yangtze river spreading beyond the wall of the manor. In the distance, the Yangtze was still dominated by fleets of the Pirates.

He wanted to break through that deployment and advance at once, but that was unrealistic. All the more reason there was no time. They had to move immediately and cross the Yangtze river by a place out of the Pirates’ reach.

Hyun Jong said.

“Sect leader.”

“Yes.”

“There is no time.”

“I’ll prepare to depart immediately.”

“I’m sorry, Sect leader.”

Un Am shook his head firmly.

“I think Master was not wrong even a little.”

“…”

“If Mount Hua’s disciples were isolated in the Southern lands and Chung Myung had been among us, what would he have said?”

Hyun Jong’s face went blank for a moment. Seeing that expression, Un Am smiled faintly.

“That fellow would, of course, insist without the slightest hesitation that he must go to Southern Lands immediately. He wouldn’t have listened to anyone trying to stop him, and if it came to it, he’d have gone to Southern lands alone without hesitation. Wouldn’t he?”

“…Sect leader.”

“It’s not because they are those kids that we’re going to rescue them. Precisely because they are them, we hesitated even for a moment, wondering if this was the right choice. If it had been others, would we even have pondered?”

Hyun Jong slowly nodded.

Un Am was right. It would have been the same no matter who was in danger now.

“They’re all disciples, children Mount Hua must embrace. There is no need for hesitation or wavering. If you’re not prepared to lay down a hundred lives to save one, then the name ‘sect’ is nothing but a facade.”

Un Am’s gaze turned to his disciples.

“Isn’t that so?”

“Yes, Sect leader!”

There were no usual cheers or grumbles. Only determined voices rose in unison.

Hyun Jong suppressed something swelling in his chest and nodded.

“Alright, let’s go.”

“Yes, Sect leader!”

Hyun Jong’s gaze turned to Tang Gunak.

“Family lord.”

But before he could speak, Tang Gunak shook his head.

“Don’t tell us to stay and guard this place, Alliance leader.”

He said bluntly, as if he already knew what Hyun Jong would say.

“There are my children there. My children, more important than anyone.”

“One thing though…”

“But that’s a secondary matter.”

A chill appeared on Tang Gunak’s face.

“For the sake of the Tang Family, I can give up my children’s lives anytime. But as the head of the Tang Family, there is one thing I can never give up.”

Tang Gunak’s face was utterly stern.

“Those who harm someone of Tang Family blood will surely pay. This is the Tang Family’s law passed down for centuries, the iron rule of the Tang Family that must be kept with blood. That rule even takes precedence over the Tang Family’s existence.”

A cold blue ferocity filled his eyes.

“Even if Mount Hua doesn’t go, the Tang Family would have crossed the river. So please don’t tell me to stay here.”

“Family Lord…”

You could tell just by looking at his face. There was no way to break Tang Gunak’s will. He, too, had restrained himself until now because of the Heavenly Friends Alliance’s name.

“Indeed, the Tang Family’s laws are well known.”

At the voice from behind, the two turned to the speaker.

“But the duty to that law is unlikely to be greater than Namgung’s urgency right now.”

“…Namgung Elder.”

Namgung Myung nodded heavily.

“The clan won’t vanish just because we don’t follow the law. But right now, for Namgung, the existence of the Young lord is greater than the clan itself.”

Namgung Myung spoke with a face as cold as if clad in iron.

“It would’ve been best not to send him in the first place, but now we must take the second-best option. Namgung’s second-best is to rescue the Young lord, even if everyone risks their lives. There is no fallback.”

A frosty chill emanated from Namgung Myung. Beside him, Namgung Dan also expressed agreement with a grave face.

The Namgung Family had lost Namgung Hwang. They could not afford to lose Namgung Do-Wei here as well.

“Namgung will go along as well.”

Mount Hua, the Tang Family, and the Namgung Family declared their intentions.

“Then the Ice Palace will join as well.”

“Elder Han…”

“We also can’t afford to lose our Palace Lord. Shouldn’t we add even one more sect’s strength?”

But Tang Gunak shook his head firmly at that.

“The Ice Palace must guard this place.”

Then Song Won of the Ice Palace, watching the situation from the side, flared up.

“Why? Because we’re not people of this place?”

“Song Won!”

Han Yi-myung shouted in an icy voice.

“What nonsense is that! After seeing how the Heavenly Friends Alliance people have treated us, you still spout such words?”

“…”

“Say that kind of nonsense one more time and I won’t be the one to forgive you.”

Song Won hung his head low. Han Yi-myung apologized to Tang Gunak on his behalf.

“I’m sorry.”

“It’s fine. I understand completely.”

To the Northern Sea Ice Palace, the Tang Family was also a fellow Central Plains house, but in truth the Tang Family had often been looked down upon in the Central Plains for various reasons. So they knew those feelings all too well.

“There are two reasons we won’t bring the Ice Palace along. One is that in hot Southern Lands, the Ice Palace people’s ice techniques can’t exert their full power.”

“…That is…”

Han Yi-myung couldn’t deny that. It’s natural that the power of cold qi techniques diminishes the warmer the region.

Normally he might have boldly said it wouldn’t matter, but facing the Four Sects Alliance, even that remark wouldn’t come easily.

“The other is that if this place becomes ownerless, they could concentrate their full strength against us who entered Southern lands. For us to return alive and set foot here again, we need a sect to check them.”

Han Yi-myung bit his lip softly.

He too desperately wanted to go rescue the Palace Lord, but he was currently standing in for the Ice Palace’s Palace Lord. Naturally, he couldn’t ignore practical concerns.

If someone had to cover the rear, it had to be the Ice Palace. Their current military strength and influence were clearly weaker than the other sects.

Above all, what made Han Yi-myung hesitate was the fear that their joining might actually be a hindrance.

As if sensing that, Hyun Jong bowed deeply to Han Yi-myung.

“We will certainly bring back the Palace Lord. So please take responsibility for things here.”

“…Understood.”

In the end, Han Yi-myung stepped back.

Tang Gunak’s gaze this time turned to Meng So, who was silent.

“In the same way, the Beast Palace must also guard this place.”

A complex expression colored Meng So’s eyes. He twitched his lips once and asked Tang Gunak in a somewhat brusque tone.

“Do you think the Beast Palace also wouldn’t be able to show its strength in the Southern lands, Family lord?”

“That’s not to say they couldn’t. But… it’s impossible to secretly cross the river with those beasts in tow.”

“…”

“And if someone must guard the rear, I think there’s no one more suitable than the Palace Lord. They can respond the fastest at any time. If it’s the Palace Lord, the Tang Family can trust them to hold the rear.”

Meng So shook his head as if he couldn’t help it.

Anyone who says the Sichuan Tang Family is cruel and selfish is an idiot. Where in them is there cruelty or selfishness?

“…So be it.”

With that, those who would go to Gangnam were decided.

Mount Hua, the Sichuan Tang Family, and the Namgung Family.

Meng So asked with a slightly worried face.

“Will three sects be enough?”

“It might not be just three sects.”

“Hm?”

“…No matter how much you try to stop the Green Forest, they won’t listen. The Green Forest people will follow even unto death.”

“Mm.”

Meng So nodded as if that made sense. Without Im So-Byeong, the Green Forest is a place impossible to control even in the name of the Heavenly Friends Alliance. They would act according to their own will.

For the Green Forest, who had stayed by Im So-Byeong’s side even in moments of crisis, that choice was virtually predetermined.

“Then now…”

“But you two cannot head to Southern lands blindly.”

Namgung Myung said with a stern face.

“Even crossing the Yangtze immediately won’t be easy. The Yangtze is completely controlled by the Pirates, and we don’t even have suitable boats to cross, do we?”

“So we must hurry to secure boats.”

“That’s not all. Even if we cross the Yangtze without losses, in that vast Southern lands, if we don’t know which route those who went to Southern Island will take northward, it will be like searching for a needle in a desert.”

Tang Gunak fell silent. It was indeed a valid point and hard to refute.

“Namgung once let his heart run ahead and lost what mustn’t have been lost. We cannot repeat the same mistake twice.”

One couldn’t miss the heavy weight carried by those words.

The corner of Tang Gunak’s eye twitched faintly. Though he pretended calm, he too was riled now—enough to miss things he would normally have noticed.

“Then…”

“I know where they’ll come.”

Tang Gunak’s gaze turned to Hyun Jong. Hyun Jong spoke calmly.

“So please finish preparations quickly. My cultivation isn’t deep; it’s hard for me to wait long.”

Tang Gunak and Namgung Myung, who had been silently watching Hyun Jong, nodded at the same time.

“Understood, Alliance.”

“We will finish preparations immediately.”

“Please.”

Hyun Jong bowed deeply.

The two immediately led their disciples and rushed to the lodgings where the others stayed, their movements urgent as if they couldn’t spare time even for greetings.

Hyun Jong, raising his head, didn’t look at them either. His gaze was once again fixed on the distant south.

‘Chung Myung… you bastards…’

He then squeezed his eyes shut.

‘Hang on a little. I’m coming soon.’

He could only fervently hope that his voice would reach the exhausted ones.

Chapter 1352: That’s Just How He’s Made. (2)

“Ugh…”

A gut-turning sensation. His body spun, and he felt endlessly falling toward some unknown floor. With that familiar yet strangely unfamiliar feeling, Jo Gul painfully opened his eyes.

Blurry shapes streaked past his vision one after another. He squeezed his eyes shut and opened them again, scanned his surroundings, and spoke in a hoarse voice.

“…What.”

“Awake?”

Only then did Jo Gul look beneath him.

“Sahyung?”

“Yes.”

A familiar back of the head was in front of him. Jo Gul realized he was being carried on Yoon Jong’s back.

“…Where are we?”

“We’ve left the Ten-Thousand Great Mountains and are heading north toward Northern lands.”

“Already… cough cough ah…”

He kept coughing, perhaps from not using his voice for a long time.

“Rest a bit more.”

“I’m fine now. Put me down…”

“Not yet, rest more.”

Jo Gul fell silent at Yoon Jong’s firm tone. He’d put on a brave face, but his body was in terrible shape. Just being carried was already hard to endure.

“Soso.”

“Yes. I’m already checking.”

“…You scared me.”

Jo Gul forced his stiff neck to turn and looked back. Tang Soso, carrying Yu Yiseol, was right behind him with her hand on his back.

“The pulse is normal… internal injuries seem okay enough now. The problem is the external wounds; they won’t heal easily, so you need to be careful for a while.”

Jo Gul blinked and looked at Yu Yiseol on Tang Soso’s back. Yu Yiseol hadn’t regained consciousness yet.

‘Well…’

The wounds he had and the wounds Yu Yiseol had were incomparable. His were from a brief overexertion, but Yu Yiseol had fought until every last drop of blood—so she would need much more time to regain consciousness.

‘Wait…’

Realizing that, Jo Gul snapped his head up. He looked intently at Baek Cheon and Im So-Byeong running ahead, and at the Southern Island disciples following behind.

“S-sahyung! What about Chung Myung?”

“Why? Were you afraid he was dead?”

“No, what kind of guy would he be to die that easily? I mean, where did he go? He was a total wreck.”

“He woke up long ago and is cutting through the Ten Thousand People Clan bastards.”

“With that body?”

“He says he’s fine.”

“…Ah.”

At this point, it wasn’t a joke—one might doubt if he was even flesh and bone. Chung Myung had taken the most wounds and looked the most exhausted.

Yu Yiseol was still comatose, Jo Gul had only just regained consciousness, and Chung Myung was already up fighting?

“…Is he even human?”

“I’m suspicious too.”

Jo Gul shook his head in disbelief.

“Is it dangerous?”

“Not to that extent. It’s not risk-free, of course, but compared to the situation in the gorge, it’s nothing.”

Indeed, to Jo Gul it also seemed so. Those ahead were fine, and even those behind looked far better than before.

Yoon Jong, thinking Jo Gul was sufficiently conscious, quickly explained the current situation.

“So the others are following behind?”

“Yes.”

“…But what if our paths split and we get separated?”

“Southern Island would never do that. And we’re keeping the pace moderate just in case.”

“Hm.”

Jo Gul nodded.

There were odd parts, but it was a plan everyone had carefully devised. Lying back comfortably wasn’t the time to question every detail.

And if it was a plan by Baek Cheon and Im So-Byeong, it was probably better than anything he would’ve come up with.

At that, Yoon Jong spoke, sounding exasperated.

“And you crazy bastard.”

“…Yes?”

“Worried about others the moment you wake up? Think about yourself! You’re patched up like some rag sewn together.”

At that, Jo Gul silently glanced at his own body. Through the wide sleeve, the marks of being stitched up like a centipede were clearly visible on his forearm.

“Hey, at least sew it properly. What kind of doctor lacks finesse…”

“What? You want me to sew your mouth shut?”

“…No.”

Tang Soso’s sharp voice from behind made Jo Gul quietly hush. He couldn’t get at her now; well, truth be told, he usually couldn’t either, but anyway…

At that moment, Lee Ja-Yang, running beside them, raised his voice.

“Keep going straight?”

“Northeast!”

“Yes!”

Im So-Byeong finished speaking and immediately changed direction. Lee Ja-Yang, having confirmed it, kicked off and leapt up, drawing his sword.

Kashak!

The sword qi he emitted etched a tiny cut on the crown of a tall tree ahead—so small it was hard to notice unless you looked closely.

Jo Gul couldn’t help frowning.

“Can you even see that while running?”

“Apparently so.”

“…No…”

Lee Ja-Yang, who returned to his place quickly, answered that question.

“No need to worry. Other sects might miss it, but Southern Island people would never.”

“Eh?”

Lee Ja-Yang continued firmly.

“Southern Island’s disciples have sailed since childhood. To catch fish on the open sea, you can’t miss even the smallest bubble rising on the waves.”

“Oh…”

“Not all, but most have sharp eyes. Don’t worry—they won’t miss a mark.”

Jo Gul nodded. He couldn’t fully grasp it, but he got the gist—it’s akin to how people from the steppes have keen eyesight.

“Sahyung.”

“Yes?”

“…We’d never spot that no matter what, right?”

“You might not even know it’s there.”

“I suppose…”

“They say every sect has a vision like that. Seeing it in person, it’s lived up to its reputation.”

“Every sect? Do we have something like that?”

“We did.”

“Eh?”

“It’s gone.”

“…Ah.”

It burned up. Of course—Mount Hua never lacked anything; it just got burned to ashes.

“Anyway, if we can’t recognize it, the Evil Sect bastards sure won’t.”

“That’s right. We’re relying on that mark to keep changing direction. Even if they find traces of us, they won’t be able to guess our route.”

“Ah.”

No wonder they were carving marks on trees while changing direction sharply…

“As long as our allies can recognize it, that’s enough.”

“Looks like they have.”

“Eh?”

“Behind us.”

At Yoon Jong’s words, Jo Gul turned to look back.

Contrary to Yoon Jong’s remark, he couldn’t see anything obvious. But when he forcibly raised his qi sense, he felt something approaching from the dense woods back there.

Tension tightened through his whole body in an instant.

‘An enemy?’

But that worry quickly eased—the sensation from the one approaching ahead felt familiar.

“It’s the Tang Young lord.”

As soon as Yoon Jong spoke, Tang Pae appeared from behind. The Southern Island people running after him were clearly visible too. One of the separated groups had successfully rejoined without trouble.

“…Wow, they really recognized it.”

“So it’s some kind of vision, right?”

Jo Gul nodded. The idea that only certain sects could recognize it made him oddly unsettled.

“Sahyung.”

“Yes?”

“When we get back, let’s make one of those too.”

“…Why?”

“They seem useful. And…”

Jo Gul lowered his voice, sounding a bit embarrassed.

“I’m kind of jealous, too.”

Yoon Jong snorted. The bastard had just been a wreck and barely regained consciousness, yet he was already talking about Mount Hua. His body wasn’t in good shape either.

“Alright, we’ll definitely make one.”

He swallowed the next words that hovered in his mouth.

‘If we can return safely, that is.’

While the two talked, Tang Pae fully joined them. He brightened at seeing Jo Gul conscious.

“Taoist Jo Gul! Are you feeling any better?”

“I’m fine!”

Jo Gul bent his arm to flex his muscle as a show, then suddenly twisted his face in pain.

“Ouch ouch…”

“Just stay still, you wriggling fool!”

“Well, the ride’s a bit rough…”

“What, you bastard?”

Tang Pae couldn’t help smiling at Jo Gul’s banter.

They hadn’t known each other long or talked much, but Tang Pae felt inexplicably glad Jo Gul was awake.

“…Quiet.”

“Oh! Sago, are you awake?”

“My head’s ringing. Be quiet.”

Yu Yiseol clutched her head with one hand and groaned. Jo Gul tilted his head.

“You’re kind of speaking like the old days. Is this regression?”

“Sahyung, do you really want to die?”

“Does ‘sahyung’ and ‘do you want to die’ really go together?”

“On Mount Hua, they do!”

“That might be true…”

It seemed others felt the same. Just his being conscious brightened the group’s mood, even though it was far from time to relax.

Perhaps this was Jo Gul’s true strength.

“By the way, where are we going right now?”

“I don’t know. That’s decided by the sasuk and the Green Forest King. Or maybe that damned Chung Myung set it beforehand.”

“Indeed.”

Jo Gul nodded as if he understood.

Then, as if on cue, Baek Cheon’s voice came from ahead.

“First priority is distance—forget direction. Because they focused their defenses to block us, this side is practically empty.”

Everyone nodded in agreement.

Up until the Ten-Thousand Great Mountains had been hell, but once they broke through, there was no one specifically blocking them. It might be premature to decide, but so far it seemed that way.

“If we can just keep the distance, we might reach the Yangtze river without issue.”

“…As long as someone doesn’t suddenly pop up ahead. Like a high priest of the Demonic Cult or something…”

“Ah, shut up!”

“Shut your trap!”

“Someone knock this bastard out again!”

Jo Gul chuckled at the flurry of voices erupting here and there.

They’d already lost many. But that didn’t mean they had to fall into despair. They could mourn the dead once everyone got out safely.

‘Somehow, at least to the Yangtze…’

At that moment, Jo Gul’s head tilted slightly crooked.

“Hey, wait a moment. But…”

“What, you again!”

“Sahyung, shut your mouth! Before I really sew it shut!”

“Ah, no, it’s not that… This mark can only be recognized by the Southern Island sect, right?”

“How many times are you going to say the same thing? Did you hurt your head?”

“No. I mean… then how does Chung Myung know and chase after us?”

“Huh?”

In that instant, everyone’s blank stares focused on Jo Gul. He fumbled awkwardly as he added.

“I—maybe I’m being dumb, but Chung Myung doesn’t know this mark, right? Yet he’s supposed to be all the way at the back.”

“…Right?”

“Then how does he know where we are and come after us?”

Jo Gul asked with an awkward look. Surely a bit of ribbing would follow, and someone would hand him the answer.

But contrary to his expectation, no scolding came. Instead, everyone glanced awkwardly at each other.

‘Huh?’

Oh… this… this isn’t good…?

Jo Gul looked ahead with one last hope. Even if these fools didn’t know, at least those two would…

“Oh…”

But at that moment Jo Gul saw.

Im So-Byeong and Baek Cheon—the two most reliable people responsible for the group—stood there looking at Jo Gul as if they’d been hit.

“That… is true?”

“…”

“How is he coming?”

“…”

You have to trust those you can trust… trust those you can…

Chapter 1353: That’s Just How He’s Made. (3)

Whooosh!

The surrounding scenery warped and stretched. Chung Myung, who had kicked off the ground, accelerated even more and shot forward.

‘This way?’

Chung Myung squinted slightly, changed direction, and accelerated further.

A distinct scent at his nostrils was guiding him.

As he followed the scent, conversations from the past flashed in his mind as vividly as if they were yesterday.

I told you I don’t need this kind of thing, right?

Just learn it like you’re told. Once you learn it, you’ll find plenty of uses for it!

I’m not a dog—what would I do learning how to smell things?

When it came to smelling liquor, you were nothing but a dog.

What, you bastard?

A low smile naturally tugged at Chung Myung’s lips.

Even through the thick scent of grass, the fragrance he clearly felt was none other than the Tang Family’s secret Ten Thousand Mile Pursuit Incense.

The incense used by the Lower District Sect or the Beggars’ Union to track people. Those trained in the corresponding qi arts never lose this scent; though it won’t be smelled from Ten Thousand Miles away as the name suggests, it can easily track for at least several dozen li.

‘Good thing I got it from the Tang Family lord ahead of time.’

Of course he had to endure Tang Gunak’s strange look when handing over the Thousand Pursuit Incense, but this was well worth the risk and trouble.

He’d hidden the scent on that slow-witted Dong-Ryong, so there was no worry about losing the trail. Just running after the scent would naturally rejoin him with the lead.

Without slowing his running speed, Chung Myung glanced down at his hand. He clenched and opened it a couple times and nodded with satisfaction.

Despite having fought for quite a while with a weakened body, he felt less tired and instead more invigorated. If his condition upon leaving the cave had been about seven-tenths of his usual, now it should be rated at least eight-tenths.

‘Better than I thought.’

Of course, in the past Chung Myung also benefited greatly from Taoist-lineage internal qi flowing through his body.

His ability to bear countless wounds and sometimes life-threatening injuries and still fight to the end was, when examined, thanks to the Taoist internal qi’s excellent healing and recovery properties.

But the recovery rate his body now displayed easily surpassed even those expectations.

Each time he swung his sword and drew up internal qi, the pure qi flowing through him pushed out dross and waste and restored his damaged body, as if it were repairing the house he lived in by itself.

‘Natural…’

The clearest and pure qi under heaven might mean internal qi closest to nature. Then it makes sense, nature is like that. Even if it crumbles and decays, over time it returns to its true form; isn’t that nature?

Yes, like Mount Hua right now.

Thud!

Chung Myung’s foot lightly touched the ground. Though he didn’t exert great force, the inner energy flowing through his leg lifted his body slightly.

It was a strange sensation.

His body moved leisurely as if on a stroll, yet the world before his eyes swept past him at extreme speed. When two mismatched sensations coexist, the familiar senses become entangled and blurred.

Whooosh!

Each step made his body accelerate faster.

Soon Chung Myung’s eyes, having reached speeds no longer visible, gradually lost focus.

‘Man is also part of nature, but actions imbued with will are artifices. Nature rejects artifice and returns to the primordial. Thus nature excludes man.’

Something indescribably deep began to fill his unfocused eyes.

‘But, if people are part of nature, then human will is also nature’s will—why reject that will as artifice? Things with will and things without will. Even animals’ actions have will…’

Thoughts flowed like water. The stream of thought, whose beginning and end couldn’t be known, soon flowed like a mighty river and soaked into him. His senses expanded and blurred at once; the world became both clearer and more faded.

The boundary separating me and the world kept blurring.

It flowed and flowed. But ultimately the current led back to the beginning.

The Taiji circle shape where yin and yang meet. But that form soon fell apart into a distorted circle.

‘A circle is equality. No beginning, no end, infinitely continuous. Life is an end and a beginning, a closure and a continuation at once…’

“Huh?”

Chung Myung suddenly opened his eyes wide. After blinking a couple times, his face contorted.

“Damn it! I’m losing it!”

He had just entered the realm of enlightenment. He couldn’t even remember the last time he’d stepped into that realm.

Gaining enlightenment is like crossing a wall. For a martial artist, it’s an indescribable triumph.

Originally, the walls faced when mastering martial arts are twofold: the physical wall and the mental wall. His body hadn’t reached past levels yet, but his mental realm clearly was going deeper than before. However…

“Huh?”

But why did I snap out of it?

Normally enlightenment doesn’t break so easily once entered. Usually you feel a lack at the end of contemplation and withdraw on your own. But now it was as if something forcibly blocked him…

Twitch. Twitch.

Chung Myung’s toes groped at empty air.

Only then did Chung Myung realize nothing had been under his feet for a while. The vast open sky stretching before him came into view belatedly.

“The sky…?”

The ground sprawled far below. Behind him loomed a sheer cliff from where he didn’t even remember leaping.

Wow, I jumped from there to here. I really leapt a long way…

“Aaaaaaaah! Hey, you lunatic!”

Chung Myung’s body began to plummet rapidly.

But no matter how he flailed his limbs, there was nothing to grab or reach. How could he catch a cliff now more than thirty paces away? He’s not the Monkey King.

“Eek!”

As the ground rushed up at terrifying speed, Chung Myung frantically emitted intangible qi. The recoil slowed his fall a bit.

Then a tall pine branch flashed into Chung Myung’s sight. At the sight of that thick protruding limb, his eyes sparkled.

‘I have to grab it!’

Pushing strongly off the air and rolling his body to the side, Chung Myung stretched his hand with all his might. The moment his fingertips touched, he snatched the thick branch at once.

‘Got it, got it…’

Snap!

“Huh?”

Kuuuuuung!

In the end Chung Myung’s body slammed face-first into the ground, trembling pitifully before going limp.

Thud.

A broken pine branch rolled indifferently beside him.

Thankfully he wasn’t dead; a breath hissed from his nose. Dry dust puffed up in little clouds.

“Uuuugh…”

He painfully propped himself up with both hands and lifted his face from the ground. Blood gushed freely from his nose.

The tree had snapped, but it had slowed his fall, so he didn’t die. Whether that’s something to be grateful for, he had to think on that.

“…Uuuugh.”

He set his twisted nose and wiped away the blood. The dark-red nosebleed dripped to the ground. Chung Myung sank down where he was with a woeful face.

“This…”

They say an unlucky man breaks his nose even falling backward—unlucky indeed, falling off a cliff even when entering enlightenment…

“If I get enlightenment twice I’ll really die and go to the underworld! Does this make any sense? Does it?”

I said I wanted to transcend, not go straight to the afterlife!

“Why does enlightenment come while I’m running?”

Chung Myung couldn’t help his regret and scratched his head vigorously.

If it hadn’t been for the cliff he could’ve grabbed something, but the cliff that appeared in front of him forced him out of enlightenment. The more he chewed over it, the worse he felt.

Of course Chung Myung knew he didn’t need to cling to that fact. Enlightenment that has once come will return again, so he could wait patiently. If you keep yearning and clinging, enlightenment drifts further away.

He knows. Knows very well. The problem is…

“Aiiiooo! It’s such a waste I’m gonna die!”

Chung Myung rolled on the ground entirely, foaming at the mouth.

If knowing were doing, would that be Chung Myung? Even knowing, his stomach twists—that’s Chung Myung.

Even if he advanced mentally a bit now, he wouldn’t immediately recover his past martial prowess to effortlessly shred Jang Ilso and the like, but it would have made things noticeably easier!

“Why of all places is there a damn cliff there! That’s why when people build paths they should just level mountains and fill rivers to make everything flat!”

Your Heavenly Excellency. That’s a Taoist.

“Kyak! Shut up!”

Chung Myung spat fire into the air.

“If you’re a person you should know when to butt in and when not to! You’re not fanning a burning house—whenever trouble starts you don’t know where to sit and always stick your butt in first!”

Gesturing angrily at the sky, Chung Myung flinched and stopped. He realized with a sudden nostalgia how long it had been since he’d heard Chung Mun’s voice so vividly in his ear.

“…Tsk.”

Feeling a bit awkward, he scratched the back of his head. Being human, after crossing the Ten Thousand Great Mountains his mood seemed to relax.

“Live long enough and you get all sorts of experiences.”

With a snort he dispelled the awkwardness and swiftly launched himself after the now much-fainter scent. His body moved like the wind again. Nothing was notably different from before, but the thread of enlightenment did not readily return.

He smacked his lips in regret, but he had no choice but to shake off his longing and keep running.

‘But…’

A strange light bloomed in Chung Myung’s eyes as he ran on in silence.

‘What was that?’

A current that flowed majestically like a river. Its start and end bent to meet each other. Like the Taiji where yin and yang harmonize. But those yin and yang soon broke down their boundary and became one.

It was the primordial circle. Or something that should be called the Void.

Chung Myung knew the concept of Void, but this was the first time he’d seen its form in the realm of images. This fact would surely guide him one step further.

But that was not what Chung Myung was thinking about now.

Just before being forced out of enlightenment, he had clearly seen.

A sign that the eternally turning circle’s flow was transforming into something.

‘There is definitely something behind it.’

It might be a place Chung Myung had not yet reached. No, perhaps something no one had ever reached, not even conveyed conceptually.

Understanding the meaning of that circle might open it. A way to confront an absolute might that couldn’t be touched, a method to contend with a godlike being.

His fingertips trembled faintly.

‘But…’

A momentary, inexplicable complexity gleamed across Chung Myung’s face. If his conjecture was right, that circle would be supremely auspicious, a deep, deep truth encompassing all he had learned and the root of the world. It would surely be so.

‘But why, does it feel ominous?’

Why did his heart grow so heavy despite approaching what he’d sought? What could be behind it? What was he destined to see…

“Tsk.”

Lost in thought, Chung Myung clicked his tongue lightly and shook his head.

“I get full of myself.”

Shaking off the unsettling feeling that had crept into his mind, Chung Myung put more strength into his legs with a fresher expression.

‘Thoughts later. For now, just do what needs to be done.’

His feet briskly hammered the ground, following the scent.

As he cut through the forest leading into towering mountains, a cloudless blue sky watched over him, warm yet indifferent.

Chapter 1354: That’s Just How He’s Made. (4)

When Jo Gul glared, Baek Cheon, Im So-Byeong, and Yoon Jong furtively looked away and bowed their heads.

“Are you people with any sense, or are you not? Yes!”

“Um⋯⋯.”

“Uh⋯⋯.”

“No matter how scatterbrained you are, you should take care of what’s necessary! How does it make sense to not arrange any way to join up, to leave them back there and come on without them! Yes?”

Of course, there was no excuse in many respects. Even those receiving the harsh words wondered what on earth they had been thinking to do such a stupid thing.

But⋯⋯.

“This is why I can’t relax my attention! If I take my eyes off for a moment they cause trouble, how could I be at ease, how could I!”

The fact that the one making that criticism was none other than Jo Gul pricked at their pride like a needle. Who would have thought they’d live to hear Jo Gul give them a scolding.

“I still thought you were sasuk, sigh⋯⋯. You should trust people who are worthy of trust.”

Baek Cheon’s head fell down weakly.

But Jo Gul, seemingly having left his pity and tears up in Northern lands, only pressed harder instead of comforting those slumped shoulders.

“Will just hanging your head down fix anything? You need a plan, a plan!”

He had a point.

In the end Baek Cheon silently glanced at Im So-Byeong beside him. Unfortunately, Im So-Byeong was of no help at all.

“Me⋯⋯ I missed something this basic⋯⋯. Me being on the same level as Taoist Baek Cheon⋯⋯.”

This bastard?

Baek Cheon glared, but Im So-Byeong looked truly despondent and didn’t register it at all. It felt as if his very spirit had left his mouth. Baek Cheon couldn’t press that face to come up with a plan.

Baek Cheon looked back at Jo Gul with a displeased expression.

“Uh, shall we stand here for a moment?”

“⋯⋯sasuk.”

“Huh?”

“This is the first time I understand why that Chung Myung guy loses his mind and curses whenever he sees sasuk.”

⋯⋯You really didn’t need to understand that.

“Good grief⋯⋯. Aaagh!”

Just as Jo Gul was about to yell, he suddenly clapped his hand over his mouth with a cry. A scabbard had unexpectedly slapped his mouth from the side.

“⋯⋯Quiet. My head’s ringing.”

Yu Yiseol lowered the scabbard listlessly and rested his forehead against Tang Soso’s shoulder.

“Ah, no, Sago! The fact your head is ringing right now is impor⋯⋯.”

“Quiet.”

“⋯⋯.”

Jo Gul, who might argue with Baek Cheon, couldn’t bring himself to argue with Yu Yiseol and stopped his scolding, pouting instead.

“Namgung young lord!”

At that moment Namgung Do-Wei and those he led joined them. The Southern Island disciples’ faces brightened at the sight. They had worried they might have gotten lost, but fortunately they had arrived correctly.

On the other hand, the faces of Mount Hua’s disciples paled a bit more.

“Sasuk.”

“Sahyung.”

“Dong-Ryong.”

“Which bastard was that just now?”

Baek Cheon glared. But seeing everyone’s blade-like stares, he quickly averted his eyes.

Namgung Do-Wei, who had joined at the front, asked straightaway.

“Has Taoist Chung Myung not yet joined?”

“⋯⋯.”

At that moment Baek Cheon understood why Chung Myung sometimes looked at him as if he could burst from frustration⋯⋯. Being oblivious is exactly what that phrase describes.

“He hasn’t joined? Surely there’s a plan, right?”

“⋯⋯.”

“You don’t?”

“⋯⋯.”

“No?”

“Well, for now⋯⋯.”

Just as Baek Cheon was about to say anything to cover things up,

“There’s no need to worry. He’ll come on his own.”

“Yes?”

Everyone turned their gaze in one direction. Within those gazes Tang Pae smiled faintly.

“Is there some method?”

“It’s hard to say, but probably.”

“⋯⋯Hard to say?”

Tang Pae awkwardly scratched his nose. He could feel it clearly: the Tang Family secret fragrance of Ten Thousand Mile Pursuit Incense spreading centered on Baek Cheon.

Tang Soso was no longer of the Tang Family, so she couldn’t use that pursuit incense, and Tang Pae himself had never used it either.

Then isn’t it obvious who drenched Baek Cheon in that incense without having to investigate further?

The only reason he couldn’t conclude the situation confidently was that they couldn’t understand how Chung Myung could have taken on the Tang Family’s pursuit incense.

If Chung Myung had learned the Tang Family’s cultivation technique and could thus wear that incense, it meant someone in the Tang Family had passed on forbidden martial knowledge to an outsider.

‘Father wouldn’t have done something like that⋯⋯.’

Tang Pae, lost in thought, slightly furrowed his brow.

Non-transfer To Outsiders is the Tang Family’s absolute rule that even the family lord cannot break. If Tang Gunak had taught Tang Family martial techniques to Chung Myung, he could be dragged from his position as Family Lord without a word to offer.

If it were the family lord, it might be something to try to contain; but what if someone who wasn’t the lord had done such a thing?

‘Even if punished by stripping their martial art and having the tendons of their whole body cut, there would be no excuse⋯⋯.’

But who would dare commit such a mad act?

It would have to be either a lunatic out of his mind or an absolute powerhouse warrior so great the family lord couldn’t do anything, otherwise impossible. Yet there is neither such a madman nor such a powerhouse in the Tang Family now.

‘There’s no way to know.’

Whatever he said could become a problem later. A single thoughtless word from him might inflict a fatal scratch on Tang Gunak’s authority.

So he could only hem and haw and smile awkwardly.

“Over there, young lord⋯⋯.”

“Ah, elder brother! Stop grinning like a fool and answer!”

Those who had been watching for any subtleness in Tang Pae’s smile widened their eyes and turned to Namgung Do-Wei.

Namgung Do-Wei added as if his patience had snapped.

“Are you mute?”

“⋯⋯.”

Do-Wei, your temperament has really hardened⋯⋯. If the previous family lord in heaven could see this, he’d be sad⋯⋯. Or maybe he’d actually be pleased⋯⋯.

With no answer coming, a rare glint of madness began to show in Namgung Do-Wei’s eyes.

“No⋯⋯!”

But then.

“What’s with the hostile atmosphere?”

“Gah!”

“Chung Myung!”

Chung Myung suddenly appeared like a ghost at their side, startling everyone. Taking in their stunned looks, he gave a small snicker.

“What is it? As if you saw someone you can’t stand.”

The Mount Hua disciples’ faces turned sour.

“⋯⋯You made it back alive.”

“You didn’t die.”

“Ah, that’s a pity.”

“You’re late.”

Jo Gul furrowed his brow and asked.

“How did you find us? I thought you didn’t know how to read Southern Island’s markings?”

“Tsk tsk. Kids these days. This elder has ways. That’s what experience is.”

“You’re the youngest, you madman!”

Those at the front began bickering as if they’d never been serious. The mood quickly grew lively. Gwak Hwan-So, running behind, narrowed his eyes.

‘Comrades⋯⋯.’

He realized that even as they traded harsh words, everyone was watching over the body of Mount Hua’s Sword Saint.

They meticulously checked whether his body might have suffered more damage while fighting alone in the rear.

Moreover, with the appearance of Mount Hua’s Sword Saint, energy returned in an instant. He hadn’t done anything remarkable after rejoining, yet his presence alone seemed to uplift them.

Just by being nearby he seemed to give strength to his sworn brothers.

‘Is that what comradery is?’

He didn’t want to admit it, but such comradery didn’t exist in the Southern Island. Relationships forged over decades were inferior to those who had only been moving together for a few years.

Gwak Hwan-So finally seemed to understand why Chung Myung hadn’t recognized the Southern Island as a proper fighting force and had treated them merely as something to protect.

“What about the sect leader?”

Baek Cheon shook his head at Chung Myung’s question.

“He hasn’t joined yet.”

“Why? It didn’t seem like anything had gone wrong, did it?”

Geum Yang-Baek should be the most skilled at identifying sect markings and trailing them. It’s certainly odd that such people would be the last to join.

“That’s why.”

“Hm?”

“Because he said that since he can recognize the markings best, he’d take the rear guard.”

“Hmm?”

Chung Myung glanced back. A peculiar smile bloomed at the corner of his mouth as he stared into the distance for a moment.

“Did you order him to?”

“As if I could order the sect leader to do that. He volunteered. He said that if anything happened he could join up immediately.”

Chung Myung nodded.

Until now the Southern Island Sect had either waited for Baek Cheon’s commands or resisted them. But this time they judged what needed to be done on their own.

It seemed clear they assigned the remaining capable elders to guard the rear and sent the young forward.

Those who had stood aloof with folded arms began to walk into the deadly path with their lives on the line, to protect what must be protected even at all costs.

The fierce battles from Southern Island to the gorge and onward to the Ten Thousand Great Mountains turned them into sword guardians rather than elders.

‘Now I can make use of them.’

Just this met the minimum standard Chung Myung had in mind.

“How about a way to communicate?”

“None in particular for now.”

Communication would come from their side anyway, so it shouldn’t be a problem. Chung Myung nodded lightly and scanned the area. After checking the condition of those running behind, he snorted a laugh.

“What a mess.”

“⋯⋯Honestly, it is.”

It’s certainly not a good situation yet. Many of the Southern Island’s disciples are wounded and have difficulty moving, and even considering the rearward personnel, their numbers have clearly dwindled.

Carrying and hauling the unconscious slows their speed greatly. Overall, they’re nothing more or less than routed survivors fleeing to save their lives.

But neither Chung Myung nor Baek Cheon were in the least disappointed by that fact.

In any case they succeeded in joining up, and as a result they had perfectly broken through the Four Sects Alliance’s encirclement and were charging south through the Southern lands region. The losses were great, but the victors of the battle in Guangdong were, without dispute, the Heavenly Friends Alliance and Southern Island.

Chung Myung’s gaze grew markedly serious.

‘If we can make it only to the Yangtze⋯⋯.’

From where things stood now, if there were no variables, there should be no major problem until then. Yes. As long as there are no variables.

Chung Myung’s gaze turned to the rear. A strange light flickered in his eyes as he looked at the jagged, ominous mountain ridges.

❀ ❀ ❀

Flap-flap.

The red hawk perched on Ho Ga-Myung’s wrist rose high into the sky, then shot off in an instant toward the far northern heavens.

Ho Ga-Myung slowly lowered his gaze and looked at the one running before him.

“Huff! Huff! Huff!”

Rough breaths burst from Yu Gong’s mouth.

From the moment he first spotted the marking until now, he had been running without a moment’s rest. Others only needed to run, but he had to run to avoid falling behind while also keeping his senses sharp to find the sect’s markings.

Naturally, his stamina was being drained rapidly.

‘Damn it⋯⋯.’

With his mind in turmoil as well, everything inside and out seemed to torment him.

It was then.

“There — your marking.”

Startled by the voice, Yu Gong snapped his head up. Sure enough, the Southern Island’s marking was carved into a large tree standing before him.

Just as he quickly began to interpret the sign and open his mouth,

“North⋯⋯.”

“It’s the same pattern as before. Northeast. Keep going.”

For a moment Yu Gong stared blankly at Ho Ga-Myung. Ho Ga-Myung, expressionless, instead stared back at Yu Gong.

“Is there a problem?”

“⋯⋯No.”

Yu Gong quickly averted his gaze. Ho Ga-Myung’s cold voice flew into his ear.

“You’d best not pull any stunts. Unless you want to see all the family you left behind torn to pieces.”

Yu Gong bit his lip until it bled. A great terror began to fill his eyes.

He was in Ho Ga-Myung’s palm. As if on a gigantic hand from which he could never escape.

Chapter 1355: That’s Just How He’s Made. (5)

“Huff! Huff! Huff! Huff!”

Sweat poured like rain. His clothes were already soaking as if caught in a downpour, and with every slap of his feet on the ground, droplets from his sweat-soaked hair plopped down.

His lungs felt like they would burst out of his mouth.

‘How much farther do I have to go?’

His vision went white, and the road ahead stretched endlessly, but he could not stop. If he failed to do his part, the thing discarded wouldn’t be just him.

Ho Ga-Myung had never issued a proper threat.

It wasn’t like the Evil Sect bastards had thrust blades at people as they usually did, nor had they growled threats about flaying flesh and killing.

But that made it all the more terrifying.

Because he knew that for them, wiping out an ordinary village where common folk lived would be no different from crushing an ant underfoot.

It wasn’t the sheer strength that mattered. It was terrifying that someone could commit such acts and feel not the slightest twinge of guilt.

So he had to run. The lives of those left behind in Southern Island depended on Ho Ga-Myung’s hands.

“Huff! Huff! Huff! Gah!”

Yu Gong, straining with all his might, suddenly pitched forward and tumbled. While searching for the marker above, he had lost his footing on a jutting stone in the ground.

It was such a ridiculous mistake it would have been laughable if not committed by a martial man—yet Yu Gong was that exhausted.

“Cough.”

Even his cough had no strength. His limbs had long gone numb with fatigue. Still, he forced himself to brace on the ground. There was no way they would pity his plight.

“Ugh…”

Yu Gong trembled and tried to lift himself up somehow. But all he could see were weeds growing wildly across the ground; his gaze would scarcely rise.

And then, in that moment.

Step.

Someone’s foot came into view. He flinched and lifted his head to find the Mind-Probing Inquisitor Ho Ga-Myung looking down at him indifferently.

The look in his eyes was utterly devoid of warmth, and Yu Gong’s ragged breathing stopped for an instant. After a moment, Ho Ga-Myung spoke.

“Rest here.”

“Yes!”

His subordinates moved quickly at the order.

Step. Step.

With that command, Ho Ga-Myung turned away from Yu Gong without another word. Yu Gong, as if in a trance, watched his retreating figure and clenched his fist tight.

“Here.”

A voice suddenly called, and Yu Gong turned dully. Beside him, where he was slumped against a tree trunk like the dead, a dew-flecked water bottle was thrust forward.

“Drink.”

Yu Gong stared at the bottle in silence, then raised his eyes a little. It was Go Hong. His expression was somehow odd.

“What are you doing, don’t want it?”

Yu Gong reached out and took the bottle without answering.

It was cold. He must have fetched fresh water from a nearby source. Not necessarily only for him.

Yu Gong looked at the bottle with a complicated expression, then slowly drank. Watching him intently, Go Hong dropped down a little ways off and sat heavily.

The two were silent for a long while. The distance between them seemed to mirror the distance between their hearts.

“How much farther do you think we have to go?”

Yu Gong lowered the bottle from his lips and tilted his head back.

He saw the night sky studded with stars like jewels. For some reason, he felt a laugh about to escape.

The nights in Southern Island he’d tired of looking at were probably far more beautiful than this. Yet back then he felt no particular emotion when looking at the sky.

Only now did he realize how beautiful that living sky was—how wonderful a sky they’d lived under.

“I don’t know.”

When Yu Gong answered belatedly, Go Hong let out a low sigh.

“In any case, resting like this only puts more distance between us.”

“Perhaps.”

Truthfully, he didn’t know. They couldn’t run forever either. Being human, they would have to rest at some point.

In the end, it all came down to who covered more distance in the same time. That’s what pursuit always was.

But Yu Gong didn’t bother to say that out loud.

Go Hong already knew it too. He would be averting his eyes despite knowing.

“Sahyung.”

“…Don’t call me that.”

Go Hong snorted and laughed softly.

“Why? Do you feel guilty?”

“…It’s not that it’s not true, but there’s no need to talk about guilt. You and I aren’t part of the sahyung system anymore, are we?”

“That’s right… Not only are we not sahyung, we’re not disciples of Southern Island Sect either.”

Yu Gong rested his head on the tree trunk and closed his eyes wearily.

What he really wanted to do was not close his eyes but block his ears. Lacking the strength to lift his hands, he could only shut his eyes.

“But, sahyung… it seems it doesn’t end that easily.”

“…What are you talking about?”

“Did you see it? In that gorge.”

Yu Gong squeezed his eyes shut tighter. This was exactly why he wanted to cover his ears—he knew what Go Hong would say and didn’t want to hear it.

“There was sahyung Myung Ho. I saw Ja-Un sajae too. Elder Gang-Myung as well…”

“…”

And why would he not have seen them?

He had seen with his own eyes the grisly sight of those who had spent decades together strewn dead.

But he could do nothing. He had only desperately run past their rotting corpses.

Because he had to live. Because he had to survive.

“The dead are just the dead.”

“Maybe… but they were alive.”

“What?”

Go Hong stared blankly into the air.

“When I passed through there… at least Ja-Un sajae was still alive. Even then his eyes moved, as if he recognized us. Even while his waist was half severed and he was dying…”

Yu Gong’s shoulders trembled.

Ja-Un was a sajae Go Hong was especially fond of. Just as Go Hong had trailed after Yu Gong, Ja-Un had clung to Go Hong.

“He looked like he was trying to say something. It couldn’t be heard or understood, but he was definitely trying to say something.”

“…”

“Sahyung. What was Priest Ja-Un trying to say…”

“If you’re going to spout nonsense, get lost. I want to rest.”

The sharp voice cut Go Hong off, but Go Hong didn’t flinch—instead he sneered.

“If I move away, will you be able to rest?”

Yu Gong glared at Go Hong fiercely for a moment.

“Isn’t the one who actually can’t rest the sahyung?”

“You…”

Yu Gong’s eyes twisted into something demonic.

“So what?”

He gritted his teeth and thrust his chin out stubbornly.

“So does that mean I should’ve become one of the corpses there? Should I have died a miserable death there and fed the beasts with my flesh to make it a ‘proper’ death? Should the gorge be left with maggots and swarms of flies because one man was gone for the ‘right’ thing to happen?”

“Sahyung!”

“Stop that bullshit! Didn’t you see it yourself? Those who stepped forward with all their righteous pretexts—what did they actually protect? While they died miserable deaths there, what did they protect? What did they save?!”

His voice grew increasingly heated.

“What could they have been trying to say? What meaning was there! I know one thing: however much the dead babble, nothing hears them and nothing understands them! That one died and we survived!”

“…”

“Guilt? That’s fucking nonsense. I will survive. I will never die such a miserable death. Somehow I will survive, somehow! And I’ll return to my homeland with my body intact!”

A venomous glint flickered in Yu Gong’s eyes. He looked as if he might tear into Go Hong at any moment.

“So… what’s so wrong about that? Is it such a great sin?”

Go Hong could not find words. He just bowed his head and shivered slightly.

Yu Gong anxiously gulped the water. It was plain water, not even liquor, yet it burned within him.

He knew it too. Everything was wrong. He was wrong and they were right. If one followed what he’d been taught and believed, that would be the correct view.

But what was the result?

Those who sought righteousness became feed for beasts over there. And he, who chose the wrong path, nonetheless still preserved his life.

If Southern Island Sect failed to escape the Southern lands and was wiped out, the most despicable—Yu Gong—would be the only one to survive and return home.

‘Go the right way?’

He kept letting out bitter laughter.

For what? For what reason must one adhere to righteousness? For what? To fill that petty sense of unity and satisfy oneself?

It’s all pointless, a load of dogshit.

“Stop the useless thoughts and rest.”

In the end, Go Hong brushed himself off and stood. Whether it was to humor Yu Gong or because he no longer wanted to stay by his side, he rose.

But where could he even go?

There was nowhere here where they could rest peacefully. From the moment they left Southern Island, they had become people who could not lay their backs on the ground.

Go Hong looked down at Yu Gong with unfocused eyes.

Blaming someone is easy. To resent and curse Yu Gong as the cause of it all isn’t hard. Compared to the suffocating guilt of recognizing one’s own responsibility, it would be much simpler.

But Go Hong could not bring himself to do that.

Because he still had a conscience?

No. He knew that directing blame at Yu Gong wouldn’t ease his heart.

He was simply curious now.

“What was different?”

At that small question, Yu Gong lifted his head.

“We left from the same place, learned the same things, lived the same life. So why are we here and they there? What on earth was the difference between them and us?”

Yu Gong’s lips moved.

It was obvious, yet a very hard question to answer. Many answers rose in his mind and fell again. None could be a complete truth.

In the end, Yu Gong gave an answer even he found absurd. A self-mocking smile faintly crossed his face.

“They were just… made that way.”

“…”

“That’s all there is to it, really.”

Go Hong watched Yu Gong in silence.

For some reason Yu Gong found that gaze unbearable. He couldn’t glare back with venom as before.

The indifferent look—not blaming, not condemning—hurt unbearably.

So he finally looked away. Even though he knew it wouldn’t change anything.

Step. Step.

He heard Go Hong’s footsteps recede and still couldn’t turn his head back for a long time. That look seemed as if it would remain there, frozen.

Only after a long while did Yu Gong lift his head listlessly. There were stars in abundance, as if spilling down. He resented the sky that simply looked down on their hellish world.

‘What was different?’

A hollow laugh escaped.

“How would I know…”

He covered his eyes with both hands.

The sky simply watched him and did not waver. It is not the sky that trembles, but the people who look up at it.

“How… could I…”

The self-mocking voice could not carry far; it dissolved at his lips.

Chapter 1356: So He’s Coming, I See. (1)

Before they knew it, they had left the cave and run for a full day.

Im So-Byeong, who had been running, abruptly stopped in his tracks without any sign.

Those who had been running hard behind him froze in alarm. Those who had almost toppled over staggered upright and shouted angrily.

“Argh! What the hell, all of a sudden!”

A chorus of complaints erupted, but Im So-Byeong paid no heed and busied himself looking around.

“Wow, he’s not even pretending to listen. We’re swearing at him like this!”

“They say the Evil Sect is used to being cursed, so this level of insults wouldn’t even register.”

“Still, when Chung Myung lost it before, his eyes went white, didn’t they?”

“…That guy doesn’t just curse; he stabs people with his insults.”

“Ah, right.”

Im So-Byeong, who had been looking around, soon nodded.

“We’ll stop here for the night. This terrain is perfect to avoid enemy eyes and dodge the dew.”

“…Here?”

“Yes.”

“No, do we really need to rest…”

Yoon Jong, who had reflexively tried to stop him, suddenly closed his mouth and looked back. The disciples of the Southern Island showed clear signs of exhaustion; considering they’d run carrying the wounded with their weakened bodies, it was hardly surprising.

“It’s important to rest while you can. If you recklessly keep running because you’re in a hurry, you’ll end up wasting more time in the end.”

“Understood.”

Baek Cheon also nodded in agreement with Im So-Byeong.

“We’ll rest here. Everyone find a place to rest. But do not light any fires.”

Jo Gul, who carefully climbed down from Yoon Jong’s back, asked in puzzlement.

“Why no fire? The Ten Thousand People Clan won’t catch up for a long time yet.”

“This is Southern Lands. Even if it’s not the Ten Thousand People Clan, eyes are everywhere. The civilians climbing the mountain aren’t necessarily on our side.”

Hearing Baek Cheon’s explanation, Jo Gul’s face soured a little. Baek Cheon chuckled and patted his shoulder.

“Do you think people who only saw a light from afar would know who we are? If the Ten Thousand People Clan asks ‘Have you seen anything suspicious?’, of course they’d say yes.”

“Ah, I see.”

Only then did Jo Gul nod as if he finally understood.

“Then we should be careful.”

“Yes.”

“Sahyung, what are you doing? Find a place to rest. If you dawdle, others will take all the good spots.”

Jo Gul urged Yoon Jong. Baek Cheon watched him and smiled wryly.

At that moment a voice with a hint of laughter was heard.

“You’re getting good at handling people, aren’t you?”

“Is that a compliment?”

“It means you’re becoming a con man in the end.”

“…Thanks for the compliment.”

Chung Myung laughed as he looked at Baek Cheon’s expression.

In truth, Baek Cheon likely suspected that even if the civilians knew their identity, there was no guarantee they wouldn’t inform the Ten Thousand People Clan. But he couldn’t say that out loud, especially not in front of someone like Jo Gul.

“You should find a place to rest too.”

“You worry about everything. Your nagging’s increased again.”

Baek Cheon didn’t deny it and gave a wry smile—the remark wasn’t entirely wrong.

The Southern Island Sect’s disciples scattered around to find places to rest, which mostly meant finding flat ground to lie down on.

Lee Ja-yang, who plopped down after finding a spot, stared ahead with an exhausted face.

“It’s too damp here. Go lie over there. Give the one at the back this spot.”

“Ah, no, great sahyung. You’re probably tired too…”

“Enough, hurry.”

Gwak Hwan-So kept moving around making sure the other sahyung-sajae were cared for. Watching him, Lee Ja-Yang let out a hollow laugh.

‘He’s really something.’

Lee Ja-Yang had no strength left to even lift a finger. Yet Gwak Hwan-So, who had suffered twice as much checking on the sajaes and wounded, was going around taking care of everyone else first.

Has he got energy left? No way.

Lee Ja-Yang could even see Gwak Hwan-So’s legs trembling. He probably wanted to collapse anywhere at that moment.

Gwak Hwan-So was just holding on because there were things he had to do.

“…He’s impressive.”

They say crisis tempers a person, and it seemed that saying wasn’t wrong.

In fact, Lee Ja-Yang had acknowledged Gwak Hwan-So as the great sahyung, but he hadn’t held him in high regard—he’d thought Gwak Hwan-So’s hot temper and selfish streak made him unsuited to the role of Sect leader.

But in the current Gwak Hwan-So, the flaws Lee Ja-Yang had seen before were nowhere to be found.

Across the harsh path from Southern Island to here, Gwak Hwan-So had become, unquestionably, someone fit to be Southern Island’s Sect leader.

Even though they’d faced the same crisis, Lee Ja-Yang seemed unchanged, while Gwak Hwan-So had used it as a stepping stone for growth.

‘He’s of a different caliber.’

Lee Ja-yang smiled bitterly.

There had been a time when he considered putting Gwak Hwan-So in the Sect leader’s position and nudging him to steer the Southern Island Sect to his will, but now he couldn’t even imagine it—how could he manipulate someone who’s grown like that?

He could only resign himself to doing as ordered, as if dead.

‘…That is, if we survive.’

A small hope quietly took root in Lee Ja-Yang’s eyes.

In the sahyung-sajae’ eyes as they looked at Gwak Hwan-So was steadfast trust—a look that had never been seen in the Southern Island Sect before.

It was unbearably hard and painful now, but if they could get out of here, have that Gwak Hwan-So become the Southern Island’s Sect leader, and have Lee Ja-Yang support him with all his strength, reviving the Southern Island Sect’s glory might not be just a dream.

Staring blankly at that scene for a moment, Lee Ja-Yang laughed in disbelief and took rations out of his bundle.

‘They’ve gone too far.’

For now, surviving comes first…

“Sahyung, do you have any rations left?”

At that moment someone approached and asked. Lee Ja-yang replied.

“Why? Didn’t we pack enough rations?”

“…We lost our supplies during the fight.”

“Ah…”

They had packed as much food as possible when leaving the Southern Island but after fierce battles, keeping supplies intact wasn’t as easy as they’d thought.

Especially among the wounded, fewer people still had supplies than those who had lost them.

“Then at least eat this for now.”

“S-sahyung, then could I have some too…”

Lee Ja-Yang’s face stiffened slightly. A few people quietly got up and began searching for food.

“Does anyone have any water to spare?”

“We’re out of wound medicine—anyone have wound medicine?”

“…This is bad.”

Watching the situation, Lee Ja-Yang’s expression grew increasingly stern.

They had thought they’d prepared well to pass through the Southern lands, but supplies were already starting to run out before they’d even reached halfway.

Normally they could have just bought more supplies with the money they’d brought, but not now. Where in the Southern lands could they spend money? They had to avoid people’s eyes.

In this situation, money was nothing more than useless baggage.

‘Even if other things can be ignored, lack of food is a serious problem.’

Of course, martial artists can endure longer without food than ordinary people.

But that’s merely endurance. If they couldn’t properly eat while exhausting themselves running at full speed, the result would be obvious.

“…I-I don’t have any rations either…”

“I only have enough for one meal; if I give this to you, what am I supposed to eat?”

“Has anyone hidden anything? This is not the time to be saving!”

At first there was just uneasy murmuring, but tension began to spread. If left like this a bit longer, a big problem could arise.

“Everyone…”

It was just as Gwak Hwan-So was about to say something.

Thud!

Something fell in front of them.

“Are the deer blind here? There’s people right here and they just walk past like nothing.”

Chung Myung tossed a hunted deer toward the Southern Island Sect’s disciples, dusted his hands, and chuckled. The disciples stared fixedly at the deer on the ground.

Baek Cheon scowled disapprovingly and nagged.

“Hey, I said don’t light a fire.”

“Ugh, nagging again! You must’ve been born nagging instead of crying. Annoying—shut up and watch!”

“Huh?”

Chung Myung slashed the deer’s hide off with his sword. It wasn’t the work of someone who’d never done it before.

“…You’re quite skilled.”

“Weren’t you a Taoist?”

“Probably.”

He then hefted a large-cut deer leg in both hands.

“What are you doing… huh?”

Baek Cheon widened his eyes. The deer leg quickly steamed white as it began to cook; Chung Myung chuckled triumphantly.

“This is Flame Yang Qi, Flame Yang Qi!”

“…Have you ever seen such a madman…”

Baek Cheon gaped.

“In all my life this is the first time I’ve seen someone cook meat with Flame Yang Qi…”

“No, sasuk. He did this before, up in the North Sea.”

“Really?”

If the people of the South Sea Sun Palace, who pride themselves on Flame Yang Qi, heard that, they’d be mortified.

While Baek Cheon blinked, Chung Myung remained busy cooking the deer leg. A moment later he casually tossed the well-cooked leg back.

“Eat.”

“…Yes?”

Seol So-Baek, who caught the tossed deer thigh in surprise, hastily shook his head.

“Ah, no! Me… others should have it more than I do…”

“It’s fine, just eat it for now.”

Chung Myung smiled faintly as he looked at Seol So-Baek.

“You did well enough. So don’t think useless thoughts—fill up on meat.”

“…Taoist.”

Seol So-Baek bit his lip slightly.

Though he was the lord of the Ice Palace, he was young and inexperienced, and it was hard for him to be a real asset in such battles. Perhaps knowing this himself, he’d been trying not to draw attention and not be a hindrance.

But that didn’t mean he wasn’t tired.

Chung Myung continued nonchalantly butchering and cooking the deer, and the aroma whetted their appetites.

The Southern Island’s disciples couldn’t bring themselves to ask; they watched blankly. When they received the pieces Chung Myung tossed, they expressed vehement gratitude.

“Thank you, Taoist!”

“Thank you so much.”

Chung Myung grinned and asked.

“You’re very tired, right?”

“…Actually, a bit…”

“Your body’s weak, right? You’d feel full of energy after some meat, wouldn’t you?”

“Y-yes! Thank you so much…”

“But you know what—I’ve tried it. If you only eat meat, it doesn’t give you that much strength.”

“…Hm?”

“There’s something else that really gives you strength.”

Chung Myung shoved his hand into the split belly of the deer and pulled something out.

A moment of silence fell. Chung Myung’s eyes shone as he spoke.

“This is the best, this is it.”

“…That?”

In his hand was the steaming liver of the deer.

“If eaten raw, this is a restorative that could make the dead sit up. Let’s all have a piece.”

“…You want us to eat that raw?”

“Come on, who wants to go first?”

Chung Myung cut off a piece of the liver and slowly approached the Southern Island’s disciples.

“Eeeek!”

The disciples of the Southern Island all recoiled at once.

Chung Myung frowned as if he couldn’t understand why.

“Strange. People eat fish raw, so why are you freaking out about this?”

“Th-that’s not the same—how can a person eat that…”

“Um…”

At that moment everyone’s gaze turned to Seol So-Baek, who stood behind Chung Myung.

Seol So-Baek licked his lips and stared intently at the hot liver in Chung Myung’s hand.

“Could you give that to me instead of meat? In the North Sea, raw reindeer liver is a real delicacy.”

“…”

“And… there’s been blood dripping on the ground for a while—why waste that? It’s so good for the body.”

“…”

“If you’re going to throw it away, give it to us! We can’t get enough of it.”

“Ah… all right.”

“Thank you, Taoist!”

Seol So-Baek lit up with joy. Watching him approach the deer, mouth watering, Chung Myung suddenly thought.

He realized that uniting the Heavenly Friends Alliance might be even more difficult and terrible than he’d imagined.

Chapter 1357: So He’s Coming, I See. (2)

“Supplies?”

Yoon Jong swallowed the meat in his mouth and tilted his head.

“Is it really necessary to talk about ‘supplies’? We have a handy fellow who hunts on his own when we need meat. Can’t we just make do by hunting now and then like we are?”

Yoon Jong glanced at Chung Myung, who was tearing into venison beside him.

Chung Myung was the best hunter he knew. With him around, the hundred or so of them shouldn’t have to go hungry.

But Baek Cheon didn’t seem to agree with that.

“If the world ran that conveniently, there’d be no need to rack our brains.”

Baek Cheon sighed deeply and began to explain.

“No matter how fast he catches game, hunting is still hunting. It inevitably takes time to find, kill, and return with an animal.”

“Well…”

Yoon Jong nodded; it was hard to argue with that.

No matter how quickly Chung Myung hunted, it still took time.

And there’s no guarantee he can hunt as quickly next time, either.

“Even if it’s fine for now, if the enemy comes within close range we’ll have to hurry on without rest. Do you expect us to hunt then?”

“You’re right.”

The enemy wasn’t only behind them, so there was no telling how many chances they’d have to put distance and rest like now.

“Southern Island’s disciples are already very exhausted. Carrying the wounded on their backs doubles the burden, doesn’t it?”

“Yes. Sasuk.”

“If they can’t even eat proper food on top of that, the outcome is obvious.”

Yoon Jong’s expression turned a little serious.

“We’ll need food that can be eaten on the move. But in that case wouldn’t it be better to make jerky in advance?”

“Impossible, drying jerky takes time… Besides, are you going to feed jerky to unconscious patients?”

A pained sound escaped Yoon Jong’s lips.

Among Southern Island’s wounded now, there are plenty who are barely conscious.

Such people couldn’t be expected to chew hard jerky.

Even the meat Chung Myung had just grilled wasn’t getting into the mouths of the wounded, was it?

“In the end, we’ll need grain.”

“I think so too.”

Grain can be roughly chewed and swallowed while running, and if ground into powder and mixed with water it can substitute as porridge, so it could feed the wounded.

“But where in these mountains would we get grain…”

The first idea was to look for nearby houses, but Yoon Jong had to shake his head.

Finding people living this deep in the mountains isn’t easy, and those who lived there usually made a living by hunting.

They wouldn’t possess the amount of grain we need.

Even if they did, it would likely be precious grain they wouldn’t sell for money.

Unless they planned to pillage, it wasn’t even worth considering.

‘But then how?’

Where, in these mountains, would they get grain?

“What do you intend to do?”

“We must choose. Either risk going down to the foot of the mountain to try to get grain from a village…”

“If we try to obtain enough to feed all these people down to the Yangtze river, we can’t avoid being noticed. Won’t the Four Sects Alliance pounce on us?”

“We’d have to take that risk.”

“…”

A deep sigh escaped Yoon Jong.

Solve one thing and another problem arises; solve that and another appears.

Waging war against the strong was like carrying water in a leaky leather vessel.

“If that was the only way to get grain I’d consider the risk, but I don’t think there are people who would sell us grain.”

“Why’s that?”

“Isn’t this Southern region? It’s ruled by the Evil Sect, so people are used to being cautious in everything. They wouldn’t readily hand grain to strangers who suddenly appear; they expect trouble later.”

Baek Cheon tilted his head as if he couldn’t understand. Seeing that, a bitter smile crossed Yoon Jong’s mouth.

Baek Cheon, who had grown up without want and never feared the powerful, would find it incomprehensible.

He couldn’t understand what it’s like to face the world as sheep living among hungry wolves.

“Anyway, buying all the grain at once is not easy. We’d have to split up and buy small amounts… that would take too much time.”

“Hmm…”

A look of worry settled on Baek Cheon’s face.

Being in enemy territory made even these small issues serious.

He could somewhat understand why many sects strove to expand land within their influence.

“Then what should we do?”

At that moment Baek Cheon frowned.

“What are you worrying about like that?”

“Huh?”

Chung Myung ambled over and flicked a now-smooth deer bone in front of them.

“If the problem is they won’t sell grain, why not go to people who will sell?”

Yoon Jong frowned.

“Didn’t you hear me? In this Southern land there’s no one who would sell large amounts of grain to strangers. They’ll test the waters before crossing.”

“No, I mean, why not ask people who don’t find us strange to sell it for us?”

“Where are there people in this Southern land who aren’t strangers to us?”

Yoon Jong paused mid-sentence and looked at Chung Myung.

“You wouldn’t mean the Ten Thousand People Clan, would you?”

Everyone’s eyes turned to Yoon Jong at those words. Startled by their stares, he waved his hand.

“Ah, no, that’s not it…”

“Sahyung, are you mad?”

“No, I mean, you idiot! Aren’t you always spouting crazy talk at times like this?”

“There’s a limit to crazy talk. Who in their right mind would go to the Ten Thousand People Clan to buy grain? Did you get a blade stuck in your head on the way here?”

“…”

He could endure Chung Myung’s reproachful gaze upon him.

What Yoon Jong found hard to bear was not Chung Myung’s look but the looks from others that screamed ‘He’s lost it too.’

Jo Gul gently patted Yoon Jong’s shoulder as he hung his head listlessly.

“I understand, sahyung.”

“…Get lost.”

Baek Cheon tore his gaze from the dejected Yoon Jong and asked Chung Myung.

“Then who are you talking about? Where in this Southern lands are there people familiar with us?”

“They may not be close acquaintances, but I know people who have surplus grain now and would sell it.”

“So who are they?”

“Didn’t sasuk stop by as well?”

“Huh? On the way?”

Baek Cheon seemed to ponder, then opened his eyes wide.

“Ah! Those folks!”

“Yes.”

Chung Myung snorted and said.

“There was grain in the village we passed on the way to Southern Island. It was the grain the Four Sects Alliance had given.”

“Indeed…”

They even cooked rice with that grain for them. Not much time had passed, so it was unlikely all of it was gone.

“Others might find us strange, but those people wouldn’t. They’re roughly acquaintances.”

“And it’s not that far from here?”

“Yes.”

“Hmm.”

Baek Cheon nodded.

Chung Myung dismissed it by saying they were roughly acquainted, but the relationship between that village and the Heavenly Friends Alliance wasn’t so simple.

They hadn’t expected payment, but they’d done them a life-saving favor, hadn’t they?

They’re not asking for grain for free; buying it with money shouldn’t be a problem.

“Ah, no. Wait a moment.”

“Huh?”

Jo Gul said with a worried look.

“But that village didn’t exactly have grain to spare. If we buy up their grain, what will become of the village?”

“Sahyung, are you an idiot?”

“Huh?”

“The villagers can go down to the city and buy grain. They might not sell to us directly, but there’s no reason they wouldn’t sell to themselves.”

“Oh?”

Would that really work?

“Ah, no. If the villagers buy grain in large quantities they’d draw suspicious attention…”

“Man, is this guy crazy? They can just buy in several places. We’re the ones in a hurry and must buy a lot, but those villagers can gather small amounts from many places.”

“Th-that’s right?”

Chung Myung clicked his tongue.

“You said the market had no grain, but that bastard Jang Ilso scattered grain everywhere, so the grain they’d tightly hidden will have come to market. It will be a bit pricey, but we can pay up.”

“…”

“We get the grain we lack, and those gentlemen earn more than enough to buy grain again—everyone benefits! It’s a win-win!”

Baek Cheon looked at Chung Myung in admiration.

Anyway, that man really has a knack for cleverness.

He’d actually use the only acquaintance we had in the Southern Lands… no, an embarrassingly slight acquaintance—in that way.

Anyway, as long as they didn’t leave traces of their visit to the village, it was a plan that could benefit both sides.

“Um, Chung Myung.”

“Hm? What now?”

“This is a little embarrassing to say, but that plan has a fundamental problem.”

“What problem?”

Baek Cheon said awkwardly.

“Well… if we do as you say, we’ll need quite a bit of money, but unfortunately I don’t have that much right now.”

“…”

Chung Myung looked at Baek Cheon with eyes that said, ‘What the hell is this guy saying now?’

“You didn’t bring money?”

“I took some when we left… but that money was given to the villagers then…”

Better not say he handed it out to the Southern Island people, thinking they wouldn’t need money on the return. That money-obsessed fellow would surely flip out.

“You have no money?”

“Ah, it’s not that I have none at all…”

It was when Baek Cheon tried to come up with an excuse.

“What nonsense are you talking? You worry about everything. It’s fine. It’s fine.”

“Huh?” Baek Cheon widened his eyes. There’s no way that money-crazed man would stay so calm upon hearing we have no money…

“We’re not the ones paying anyway.”

“Huh?”

At that remark Baek Cheon’s gaze naturally turned to Namgung Do-Wei.

Namgung Do-Wei shook his head as if he too had brought no money, and Tang Pae shrugged his shoulders.

Even Im So-Byeong turned his sleeve inside out to show it.

“Are you now going to even fleece mountain bandits?”

“It’s not even surprising anymore.”

“So you don’t think that’s more of a problem? Huh?”

In any case, none of them seemed to have money to pay.

“They say so.”

“In short, they’re good for nothing.”

“That phrasing seems a bit tangled…”

“Who told you to pay them?”

“Then, what?”

“The ones who will eat are separate—why should we pay? Look over there. Those who left home brought all manner of expensive and precious things.”

“Huh?”

“There.”

Everyone turned their heads toward Chung Myung’s chin gesture.

And they saw.

Exactly as Chung Myung had said: the rich man who had scraped together all his precious things when leaving home, just in case.

“There he is.”

“Seems so.”

“Fortunately.”

Now, having finally joined them, Southern Island’s sect leader Geum Yang-Baek shivered with a creepy feeling.

“W-what?”

Smiles of satisfaction spread across the faces of the Heavenly Friends Alliance members.

A smile that was exactly like the one Chung Myung wore behind them.

Chapter 1358: So He’s Coming, I See. (3)
