“…When practicing the Perfection of Wisdom, one perceives that the five aggregates are all empty and is delivered from all suffering and distress.”

The sound of sutra chanting echoed through the quiet room.

Beop Jeong, who had been reciting the Heart Sutra with half-closed eyes, slowly quieted his voice.

“…freed from all suffering and distress…”

Beop Jeong’s eyes slowly closed.

“Endure all suffering…”

The Heart Sutra: a roughly 260-character mantra that condenses the essence of the Prajnaparamita scriptures brought back by the Tang monk Xuanzang in some six hundred scrolls.

It is chanted countless times because it contains the essence of ‘emptiness’, yet precisely because of its weight it is also often taken for granted.

But recently Beop Jeong had once again been realizing the true meaning contained in the Heart Sutra.

“You see as much as you know… and you are opened as much as your earnestness.”

Clack.

The prayer bead in Beop Jeong’s hand gave a clear sound.

The world is full of suffering, and a Buddhist is one who endures all that suffering. So whoever is not prepared to walk a path of thorns should not call themselves a Buddhist.

The Other Shore is not beyond a turned head; it comes only to those who endure and persevere.

The river of suffering he is crossing now is merely the ascetic ordeal a Buddhist must naturally endure. So endure and endure again. Then in the end…

But sadly his true patience could not hold on.

“Abbott.”

At the voice, Beop Jeong opened his half-closed eyes and looked at the person before him.

His disciple, Beop Gye, was looking at him with a stern face.

“Are you really going to leave it as it is?”

A small sigh escaped Beop Jeong’s lips. He was frustrated by the disciple who kept clutching at a matter already settled.

“Did I not say so?”

“But, Abbott.”

Beop Gye sighed and then spoke again.

“Of course I do not agree with the methods of the Mount Hua’s Sword Saint either. He is extremely arbitrary and vicious.”

Beop Gye pictured the Mount Hua’s Sword Saint in his mind. Certainly, to them he was someone they could not possibly cooperate with.

“But it is an undeniable fact that he has done much for the people of the world. Judging by his abilities, losing him now would be a great loss, would it not?”

Beop Jeong only stared silently without answering. Beop Gye glanced around furtively and spoke again.

“Even if he is the Mount Hua’s Sword Saint, the opponent is that Jang Ilso. The Heavenly Friends Alliance’s strength alone cannot save him. But if we lend our strength…”

“It’s a finished matter.”

“Abbott.”

Beop Gye bit his lip slightly.

“Abbott, why are you so wary of him?”

Beop Jeong’s eyebrow twitched for a moment.

“You say I’m ‘wary’?”

“Yes, Abbott. I also know the Mount Hua’s Sword Saint is not someone to be defended blindly. But… I cannot help but think you treat him differently than others.”

“…”

“Aren’t you the one who said that even if the opponent is an evildoer, one must not abandon a grain of mercy and the will to inspire?”

“…”

“But the Mount Hua’s Sword Saint is not even a villain. Though his methods differ, isn’t it true that he too walks the righteous path? To face the Four Sects Alliance and the Demonic Cult, we cannot lose him, the pivot of the Heavenly Friends Alliance…”

“Beop Gye.”

For a moment Beop Gye flinched and could not continue; Beop Jeong’s voice was chillingly cold.

“What would you do if someone carried the wood and threw themselves into the fire?”

“Well…”

“I would try to save him.”

“…”

“But one cannot save someone who pours oil on people and sets them aflame. Saving that one would be to cast many others into suffering.”

“Abbott…”

“The Buddha showed compassion to all things, but did not show leniency to Mara, who deludes people.”

“Are you saying he is Mara?”

Beop Jeong shook his head.

“Not exactly. Mara is an entity filled with the will to harm people, but he would at least be someone who believes himself to be righteous. That is why he could run toward the fire without the slightest hesitation.”

“…”

“But regardless of intent, if the act produces results indistinguishable from Mara’s, then even showing mercy to him would be folly.”

A deep sigh escaped Beop Gye.

‘How did it come to this…’

He too was not without resentment toward the Mount Hua’s Sword Saint. He had thought many times that if the Mount Hua’s Sword Saint had bent even once, the world could have been much more peaceful.

But if they hoped the Mount Hua’s Sword Saint would yield, could not Shaolin have chosen the same?

Could Shaolin, not Mount Hua, have been the first to respect and understand their wishes?

‘He is someone who believes only in his own correctness.’

But isn’t that also true of Shaolin?

Beop Jeong’s face showed no change. Sometimes stern, sometimes gentle, it was the same face that held deep insight as before.

Yet Beop Gye could not shake the feeling that Beop Jeong had changed slightly. It was hard to pinpoint what, but he was certainly not the Beop Jeong he had known before.

“Let’s end this conversation here.”

“Abbott…”

In the end Beop Gye bowed his head.

No matter what was said, Beop Jeong would not change his mind. Even knowing that, regrets lingered—perhaps the vain hope that everything could be turned back and the right path found.

“Abbott, if…”

It was the moment Beop Gye, unable to cast off his last regret, opened his mouth again.

“Abbott! Abbott, are you inside?”

An urgent voice called from outside the door. Beop Gye turned reflexively and shouted quickly.

“What is it?!”

“Jang Ilso’s trail has been picked up again!”

“Come in at once!”

Beop Gye, eyes wide, sprang up as if waiting were a waste and flung the door open.

Then Hae Jong, who had been outside, hurried in.

“I have come to see the Abbott.”

“Enough of the formalities. You say his trail was found?”

“Yes! It’s a dispatch from the Beggars’ Union!”

“Tell me.”

“The Beggars’ Union reports that beggars hastily deployed to the Southern Land discovered a large movement of warriors. It was too dangerous to approach, but there was a splendid four-horse carriage drawn by four white horses, figures in white robes as if escorting it, and martial men dressed in deep crimson…”

“It’s the Hegemony Lord.”

Beop Jeong declared.

Of course, not everyone who rides a splendid four-horse carriage is Jang Ilso. But only one person in the world could ride a carriage guarded by the Ten Thousand People Clan’s elites and accompanied by the red hounds.

“Yes! The Beggars’ Union is that certain. And many warriors of the Four Sects Alliance are following behind.”

Beop Jeong’s eyes sank deeply.

“How many are there?”

“They say roughly two thousand…”

“Heh…”

Beop Jeong gave an incredulous chuckle and shook his head.

Two thousand…?

“The Hegemony Lord has resolutely made up his mind.”

At that strength, not just the Mount Hua’s Sword Saint but several respectable sects could be wiped away without a trace. Those in the Southern Land would struggle even against Jang Ilso alone; with two thousand elites guarding him, the outcome would be as plain as seeing fire.

Beop Jeong asked Beop Gye.

“What do you think?”

“…”

“Is the Mount Hua’s Sword Saint you imagine worth saving even if it means waging war against the two thousand elites of the Four Sects Alliance? Is that one life more precious than the over a thousand lives that would be sacrificed in such a war?”

“Abbott, I…”

“If he could be saved without damage, I too would not have hesitated. But this is not a matter for hesitation.”

Beop Gye could not speak and lowered his head. He had never imagined that Jang Ilso would mobilize such forces to face them.

If what they see is two thousand, how many are actually on the move? It’s impossible to even guess, isn’t it?

“Why to this extent…”

“He cannot be allowed to go alive.”

Beop Jeong shook his head.

“One cannot say that the choice to cross the Southern lands was necessarily wrong. I did not expect it either, so perhaps even that Hegemony Lord was taken by surprise.”

“…”

“But precisely because of that, the Hegemony Lord had no choice but to move.”

“Why…?”

“Because defeat is not permitted for the Hegemony Lord.”

Beop Jeong’s voice was colder than usual.

“For the Hegemony Lord to conquer Northern lands, he needs the absolute loyalty of the entire Four Sects Alliance. He must be able to command them like limbs. But the Evil Sect are not people who pledge loyalty to someone by nature. What made this impossible thing possible was none other than the Hegemony Lord’s absolute status—the myth that he never loses.”

Beop Gye nodded.

He seemed to understand.

Jang Ilso was the one who brought victory to the Evil Sect, long oppressed by the Justice faction. He even toyed with that Justice faction and gradually crushed it, did he not?

That is why the desire-filled Evil Sect pledged loyalty without complaint: he promised greater victories and more gain.

“But absolute status is like fragile glass; it can collapse with a single defeat or misstep. At a time when one must discipline the Four Sects Alliance and rally the entire Evil Sect to strike the Southern Land, what would happen if the Heavenly Friends Alliance’s Mount Hua’s Sword Saint led the Southern Island and cut through the Southern lands?”

“Ah…”

The myth of Jang Ilso’s inevitable victory would collapse in that moment. Then the number of those who view the ensuing war with the Justice faction skeptically would rapidly increase.

When intense heat cools, a chill greater than usual is bound to follow.

“Do you understand? If he had fled, we would not have pursued. We might even have let him go and he could have schemed strife between us. But…”

“I cannot let my inner chamber be trampled by filthy feet…”

“Yes. That’s it.”

Beop Jeong said calmly.

“I do not know why the Mount Hua’s Sword Saint committed such a foolish act, but he will never set foot on the Northern Lands alive—even if we intervene.”

Beop Gye squeezed his eyes shut.

‘It was a thought without meaning from the start.’

Now that that Hegemony Lord had so firmly made up his mind, the outcome was almost decided.

Seeing Beop Gye’s sullen expression, Beop Jeong clicked his tongue shortly. A strange pity also seeped into his chest.

‘Mount Hua’s Sword Saint…’

Why make such a foolish choice? I foresaw that arrogance would tighten around your neck, but I did not imagine such an ending.

‘It’s a bit excessive.’

He must be killed, yes, but two thousand… The Hegemony Lord is hardly the faint-hearted type.

“So where are they now?”

“The last place his trail was found was east of Jin county.”

“Jin County?”

“A small village at the southernmost part of Payang Lake.”

“I see.”

Beop Jeong, about to nod as if it were of little consequence, paused for a moment.

“…What did you just say?”

“Yes?”

“You said east?”

“Y-yes… that’s right.”

Beop Jeong’s eyes were shaken violently for a moment.

The southern part of Payang Lake—nothing strange about that. To encircle the Mount Hua’s Sword Saint they’d naturally move south.

But why was the spot east of Jin County rather than south? They would have no reason to move east, would they?

“Did the information sent by the Beggars’ Union say where they were heading?”

“I-I didn’t hear that far, but their direction of movement was certainly…”

Hae Jong, as if wringing his memory, was silent for a moment, then let out a cry and raised his head.

“Southeast! It was east!”

Beop Jeong opened his eyes wide, bit his lip hard, and then spoke.

“…Confirm it again.”

“Yes?”

“Go to the Beggars’ Union immediately and have them determine their direction of movement! Hurry!”

“B-but, Abbott. The Beggars’ Union can’t do more than this…”

“Tell them that even at the cost of sacrifices they must find out! No—I’ll go myself!”

Beop Jeong sprang up from his seat and ran outside.

“Abbott!”

“Abbott, what’s going on? Abbott!”

Shouts came from behind, but Beop Jeong ran as if he could not hear them.

‘Hegemony Lord!’

Beop Jeong bit his lip until it bled.

‘What on earth are you thinking! That utterly treacherous man!’

Jang Ilso’s image rose vividly in his mind.

Dressed in ornate robes, with bright red lips wide open, he erupted into a leering, obscene grin. It was a terrible laughter.

That hallucinated sound mercilessly stabbed at Beop Jeong’s ears as he ran.

Chapter 1359: So He’s Coming, I See. (4)

“Perhaps?”

“Yes!”

Hong Dae-Kwang nodded vigorously.

“Those royal beggars… Ah, no, the Beggars’ Union leadership interprets Jang Ilso’s movement this time not simply as an attempt to seize the Mount Hua’s Sword Saint, but possibly as a move to advance north.”

Tang Gunak’s face hardened rather seriously.

“If east…”

That’s possible.

If one takes the Yangtze river east from here, Zhejiang appears. And the ones who should be stopping the Four Sects Alliance attempting to cross in Zhejiang are none other than the Namgung Family; the Namgung Family is the hegemon of Zhejiang with Anhui as their base.

But right now the Namgung Family has lost half of its strength, and the remaining forces are staying here in the mouth of the river.

So in other words, the east of the Yangtze is a complete no-man’s land. If Jang Ilso decides to enter Zhejiang, there will be no force to stop him.

“How have the Nine Great Sects reacted?”

“Of course it’s chaos. Right now… orders have come from the Shaolin Abbott to track Jang Ilso’s movements at any cost, even if sacrifices must be made.”

A hollow laugh escaped from Tang Gunak’s mouth.

Jang Ilso hasn’t even arrived at a poorly defended spot yet, nor has he shown anything remarkable. He only made a show of heading east.

Yet the Heavenly Friends Alliance and indeed the entire Nine Great Sects are shaken. No matter how many people there are in the world, only Jang Ilso could overturn the world with such a trivial action.

“From the Abbott’s point of view, it’s like a fire has fallen on his foot.”

Han Yi-Myung asked Tang Gunak with a puzzled look.

“What do you mean?”

“Right now the Abbott probably thinks affairs in the south of the Yangtze have little to do with the Nine Great Sects. Admittedly the Southern Island was involved, but the Abbott has already written off the Southern Island Sect as a dead stone.”

Everyone nodded at that.

“But if Jang Ilso moves east, the situation reverses. The Namgung Family’s Young lord is in the south of the Yangtze, and if their main force is here, then it is the Nine Great Sects who must stop the northward Four Sects Alliance.”

“Indeed…”

If Namgung had still been there, the one in trouble would have been the Heavenly Friends Alliance. But with Namgung staying here, the situation is completely reversed.

Beop Jeong must now, by any means, prevent them from stepping into Anhui.

If the Four Sects Alliance crosses into Anhui, the next place that opens up is Henan, where Wudang and Shaolin are located.

This means that if Jang Ilso so wishes, he is moving to a position from which he can strike a dagger into the heart of the Nine Great Sects in an instant.

“Wait! Then…!”

At that moment Hyun Jong interjected with a face full of worry.

“The children? What will happen to our children? Won’t it become more dangerous?”

Tang Gunak shook his head.

“In either case it won’t become more dangerous. If Jang Ilso is truly aiming north, the children will be much safer; even if not, the encirclement will loosen.”

“Ahh.”

Hyun Jong only then nodded with a slightly relieved expression.

Tang Gunak glanced at Hong Dae-Kwang and spoke again.

“From the Nine Great Sects’ perspective the response is complicated, but when you calculate it, there’s nothing for us to really lose. Even if this is Jang Ilso’s tactic to induce chaos, we have nothing to lose.”

Everyone nodded at those words.

In truth, putting the complicated calculations aside, it was a relief just to see the Nine Great Sects—who always watched the other side of the river like bystanders—panic as if a spark had landed on their feet.

If Jang Ilso drags the Nine Great Sects into a fight and thereby cuts off the attention given to the special envoys sent for the children, so much the better. Even if it doesn’t go that far, the Nine Great Sects will no longer be able to stand by and watch across the river.

“Truly, it’s very like Jang Ilso…”

There is no one in the world who matches him at putting people in awkward situations and catching them off guard until they break out in a cold sweat.

“Namgung elder.”

“Yes, Tang Lord Family.”

Namgung Myung responded immediately to Tang Gunak’s call.

“Just in case, it would be wise to pull back the retainers remaining in the Namgung Family.”

“Understood. They have ties in Hebei, so I will have them pack at once and move to Hebei.”

“Will that be all right?”

“Even if the Young lord were present, he would not hesitate. It’s worrying that the Namgung base in Anhui would crumble, but people must survive first, shouldn’t they?”

Tang Gunak nodded. He thought the same.

“Alliance Leader. Then we will exploit that gap and head to the south of the Yangtze river…”

Tang Gunak turned to Hyun Jong to discuss what would follow, then abruptly paused and fell silent.

Hyun Jong’s face had gone completely pale, drained of color.

“…Alliance Leader?”

Tang Gunak called again but there was no response for a long moment. After a little while Hyun Jong slowly turned his head toward Hong Dae-Kwang.

“You said east?”

Hong Dae-Kwang nodded with a puzzled expression.

“Y-yes.”

“Which part of the east?”

“…Yes?”

“Where in the east are they heading? Not all of the east is the same east, after all.”

“Ah… Well… At first it seemed they were heading southeast, but now the direction has changed to northeast. That’s why the Nine Great Sects are in even more of an uproar. From the lower reaches of Payang Lake, if they take a northeast course, Zhejiang comes up.”

Hyun Jong’s face went even whiter.

“Alli…Alliance Leader?”

Sensing that something ominous was happening, Tang Gunak called to Hyun Jong in a grave voice.

After a moment, Hyun Jong’s mouth opened weakly.

“Nanjing.”

“…Yes?”

“If they move northeast from there, the place they will arrive at is Nanjing.”

“Y-yes. It would be around Nanjing. If they cross the river, Hefei comes up right away. Hefei is where the Namgung Family is situated and the gateway to Henan. It’s actually closer to Henan than Wuchang in terms of distance.”

Hong Dae-Kwang spat as he explained enthusiastically.

“If Jang Ilso aims to push north, there is no place more fatal than this. Perhaps the destination is Nanjing…”

“How…”

Hyun Jong’s fingertips trembled slightly. Tang Gunak couldn’t hold back and asked again.

“Alliance Leader. Please tell us why you are like this.”

“Nanjing.”

“…Yes?”

“It’s the place written in the letter Chung Myung sent… it’s where those children are heading.”

Tang Gunak did not immediately grasp the words, but soon the meaning sank in and pierced his mind.

“…You mean the children are heading to Nanjing?”

“…”

“Oh, no. Why?”

In truth, asking why won’t mean much.

The children’s destination would have been decided before Jang Ilso moved.

But for now Tang Gunak had almost nothing else to say.

“If Jang Ilso were aiming for the north of the river… then it would be less of a problem…”

Hyun Jong muttered, his face pale as if he’d seen a ghost in broad daylight.

“If… if not that, but he knows the children’s movements and is trying to block them ahead…”

You didn’t need to hear the next words to know what followed. The fate of the children in the south of the Yangtze would be essentially sealed.

The color drained from Tang Gunak’s face as well.

“Is there no way to contact the children before they encounter Jang Ilso? That creature…!”

Baek Sang, who had been silent, shook his head.

“It’s impossible. Even Baek Ah, as skilled as she is, cannot find children whose whereabouts across that vast southern land are unknown.”

Tang Gunak was momentarily stunned. Only now did the situation fully dawn on him.

Jang Ilso simply made one move east. With that single move he put an unremovable noose around the necks of both the Heavenly Friends Alliance and the Nine Great Sects.

Even the throats of the children in the south of the Yangtze…

“Hegemony Lord…”

The horrific weight of those two words pressed down on Tang Gunak’s whole body like a thousand catties.

❀ ❀ ❀

“Northeast, right?”

“Yes. I’m certain.”

The beggars hidden in the thick underbrush scrutinized those moving in the distance.

Surrounding the enormous four-head carriage in the center were the Four Sects Alliance members—indeed, those worthy of being called their ‘main contingent.’

The beggars’ spines were soon damp with cold sweat.

If they were spotted by those men, they would die with no dignity. Yet fear didn’t allow them to retreat either; perhaps the fate of the world depended on their movements.

“You go at once and report this.”

“And you, squad leader?”

“I’ll track a little further. The opponent is Jang Ilso. I don’t know when or what he’ll do.”

“Understood.”

The subordinate held his breath and crept away, quieting his steps. The beggar glanced back and resumed watching the Four Sects Alliance’s movements.

His gaze fell on the ostentatious four-head carriage—its splendor alone was intimidating.

‘Are they going to wage war on the world or something?’

A demon rides within.

A demon that molds and mocks the world as he pleases.

❀ ❀ ❀

“Hmm.”

Buried in a luxurious silk-gold bed and reclining at an angle, Jang Ilso lazily watched the scenery outside the window.

The scene framed by the square window seemed very small. But Jang Ilso felt no confinement at all.

Even if it wasn’t all visible to the eye, the panorama outside was entirely clear in his head—perfectly drawn and unfolding before him.

Those who cannot see unseen scenes and cannot move unless moved are not fit to seize the world.

“Alliance Lord. The beggars are moving.”

“Hmm… I detest filth, but…”

Jang Ilso, who had briefly frowned, then let out a suppressed chuckle.

“But that much must be endured. In exchange for bearing the nasty smell, I’ll leave behind much.”

Everything those beggars confirm with their own eyes will be conveyed to the other side of the river, without them having the slightest idea that Jang Ilso aided it.

“Now then… How will he move next? Hmm?”

Jang Ilso gazed out the window with eager eyes and opened his hand.

On his palm lay a vast world. The once-quiet realm slowly began to burn again.

“The Nine Great Sects, the Heavenly Friends Alliance, the Four Sects Alliance, and the Mount Hua’s Sword Saint…”

The fuel is amply gathered. Enough kindling to ignite a terrifying conflagration that would burn the whole world, and the one to set that fuel alight will be Jang Ilso himself.

Jang Ilso tore his eyes away from the small north-facing window and looked out the opposite window, which faced south.

In that instant, a cold madness rippled across his eyes.

“I’d better hurry a bit. If I delay anymore, everything will already have burned. Heh heh heh.”

A cruel smile graced Jang Ilso’s red lips. Like a cruel fate awaiting all under heaven, it was a heavily twisted smile.

Chapter 1360: So He’s Coming, I See. (5)

“East?”

“Yes.”

Baek Cheon glanced briefly at Chung Myung.

“So there’s really no other way?”

“Charging into Gu-gang would be suicide. We wouldn’t even reach the Yangtze river before dying.”

“Hmm…”

Baek Cheon nodded quietly in agreement.

It was certainly true. The mouth of the river was literally a tiger’s lair now, one of the most lethal spots where the Four Sects Alliance had concentrated its full force against the Heavenly Friends Alliance and the Nine Great Sects.

Charge and break through a place like that with only this number of people?

‘You’d be better off biting the blade and falling; at least that’s a cleaner death.’

Courage and recklessness are different. This was beyond recklessness, mere bravado. You couldn’t risk so many lives on a stunt like that.

“But there’s the left flank too. If we turn the direction at the right moment and aim for Yueyang, wouldn’t that be not bad? The left side has a narrower Yangtze width, so it’s better for attempting a river crossing.”

“Oh?”

Chung Myung looked at Baek Cheon with a new surprise. The look seemed to say, ‘For Dong-Ryong, that’s a rather good idea,’ and Baek Cheon’s face twisted at it.

“I’m your sasuk, you bastard!”

“Alright, alright. Amazing, sasuk.”

Chung Myung snorted and shook his head.

“The thought isn’t wrong, but you have to consider one more thing.”

“What?”

“If we head that way, the Four Sects Alliance will more easily notice our movements.”

“So the east is different?”

“Yes. With Black Ghost Fort annihilated, the eastern intelligence network has been completely smashed.”

“Ah…”

Only then did Baek Cheon seem to understand Chung Myung’s words.

Before the Evil Sect unified under the Four Sects Alliance, Southern land was divided into four powers.

The Ten Thousand People Clan ruled the southern part of Southern Lands, Black Ghost Fort the east, Lower District Sect the west, and the Pirates held the Yangtze river basin.

So the western Gu-gang Baek Cheon mentioned earlier was an area firmly controlled by the Lower District Sect. For those needing to conceal movement, there was no more burdensome place.

“Of course Black Ghost Fort isn’t exactly weak compared to the Lower District Sect, but their head has been cut off and their limbs broken. It’s hard for them to show the same grip as before.”

“Indeed…”

“And that damned Jang Ilso chose to gather everyone’s forces and leave the east unattended rather than stabilize the eastern situation. So the east of the Southern lands is a vacuum now.”

Yoon Jong, who had been listening in silence, quietly spoke up.

“If we could move only through the mountains it might be different, but if we eventually have to descend, setting our course east increases our chances of survival.”

“Yes.”

Chung Myung nodded.

“There is no other way. East is the only choice. We head to Nanjing without being detected as much as possible.”

“Nanjing…”

Baek Cheon hesitated, then asked.

“The sect leader… no, have you told the Great Elder Sect Leader about this?”

“I wrote it in a letter.”

“Hmm…”

Baek Cheon’s eyes sank into a grave expression.

Then there really was no alternative. They might somehow reach the Yangtze river, but to lead the Southern Island and cross the river they absolutely needed the Heavenly Friends Alliance’s help.

Since there was no way to inform them of an unarranged rendezvous, they had to head to Nanjing even if it cost sacrifices.

“Then it’s decided. Finish supplies and then head straight to Nanjing.”

“Yes, sasuk!”

“Understood!”

With a clear objective set, the Mount Hua disciples’ gazes hardened.

It was still bleak, but far easier than punching straight through the mouth of the river. With luck, they might reach Nanjing without revealing their trail.

“I’ll go ahead then!”

“Hey, you’re not fully healed yet!”

“You have to run to get better faster! Haap!”

When Jo Gul charged forward, Yoon Jong shook his head as if he couldn’t stop him and followed.

Watching them, Baek Cheon asked Yu Yiseol quietly.

“Samae, are you feeling okay?”

“Running is no problem.”

“Still, be careful just in case.”

“…Even if I don’t want to…”

Yu Yiseol turned her head slightly and glanced at Tang Soso clinging to her side. Tang Soso’s eyes were practically shooting flames. Yu Yiseol’s face grew unusually pale.

“As you can see…”

“…I was worrying about you for no reason.”

Baek Cheon nodded.

“Then let’s move quickly.”

He pushed off and went forward. As Chung Myung prepared to follow, a voice laced with laughter drifted to his ear.

“I wish things would go that smoothly, wouldn’t that be nice.”

Chung Myung turned and saw Im So-Byeong half hiding his face with a fan, smiling oddly. Chung Myung said bluntly:

“You were all but dead; now suddenly you’re feeling alive, huh?”

“Isn’t victory or defeat part of the ways of life? If you get beaten and just stay down, you’ll just be beaten again. You have to stubbornly get up…!”

“…You could just die and be done with it.”

Chung Myung laughed incredulously.

The Evil Sect men could be useless at times, yet stubborner than weeds at others. It was amusing that Im So-Byeong, who rejected being of the Evil Sect, showed more Evil Sect temperament than anyone.

Then Im So-Byeong spoke in an unusually serious tone.

“As you know, Taoist.”

“…”

“He won’t stay idle. By now, no matter how hard Ho Ga-Myung muffles the screams, he would have already grasped the whole situation.”

Chung Myung nodded without speaking.

It wasn’t just anyone but the Hegemony Lord, Jang Ilso. If such chaos swept Gangnam and he still didn’t know, he wouldn’t deserve to call himself Hegemony Lord. Such a coincidence would never happen.

As Im So-Byeong said, Jang Ilso would already have grasped the situation. And he would likely try to steer it to the advantage of himself.

‘So he’s coming.’

Jang Ilso’s face, breaking into a wild grin, flashed in his mind, a visage so intense no one would forget once seen.

“What do you think?”

“About Jang Ilso?”

“Yes.”

“Why are you asking me that?”

Im So-Byeong scratched his head with the tip of his fan.

“To be honest, I can roughly guess what Ho Ga-Myung is thinking.”

“Ah, you guessed but still got trounced?”

“That’s why I said ‘roughly’! I might not know everything, but I can know the direction, the direction!”

Im So-Byeong, looking glum, continued in a slightly dispirited voice.

“This time I got the direction right, but I couldn’t read him to the end…”

“So?”

“But Jang Ilso is someone I simply cannot read. It’s not that he’s numerically overwhelming; it’s that I can’t discern his direction at all.”

Im So-Byeong, speaking in frustration, glanced at Chung Myung and smiled softly.

“But strangely, Taoist, you seem to read Jang Ilso’s thoughts to some extent. Perhaps your wavelengths match…”

“Should I see if that mouth can still talk after being smashed?”

“Ha. Ha… Just joking, just joking.”

When Chung Myung bared his teeth, Im So-Byeong recoiled slightly, but he didn’t lose that brazen smile.

“Anyway, Taoist, what do you think?”

“How would I know what that damned man is thinking?”

“Yes, yes. It would be like me gauging Ho Ga-Myung. But you can roughly guess his direction, can’t you?”

“…”

“It’s important. It might even be a matter of everyone’s lives.”

As Chung Myung frowned to speak, Im So-Byeong added one more remark.

“Don’t overthink it; just tell us how you would move if you were the Hegemony Lord right now.”

“…First, I move east.”

“Why?”

“Because that way I can draw in the Nine Great Sects.”

“Oh?”

“Anywhere east is fine. If I were Jang Ilso, the last thing I’d want is a head-on clash with the Heavenly Friends Alliance and the Justice faction.”

“He’d avoid clashing with the Heavenly Friends Alliance?”

“Yes. If that happened, the Nine Great Sects would watch from across the river and reap the windfall.”

“But couldn’t that be interpreted as wanting to defeat them in detail one by one?”

“Picking off one by one is for dealing with those who can’t move, not for those who choose not to intervene.”

“…”

“If the Heavenly Friends Alliance and the Four Sects Alliance fight, that old man Beop Jeong will surely target the Four Sects Alliance. Then everything becomes so clear. The Nine Great Sects, who have been standing aside, will unite under Beop Jeong to strike the Four Sects Alliance, for glory and gain.”

Im So-Byeong nodded.

It matched his understanding of the Justice faction’s nature: they sit out for any number of reasons, but when a sure victory and profit appear, they pounce greedily.

Compared to that, the Evil Sect, who don’t hide their desires, almost seemed more honest.

“That’s the situation Jang Ilso would least want. What he seeks is, quite literally, chaos. To produce such chaos, he must stir things into confusion, and that means drawing in the Nine Great Sects.”

“Even though the Four Sects Alliance would then be facing double the forces?”

“War isn’t fought by numbers or strength alone.”

“…”

“There’s also momentum. He would see it that way. If I were him, I’d go east. Then I’d slick oil on the feet of the Nine Great Sects who’ve been holding back and set them ablaze.”

“And then?”

Chung Myung shook his head after a moment of silence.

“Well… I think I’m no sane man either, but I’m not as twisted as he is.”

“…Understood. So Jang Ilso will be blocking our path.”

Chung Myung nodded.

“Probably.”

“Then… it becomes simple.”

Im So-Byeong chuckled.

“They probably don’t have our exact position, and even Jang Ilso can’t seal off the vast Yangtze river basin entirely. In the end it’s a gamble: either we’re lucky and reach the Yangtze, or we meet them before that. It’s a fifty-fifty bet.”

Chung Myung’s gaze darkened.

Seeing that look, Im So-Byeong felt his guess hadn’t been far off.

‘A gamble with lives at stake.’

It sounded terrifying at first, but thinking calmly, it wasn’t anything new—they’d done this countless times. It might even be slightly easier than before.

“Understood. We’ll support you with that understanding.”

“Good.”

Chung Myung glanced at the Southern Island Sect’s disciples following behind. They looked very tired, but they were keeping up.

The situation hadn’t deviated much from what he’d expected, at least not yet. But…

Chung Myung’s face hardened strangely as he watched ahead. An expression passed that others didn’t see; his eyes began to boil with intensity.

‘What is this foreboding?’

He hadn’t missed anything; he was certain of that. Yet oddly he kept feeling he’d overlooked something, something crucial that mustn’t be missed…

He closed his eyes briefly as if to press the feeling down, then opened them.

‘Not now.’

Even if he’d missed something, there was no way to undo it now. What he had to focus on was forcing a path to survival.

‘We will cross the Yangtze river no matter what.’

So that no one else would have to die or be sacrificed.

Unconsciously, Chung Myung gripped his sword and a blue vein stood out on the back of his hand.

Chapter 1361: You Really Are Generous, After All. (1)

“This way.”

Yu Gong said in a slightly hoarse voice and turned to look back. Then he unconsciously bit his lip.

He only said, ‘This way,’ without even gesturing, yet Ho Ga-Myung naturally turned his body toward the correct direction.

As if he had perfectly interpreted the sign even before the words were spoken.

Yu Gong felt unbearably awkward about the situation.

It wasn’t fear that Ho Ga-Myung understood the signs known only to Southern Island.

No matter that Southern Island’s swordsmanship was the medium; the rest was a matter of interpretation. By seeing them repeatedly, understanding which sword marks correspond to which directions wouldn’t be difficult.

The reason Yu Gong felt uneasy was that his value was steadily diminishing. If Ho Ga-Myung came to fully interpret Southern Island’s signs, he would be useless.

Then what would Ho Ga-Myung be like at that point?

“You look deep in thought.”

At Ho Ga-Myung’s words, Yu Gong flinched and turned back. His face remained expressionless, making it hard to read his thoughts.

“Worried you’ll be discarded when you outlive your usefulness?”

The remark struck a nerve in Yu Gong.

“No need to think that. If someone uses people and then discards them when they’re useless, that’s more the Justice faction than the Evil Sect.”

Yu Gong flinched and watched Ho Ga-Myung again. Even though he had never said those thoughts aloud, Ho Ga-Myung seemed to see right through him.

“The Evil Sect doesn’t throw people away.”

“…You mean you people?”

“It’s obvious. You may divide people into useful and useless, but in the Evil Sect there is no such distinction. More than half are trash anyway.”

Yu Gong fell silent. He didn’t know how to respond.

“So even if someone is trash, if they show effort there’s no reason to discard them. Even if they’re traitors to the Justice faction.”

“…Am I supposed to be grateful?”

When Yu Gong asked, a faint smile flickered at Ho Ga-Myung’s mouth.

“A strange reaction.”

“…”

“It was you who chose betrayal. No one forced you.”

Yu Gong bit his lip hard.

“If you want something, shouldn’t you only think about how to get that one thing? If you bother considering every other nuisance, you’ll lose everything.”

Ho Ga-Myung nodded toward the front.

“So stop wasting thoughts and focus on your task. Your usefulness is decided by me, not you.”

“…Understood.”

Yu Gong sighed and was about to quicken his pace again.

“And.”

“…Yes?”

Swoosh!

Ho Ga-Myung’s flying strike brushed past Yu Gong’s cheek. The blade-like force left a long torn wound on his face.

Drip.

Red blood ran down his cheek and fell from his chin.

“If you’re going to do it, do it properly. If you find something out, don’t hide it—report it. For example, you might note their headings are slightly skewed, suggesting a new destination, or that the spacing of their marks has tightened, implying their movement has slowed.”

“…”

“You just report what you’ve learned. Interpretation is my job.”

Yu Gong squeezed his eyes shut.

“…Understood.”

“Increase the pace.”

Yu Gong gritted his teeth and moved forward. A strange look of contempt and sneer flashed across Ho Ga-Myung’s face.

‘Pathetic.’

Yu Gong was an inexcusable traitor.

Maybe at first he acted to save others, but now he was reliably trading his comrades to survive. Yet he still pretended some shred of conscience remained.

From the moment he betrayed his sect and sold out his comrades, discussing conscience was meaningless.

Ho Ga-Myung, who had been staring at Yu Gong’s back, soon lost interest. He was nothing but a hunting dog; there was no need to probe his psyche.

‘The important thing is them.’

The enemies’ movements had shifted subtly, but still within expectations. That meant they hadn’t yet noticed they were following Southern Island’s marks and interpreting them.

‘No, even if they did, it wouldn’t matter.’

With Jang Ilso already in motion, their fate was set. Ho Ga-Myung’s task was merely to buy Jang Ilso a little more time.

Having ordered everyone to increase speed, Ho Ga-Myung pushed off the ground and moved forward.

❀ ❀ ❀

Elder Hyung, the village head of Xingjian Village, watched the sinking sun. Beside him, Hyung Euk, who had been watching too, spoke softly.

“Looks like not today.”

At that, Elder Hyung looked at Hyung Euk blankly. Sensing the meaning in his father’s gaze, Hyung Euk bowed his head deeply.

“It’s time to start unpacking slowly.”

“Father. Not yet…”

“Are you still clinging to hope?”

Hyung Euk lowered his head even further. Though his gesture suggested obedience, his voice still held lingering hope.

“Only a few days have passed. You know it wouldn’t be strange if it took not ten days but a month to come from the Northern lands to here, don’t you?”

“That’s talk for simple folk like us. Would those riverland men who can cross a river in a single bound or leap a mountain when they will take more than ten days to get here?”

“I hear that the Four Sects Alliance has control over that river. So crossing it…”

“Tsk, tsk.”

Elder Hyung clicked his tongue before Hyung Euk finished. At that, Hyung Euk let out a deep sigh.

“Why are you being so foolishly sentimental?”

“…”

“Of course I’m grateful. If not for them I wouldn’t be alive now, and the villagers would have suffered. How could words express that gratitude?”

Hyung Euk nodded at that. A life saved can’t be repaid by anything.

“But, my son, you mustn’t expect more than that.”

“…”

“Not because they’re bad—they are unparalleled saints. But people are naturally such.”

“Those people were different.”

The old man shook his head at Hyung Euk’s words.

“Even an ordinary person would help a rabbit fallen into a snake’s hole at first sight.”

“…”

“But they won’t go out of their way to follow the rabbit to check for injuries and secure a living for it. Isn’t that only natural?”

“But…”

“To such people, folk like us are exactly that rabbit. They may be charitable if we appear before them, but they have no reason to spend time protecting or supporting us.”

“Father, those people…”

“Why can’t you understand? It’s not that they’re bad; that’s just how the world is.”

“…”

“So stop it now. The villagers are failing to do their work because of pointless hopes. To settle your heart in a place that may soon be left is not easy. Your expectations could poison the village.”

Hyung Euk looked at his father with a complex, indescribable feeling.

His father had been the head of Xingjian Village for decades—before he was born, when he was young and ignorant of the world, and up till now.

So his view of the world was far deeper than his son’s.

But Hyung Euk, who would normally bow and accept his father’s judgment, couldn’t easily give in on this matter.

“I understand what you’re saying, Father, and I know it makes sense.”

“…”

“But… Father. Those people were truly different. I…”

A deep sigh escaped Hyung Euk. He still wanted to believe the people’s promise to help the villagers move to the Northern Lands.

“Please wait a little longer. Something will surely happen.”

“Hmm.”

Elder Hyung cast a pitying look at his son.

Everyone harbors such hopes at times—yearning upon hearing tales of heroes who save the helpless.

He too had told Hyung Euk stories of legendary wandering heroes when he was young, saying he should grow up to be like them.

But the greater the expectation, the greater the disappointment.

‘In the end it’s poison…’

Stories are just stories. If such people were common—or even a few existed—the world wouldn’t be so harsh.

But Elder Hyung could say no more.

Once hope is abandoned, all that remains for the villagers are days of silent survival under the brutal rule of the Four Sects Alliance.

How easy is it to tell those who cling to dreams in hard times that their dreams are worthless?

So he could only wait for the villagers to give up and accept reality.

The old man said with a sigh.

“Yes. Do that. But… you can’t just sit idle forever, so it’s time to…”

It was at that moment.

“Vi-village head! Hey, Hyung Euk!”

Suddenly the door flew open and a burly man rushed in, panting.

“Wha—what’s the matter?”

“Has something happened?”

“Th-they’re here! They’re here!”

“They?”

“No, um…”

The man who entered was so surprised he stammered, then, frustrated, pounded his chest.

“Ah, you know—the ones you’ve been waiting for!”

“Waiting? I mean the people coming from the Northern Lands…”

“Yes! The ones who called them!”

Though his words came out jumbled, Hyung Euk immediately understood.

“Are you saying those wandering heroes who visited before have returned?”

“Right! Right!”

“Why would they?”

“They’re right out front now!”

“Huh?”

Hyung Euk blinked in confusion, and the man, impatient, raised his voice again.

“I’m telling you they’re back! They’re out front looking for you! What are you standing around for? Go see!”

Unable to contain his impatience, the man grabbed Hyung Euk’s arm and pulled him.

“Th-they’re here?”

“I said they are!”

“Get out of the way!”

Snapping awake, Hyung Euk shoved the man aside and dashed out. At the village entrance he indeed saw a few figures.

“Ah…”

For a moment he almost didn’t recognize them—their appearance had changed so much since the first meeting.

At first they had seemed neat and heroic, but now they looked haggard, as if they’d endured unimaginable hardship.

But their eyes were unchanged from the first time.

“Hey there.”

The slightly smaller man standing beside the tall fellow smirked and waved.

“Mister, have you been well?”

“H-heroes!”

Color returned to Hyung Euk’s face.

His father’s words were wrong. They hadn’t forgotten the villagers—that’s why they had come back.

“Excuse me, could we possibly have a meal?”

Taken aback by the sudden request, Hyung Euk nodded eagerly.

“Of course! Naturally we’ll have you! Come inside. I’ll have them prepare it right away!”

“Really?”

“Of course. Who would skimp on grain for lives they saved?”

“Well… they have fairly large appetites; is that alright?”

“Absolutely!”

“They really eat a lot.”

“No problem at all.”

As Hyung Euk answered, the slightly smaller man, Chung Myung, turned and said.

“It’s fine! Everyone, come out.”

As soon as he finished, a group of warriors emerged from the woods behind them.

The men who approached hesitated but counted at least over a hundred by rough estimate. Hyung Euk’s mouth fell open.

“…Is that okay?”

“…”

“Ha. You really are generous. We’ll eat well.”

Hyung Euk was speechless for a while. Chung Myung’s face felt… maddeningly infuriating.

Chapter 1362: You Really Are Generous, After All. (2)

Click. Click.

Gulp.

Click. Click. Click. Click.

……gulp.

Clank! Clank! Clank!

“Um…”

The villager sitting in front of the large cauldron couldn’t stand it and quietly looked back. Those holding spoons and empty bowls all stood there drooling and swallowing hard.

“It should steam a little longer…”

“You can eat it even if it hasn’t steamed fully…”

“Still, well you know, rice needs to steam…”

“You can even eat raw rice if needed.”

The villager’s face twisted oddly.

“Ahem.”

Geum Yang-Baek, watching the situation, coughed awkwardly with an embarrassed look.

“Um… is it not okay to eat it now?”

“You can eat it, but if you haven’t had food for a long time, you might get sick from eating something undercooked…”

“Our boys are warriors, so I think they’ll be fine.”

At that the villager reluctantly nodded.

“Yes, well, if that’s the case… then give me the bowls…”

“You may eat!”

“Move!”

“I’m standing up front! Step back!”

“Aah! Who is that? Someone’s grabbing my face!”

The Southern Island Sect’s disciples lunged at the cauldron like piranhas. White steam billowed massively from the opened lid, but rather than retreating from the heat, Southern Island Sect’s disciples charged more fiercely and scooped rice with the bowls.

“Ah, my goodness, what is this…”

The villager, half pushed over, blinked and stared at the scene unfolding before him.

They said they were honored guests from the South Sea, but this is no different from a bunch of Beggars’ Union ruffians. No, even those Beggars’ Union scoundrels would have backed off seeing this.

“Aah! I told you not to shove me!”

“Please move!”

“Hey, someone took it all! Do you want to die?”

Faced with that indescribably dreadful sight, the villager shook his head in despair and said.

“Um… We have several cauldrons going, so if you wait a little longer…”

“Right! We can eat that!”

“Huh?”

Southern Island Sect’s disciples then ran toward the other cauldrons. Though the firewood blazed under the pots, such infernos were no more than a cozy hearth to them now and posed no obstacle.

The lids of the boiling cauldrons lifted one after another, sending white steam billowing across the village.

It looked as if the village had been raided by bandits.

Hyung Euk, watching the sight, blankly turned to Baek Cheon. Baek Cheon quietly avoided his gaze and coughed awkwardly.

“…they’ve been starving a lot…”

“…”

“Please try to understand them. They’re not usually like this.”

“…Yes.”

Baek Cheon awkwardly brushed his flushed face. He hadn’t expected those Southern Island Sect fellows to throw off all semblances of decorum so quickly.

Those who had scooped rice sat down right where they were and shoved rice into their mouths without even looking for proper seats.

“Ahh. This is the taste of being alive.”

“After so long of only eating inedible meat, I thought I was done for.”

“A person needs to eat proper food to live!”

“…If only there were a grilled fish, that’d be perfect.”

“Where are you spouting nonsense about being full? You don’t get to eat!”

“No! Who said they’re full? Give me back my rice!”

Lee Ja-Yang, watching the scene, sighed deeply.

‘Still, at least they didn’t sit on the floor.’

The sahyung-sajaes of the Southern Island Sect sitting down on the village floor and practically scooping rice with their hands—he shuddered at who might see this…

“Ja-Yang sahyung. If you won’t eat this, may I…”

“Don’t touch my rice! I’ll chop your hand off!”

“…”

An awkward silence hung in the air. Lee Ja-Yang deliberately avoided his sajae’s gaze and coughed.

…Embarrassment is one thing, but hunger is hunger.

He awkwardly picked up his bowl to eat, but noticed Gwak Hwan-So still hadn’t served himself rice.

“Sahyung, please have something to eat.”

“Mm. I’ll eat a bit later.”

“Later? Do you think your rice will still be there by then?”

Lee Ja-Yang snapped, and Gwak Hwan-So gave a small chuckle.

Gwak Hwan-So’s gaze was fixed on the pot placed in the far corner. Noticing this, Lee Ja-Yang let out a small sigh.

That pot wasn’t for cooking rice—it was boiling gruel for the wounded.

“…I know your intention, but you should eat first and gather strength so you can treat the wounded.”

“I know. I’ll make sure to eat, so don’t worry.”

Lee Ja-Yang’s expression somehow turned sour.

‘I never would have thought…’

He had been preoccupied with maintaining Southern Island Sect’s image in front of others, while Gwak Hwan-So, in the meantime, tended to the wounded who couldn’t eat properly.

These were people even the sect leader had failed to pay attention to.

By now there was no need to argue who had the bigger heart between him and Gwak Hwan-So. And…

“Sahyung! Try this.”

“If the rice isn’t great, how about this? The villagers offered it; it’s good for a quick bite.”

“What are you saying! You should have a proper meal!”

“Ah, no. It’s just that you said you wouldn’t eat… you can always have a meal later…”

Other Southern Island Sect disciples, hearing that the great sahyung wasn’t eating, put down their bowls and noisily gathered around Gwak Hwan-So.

“I’m fine, so finish your meals first.”

“How can we eat when the great sahyung isn’t eating…?”

“Don’t worry about me. I still have strength.”

“Still…”

It wasn’t merely because of his title as senior sahyung. Ungodly as it sounds, even if Southern Island Sect’s sect leader Geum Yang-Baek had done the same, it would’ve been hard to provoke this kind of response.

They’d endured hardships to get here, and now everyone knew how much Gwak Hwan-So cared for them and how much he’d given up for their sake. So without being told, they naturally worried for and cared for him.

Gwak Hwan-So smiled at the crowd that had gathered.

“You lot, what poor tact. If you eat now you’ll only get coarse grains, right?”

“Huh?”

“If you eat what the patients would eat, you’ll get the fine white rice—so it’s to your advantage.”

“Oh, is that what you were thinking?”

“Wow… so this is our great sahyung.”

His joke brightened the atmosphere instantly.

“So just finish what you’re eating. And make sure to look after the Heavenly Friends Alliance folks too.”

“Yes. We will, sahyung.”

❀ ❀ ❀

After checking the yard, Baek Cheon entered the village head’s house and stared at the old man sitting before him.

The old man bowed deeply to Baek Cheon.

“First… I’m not sure of the details since I wasn’t conscious, but I’ve heard you saved and treated a fool like me. How can I ever repay this kindness…?”

“Please don’t mention it.”

Baek Cheon scratched his cheek with a slightly embarrassed look.

“I should have let that matter end there, but it’s embarrassing to intrude again so shamelessly.”

“What are you saying! I’m the one who should be grateful that you allowed us to repay even a little of that great kindness.”

The elder repeatedly kowtowed to Baek Cheon.

Hyung Euk watched the display and couldn’t help but furrow his brow slightly.

Until just a moment ago, his father hadn’t seemed so friendly toward them. Though he kept saying he was grateful for saving lives, there was an undeniable subtle distance in his heart.

But now the elder treated Baek Cheon as if he were a deity descended from heaven. The excessive kowtowing felt inwardly uncomfortable.

Then the elder, who had been bowing as if to smash his forehead to the ground, quietly checked Baek Cheon’s expression and began to speak.

“Um, by the way… did you say you needed grain?”

“Yes. That’s right.”

Baek Cheon quietly nodded.

“It would be proper to explain the situation in detail, but unfortunately we can’t stay here long.”

“…”

“We will pay a fair price. So please share the grain you have.”

Baek Cheon bowed deeply in thanks. Hyung Euk, who was beside the elder, was startled and tried to stop him.

“Hey, warrior, why are you doing this! Of course we should give grain! What kind of payment are you talking about for sharing grain with the ones who saved our lives? Please don’t do this…”

“You stay quiet.”

But the elder gently hushed Hyung Euk. Flustered, Hyung Euk said.

“No, Father. This is only natural…”

“I said be quiet!”

Hyung Euk was visibly taken aback and stared blankly at the elder. But his father’s gaze was sterner and colder than usual, so he had no choice but to bow. After all, wasn’t his father the village head?

“Ahem.”

The elder gave a small cough, as if to break the awkward atmosphere.

“I’m sorry.”

“Not at all.”

“Um…”

The elder, watching Baek Cheon’s reaction, opened his mouth.

“I understand what you’ve said. We’re only human—we should naturally do that.”

“Thank you for understanding.”

“However…”

The elder let out a deep sigh.

“As you may know, warrior, this year a severe drought ruined the crops and we ourselves have no way to survive. If you take our grain, we have no means to get through the winter.”

“I know. That’s why we intend to pay well. We’ll pay generously for the grain, so you can buy grain in the nearby Hyeong yang and replace what you give.”

The elder bowed deeply, as if grateful they would think so far.

“To repay us and even offer payment—I’m truly grateful.”

Baek Cheon’s expression grew even more awkward.

He wasn’t used to such excessive politeness. He was beginning to understand how too much courtesy could make the recipient uncomfortable.

Then the elder looked at Baek Cheon with a strange expression.

“But, warrior, there’s one thing I’d like to ask.”

“Yes?”

“Right now… are you perhaps being chased by those wicked ruffians?”

Baek Cheon hesitated briefly and answered. It wasn’t something he could hide.

“Yes. That’s what’s happened. But…”

“Oh my…! Please spare us! I’m begging you like this!”

At that moment the elder cried out and flung himself face-down on the floor. Baek Cheon’s eyes widened.

“W-what are you doing all of a sudden!”

“If those wretches learn we gave you grain, not a single one of us will survive!”

“Ah… you don’t need to worry about that. We’ve wiped any traces of our presence here. And money doesn’t have names on it anyway…”

Baek Cheon said with as much calm as he could muster, smiling, but the elder hit his head to the floor again before he could finish listening.

Thump!

“I beg you!”

Baek Cheon’s face twitched in shock. The elder’s plea continued.

“Think of it as sparing these miserable people once—could you not take grain from here? I’ll tell you of a nearby village. Please…”

“…”

“If by some one-in-a-thousand chance word gets out that we did such a thing, we will all be killed. You might erase traces, but you can’t seal people’s mouths, can you?”

Baek Cheon opened his mouth to say something, but the elder was quicker.

“Please.”

With tear-wet wrinkles at his eyes, the elder looked at Baek Cheon desperately. Seeing that, Baek Cheon’s strength drained from his body.

The elder bowed his head and said.

“You need not pay for the grain you just ate. So please take everyone and leave here as soon as possible. I beg you like this!”

Baek Cheon squeezed his eyes shut without meaning to.

Chapter 1363: You Really Are Generous, After All. (3)

When performing acts of charity, one must not expect repayment. This is a principle of those who act as righteous warriors.

The moment you expect repayment, it ceases to be a charitable act and becomes a transaction. Thus, Baek Cheon also did not expect reward for what he did.

Even if it meant risking countless dangers and losing much. However…

Baek Cheon slowly nodded and spoke.

“I understand your situation.”

He said it while suppressing an involuntary sigh.

“But, village chief. Our circumstances are not that good either. So could you help us just this once? If we must avoid the Four Sects Alliance’s pursuit and even stop eating, it would be hard to guarantee our lives.”

If it were the usual Baek Cheon, he wouldn’t have gone into such lengthy pleas.

His reason for saying this was not to elicit pity by revealing hardship. He simply wanted to know.

He wanted to see how benevolence given without expectation of return would truly come back. Whether their acts of charity were actually making the world a bit better.

“Righteous warrior…”

But that sentiment of Baek Cheon didn’t seem to reach the old man.

“How could ignorant people like us discuss the circumstances of righteous warriors?”

“……”

“We know nothing. We only hope to scrape by day by day. Please pity us.”

The old man bowed his head again. Seeing that, Baek Cheon let out a hollow laugh.

What could he do?

If the strong pressed down by force, they might at least resist. But what could one say when the weak lay out their plight and beg for understanding?

“No!”

Jo Gul, who had been holding his breath at the side, reddened and raised his voice.

“We’re not saying we’ll steal—we’re saying we’ll buy with money! We’ll pay several times over. You could just go down to a reasonably big village and use that money to get more grain…!”

“Nonsense.”

“Sasuk, isn’t that a bit much? It’s not that terribly dangerous!”

“Stop it!”

Baek Cheon shouted firmly at Jo Gul. Jo Gul shut his mouth but couldn’t hide his displeasure.

“Please, righteous warriors.”

The old man kowtowed and spoke again.

“What may seem trivial to you righteous warriors is something we ignorant folk must stake our lives on. Sleeping during the day might be nothing for a mountain tiger, but for a rabbit it is a life-or-death matter.”

As Jo Gul tried to retort, a hand quietly covered his face. Jo Gul, reflexively about to swipe it away, froze—it was none other than Yoon Jong who had silenced him.

Yoon Jong quietly stared at the old man and then spoke.

“Village chief. I understand what you’re saying. But we too risked our lives to help these villagers.”

At those words, the old man also fell silent, seemingly without a reply.

“To deliberately set foot in these Southern lands and eliminate the villains of the Four Sects Alliance is neither a simple thing nor an easy decision. But none of those here opposed it.”

“Righteous Warrior…”

Yoon Jong looked at the old man with a slightly different expression than usual.

“Therefore we beg that you give only something far smaller than what we’ve given. Still, is it truly impossible?”

“Father!”

Hyung Euk could no longer hold back and shouted, and the old man turned with blazing eyes.

After glaring fiercely at Hyung Euk, the old man looked back at Baek Cheon and his group. The glare he’d shot at Hyung Euk relaxed as if it had been feigned, replaced by awkwardness and embarrassment.

“How could I not know that. But, warriors… if we help you and suffer some calamity as a result, what meaning would that have?”

Yoon Jong for a moment was dumbfounded, staring blankly at the old man.

Even Yoon Jong, who had never hesitated to stand with commoners, hadn’t expected such words. The old man drove in his wedge.

“Please understand our position. The favor we’ve received is as vast as the sea, but sadly we are foolish people without the means to repay it. We barely scratch the earth to live, surviving on grain supplied by the Evil Sect’s villains—what could we possibly do?”

“……”

“We beg this much. Please, withdraw that request.”

The old man’s abject posture made those seated before him uncomfortable, as if they were oppressing the helpless.

Baek Cheon, who had been quietly watching the situation, finally spoke.

“So in the end, you’re saying not to ask for grain and to leave this place as soon as possible.”

“That is…”

The old man’s voice trailed off. There was no outright denial. Baek Cheon did not press further. With positions so entrenched, persuasion was pointless.

And in truth, persuasion was impossible.

Even if those with swords tried to reason kindly, would it be heard as anything but coercion? The moment the initial proposal was rejected, Heavenly Friends Alliance and the Southern Island’s party had no choices left. Those who claim to walk the path of the Justice faction could do nothing else.

The old man continued to look guilty and wary, while Hyung Euk bit his lip.

Baek Cheon, who had been watching the two, turned to those seated behind him.

Most could not hide their stunned and aggrieved expressions. Especially Tang Pae, Namgung Do-Wei, and Seol So-Baek found it hard to accept the situation.

On the other hand, two people reacted completely differently.

Im So-Byeong, sneering as if ‘I knew it,’ and Chung Myung, maintaining an inscrutable expression as if thinking nothing at all.

A sigh escaped Baek Cheon’s lips.

“Then…”

“W-wait a moment!”

At that moment Hyung Euk hurriedly spoke. The old man tried to dissuade and pressure him with that same look again, but this time Hyung Euk did not yield.

“This doesn’t make sense.”

“What now…”

“It was the villagers, not others, who benefited from these people. Father, weren’t you unconscious at the time? You didn’t see how hard they’ve worked to protect the village.”

The old man lifted his graying eyebrow.

“What are you trying to say?”

“This means it’s not for us alone to decide. We must ask the villagers too! Convey the situation as it is and leave it to their choice. No matter the fact that you are the village chief, you can’t decide everything on your own, can you?”

The old man sighed.

“Will that do?”

“Yes! Then I will accept it too.”

Hyung Euk quickly nodded and addressed Baek Cheon and his group.

“Righteous Warriors! I’ll gather the villagers and ask for their opinion. Sorry for the trouble, but could you wait until then?”

Baek Cheon looked back at those behind him. They, too, had no particular objections and just stared at Baek Cheon.

“…Then please.”

In any case, this was the only remaining option. There was no other choice.

“Yes! Then just a moment…!”

Hyung Euk dashed out. As Baek Cheon stared at the wide-open door, he heard a small cough at his ear.

“E-excuse me, warriors.”

“…Yes?”

“Before that, one thing…”

It was the old man. His face was once more filled with a contrite expression.

“Again I say…”

Baek Cheon looked over the villagers clustered before him and spoke. He wondered if this was necessary, but the old man’s point that it was the fair thing to do was not wrong.

“Whatever choice you make… we will not harm you. That is guaranteed by me, the Acting Sect Leader of Mount Hua…”

When Baek Cheon turned his head, Geum Yang-Baek grimly nodded. He couldn’t feel pleased being asked to make such an absurd proclamation in front of everyone without knowing the reason.

“I, Geum Yang-Baek, the sect leader of the Southern Island Sect, will vouch for this.”

With even Geum Yang-Baek solemnly declaring it, the villagers waved their hands vigorously.

“Oh my! What are you saying!”

“Yes! Yes! Isn’t it thanks to you that we survived? How could we entertain such dishonorable thoughts toward those who saved our lives?”

“Yes! Exactly. Only a beast who doesn’t know gratitude would do that.”

Still, the villagers who had seen them fight the Four Sects Alliance were certainly more favorable than the village chief.

“Is that settled now?”

Hyung Euk glanced at the old man and asked, and the old man bobbed his head.

“Hold the discussion back there.”

“Yes. Then this way.”

Hyung Euk led the villagers toward the back of the house. Perhaps worried that voices might carry, they moved farther away than expected.

After even the old man turned and went inside, Jo Gul, who had been watching the situation, spoke.

“Still, the villagers will think differently, won’t they?”

“Probably.”

“Right. That old man was unconscious when we saved those people. Of course they’d think differently.”

Baek Cheon merely nodded silently.

“I think so too.”

It was less a confident answer than a pure wish—the last hope Baek Cheon offered them.

But then a mocking voice was heard.

“It seems our Taoists have lived in the mountains for too long.”

It was Im So-Byeong. He sneered faintly as he looked at Baek Cheon.

“If you put your hopes in people, all that remains is disappointment.”

“I understand what you mean, but…”

“No. The Taoists do not understand it.”

“…Sorry?”

Im So-Byeong snorted and said.

“Why Mount Hua became the talk among so many people. And why, despite still being a force incomparable to the Nine Great Sects, it draws the attention of so many sects.”

“Well…”

“You’ll probably find out soon.”

At that moment, as if to add to Im So-Byeong’s words, Hyung Euk returned faster than expected.

That he returned so quickly without prolonged discussion likely meant most villagers shared the same opinion.

‘Then?’

Just as he hoped it might be good news, Baek Cheon saw it.

On Hyung Euk’s approaching face, a complicated mix; in his steps, a heaviness.

Hyung Euk, meeting Baek Cheon’s gaze, silently bowed his head deeply.

He felt he didn’t need to be told to know the conclusion.

Im So-Byeong’s mocking voice was heard.

“Show kindness to those in distress. Repay kindness when you receive it.”

“……”

“Because there are no others who keep those obvious things, the name Mount Hua became special.”

Before Baek Cheon could reply, Hyung Euk approached and, with a long sigh, spoke.

“…I’m sorry, righteous warriors.”

Baek Cheon’s shoulders dropped involuntarily.

“The villagers… want you righteous warriors to leave the grain and depart… Yes, they want that…”

Grit.

Baek Cheon clenched his fist tightly. The dull crack of his joints resounded.

Chapter 1364: You Really Are Generous, After All. (4)

Baek Cheon’s gaze turned toward the distance. Everyone had already hidden themselves behind the house so they wouldn’t be seen.

I did not expect any reward.

I didn’t ask them to give their grain away for free, did I?

I only wanted a fair price, no, I wanted the chance to buy the grain even if we had to pay more than that.

Was that really too much to ask?

Baek Cheon involuntarily looked back at Yoon Jong. Whenever he lost his way as a cultivator of Taoist, Yoon Jong was the one who set the standard. But Yoon Jong’s expression was also harder than usual.

If so, then the sense of betrayal and anger Baek Cheon felt now wasn’t simply because he was petty.

“I’m sorry…”

Hyung Euk bowed his head deeply as if ashamed. His face was flushed, as if it might burst from embarrassment and guilt.

Even knowing it was pointless to blame someone like this, Baek Cheon finally spoke.

“I didn’t realize it was such a difficult request.”

“…Chivalrous guests.”

Hyung Euk shut his eyes tight.

It couldn’t have been a difficult request. At least, not to him.

Of course, as the village chief had said, helping them might bring danger. But hadn’t these people helped the villagers without hesitation even when it could have made them dangerous?

Being strong doesn’t obligate you to help, and being weak doesn’t justify turning away.

But there was nothing he could do. Those who had been watching the situation ultimately said they’d prefer not to help.

The future danger the elder spoke of resonated far more than the grace already bestowed.

“Wh-what am I to say…”

While Hyung Euk stammered, the elder inside the house came out again, cautiously. He glanced at Baek Cheon and bowed his head. There was no sense of triumph or smugness, only a pleading expression that Baek Cheon’s temper remain intact.

“Righteous warriors…”

The old man spoke in a trembling voice. Understanding the meaning behind it, Baek Cheon let out a hollow laugh.

“There is no need to worry.”

Though a chill remained in his voice, Baek Cheon spoke as politely as he could.

“We are not the Evil Sect that slaughters on a whim when displeased. You needn’t worry that we’ll act brutally.”

“Thank you. Thank you, righteous warriors!”

The elder hurriedly thanked them, then again sneaked a look at Baek Cheon.

“Please… don’t think of us as bad people.”

“…What do you mean?”

“We know it must seem absurd from your position. But we have no choice.”

Baek Cheon looked at the elder silently. A weary sorrow settled on the man’s tired face.

“The mercy you speak of is something only those with means can afford. How can people who struggle to survive care about others’ situations or provide them a path to live? I myself am desperate right now.”

Baek Cheon’s eyebrow twitched. He could endure many things, but this remark was hard to tolerate.

The Heavenly Friends Alliance didn’t help them because they had plenty to spare. Charity is…

“People like us can only pretend to die if commanded. That’s what we’ve seen; it’s the world we’ve lived in.”

Just as Baek Cheon, unable to hold back, was about to speak,

“Let’s go.”

A voice called briefly. Turning around, they saw Chung Myung scratching the back of his head.

“I guess I was in too much of a hurry. I had no thought. I should’ve considered that their circumstances here weren’t good. Tch. I’m done for too. When you get old you ought to die.”

“Chung Myung…”

“Let’s go. We are asked to leave them without drawing attention, so we should go quickly. It’ll be easier on everyone that way.”

Baek Cheon felt strangely deflated.

If it had been any other time, Chung Myung would have raged wildly, but hearing him say that, Baek Cheon had nothing more to add.

“It looks like most of you have eaten…”

Chung Myung glanced over the Southern Island Sect people and said.

“If your bellies are full, it’s time to leave. We don’t actually have much time, do we?”

“…That’s right.”

“Tell them to prepare quickly. They’ll have a lot to do once we’re gone.”

Baek Cheon still had much to say, but he finally answered with a deep sigh.

“Alright… let’s do that.”

His chest burned. It was an indescribable ache.

“You needn’t pay for the grain you ate…”

“No.”

Baek Cheon looked the elder firmly in the eyes.

“We will pay exactly what the grain is worth. We must do so.”

His emotionless voice sounded somehow a little sorrowful.

Led by Baek Cheon, they left the village.

The atmosphere was markedly different from when they’d left this village before. No one spoke rashly.

Even the Southern Island Sect’s disciples, who didn’t fully know the circumstances, couldn’t miss the mood and kept quiet.

“So shall we just leave like this?”

In that situation, the one who boldly spoke up was Im So-Byeong.

“Of course?”

“Isn’t the end result all that matters? There’s grain left—why not take some and throw them money? The outcome would be the same as a willing trade.”

“…”

“Then they could claim that if anything happens later, we robbed them of grain, and thus bear no guilt. It’s obviously reasonable; let’s turn back right now!”

Baek Cheon snorted. That might indeed be the reasonable path, one where no one suffers.

But they couldn’t choose that path. If they said the end justified the means, they’d be admitting they were no different from the Four Sects Alliance they were fighting.

“Argh!”

At that moment Jo Gul shouted angrily.

He knew, like Im So-Byeong suggested, they could act that way, but he couldn’t contain his rage.

“A person should at least have some conscience!”

“…”

“We didn’t help because we had strength to spare on our journey. Even if they can’t repay, they can at least show us some consideration, can’t they?”

“Enough.”

“Before they looked at us with naive, tear-filled eyes! Now they won’t even show their faces until the moment they leave! If people like them become warriors, the Evil Sect will…”

“I told you to stop.”

When Baek Cheon weakly snapped, Jo Gul clamped his mouth shut.

“…It’s because I’m frustrated. Because I’m wronged.”

“…”

“We didn’t expect repayment, but still… still there are things like that, you know. Still…”

Baek Cheon started to say something, then shook his head and fell silent.

In truth, his feelings were no different from Jo Gul’s. Speaking at length in such confusion would only reveal his thoughts.

When Baek Cheon didn’t properly answer, Jo Gul’s gaze turned to Yoon Jong.

“Isn’t that right, sahyung!”

At those words, Baek Cheon also looked at Yoon Jong.

Yoon Jong might say something different.

“Aren’t you angry, sahyung?”

“Anger…”

Yoon Jong calmly shook his head at Jo Gul’s question.

“It’s not anger.”

“…Not some kind of living Buddha or anything.”

“It’s not anger; I’m simply confused.”

“Huh?”

When Jo Gul looked puzzled, Yoon Jong glanced up at the sky and spoke.

“As a Taoist, and as a member of the Justice faction, I believed we should help and protect the common people because there is goodness within them. They are decent folk oppressed by the powerful, so I thought we should lend whatever strength we can.”

“…”

“But today, for the first time, that thought occurred to me differently. Not just regarding that village, but in any case.”

“Yes.”

“If those we aim to protect are not good, then why risk our lives to protect them? Simply because we are stronger than them?”

“…”

“The saying that we shouldn’t expect repayment for our acts of charity—perhaps it stems from the fact that there is no way to receive that repayment in life. Yes, that’s the thought that comes to me.”

His voice was calm, but the words were heavy, especially coming from Yoon Jong. Everyone fell into their own thoughts.

“I’ve heard a saying like that before.”

All eyes turned to Chung Myung, the one walking at the front as if he had no regrets.

“Commoners are neither as good nor as remarkable as people imagine them to be. Their strength is weaker than expected, and the morals they preach are often trivial.”

“…Isn’t that almost an insult to them?”

“But that’s precisely why we must protect them.”

The Mount Hua disciples who listened quietly frowned. They couldn’t understand—if the people were truly that insignificant, why protect them?

“Because they are people who could have become good.”

“…”

“The old man’s words are not wrong. Most people don’t even have the luxury to keep their goodness. That’s because those with power did not protect them.”

Yoon Jong watched Chung Myung’s back as he walked and spoke, his face stern and thoughtful.

“The good remain good even without help. But small acts of kindness toward those who are not good might lead them toward goodness. A Taoist is someone who doesn’t let go of that small possibility, and charity is the attempt to use what one has to change the world.”

Muttering, Chung Myung’s gaze lifted to the sky, as if following something far away.

“Honestly, I don’t fully understand it either. I know only one thing: our predecessors didn’t give because they didn’t know—they gave because they knew and still chose to protect.”

Chung Myung’s words ended there. He said nothing more.

He didn’t tell them they must do the same or that it was the right path; he merely relayed what he’d heard.

But everyone who heard it fell into thought, wondering what they truly intended to do.

It was then.

“Wait, please! Righteous warriors!”

A loud voice came from behind.

Hyung Euk was running hard, carrying something on his shoulder.

“Huff! Huff! Huff!”

Hyung Euk, breathless from running, stood before them, panting. Baek Cheon blinked, not understanding.

“No, why…?”

“Th-this!”

Hyung Euk set down a large sack he’d been carrying on his shoulder. It thudded.

“It’s grain. Please take it.”

“Huh?”

Guilt still filled Hyung Euk’s face.

“The villagers decided not to, but this is my share. If I’m giving my share, what choice do they have?”

“Ah, no…”

Baek Cheon, taken aback, looked between the sack of grain and Hyung Euk.

“This is something we cannot accept…”

“Please accept it!”

Hyung Euk bowed deeply without waiting to be heard. The gesture seemed reminiscent of his father’s, yet felt entirely different.

“I’m useless, so this is all I can do. Please allow me to do even this.”

“…”

“The villagers aren’t evil. They’re just… just fearful. Please don’t see them only as bad people.”

“Sir…”

“It’s insignificant, but please take this. I’m truly… truly sorry.”

Baek Cheon stared at the sack of grain with a complicated feeling. It was cheap grain, probably only enough for a pinch for each of the many people.

But the heart contained within it was by no means insignificant.

Silently looking at the sack, Baek Cheon clasped his hands in salute. Then, with utmost respect, he bowed deeply.

“…We will gratefully accept it.”

His hands trembled faintly as he saluted.

They had a long road ahead and little time.

Yet everyone there silently gazed for a while at the sack between Baek Cheon and Hyung Euk.

At the few humble but most precious measures of grain inside that tattered sack.

Chapter 1365: You Really Are Generous, After All. (5)

“Then we’ll be going now.”

“Yes, righteous warriors. Take care on your way!”

Hyung Euk bowed at a right angle and greeted them.

Many things had happened, but it was clear that Hyung Euk was sincerely grateful. For that reason, even the Heavenly Friends Alliance party showed him the utmost courtesy.

“Ah, um…”

Having finished his farewell, Hyung Euk awkwardly scratched his head.

“May I ask… where you are heading?”

“Yes?”

Baek Cheon glanced at Hyung Euk with a slightly curious look. Hyung Euk quickly waved his hand anxiously and said.

“Ah, it’s nothing like that. What good would it do me to know where you warriors are going? I just wanted to warn you to be careful if you plan to head up along the mountain range.”

“Be careful how…?”

“No matter how remote this mountain backwater is, it’s not like we can’t hear what’s going on in the world. I heard things have been a bit noisy up north lately, so if you’re heading toward Northern lands, you might want to choose another route…”

Baek Cheon smiled at Hyung Euk’s cautious words.

“Ah, that’s what you meant. No need to worry. We’re planning to head toward Nanjing.”

“Ah…”

Hyung Euk quickly nodded.

“S-sorry. You wanderers surely have your plans. Someone ignorant like me needn’t…”

“Don’t say that. We’re very grateful for your concern.”

Baek Cheon bowed politely and said.

“There are many things I’d like to talk about, but we’re pressed for time, so we must hurry on our way.”

“Yes! Of course! You should do that.”

“Then.”

Baek Cheon smiled gently and waved farewell.

When he turned, the others bowed to Hyung Euk and then all turned to leave together.

Hyung Euk stood there for a long while, watching until their figures faded and finally disappeared.

Wooooosh!

The disciples of Mount Hua, at the front, shot off like the wind.

The heavy steps they had taken leaving the village were now much lighter.

Baek Cheon ran at the very front, followed by Jo Gul. Jo Gul’s gaze was fixed on the sacks of grain slung over Baek Cheon’s shoulder.

The grain, which would barely amount to a handful if shared among so many, was being carried by Baek Cheon himself—as if it were a treasure that must never be lost.

“…When I think about it, it’s really strange.”

“Hm?”

Jo Gul muttered, and Baek Cheon turned. Jo Gul’s eyes were deeper than before.

“If you examine it, it’s that Hyung Euk fellow.”

“…Yeah.”

“Wasn’t he the one who distrusted us the most when we first stopped by the village?”

“Was he?”

“I’m telling you, yes. Other villagers were friendlier; that Hyung Euk treated us like thugs who’d come to show off our strength.”

Baek Cheon glanced up at the sky for a moment and recalled the first time they met Hyung Euk.

“Right. I guess he did.”
