Thinking back, Jo Gul was right. When they first arrived, Hyung Euk had plainly shown his distrust towards them.

“But seeing that man act like that now…”

“Does it seem strange?”

“Not so much strange as…”

Jo Gul trailed off as if it were hard to put into words.

Fortunately, Yoon Jong articulated what he had been trying to say in a slightly more refined way.

“I think I understand a bit of what Chung Myung meant earlier.”

Baek Cheon looked at Yoon Jong with intrigued eyes. Yoon Jong shrugged and said.

“Of course, showing malice toward us doesn’t automatically make someone evil, and showing goodwill toward us doesn’t necessarily make someone good.”

“That’s obvious.”

“Yes. But… what changed that person’s attitude was probably the goodwill we showed earlier.”

Baek Cheon nodded silently.

There was, of course, concern. Calling the favor shown toward them a righteous change might be presumptuous. So Baek Cheon and Yoon Jong were cautious.

But what mattered here was not ‘righteousness’ but the change itself.

As Chung Myung had said, the association might not only be easing their lives… it could be changing something.

Something small and faint now that might one day grow and become greater.

Perhaps they had just witnessed that possibility with their own eyes.

“I believed the association was changing the world. That’s why I felt despair and was speechless at the villagers’ attitudes for a moment.”

“Yoon Jong…”

“But thinking now, that’s true. The association and goodwill might not change the world directly. Yet they might change people, even if just a little.”

Jo Gul nodded at those words.

Because they had seen it with their own eyes. They had witnessed a person changing.

“In the end, it’s people who change the world. It may be a slow process, but as one person after another changes, perhaps one day the world will be filled with goodwill.”

“That’s a difficult path.”

“Isn’t that what the tao is about? If it were easy, there’d be no need to seek it for a lifetime.”

Baek Cheon gave Yoon Jong a sidelong glance. Yoon Jong’s face looked unusually relieved and fresh. Baek Cheon felt merely comforted by Hyung Euk, but Yoon Jong seemed to have seen something Baek Cheon hadn’t.

That was enough. For now, that was sufficient.

“Let’s pursue the Tao a little later. For now, we must quicken our pace.”

“Yes, sasuk.”

“Although supplies didn’t turn out as planned… we’ll handle that as the situation allows.”

“Yes!”

Baek Cheon increased his speed. The wind rushed across his face.

The villagers who had turned away from them, the elder who had pressed them politely until the end, and Hyung Euk’s apologetic expression still lingered vividly before their eyes. It seemed they would not be easily forgotten.

But now all that remained was the heavy sack of grain on his shoulder.

‘Let’s not try to draw conclusions.’

Perhaps neither he nor his sahyung-sajaes were ready to make definitive judgments. No matter how famous or entrusted with grave duties, they were still young practitioners.

How to live in order to uphold the Way they believe in is a lifetime’s worth of contemplation. So don’t judge hastily—just remember. Someday these memories and what they saw here will blend into them.

Only…

‘Who could it be?’

The ‘someone’ Chung Myung occasionally spoke of—Baek Cheon found himself newly curious about that person.

He once thought Chung Myung was invoking someone non-existent to express his intent, but recently he realized that when Chung Myung referred to ‘someone,’ his words were full of a rare aura, so profound it was hard to find in Chung Myung himself.

“Respect, huh…”

He muttered, and Chung Myung, running in silence, tilted his head in curiosity.

“Hm?”

“No, it’s nothing.”

“How bland.”

When Chung Myung dismissed it as if incredulous, Baek Cheon gave a wry smile.

‘Someday…’

Then he stamped the ground hard, clenching tightly so as not to let slip any of the many things that had arisen within him.

❀ ❀ ❀

“None.”

Yu Gong flinched at the low voice. Forgetting to keep scanning in panic, he froze in place. Cold sweat began to bead on his forehead.

When he turned with a creak, he saw Ho Ga-Myung’s impassive face. Their gazes met in the air and Ho Ga-Myung asked.

“Isn’t this right?”

Yu Gong’s fingertips began to tremble.

Ho Ga-Myung was right. The sect’s markings were no longer visible. They searched desperately, but there wasn’t a trace.

He thought perhaps they’d misread the previous signs and taken the wrong direction, but that was highly unlikely. Ho Ga-Myung had already fully understood the Southern Island Sect’s markers. It was impossible for both Yu Gong and Ho Ga-Myung to be wrong at the same time.

“If the markings stopped, it means the rear guard has fully rejoined.”

“Ah, perhaps we simply haven’t found them yet. If you give me a little more time…”

“If I give you time?”

Ho Ga-Myung asked calmly.

“You mean you can find them?”

Yu Gong couldn’t bring himself to answer confidently; he knew well that he was clutching at straws.

Ho Ga-Myung narrowed his eyes. At the same time, Yu Gong recoiled.

But whether fortunate or not, Ho Ga-Myung had no regard for someone like Yu Gong at the moment. His mind was full of doubts about why the Southern Island Sect and the Heavenly Friends Alliance group had shown such sudden changes.

‘Logically, it means they realized we were tailing them.’

Otherwise, there would be no reason for them to suddenly rejoin like this.

What bothered him was that he couldn’t figure out how they detected the pursuit. Yu Gong had been desperately tracking them, but from Ho Ga-Myung’s perspective, they had been keeping a reasonable distance.

Without any special variables, they shouldn’t have noticed by now.

“Hmm.”

After a moment of thought, Ho Ga-Myung glanced at Yu Gong.

“What do you think?”

“…Sorry?”

“Why do you think they’ve stopped leaving markings?”

“How would I know…”

Ho Ga-Myung narrowed his eyes.

“I’m suddenly curious.”

“…About what?”

A clear sneer curled his lip.

“You have plucked out your own claws and become dogs. I’m curious whether other wolves can guess what you’re thinking.”

“…”

“But it seems a futile effort. Yes. A dog is only a dog.”

Yu Gong bit his lip between humiliation and anxiety. Ho Ga-Myung coldly shook his head at the sight.

“It’s become clear. Your role is over.”

In fact, Ho Ga-Myung had spoken without particular meaning—it was merely a way to summarize the situation.

But Yu Gong couldn’t accept it that way. The combination of dog, hunt, and end brought to mind the proverb ‘use and discard.’

The color drained from his face in an instant.

“So step back…”

“S-something! Something!”

Ho Ga-Myung was about to tell him to fall back but closed his mouth and stared at Yu Gong. Yu Gong desperately racked his brain and, afraid his words might be cut off, rambled out a torrent of speech.

“Th-there must be something they want to hide! Something that must never be discovered! Something whose traces must never be found by anyone in the future!”

“…”

“They want to avoid leaving artificial traces. Whatever it is! They must hide something that must never be exposed, so it’s not themselves they’re hiding but something else! So that thing…”

Though his words tumbled incoherently, Ho Ga-Myung understood what he was trying to say.

“Something they want to hide.”

A meaningful light kindled in his eyes.

“There’s sense to that.”

Snap!

When Ho Ga-Myung snapped his fingers, the lieutenants quickly moved to either side.

“Search the nearby area. Even trivial things are fine. Report anything noticeable. You have one watch; no more—nothing beyond that is worth wasting.”

“Yes, sir!”

At Ho Ga-Myung’s command, the lieutenants set the Ten Thousand People Clan members in motion, scattering them in all directions.

Drop.

Sweat trickled down Yu Gong’s face, pooling at his chin and dropping in beads. Anxiety seemed to eat away at his whole body.

Before that sweat had cooled, several of those who had dispersed returned.

“There’s a small village about 6 kilometers northwest.”

“A village?”

“Yes!”

Ho Ga-Myung briefly stroked his chin and murmured.

“A village…”

Could that village be the place they wanted to hide at all costs?

He couldn’t make sense of it. If they had been chasing some other entity, they’d naturally have ignored that information. But…

Ho Ga-Myung, staring intently at Yu Gong, slowly nodded.

“Move out.”

We’ll know when we see it. What, indeed, is being hidden.

Chapter 1366: Do You Know What Shame Is? (1)

The first thing that came from those who faced each other again after the disciples of Heavenly Friends Alliance and Southern Island Sect left was an excuse.

“…It was right to help them, though.”

“Who doesn’t know that? Of course it’s right. We’re not animals; if someone showed us kindness, we should repay it.”

“That’s right. It is right… but how easy is that? What if those beastly Evil Sect fellows find out? Nothing in this village would survive, not even an ant.”

“That’s exactly my point. Those people don’t know what the Evil Sect are like. They could at least fight if things went wrong, right? How would they know the feelings of people like us who must lie flat and pray to be spared?”

“Still… they must have felt hurt, I suppose.”

“Of course. I’d be deeply hurt too. They helped us with good intentions.”

“That’s enough. What’s the use of saying it? Being powerless is a sin, living here scraping the soil is a sin. If it weren’t for those Evil Sect bastards, who would begrudge a bit of grain?”

But that wasn’t enough to soothe the uneasy hearts; as time passed, words of complaint began to emerge.

“To be blunt, if those gentlemen hadn’t been caught by the Evil Sect in the first place, none of this would have happened, would it?”

“Oh ho, what are you saying like that?”

“Am I wrong? They bragged they’d deal with the Evil Sect, showing off, and yet they got their asses handed to them and were driven out of the Southern lands, weren’t they?”

“Well now, even so, you shouldn’t say that so carelessly.”

“If those Justice faction folks and these people hadn’t been meddling in the first place, we wouldn’t be living in such misery. After all, this happened because of them — why should we feel guilty? If anyone should feel guilty, it should be them!”

“There’s nothing entirely wrong with that… but in any case, weren’t they the ones who helped us with good intentions?”

“Even the helping is questionable! Aren’t they people who plainly know our situation? Are they the sort of people who can’t guess what would happen if we recklessly helped them? They’re educated and knowledgeable — what are they doing?”

“…”

“And helping is just words. They said they’d send us to Northern lands or whatever, so who actually came? They just left us again this time!”

At those words, the villagers’ faces hardened strangely.

“From the start, those gentlemen couldn’t care less whether fools like us live or die. When times were good they’d leave grain like alms, but when things go sour they demand even that be handed over, don’t they?”

“Didn’t they say they’d buy it? They said they’d buy it…”

“Did we even get money? How would we know whether they’d actually give it! And even if we got the money, could we use it properly? In a world like this, grain you can put in your mouth right away is worth more than a lump of metal!”

The truth is a little different.

It’s not wrong that in troubled times grain matters more than money, but Southern land isn’t so chaotic that money is useless. Even if you go to a big city right now, as long as you have enough money you can still get grain.

Most of those here knew that fact, but they didn’t bother to say it aloud. Bringing it up would only make them look worse.

“What would we have done without the village chief? If not for him, they’d have surely taken all the grain here.”

“You’re exaggerating.”

“What do we know? If someone who’s lived longer and is thoughtful says so, we have no choice but to follow.”

“That’s true.”

They made the choice themselves. The old man merely conveyed what he thought.

But avoidance was easier than confrontation. Even if it might bring greater trouble later, for now it would be easier.

So they could simply tell themselves they followed the old man’s coercion. He was the village chief of Hyung Village and had the strongest say.

“That’s enough. At least we fed them a proper meal before they left, didn’t we? That should count as repaying the favor.”

“…Stop it.”

“It’s not wrong, you know? That one meal may mean nothing to them, but because we gave that grain we might have to go hungry for over two weeks. That much we did — we’ve shown all the goodwill we can…”

“I said, stop it.”

A man grabbed the speaking person by the shoulder to pull him back. But the one raising his voice shrugged off the restraint and snapped.

“Ah, why? Can’t I even speak my mind?”

“No, it’s not that! Behind—huh? Behind!”

“Huh?”

The man turned to look behind him.

Hyung Euk stood there looking at him with disdain. The man’s face flushed and he awkwardly cleared his throat.

“Ahem. You’re back?”

“Do you consider the debt fully repaid?”

“…No, I mean…”

“Hyung, you once told me something.”

“Hmm?”

Hyung Euk spat the words out as if chewing them.

“Being uneducated and poor doesn’t mean one lacks shame. Shame isn’t learned; if you’re human, you simply know it.”

The man closed his mouth like a clam.

“Then what do you call someone who doesn’t know shame?”

No answer came. Hyung Euk didn’t wait and left the place.

Those who watched his rough stride each let out a long sigh. With the mood like that, people quickly sized each other up and then dispersed.

The village was soon wrapped in deep silence.

Those who felt deep guilt, those who thought Mount Hua’s party had been excessive in their sudden demands, even those who believed the choice was already made — none could speak up readily.

The sun set, night came, and after a rare still dawn the eastern sky beyond the mountains began to lighten.

“Tsk.”

The old man opened the door, went outside, and clicked his tongue.

Hyung Euk hadn’t returned home the previous night. Having lived his whole life on the mountain, it was unlikely he’d met with an accident; he had most likely spent the night outside simply to avoid facing his father’s face.

“He’s not that young, yet he’s so full of foolish pride.”

The world isn’t that kind. He should already know that by now…

The old man shook his head in frustration.

‘In a few days he’ll be fine.’

He knew. Wounds that sting as if they’ll kill you heal and scab over with time. Repeat that a few times and the same wound hurts less.

The human heart is the same. No matter how sore and painful, after enduring the same thing several times it becomes dull. Guilt is no different.

The old man only hoped this would make his son face reality a bit more.

Step. Step.

At that moment the old man heard footsteps. Turning his gaze to the village entrance, his face went pale and his eyes widened.

“Ah…”

A group whose villainy could be felt by sight was pouring into the village. A chill ran down his spine.

Snap.

At the head of the group, a man who looked clear-eyed, unlike the rough-looking men behind him, clicked his fingers and spoke.

“Bring them all — not a single one left behind.”

“Yes!”

The old man’s face was covered with black despair.

“P-please spare us!”

“I did nothing, my lord!”

The dragged-up villagers were forced to kneel in the center of the village. The gaunt old man with legs thin as if they’d snap at any moment was no different.

They had been suddenly dragged out and forced to kneel since morning, but none dared properly protest or question it.

It was natural. After all, warriors were no less than death’s messengers to ordinary commoners.

If even one appeared the village could be overturned; with hundreds surrounding them, what could they possibly say?

Beyond fear, it felt as if their minds might shatter at any moment.

“Aah!”

“Hyung Euk!”

At the cry from beside him, the old man who had his head bowed startled and snapped his gaze up.

His son, who hadn’t come home the previous night, was being dragged in by the hair. He had hoped the boy might have escaped by being far away, but the weak-hearted lad apparently had been lurking near the village even in anger.

‘No, that’s not it.’

The old man clenched his thin fist tight, widened his eyes and collected himself. If he could have acted without worrying about looks, he would have slapped himself hard.

‘Even if a tiger takes you, if you keep your wits you survive,’ he thought.

Wasn’t their refusal to help the benefactors originally because they feared such a situation? The villagers had done nothing wrong. So…

“These are all of them!”

At someone’s shout, the cold-featured scholar at the front nodded.

“If you answer plainly without hiding anything, you won’t be harmed.”

Everyone swallowed dryly at that blunt remark. A question followed.

“Did any Justice faction people come to this village?”

The villagers glanced at each other. Not out of conscience — they feared that simply admitting the Justice faction had set foot here might bring them harm.

But the scholar, Ho Ga-Myung, was not the kind to show leniency for such circumstances.

“You don’t understand? Kill two or so as an example.”

“W-we understand! We understand, my lord!”

At that moment the old man shouted at the top of his lungs.

Thud! Thud!

He struck his head to the ground and kowtowed.

“They definitely came here!”

“Oh?”

“Yes! They said they needed grain. They promised to pay, so they asked us to hand over grain…”

“Grain?”

“Yes, that’s right!”

Ho Ga-Myung nodded as if he understood.

“So? Did you give it to them?”

“N-no way! Though we’re ignorant, we’re not foolish enough to not know who is looking after us now! We refused firmly!”

The old man prepared to answer whatever came next — whether reproach or praise, whatever it might be.

But what followed was far from his expectations. Ho Ga-Myung had no interest in whatever grain they might have handed over.

“I see. Fine. Then where did they go?”

“…Sorry?”

The villagers looked at one another.

Where did they go? How on earth would the villagers know that?

“You don’t know?”

“Th-that is…”

The old man reflexively glanced up to gauge the mood. He saw Ho Ga-Myung’s face — specifically, his eyes.

The light in those eyes was calm. That made it all the more chilling. A deadpan gaze as if looking at stone or wood. From that alone the old man knew how this man regarded the villagers.

“How can I believe such words?”

“…What?”

“I do not trust people. The only thing I trust is the simple truth that life matters more than conscience.”

At Ho Ga-Myung’s light nod, those flanking him stepped toward the villagers.

“Spare the one who speaks. Kill the rest.”

“Yes!”

The old man’s face turned ashen.

It was at that exact moment.

“He, he went!”

One of the villagers shouted desperately.

“He followed them at the end! He knows where they went!”

“So, So Gil! What are you saying right now!”

The old man glared and shouted like in a fit. The man had pointed at none other than Hyung Euk.

“Why, why! Isn’t it true!”

“What is this among villagers…!”

“What kind of thing is that for someone who said conscience be damned and that we must survive at all costs! Yesterday you said people like us should do whatever it takes to live, didn’t you!”

The old man’s eyes trembled.

He hadn’t meant it in that way. It was only meant to get those Justice faction people to leave…

“He went! Hyung Euk!”

“He followed them!”

“We all don’t know anything! That lad knows!”

The villagers, having found their way to survive, cried out frantically one after another.

“Enough.”

The warriors advancing menacingly at Ho Ga-Myung’s command stepped back.

Ho Ga-Myung glanced at Hyung Euk, whom all the villagers were desperately staring at. Hyung Euk’s face was pale, but his expression was surprisingly calm.

“Do you know?”

“…Yes.”

Hyung Euk quietly admitted it.

“There’s no use pressing these folks. It’s just me, isn’t it?”

“Then answer. Where did they go?”

“Before that, I want to ask something — are you a high member of the Four Sects Alliance?”

Ho Ga-Myung slightly knit his brows.

“You may say that.”

“Then you must be well-educated, so let me ask one thing. If I may.”

Hyung Euk paused briefly and snorted with a laugh.

“Do you know what ‘shame’ is?”

For a moment everyone’s eyes flickered — the villagers and even the Ten Thousand People Clan guarding behind Ho Ga-Myung. They couldn’t help trembling at the outrageous question dared at Ho Ga-Myung.

But Ho Ga-Myung and Hyung Euk, who had spoken the words, looked at each other without a flicker of agitation.

Ho Ga-Myung opened his mouth.

“Baek Bi.”

“Yes, commander.”

“Find out.”

“Yes!”

The one called Baek Bi approached Hyung Euk and drew something from his sleeve: a razor-sharp dagger gleaming blue.

Hyung Euk snorted at the dagger and muttered.

“Now I’ll find out too. Whether I’m a man with shame.”

He closed his eyes and exhaled deeply.

Chapter 1367: Do You Know What Shame Is? (2)

“Sahyung.”

“Hmm?”

Lee Ja-Yang, running after the Mount Hua party ahead, quietly asked Gwak Hwan-So beside him.

“Did you understand the conversation they just had?”

Gwak Hwan-So’s eyebrow twitched slightly. Then Lee Ja-Yang went on to say something unnecessary.

“Honestly, I don’t quite get it. From what I heard, they helped those villagers who were in danger of losing their lives to the Evil Sect while on their way to the Southern Island Sect, didn’t they?”

“Seems so.”

“Then it means the villagers, after receiving such a great kindness, didn’t even give a single sack of grain back, right?”

“Probably……”

Lee Ja-Yang looked ahead as if baffled, at the Heavenly Friends Alliance members running in front.

“How can they be so calm? Just spouting meaningless phrases like that.”

Frustration was plain on Lee Ja-Yang’s face. Seeing this, Gwak Hwan-So casually asked.

“What would you have done?”

“Huh? What do you mean……”

“I mean, if you were in Mount Hua’s position, what would you have done?”

Lee Ja-Yang frowned, then answered.

“…I’d have used disciplining staff to hit them.”

The thought alone made him angry; his voice rose slightly in agitation.

“People should know gratitude, that’s what separates people from beasts, right? How can you call those people human when they say ‘thank you’ with their mouths and lament that they can’t repay the favor, yet refuse to give even a grain they have?”

“True.”

“What makes people different from beasts is knowing gratitude. Being thankful when receiving, but turning away as if they’ve never received when it’s time to help, such people don’t deserve to be helped!”

Gwak Hwan-So didn’t offer any particular agreement; he simply nodded slowly as if to say he understood Lee’s point.

“Sahyung, don’t you think?”

When Lee Ja-Yang asked, Gwak Hwan-So thought for a moment and snorted a laugh.

“I’d probably have done the same. As you know, it’s unlikely my temper would let me endure seeing that.”

“Right?”

Lee Ja-Yang nodded as if it were obvious, as if implying there was nothing odd about his reaction.

“But… I don’t know. Would they really feel differently than we do? We’ve only imagined it, but they experienced it directly, so if anything they’d be angrier, not less.”

“Of course.”

“And, well… as you know, their temperaments don’t seem especially milder than ours.”

Those words twisted Lee Ja-Yang’s face.

Honestly, it made sense. Now few would deny they are true Taoists, but when first seen at the Worldly Martial Arts Tournament they were nothing but rakes.

Especially that Acting Sect Leader, apart from his looks, he was one of the craziest men Lee Ja-Yang had ever seen.

He still vividly remembered when they suddenly attacked and a brawl broke out. Weren’t those the ones who rolled their eyes and charged just because their sect was insulted? And it happened at Shaolin of all places.

‘Then why are they tolerating it now!’

He understood the situation likely prevented escalation or delay. Still, leaving a sharp remark wouldn’t have been that difficult.

At that moment Gwak Hwan-So spoke softly.

“There’s a saying in the way of Tao.”

“Yes?”

“If you call the Tao as ‘the tao,’ then it is no longer the Tao.”

Lee Ja-Yang frowned. What sort of vague nonsense was that?

“If you take it literally, the moment you call a favor a ‘favor,’ it ceases to be one. The instant you say ‘I gave, so you must repay,’ it is no longer a sincere act of giving.”

“Huh……”

Lee Ja-Yang laughed in disbelief.

Does that make any sense?

Those who act as wandering chivalrous men are still human. How can anyone continue doing things without any return, or risk their life for something worthless?

Isn’t it the dream-filled young warriors aspiring to be knights-errant who want, through that path, to make their names known across the Central Plains and become heroes?

“So we’re supposed to accept not being repaid for kindness, even being scorned for it, as something natural?”

“……”

“Then who would ever do that?”

“They do.”

For a moment Lee Ja-Yang was speechless, forced to stare blankly at those silently running ahead.

“Of course they are human and can’t always be happy. But they seem to think that one sack of grain is sufficient repayment for their good deed.”

“Are they mad……”

Gwak Hwan-So snorted at Lee Ja-Yang’s overly blunt reaction.

His thoughts weren’t much different from his. Still, he somewhat understood why they thought that way.

“They don’t expect immediate reciprocity; they hope that those who receive their kindness will in turn become people who can give without expecting return.”

“Can that really happen?”

“Will it happen right away? No. They just keep doing it endlessly. Then, little by little, people who understand that intent will appear.”

Lee Ja-Yang shook his head with a slightly sickened expression.

He understood the meaning, but it was an arduous road. People don’t easily give what they have, even when powerful warriors stand before them; how could they possibly change?

“I think it’s a ridiculous pipe dream.”

Lee Ja-Yang sneered.

“They dress it up grandly, but in the end, isn’t it just saving dozens of lives and getting chased away with barely a single sack of grain as reward?”

“……”

“The one who gave that grain probably did it for appearances, do you think they’re truly grateful? People don’t change that easily. You know, sahyung, how cruel and selfish people can be.”

Gwak Hwan-So nodded slowly.

Having faced a harsh sea in their lives, they knew all too well how selfish a person could become when life and livelihood were at stake.

“I don’t mean to belittle them, but if you think one sack of grain changed anything, that’s just willful blindness to seek comfort. Nothing’s changed.”

“…Yes. That might be true.”

“Sahyung, do you not agree?”

“To be honest, I think you’re right too.”

Gwak Hwan-So smiled bitterly.

He didn’t deny that their chosen path was wrong, but the world isn’t so simple. Commoners aren’t that naive; because they have nothing, they can be even more selfish.

Gwak Hwan-So looked at Chung Myung’s back running ahead. Chung Myung’s clothes were in tatters, ‘rag’ wouldn’t suffice, and the blood he’d shed had dried on them.

‘What you aim to do might be harder than placing the whole world under Mount Hua’s heel.’

And it will probably be in vain, merely suffering without even repayment. But…….

“Still, I want to believe they are not wrong.”

“…Yes?”

“That’s just how I want to think.”

Gwak Hwan-So muttered softly and stomped the ground.

“Brutal……”

Baek Bi’s face twisted.

Baek Bi, Ho Ga-Myung’s guard and right hand, had tortured countless people to extract information from their mouths.

The Ten Thousand People Clan members often feared Ho Ga-Myung, the militarist, more than their leader Jang Ilso, because of what Baek Bi had done behind the scenes.

If someone fell into his hands, even the toughest man would speak within a moment. Given just a moment’s free rein to toy with a person, he could make them recall even their earliest baby memories.

For someone like Baek Bi, making a powerless peasant speak was nothing. Not a moment, but just a few breaths would be enough.

It had to be so, surely.

But now, looking at the fallen figure before him, Baek Bi’s gaze was full of aversion. Even seeing the blood, pus, and filth pouring out, his usually cold eyes flickered faintly.

“How……”

A voice like a sigh escaped.

In truth, the figure before them could hardly be called human anymore. Mangled into a lump of blood, it couldn’t even struggle or twitch; it seemed nearly spent.

It was clearly still alive, the blood-red chest rose and fell faintly. But it was evident there wasn’t much left.

Baek Bi clenched his teeth. He wanted to grind that unrepentant body alive. But more torture was impossible; even touching that thing now might end its breath.

He had failed to extract information from him. Utterly.

“Ugh……”

Then, a sound, whether a sigh or a laugh, crept from the man’s mouth. Baek Bi gritted his teeth, bowed his head, and went to Ho Ga-Myung.

“…I’m sorry.”

Ho Ga-Myung looked at him silently for a moment, then said, puzzled:

“So odd.”

“……”

“Did you go easy on him?”

“No. I don’t think there was any lack from my side.”

“Then it means that fellow is impressive. Even you, who’ve made formidable warriors cry like children, couldn’t make him speak.”

A flash of indignation crossed Baek Bi’s face.

Bandit lords who ruled regions, brutal horse leaders, murderers who killed countless people, none withstood his torture in silence.

Yet this simpleton from a mountain backwater had finally endured his torture. How was he to explain this situation?

Thud.

As Ho Ga-Myung stepped forward, Baek Bi stepped aside and bowed his head. Ho Ga-Myung walked at an unhurried pace and coldly looked down at Hyung Euk, who still clung to life.

“Why are you holding on like this?”

There was no answer. Ho Ga-Myung lowered himself and whispered into the hole where ears should have been.

“Don’t you want peace? Even if you were shown favor by them, they’re already gone. You’ll never meet them again.”

“……”

“Just look away for a moment. I’ll heal all your wounds and grant you vast wealth. This is the first time I’ve promised such things in exchange for information.”

“……”

“It means I acknowledge you. You’ve done enough. Even if you speak now, no one in the world would dare blame you.”

Surprisingly, Ho Ga-Myung’s words were sincere. He truly admired the man before him.

“Even if you die here like this, no one will remember you. No one will regard you as great. Everything is only attainable by being alive.”

“……”

“Now, this is my final offer. Answer me. Tell me only where they went, and I’ll heal you immediately and promise you status and wealth that no one would dare ignore. I’ll also save all the villagers. I promise all this in the name of the Four Sects Alliance’s military Mind-Probing Inquisitor.”

Ho Ga-Myung’s voice slid sweetly into Hyung Euk’s ear.

“But if you refuse, neither you nor anyone here will survive. And those you risked your life to protect won’t even know you died so miserably here. You wouldn’t want to die without even that worth, would you?”

“……”

“So……”

Ho Ga-Myung’s eyes glinted coldly.

“Answer. Where did they go?”

At that moment, a faint sound came from Hyung Euk’s mouth, so small even the warrior Ho Ga-Myung struggled to hear it clearly.

“Speak more clearly.”

Ho Ga-Myung pressed his ear close to his mouth.

Hyung Euk’s lips parted and thick blood ran down. A frail sound that seemed about to break crept from the opened mouth.

“…Uh.”

“Which way? Again.”

“……”

“Say it clearly. Come on.”

A fleeting gleam of triumph crossed Ho Ga-Myung’s face.

Considering how rare any emotion was on his face, one could guess the kind of elation that flickered within.

As everyone held their breath, Hyung Euk’s voice finally reached Ho Ga-Myung’s closely pressed ear.

“…Go f— yourself……”

Silence fell.

Ho Ga-Myung’s face slowly contorted like a demon’s.

“You…!”

Ho Ga-Myung’s fist, filled with qi, struck Hyung Euk’s head hard.

KRAAAASH!

Chapter 1368: Do You Know What Shame Is? (3)

“Which side? Again.”

When he heard Ho Ga-Myung’s voice, Hyung Euk desperately tried to clutch at the consciousness that was slipping away.

‘Why was I holding on?’

Why resist? A sudden question arose. Not that so much time had passed, yet he couldn’t remember. Why was he enduring this cruel torture?

What on earth did he want to protect? Was the man’s claim wrong?

No. There was nothing wrong with it. Hyung Euk knew that too.

Even if he honored his loyalty here, those who left would not know. They wouldn’t know whether he died here or betrayed them and enjoyed wealth and glory.

To them, Hyung Euk would be nothing but a trivial connection who passed by for a moment. People will readily give a coin or two to a pitiable beggar, but they don’t remember the beggar.

Isn’t it human to throw a coin and turn away and immediately forget? Perhaps Hyung Euk’s name has long since been erased from the minds of those Mount Hua people.

So Ho Ga-Myung was right. If he were even slightly wise, it would be better to feign defeat here and sell them out.

He only had to close his eyes once. It’s not that hard, is it? Compared to the mistakes he’d made in life, it was a small thing.

He wasn’t a saint, and he certainly wasn’t a man of great spirit. He was a dullard struggling day by day to survive. Wouldn’t this be enough?

If he stubbornly held out to the end, everyone here might suffer misfortune.

Is that really the right thing? Is that really the path he should take? The answer was decided. What he had to do was far too clear.

He only had to open his closed mouth and utter the two syllables ‘Nanjing.’ Then everything would be eased.

And just live on.

Even if he didn’t receive the vast wealth the author promised, as long as he could live as before. Plow the fields, raise the children, endure physical toil but have somewhere to lean his heart—isn’t that ordinary life enough?

Hyung Euk’s mouth slowly opened. Nanjing. Just Nanjing. He only had to say those two syllables.

No one would blame him. In fact, they wouldn’t even know he’d done something blameworthy. After all, the villagers had all abandoned the Mount Hua party, so no one could judge Hyung Euk’s conscience.

So he could just utter the two syllables and everyone would regain ordinary lives. What’s wrong with that?

“Say it exactly. Hurry.”

Ho Ga-Myung even offered the courtesy of bringing his mouth close to his ear. So that Hyung Euk wouldn’t have to endure all the shame and convulse—just a whisper would suffice.

Hyung Euk’s father had said that only those with power can keep their conscience. He now understood the meaning of those words. So there was nothing more to hesitate about.

Hyung Euk opened his mouth weakly.

He forced his throat and let the two syllables ‘Nanjing’ slip from his mouth.

“…Go…f…yourself…”

But what actually came out wasn’t those two syllables.

Ho Ga-Myung’s face twisted like a demon.

‘I don’t know.’

Hyung Euk couldn’t explain the reason for this choice. Even if he were torn apart and died and received the villagers’ condemnation in the afterlife, he would only bow his head.

Yet why was it that one part of his heart felt strangely relieved and refreshed.

Even as Ho Ga-Myung’s hand shot up and something glowing blue appeared, Hyung Euk snorted and laughed.

‘Chivalrous men.’

That hand came down toward Hyung Euk.

‘I suppose I had more shame than I thought.’

KRAAAANG!

A huge blast roared out. Dust shot up and the surroundings were enveloped in a cloud of dirt.

The villagers who had been watching Hyung Euk and Ho Ga-Myung couldn’t even breathe and squeezed their eyes shut. The torture they’d inflicted had already been horrific, but they absolutely did not want to see the final sight of a head bursting open.

But when the dust cloud slowly cleared, a slightly different scene unfolded than expected.

Crack.

Ho Ga-Myung was holding only the clump of earth that had sunk into his hand. Then he pulled his hand out from the hole next to Hyung Euk’s head.

His subordinates widened their eyes in slight surprise.

“Commander!”

“What a waste of time.”

Ho Ga-Myung straightened up. He looked down coldly at Hyung Euk, who still had a breath in him.

Baek Bi immediately ran up and bowed his head.

“Commander, I’ll take care of him.”

“Leave him.”

Ho Ga-Myung cut him off with an indifferent voice.

“Anyway, if he doesn’t receive proper treatment he won’t last another day. No need to make his passing comfortable on the way out, right?”

“…Yes, Commander.”

There was not a speck of mercy in Ho Ga-Myung’s eyes.

If they killed Hyung Euk here and now, it would only result in a man who failed to achieve his aims losing control and lashing out at a civilian unable to use martial arts.

“In the end, a dullard is just a dullard.”

Leaving that cold, malicious remark, Ho Ga-Myung spun his body around.

“Commander, then what about those people…?”

Baek Bi glanced at the villagers and asked. Ho Ga-Myung waved his hand irritably.

“Leave them be.”

“But…”

Baek Bi, who rarely spoke up, trailed off. He meant that since things had gone wrong, meting out punishment might at least save face.

If it were during the Ten Thousand People Clan days, Ho Ga-Myung would have wiped them all out without pity. But now his judgment had to be different.

“Have you forgotten the Alliance lord’s order?”

“Ah…”

Baek Bi turned pale in an instant and hurriedly bowed his head.

Hegemony Lord Jang Ilso had ordered the entire Four Sects Alliance not to lay hands on civilians. One or two might be excused, but touching an entire village would be tantamount to openly disobeying that order.

Others might get away with acting as they pleased if not caught, but Ho Ga-Myung was not one of them.

Jang Ilso was even distributing grain to win the goodwill of the Southern land people. In this situation, it made no sense for Ho Ga-Myung to cling to petty face and oppose that intention. One Hyung Euk was already more than enough.

“It’s not a bad thing to let those who defy the Alliance lord see how they die and engrave that fact into their bones. More than that…”

Ho Ga-Myung said coldly.

“There will surely be traces left behind. Search thoroughly.”

“Yes!”

At Ho Ga-Myung’s command, everyone moved in unison. Ho Ga-Myung’s gaze watching them was dark.

‘Such a stupid act…’

He had wasted time.

Normally, the moment he thought the man was holding out better than expected, he would have issued the next order at once. But this time he couldn’t make that judgment.

Ho Ga-Myung also knew that it was an action born half of stubborn pride.

Ho Ga-Myung’s strength originally came from his cool-headedness. Unlike the fiery Jang Ilso, he was endlessly calm and composed. But in this matter he had lost his composure. He could not help but admit it to himself.

Ho Ga-Myung closed his eyes and exhaled a long breath.

‘Mount Hua…’

If the man had been protecting a sect other than Mount Hua in the name of loyalty, Ho Ga-Myung would have sneered that those stupid peasants didn’t even know how to look after their own bowls.

But the moment the two syllables Mount Hua were involved, a stubborn urge rose in him to see with his own eyes that will be broken.

Truly a foolish bravado. What significance did the breaking of a petty man’s will have?

Ho Ga-Myung, who had worried about the Alliance lord’s excessive obsession with the Mount Hua sect, had himself become even more obsessed with Mount Hua than that Alliance lord.

“Commander! There’s a trace here.”

“Attach pursuers there. Follow that immediately.”

“Yes!”

Ho Ga-Myung slowly lowered his gaze.

Hyung Euk lay collapsed, looking like a lump of blood. Like a worm… no, perhaps worse than a worm.

“So I lost to a worm?”

Ho Ga-Myung muttered self-deprecatingly and strode away. As he left, the Ten Thousand People Clan men who had been waiting with bated breath followed behind.

The Ten Thousand People Clan men who had filled the village drained away like a recession tide. It made the nightmare time they had held over the village seem almost like a lie.

Only Hyung Euk’s desolate, mangled body and the dark red blood soaking around it proved that that time had been real.

“Is he…alive…?”

Those who had thought everyone surely dead muttered in stunned disbelief.

Those who had been staring blankly toward the direction the Ten Thousand People Clan men left slowly regained their senses one by one and turned their eyes to Hyung Euk.

“Hyung Euk!”

“You!”

They rushed over hastily, but none dared to touch him and hesitated.

Because there was nowhere they could touch. There wasn’t a single uninjured spot, so they had no idea where to start helping.

“Hey, you damn fool! You, you stupid idiot!”

But the old man unhesitatingly grabbed Hyung Euk and pulled him into his arms, as if already knowing his child’s fate.

“I…! Didn’t I tell you it was meaningless! Grace, whatever…! I told you people like us only receive harm! You foolish idiot! You! You…!”

The old man’s desperate wails echoed through the village.

Hyung Euk’s wife couldn’t bring herself to look directly at his body and wept as if in a daze, while the young children were too frightened to come near. They all sensed Hyung Euk’s fate.

“To people like us… to beings like us, even that would be greed, I told you… What is so difficult about saying that one word! You stupid… you stupid thing! You foolish man! Sob…”

The tears that streaked the aged face poured straight onto Hyung Euk’s face.

Perhaps because of those tears, Hyung Euk, who briefly regained consciousness, slowly lifted his eyelids.

“Fa… Father…”

“Y-yes! You brat! Yes, it’s me! Your father!”

Hyung Euk weakly raised his hand. The old man quickly grabbed the trembling hand.

“You, pull yourself together! You mustn’t die like this! This is not the time to go! Even this old father is still alive, why are you going? Why!”

Hyung Euk, as if even keeping his eyes open was painful, looked at the old man and lifted the corner of his mouth.

“S-sorry…”

The hoarse voice was barely intelligible and could cut off at any moment.

“I’m… sorry…”

After those words, Hyung Euk’s head dropped with a thud.

When the grip on his hand loosened, the old man hugged his son tighter and wailed at the top of his lungs.

“Hyung Euk! Hey, you bastard! Hyung Euk!!!”

The villagers, unable to leave the spot, began to wail one by one. The village entrance was now filled only with mournful cries.

And in that place, where all the Ten Thousand People Clan men had left, there remained one who had not yet departed.

Yu Gong stood staring blankly at the villagers’ wailing.

Countless inexpressible emotions welled up in his eyes.

Chapter 1369: Do You Know What Shame Is? (4)

A mound of red earth was piled high. The older brother wiped the tears from his eyes with his dirt-covered hand after covering the grave with the last handful of soil.

“When does a child go before his father, so heartless.”

The elder knew it wasn’t Hyung Euk’s fault. Still, he couldn’t bear it without saying as much.

His child had been subjected to cruel torture before his very eyes and had finally died.

It wasn’t that he had turned away to cling to his own days. What life could be more precious than a child’s in their prime?

If the old man, unable to contain his rage, had attacked the villains, it wouldn’t have been only him who suffered. The villagers, his daughter-in-law, and young grandchildren could have been made to suffer like Hyung Euk.

Thus the powerless can only look away. Ordinary people have eyes and yet cannot see; they have mouths and yet cannot speak.

“That’s right… that’s right, I…”

He could not continue and sobbed blankly. Villagers approached the slumped man to offer words of comfort.

“Village chief… Hyung Euk died like a man.”

“He won’t have regrets.”

But those words brought no comfort; they seemed only to draw out the old man’s fury.

He spat and shouted as if having a fit.

“What good does that do? When you die, it’s over! It’s because you worship empty things like loyalty and favors that this happened!”

He kept slapping his own thigh. His eyes were bloodshot, and words of blame poured from his mouth without pause.

“‘Live righteously’? Who doesn’t know that’s right? We turned away because we knew living righteously would get us treated like this! Do you think people like us have no conscience or shame? You wicked ones! Wretched scoundrels!”

The wails of a widow and the bereaved children could be heard constantly.

“So-called heroes and the like! How many have strutted in saying they’ll help if they just happen to see it? So? What changed? Where were they when we were suffering like this?!”

Everyone fell silent.

“We should’ve looked away… We shouldn’t have stuck our necks out, I told him so many times…”

The old man collapsed and wailed. Everyone watched, their feelings tangled and helpless.

It was true they had been saved from imminent death thanks to the people of Mount Hua. Yet the one who tried to repay that favor suffered a fate even worse than dying at first and lay buried in that earth.

What would have been the right thing to do?

“…Still, isn’t it true that we’re clinging to life because of them?”

“How do you know? Even if they hadn’t helped, things might have turned out well by luck!”

“How can you say that? To those who helped with good intentions…”

“Good intentions? Is that why we were treated like this? The village chief was right. While we were trembling under those wicked men’s grasp, what were they doing? Didn’t they run off to save themselves?!”

They raised their voices one after another around the grave.

It wasn’t a sight to show before the dead, but their emotions were inflamed by what they had suffered the day before.

“If they’re not going to take responsibility to the end, they shouldn’t have helped at all!”

“Is helping a little something? There are many who wouldn’t even do that!”

“Isn’t this all because of them? Foolish hopes led to this! If only Hyung Euk had told where those men went, he wouldn’t have ended up like that!”

At that, the dazed elder suddenly turned on them with bloodshot eyes.

“…Whose fault was that…?”

“…Village chief?”

“If everyone had kept their mouths shut, it would’ve passed without this incident, if only you all had kept quiet! Yet among you, who was it that sold Hyung Euk out?”

The old man’s distorted face had become truly dreadful.

He had seen and heard it clearly: villagers he believed family to have sold his son out.

If only these people had kept quiet, Hyung Euk might not have died. At the very least, he wouldn’t have died so miserably after such torment.

Seeing those who had pushed his precious son into suffering brazenly act as if nothing had happened filled him with sudden rage.

“How could you do this? How! My son treated you so well, thought of you so much! Didn’t he plead he’d save you even if his father took the blade? How could you… how?!”

He screamed as if tearing his throat.

“If you have a mouth, say something! Huh?”

Some bowed their heads low. Others quickly shook their heads as if they’d never done such a thing. The old man gritted his teeth.

“You damned lot…”

“How is that our fault?”

But someone couldn’t just leave that remark unanswered.

“…What?”

The elder narrowed his eyes. One disgruntled man brusquely asked again.

“I said, how is that our fault?”

“…You, you shameless…!”

“Yes! I’m a man without conscience or shame. But didn’t you, village chief, tell us to do that?”

The elder’s eyes went wide as if struck.

“Me?”

“Isn’t that right? You told us fools that to survive we shouldn’t think of favors or loyalties but only how to save our lives right now. You said gratitude, chivalry, and affection are things only those with means can afford—didn’t you tell everyone that two days ago?”

The elder looked at the man dazed, as if he’d been struck.

It wasn’t wrong. He had indeed said it. But he hadn’t meant it in this sense. The elder opened his eyes wide and said,

“Hey, is Hyung Euk an outsider? Is he a martial arts man? People like us must help each other…”

“We have to survive first!”

The man shouted rabidly. As his voice rose, others tried to restrain him, glancing at the elder.

“Hey! What are you doing to the village chief!”

“Can’t you stop?”

“No! Did I say something wrong?”

But the man wouldn’t be silenced.

“Turning our backs on the very heroes who saved lives and scheming how to save ourselves—what great thing did Hyung Euk do that I should risk my life to protect him? I have to live! I do!”

“….”

“Weren’t you all thinking the same? Was I the only one who confessed that Hyung Euk knew where those warriors went? You were all thinking the same!”

“Hey, you! No matter what, we’ve just buried Hyung Euk! This is not the place to be—”

“Damn it, don’t pretend to be righteous now! Do you think I didn’t hear the elder say Hyung Euk was sold out?”

“I, when did I…”

Blue veins stood out on the man’s neck.

“If we’d kept quiet then we’d all have been dead! So should we have all died to save that one bastard?”

The man glared fiercely at the elder.

“What’s the difference! We did as the village chief told us! You said don’t think about honor or anything! What did I do wrong? Tell me!”

The man, screaming until his last strength, finally collapsed to the ground as if his legs had given out.

“What do you want us to do, what!”

He slammed his fist into the ground and howled like a beast.

Hearing that, the old man’s strength drained away.

Who could he blame? There was no part of that speech that was wrong. It was the elder who had repeatedly stressed they had to live that way.

Those words had led to the death of his beloved son and brought the village to this state.

‘Right. Survive.’

Isn’t being alive enough?

Even if it means selling out the benefactor who showed mercy or pushing someone you’ve lived with for decades under the villains’ blades, as long as I keep my life, isn’t that survival?

Isn’t that the right thing to do?

“Heh heh…”

For a moment a laugh, like madness, escaped the old man’s lips.

“Heh heh heh heh…”

But everyone knew it wasn’t laughter. It was the lament and cry of someone who had lost his way.

The villagers couldn’t bear to look at him and averted their eyes.

After long moans that were neither sobs nor laughter, a firmer voice came from the old man.

“…Prepare to leave.”

“What?”

Startled by the abrupt order, everyone looked at him.

“Village chief, what do you mean…”

“I’m not telling you to leave the village. What power have I to order you to go or not?”

At that, some people pursed their lips.

“I only mean that we cannot live here anymore, so we must leave.”

“W-we can’t live here anymore?”

“Do you think they’ll leave us be?”

“…B-but they left, didn’t they?”

It was a naive remark. The old man snorted and said weakly.

“If things went so smoothly in life, we wouldn’t be suffering like this. Even with Hyung Euk in the ground, the fact remains that we defied them. If not now, trouble will come to the village sooner or later.”

The villagers, who had not dared think that far, looked at one another in dismay.

It was true when you thought about it. The village had already drawn the attention of the Four Sects Alliance. Even if they survived this incident, any small pretext would bring fierce persecution incomparable to other places.

“We must leave… We can’t put roots down on land that will kill us.”

“W-wait, village chief. We’ve lived here our whole lives. Where are we supposed to go?”

“How would I know?”

“…Our homes, our fields, everything is here…”

The old man laughed at the talk of fortune.

“Then you stay.”

No one answered. Though he spoke practically, the old man’s eyes were hollow.

“We must live… That life has clung on so cruelly, we must somehow survive. Even if it means going somewhere no one knows and carving out fields, we must live…”

Everyone’s face darkened as they realized how bleak the life ahead would be.

The old man stared blankly at the earthen mound. His chest felt empty.

What had he lived for? In the end, this is what he had to see… why had he clung to life so stubbornly?

He had known that if he abandoned others, they could abandon him too, so why had he acted so clever alone?

In the end, he knew nothing.

“Prepare at once.”

“What? Right now?”

“Can you be sure those who left for appearances won’t send people back later?”

“….”

“If I were them, I’d wipe out the village and then report above that the village suffered tragedy regardless of who they were. Survive, you people. Somehow keep those miserable lives going. So prepare…”

The villagers bowed their heads.

Where had it gone wrong? At what point had it all become so twisted? They had done nothing—why must they suffer like this?

“Don’t dawdle, move. Leave before they act.”

“Um… vi-village chief?”

“What are you all standing for…”

“N-no, there, over there!”

A man turned ashen and pointed somewhere. The old man strained his weak body to look.

“Heh heh…”

At the village entrance, a group of men were approaching—cold faces and large swords at their waists. The old man laughed helplessly.

“It seems we’re too late…”

Perhaps this was the fitting end for this village. The elder thought so and quietly closed his eyes.

Chapter 1370: Do You Know What Shame Is? (5)

The one at the head of the approaching group looked utterly menacing.

Of course, the old man who had lived long knew that judging a person by their face alone was futile.

But he also knew that while one person might not mean much, a whole group giving off the same air made the outcome obvious.

The group entering the village exuded a dangerous aura, one you couldn’t sense from ordinary peasants. It was the dangerous stench only someone who’d stained their hands with human blood countless times could give off.

Apparently the old man wasn’t the only one to sense it. The villagers all fell silent at once.

What filled the old man’s eyes was resignation, but the villagers’ eyes were thick with despair. They knew these people hadn’t merely passed through; they had come with a clear purpose. Having spent their lives watching for others’ reactions, they could sense it from the way the group approached.

‘In the end… this was bound to happen.’

The old man let out a hollow laugh.

If mere compliance wouldn’t change anything and this was the inevitable end, he wished he had at least raised his voice in defiance.

Then he wouldn’t have felt shame before the child who had gone before him.

The strange men who had swept through the village turned their gaze toward those gathered at the grave mound.

“There they are.”

The leader strode toward them.

A rabbit might flee when faced with a tiger, but ordinary people couldn’t do that before a warrior. The old man meekly accepted the fate about to befall them.

But others were not the same. Those who hadn’t lost a child and who still possessed a stronger will to live couldn’t resign themselves as easily as the old man.

“P-please spare us!”

The man who had just been shouting excuses for selling out Hyung Euk prostrated himself as the strange men approached.

“Didn’t I tell you everything? There’s nothing left to give. If you spare us, we’ll keep silent for life, warriors!”

The man knew it was pointless, but he couldn’t just lie down and die. He’d sold everything he could to preserve that life—how could he let it go easily?

“W-wait, now that I think about it, those bastards definitely went north! Up there! I saw them going up! It’s north over there, s-so if you search the north, you’ll find the Justice faction hypocrites’ trail!”

The strange men who had reached them narrowed their eyes and muttered.

“…The Justice faction are hypocrites?”

The man hushed, feeling his scalp prickle as if he’d blurted out a mistake while flustered.

“A rare thing to hear—music to my ears.”

Luckily, the answer he hoped for came. Bolstered with sudden courage, he cried out louder.

“Yes! Yes! I mean those selfish Justice faction bastards! How could they compare to the heroes of the Evil Sect! If you spare us, we’ll help. We— we can recognize their faces, warriors!”

“Their faces…”

The strange man let out a peculiar smile and asked.

“Are you saying that if we take you along, you’ll recognize those faces and betray them?”

“Yes! Yes! If not, we can at least make a picture! One of us is good at drawing, so we’ll definitely help! Please, just spare us!”

“First, one question. Is this Hyung village?”

“Y-yes…”

Taken aback by the odd question, the man stammered. Why would those who had just passed through yesterday ask this?

At that moment, one of those scanning the village shouted.

“Leader! There are signs someone passed through here.”

“Many. At least several hundred, it seems.”

Hearing that, the strange man’s brows twitched.

He inspected the prostrating man, the grave mound, and all traces left by the Ten Thousand People Clan members, then curled his lip in derision.

“It’s true the Justice faction are hypocrites.”

“Yes, yes! Of course!”

“But… our boss is in that Justice faction group. From the looks of it, you seem to have sold our boss to the Four Sects Alliance.”

“…What?”

The strange man’s face turned cold.

“The thought that we risked our lives crossing the Yangtze to save trash like this makes my stomach turn.”

The man was dumbfounded, not understanding what the stranger meant.

The stranger spat out his words.

“We came under the Green Forest King’s orders to take you north. And yet… you repay kindness with treachery? You beasts are worse than animals!”

Not only the speaker but the whole group bristled with a cold, murderous aura.

They were the Green Forest King’s direct Green Contingent, devotion to the Green Forest King unmatched anywhere. Hearing that someone would sell out their king naturally provoked that reaction.

Under the menacing pressure, the man scrambled to rise but fell flat on his rear.

“I, I…”

Why were these people here?

The villagers had refused the Heavenly Friends Alliance’s plea for help. They believed the promise was impossible from the start and that refusal had ended it completely.

Who would show kindness to those who coldly betrayed a favor? Yet absurdly, those who had come to help the villagers arrived at that moment, and the man babbled what he shouldn’t.

Realizing what he’d done, the man stood aghast as if he’d tumbled into a valley of despair.

“Filthy worms…”

Gwak Min, known as Black Night Tiger, the Green Forest King’s close retainer and one of the Ten Shadows of the Green Forest, twisted his face. Those watching from behind muttered among themselves.

“It seems the Green Forest King misjudged people this time.”

“The Green Forest King wouldn’t do that! Surely those Mount Hua fellows pushed him into it.”

“Wouldn’t it be better to just deal with them all? Why bring scum like this to the north?”

“Still, it was the Green Forest King’s order.”

“Who would know if the Four Sects Alliance had come and killed them already?”

Frankly, betrayal wasn’t unfamiliar to the Green Forest disciples. Abandoning favors wasn’t uncommon either.

But precisely because of that, punishment for such people was harsher. They knew from experience that half-hearted mercy would shake the sect’s foundations.

Black Night Tiger narrowed his eyes at his subordinates’ words. His thoughts weren’t much different from theirs.

The Green Forest King’s orders were solemn. Yet lately he seemed too swayed by Mount Hua people. A past Green Forest King would never have given an order to save these people.

“…Answer me.”

“Yes?”

“Where did the Four Sects Alliance go?”

“W-we don’t know that…”

Black Night Tiger snorted.

“You sold off your benefactor easily, but you won’t sell out the Four Sects Alliance?”

“I-it’s not like that…”

The man’s face went pale; the Black Night Tiger now radiated blatant killing intent.

“Spit.”

“Need I say more?”

Ssshh—

The Green Forest disciples drew the large swords at their hips. There was no more need for a pretense of kindness; their rough Green Forest aura flowed out unabashedly.

Thinking it was over, the man squeezed his eyes shut.

But then one of the Green Forest disciples at the rear spoke up.

“Wait a moment. What’s with that grave mound?”

“…Yes?”

“Who is buried there?”

It looked like someone recently dead had been buried there. The villagers glanced at the mound; their looks toward each other were complicated.

Black Night Tiger made a quiet sound and stared at the mound with a grim face.

“Did you check?”

The man who approached nodded at his question.

“Yes. There were definite signs of torture in the village center. The smell of blood hasn’t washed away yet, so it seems they endured very brutal torture.”

“…”

“If it was one person, they might have bled them dry of all the blood in their body…”

Black Night Tiger was from the Evil Sect too. He could hardly be unaware of tales about the Ten Thousand People Clan’s unimaginably cruel tortures. If Ho Ga-Myung himself had been present here, the severity would have far exceeded normal levels.

‘They endured that…’

It was hard to believe an ordinary peasant, not a warrior, had withstood such torture and kept silent to the end, but the traces here and the villagers’ faces proved it.

Even without digging up the mound to confirm the body.

“Hah…”

With a slightly startled face, Black Night Tiger shook his head and scanned the villagers who looked at him with fearful eyes.

After a long silence, Black Night Tiger slowly nodded.

“Sheathe your weapons.”

“…Yes.”

The subordinates sheathed their weapons without protest. Their ill will toward the villagers hadn’t vanished, but they could not lay hands on them now. Even if ordered to, the unease already formed wouldn’t disappear.

The Green Forest disciples were of the Evil Sect, prone to betrayal and indifferent to benevolence, yet they understood true resolve better than anyone.

“Old man.”

“Yes…?”

“Prepare yourself. We will escort you to the north.”

“W-who, us?”

“Yes.”

The old Hyung blinked in bewilderment.

“B-but we…”

He should have immediately nodded, but a peculiar unease made him hesitate, an unease he wouldn’t have felt without everything that had happened that day.

But Black Night Tiger simply shook his head and spoke firmly.

“We only follow orders. Choose: stay here or come north with us. If you choose the latter, I guarantee you’ll reach northern lands alive.”

The old man glanced back.

The villagers gazed at him desperately, but his eyes fell on the mound where his son was buried.

After staring silently for a long time, the old man’s eyes welled with tears.

‘You bastard…’

In the end, it was Hyung Euk who saved this village.

Had Hyung Euk sold them out too, everyone might have survived briefly but could have faced massacre at these men’s hands. Even if luck spared them that, the villagers would never have been saved.

Because his son remained loyal to the Heavenly Friends Alliance to the end, the villagers found a way to live.

“The sooner you choose, the better. The Ten Thousand People Clan could return at any time.”

“…We’ll go.”

The old man nodded with difficulty.

“We must live… somehow we must live.”

“Then prepare. Travel light. No need to take useless grain; the north is full of provisions.”

Those words stabbed the old man’s heart even more painfully. If only he’d known he’d hear that sooner…

“I-I will.”

Black Night Tiger nodded. The villagers, who had only been watching for cues, hurried back to their homes one by one.

Watching them scatter, Black Night Tiger gave a bitter smile.

‘The world is…’

The one who kept his honor to the end was buried in cold earth, while the betrayers lived on at the cost of those lives.

Perhaps this is why goodness rarely spreads in the world.

But…

Black Night Tiger murmured as he looked at the red mound where grass had not yet sprouted.

“…At least you won’t have regrets.”

He felt as if someone he’d never seen before was smiling at him.

Chapter 1371: I Will Make It Like That. (1)

“Ugh.”

“……”

“Uuuugh.”

“……”

“Uuuuuugh.”

“Ah, for heaven’s sake!”

Finally losing his patience, Baek Cheon snapped his head around and shouted at Im So-Byeong.

“If you have something to say, then say it!”

“Can I really say it?”

“…No, please don’t.”

“I’m about to burst from holding it in!”

“…I said don’t.”

Baek Cheon tried a futile attempt to dissuade him again, but Im So-Byeong simply ignored him and continued.

“I told you we can still make contact now! I’m saying we should just turn back the ones heading to the village to help them!”

“No…”

“Acting Sect Leader, do you have no sense? You got driven out and still want to escort those people to the Northern lands?”

“……”

“Grain? Grain? Tell me honestly, will they carry those sacks of grain on their backs when they head to the Northern lands? They’ll have to leave it all behind anyway; why would we bother escorting people to the Northern lands who won’t even give up that one thing? What’s so admirable about them that we should take them along?”

“……”

“I said just say it! We can turn back those who are heading to Hyung Village right now!”

Baek Cheon, who had been silent the whole time, let out a deep sigh.

“…Still, there are human duties…”

“Dutyyy?”

Im So-Byeong, finally exasperated, rolled his eyes. Baek Cheon suddenly thought the Green Forest King was really starting to resemble Chung Myung, and that, of all things, he was taking after him in just that way.

“Duties? Duty! Well said! Did we break that duty? Aren’t those bastards the ones who abandoned their duties as humans?”

Baek Cheon had no response to that. He still believed they should be saved, but logically he couldn’t win against the Green Forest King.

Moreover, weren’t those people the sort to cover over parts that reason couldn’t solve with grand notions like the greater good or benevolence?

“Taoist!”

“Ahem.”

“Taoistt!”

Even with prodding, Baek Cheon remained unmoved, so Im So-Byeong’s eyes literally lit up.

“I mean, what kind of suckers are these in all my life…”

Smack!

“Aaaah!”

Im So-Byeong, who had been running, was suddenly flung forward and fell. Namgung Do-Wei, running behind him, casually picked him up and took care of him.

“Man, you really won’t stop jabbering.”

Chung Myung, having kicked Im So-Byeong away, slapped his innocent hand and dusted it off.

“Uuuuh.”

Im So-Byeong clutched the back of his head and groaned for a while before glaring fiercely.

“Why did you hit me! Did I say anything wrong…”

Pop!

“Aaaah!”

“Has this Evil Sect bastard gone mad? Where do you get off opening your eyes wide like that? Wanna get stabbed for it!”

“…Chung Myung. You should say that before stabbing.”

“Ah. Right, of course.”

Baek Cheon let out a long sigh. If only that were true, you…

Chung Myung glared at Im So-Byeong, who was clutching his eye and groaning.

“Really, as expected of a bandit!”

“Am I saying that because I’m a bandit?”

Im So-Byeong, on the verge of tears, scanned around wildly. His stabbed eye had red veins standing out.

“Isn’t that what everyone really thinks? Why save those who don’t know gratitude? If we meet them again in the Northern lands, what then? Will we just laugh awkwardly? Do we have to see that sight?”

“Shut up!”

Chung Myung smacked the back of Im So-Byeong’s head again with a loud crack.

“You’re a bandit because you just do whatever you feel like!”

“……”

“What?”

“Well… it’s not entirely wrong, but it’s not really something the Taoist should be saying, is it?”

“What, you punk?”

Around them, people only nodded in agreement.

“Right.”

“That’s definitely true.”

“Sometimes a bandit’s got the more righteous thing to say than a Taoist.”

“Seriously these bastards?”

When Chung Myung glared, everyone flinched and hurriedly turned their heads to act nonchalant, grumbling inwardly.

‘Did he come back alive after resting a few days and to start acting like that…’

‘As pitiful as he was when injured, it was at least easier then.’

‘He’s the kindest when he’s asleep.’

Im So-Byeong pressed the corner of his hollow eye with the tip of a fan and asked.

“So, Taoist, you’re saying it doesn’t bother you at all?”

“Bother?”

“Yes! Does it really not bother you at all?”

“…You want me to tell you what’s really in my heart?”

“…Yes? Ah, yes.”

“Honestly, by temperament I’d like to rip off their skins—civilians and all of them…”

“Eheheh! Chung Myung!”

“Mouth, mouth! Oh! That mouth!”

“Shut that bastard’s mouth!”

As words that a Taoist shouldn’t utter spilled from his mouth, Baek Cheon and Yoon Jong desperately ran up and clamped his mouth shut.

Still, Hae Yeon, standing nearby and unable to avoid hearing the muffled sounds, went pale and repeatedly expressed disapproval. A demon was crawling into his head; he had to somehow drive it out.

“You want me to say more? I could go on for a long time.”

“…Taoist, you’re really something…”

Even a bandit wouldn’t go that far… Im So-Byeong shook his head in dismay.

Chung Myung snorted and said.

“Still, what can we do? Among them, there were people who helped us.”

“There was a way to save just that person, wasn’t there?”

“This bandit… did he crawl up from hell rather than a mountain? If we told him to leave the villagers behind and go to the Northern Land alone, would that gentleman obediently go?”

Im So-Byeong closed his mouth tight. He knew Chung Myung wasn’t wrong.

The one who had handed sacks of grain to the Heavenly Friends Alliance had shown at least that much goodwill. Then that person ought to be saved. And if saving that person meant rescuing others as well… couldn’t that be unavoidable?

“And… even if I don’t care about the others, you shouldn’t be spouting that! If I judged like that, I’d have cut off your bandit neck first.”

Im So-Byeong, his hair standing on end, smiled awkwardly and unconsciously rubbed his pale throat.

“Heh, hehe… Haven’t I reformed?”

Chung Myung looked at him with a look of contempt.

“I told you.”

“Yes?”

“Even a bandit who went around killing people, if you beat him up he’d reform and become a decent person — so why should we let those people die over whatever mistakes they made?”

“…No wait, mistakes…”

“Everyone makes mistakes.”

Chung Myung’s gaze drifted briefly to the distant sky. Then he murmured to himself.

“Yes. Everyone makes mistakes. Sometimes mistakes that are hard to forgive.”

Im So-Byeong said nothing. He felt like he shouldn’t open his mouth.

“Still, with someone to understand them, people can change.”

Just then, a few people were about to nod in agreement.

“That’s right, Dong-Ryong?”

“I knew you’d say that, you bastard!”

Baek Cheon, enraged, swung a fist. Chung Myung feigned dodging it and chuckled.

“When did I say that it was only about sasuk? Namgung’s the same, anyway.”

“…Let’s bury the past. How many people here can speak of their pasts with a clear conscience?”

“Ahem.”

“Hehehem!”

Several people coughed awkwardly as if pricked.

Despite Mount Hua being one thing, Tang Pae had once stabbed Chung Myung in the stomach, Namgung Do-Wei had shown off in front of him and had his groin punched until he foamed from his mouth, and Hae Yeon’s words spat at Chung Myung in the martial arts tournament finals were still often talked about, weren’t they?

So they could only cough and fall silent at Namgung Do-Wei’s remark.

“So I don’t really buy the idea someone’s rotten to the core. Of course, I was a promising shoot from the start!”

“…What a load of crap.”

“You weren’t just a yellow shoot — you were black and dead…”

Chung Myung shrugged.

“Even if you end up resenting them, save them first and resent them later.”

“……”

“If the former Sect Leader Hyun Jong were here, he would surely have said that.”

Hua Mountain’s disciples nodded at that. Hyun Jong would have said just that. When one’s heart was troubled, thinking what Hyun Jong would have done provided an unfailing path for Mount Hua’s disciples.

Im So-Byeong, who had been listening silently, spoke up in dissatisfaction.

“Well… the sentiment is fine, but it’s our kids who’ll have to take the risk because of it, you know?”

“Do I care if a few bandits die or not? There are too many of them to handle anyway, right?”

“…Are you even human? Seriously?”

“Anyway, let’s drop what happened.”

Chung Myung cut off Im So-Byeong’s grumbling abruptly.

“There’s no time to worry about that now. They’ll start pursuing us soon.”

Baek Cheon nodded in agreement and said.

“It’s quicker to Nanjing than you think. Once we descend the mountain we’ll pick up speed; it’ll be at most a day or two.”

For a moment, tension spread across everyone’s faces.

Both descending the mountain and the fact they’d reach Nanjing in two days were news that couldn’t help but raise anxiety.

It meant they had to overcome a greater mountain ahead and face correspondingly greater danger.

“For now we chose Nanjing thinking it would be the least dangerous, but in truth…”

“It’s uncertain.”

“Yes.”

Baek Cheon calmly agreed with Im So-Byeong. “Uncertain” was the right word. Southern land is already being shaken to its core because of their movements. There’s no guarantee the Nanjing they would soon reach would be the Nanjing they knew.

“Steel yourselves. We will cross the Yangtze river.”

“Yes!”

Everyone raised their voices to answer Baek Cheon’s words. It was both an answer and a personal vow.

After running for some time, Im So-Byeong matched his pace and came up beside Chung Myung, who had pulled slightly away from those in front. His face was serious, devoid of the earlier complaint.

“Taoist.”

“Speak.”

“…The pursuit is later than expected. And… we don’t see anyone blocking the way ahead. You know what that means, don’t you, Taoist?”

Chung Myung nodded silently.

During war, the absence of small skirmishes indicates only one thing: the enemy is preparing a powerful blow to utterly crush this side.

“By now… yes, if it’s him, he’d have figured out where we’re headed.”

“You weren’t really expecting him not to know anyway, were you?”

“Yes, that’s true. But…”

The Heavenly Friends Alliance and the Southern Island Sect’s party heading to Nanjing wasn’t to deceive the enemy’s attention. Considering time, distance, and the positions of each sect, Nanjing was the battlefield that gave them the best chance.

“Will he move?”

“It doesn’t matter whether he moves himself or not.”

“…Yes?”

“The point is that even if he doesn’t move personally, someone will move toward Nanjing. That’s enough.”

Chung Myung’s eyes darkened.

‘Then Beop Jeong will have no choice but to follow.’

Beop Jeong abhors risking unforeseen losses. His past behavior had already made that abundantly clear.

So Beop Jeong would have to follow to Nanjing. He would want to keep every situation under his control, to block any variables Chung Myung and Jang Ilso might create.

It might even play into Chung Myung’s plan.

“Someone once said.”

“Yes?”

“That the world ultimately moves according to natural order.”

“……”

“But I think differently.”

Chung Myung’s mouth twitched into a smile. It was somehow a chilling smile.

Originally, the Nine Great Sects should have been the ones to save the Southern Island Sect. So, in truth, the hardships the party was enduring should have been the Nine Great Sects’ responsibility.

“If things don’t follow natural order, then we’ll force them to. So that no one is left wronged.”

His bleak gaze turned to the distant north.

To Nanjing — the chaotic land where all the powers of the world are converging.

Chapter 1372: I Will Make It Like That. (2)

Inside a carriage of unsurpassed luxury.

Lying back with a languid face and enjoying the sway, Jang Ilso turned his head toward a small window in the wall.

The world seen through the tiny window, barely large enough to stick out a head, felt very small. From the scant scenery he couldn’t tell where they were heading or roughly where they were.

But he felt not the least bit stifled.

It amused him simply to imagine what ripples a small stone would make when thrown into a still lake.

Moreover, he was curious, how large those ripples might swell into waves in the end.

“Heh heh heh.”

He let out a low chuckle as if pleased.

“Alliance lord.”

At that moment a cautious voice came from outside the carriage. Jang Ilso replied slowly.

“I’m listening.”

“A report on the situation in Northern lands has arrived.”

Jang Ilso’s long eyelashes twitched faintly.

“Continue then.”

“Both the Nine Great Sects and the Heavenly Friends Alliance are bustling as if they’ve grasped the situation, but it seems they have not yet begun to move in our wake.”

“Hmm…”

“At least the Heavenly Friends Alliance appears to be preparing to move in, but the Nine Great Sects show no particular movement yet. It even seems possible they might just sit tight…”

Jang Ilso laughed, a little incredulous.

“Ga-Myung’s absence shows itself in moments like this.”

“…My apologies.”

“Not that they’re sitting tight; they’re just slow. Bigshots always fear any possible mistake, so they wait around until everything is certain.”

Especially Beop Jeong would act that way.

They had already assessed that old man Beop Jeong during the Namgung Family incident. He is someone who does not want his faction to suffer even the tiniest loss. More precisely, he is terribly wary of any responsibility falling on him.

Such a man would not move rashly in a situation still so uncertain. Even if it meant bearing some loss, he’d wait until things were clearer, then, though the losses might be larger, his personal responsibility would be reduced.

There was nothing particularly new or strange about that.

It’s a common trait of those who stiffly insist on face and moral pretext, the ones who hide behind the mantle of the Justice faction and have acted this way as a matter of course.

Rather, it was Mount Hua and the Heavenly Friends Alliance, who had always countered Jang Ilso’s moves like spirits, who had been uncharacteristically unlike the Justice faction.

“And… ah, never mind.”

“Hmm?”

The trailing off piqued Jang Ilso’s interest. There shouldn’t be any new news to speak of, right?

“Do you have more to say?”

“W-well, it’s a minor piece of news… I’m not sure it merits reporting to the Alliance lord…”

“Speak freely.”

“Yes! Uh… about thirty people have gone missing in the south.”

“Thirty?”

“Yes. The missing are only just entering first-rate level, so it’s not particularly important… but the cause is hard to find…”

The corner of Jang Ilso’s mouth twitched up slightly.

“Location?”

“Near Fuzhou. To the south.”

“Fuzhou?”

“Yes! We thought it might be the work of the Heavenly Friends Alliance moving northward, but the place where the disappearances occurred isn’t totally deserted, and it’s somewhat out of the direction we expected them to be. It doesn’t seem like they’d reveal themselves and fight there. Perhaps there’s another cause.”

“Heh heh heh heh.”

Jang Ilso, who had been silently listening, burst out laughing. Not his usual uncanny, chilling laugh, but the kind he couldn’t hold back because it was too funny.

“Ha ha ha ha. Anyway!”

Laughing cheerfully, he ran his long fingers over his face as if all the frustration he’d felt toward Beop Jeong’s handling had vanished.

“Really, what a nasty-tempered child.”

“Should we send an investigation team…?”

“Leave them be.”

Jang Ilso murmured softly to himself with a laugh.

“It’s like an invitation sent to me. It’s nothing you need worry about.”

At that puzzling remark, silence returned from outside the carriage instead of any reply.
