Jang Ilso didn’t bother relaying what he had learned. There was no reason, and even if he said it they wouldn’t understand, so it would just be wasted breath.

He heard it in his mind.

‘I am here. Don’t you want to come kill me?’

The voice of that fellow, as if speaking.

“Ha ha ha.”

Unable to contain the laugh that pushed through, Jang Ilso laughed once more.

“I have absolutely no taste for falling for a man’s temptations, though.”

In any case, the Ten Thousand People Clan suffered losses because of the Heavenly Friends Alliance this time. Of course those losses could be fully compensated by catching the ones who infiltrated the Southern Island, but…

“Even so, the arithmetic still doesn’t add up.”

Even if the Four Sects Alliance profits and the Heavenly Friends Alliance takes a big hit, that wouldn’t stop the Nine Great Sects from reaping the benefits. From the Four Sects Alliance’s perspective, even if they don’t lose out, the gain would still taste bad.

And the same goes for Mount Hua’s Sword Saint, that is, the Heavenly Friends Alliance. They will try to draw in the Nine Great Sects somehow. From Mount Hua’s Sword Saint’s perspective, the biggest bait to move the Nine Great Sects is Jang Ilso, and…

“They think the biggest bait to move me is themselves? Tsk tsk tsk. Their self-importance is off the charts.”

Jang Ilso smiled and gazed quietly out the window.

This is when it’s interesting.

Mount Hua’s Sword Saint tries to lure him out to move the Nine Great Sects, and he is already moving just as Mount Hua expected. They’ve never spoken about it, yet they think the same.

Having dealt with fools who couldn’t react properly to obvious moves, he couldn’t help laughing at someone trying to seize his head and shake him about.

“You don’t have to hurry them, they’re already doing it.”

From Jang Ilso’s standpoint, there was no reason to watch the Nine Great Sects stand idly by across the river. Flames grow more splendid the more fuel there is.

A long finger tapped his pale cheek.

“But Mount Hua’s Sword Saint…”

The corners of his lips, red as blood, curved up fully.

“Be careful. Those flames might swallow you whole.”

Tilting his head back, he spoke in a languid voice.

“Tell the Pirates to move.”

“Yes, Alliance lord!”

“If those old men with little time left dither, then force them to move.”

Jang Ilso closed his eyes slowly with a triumphant expression.

❀ ❀ ❀

“Report!”

The man who burst through the door raised his voice without a care for propriety.

“Word has it the Four Sects Alliance’s forces, led by the Hegemony Lord, have reached Mount Huang!”

Beop Jeong raised an eyebrow and asked back.

“Already near Mount Huang?”

“Yes! The Beggars’ Union transmitted the news.”

“…Understood.”

“Yes.”

When the door closed, Beop Jeong sighed as if he’d been waiting; Jong Ri-Hyung, sect leader, who had been silently grim, spoke up.

“If they’re at Mount Huang, isn’t that a position that reaches Hefei when moving north?”

“…Yes, that’s right.”

“H-how could this…”

Jong Ri-Hyung asked anxiously with a dismayed face.

“Then shouldn’t we do something as well, Abbott?”

Beop Jeong closed his eyes without a word.

‘How…’

Jong Ri-Hyung felt urgency about the immediate situation, but what was truly boiling Beop Jeong’s blood was something else.

‘That Namgung attaching himself to the Heavenly Friends Alliance would lead to this!’

This situation would never have occurred normally. Beyond the lower Yangtze river, the land they should tread is Anhui, the place where the Namgung Family had held sway like landlords.

If Namgung, once called the head of the Five Great Families, had preserved their strength, even the Four Sects Alliance would not have easily invaded Anhui.

Even if the Four Sects Alliance is powerful, if Namgung had only momentarily held them back, Wudang and Shaolin could have sent reinforcements.

But that once-solid wall has crumbled now, due to the absence of the Namgung Family and Wudang’s sealing of the gates.

Wudang aside, Namgung Family joining the Heavenly Friends Alliance is terribly fatal. If they’d moved to river mouth instead of staying to guard Anhui, this would not have happened.

‘Namgung Hwang’s reckless move has ultimately produced this result.’

Beop Jeong sighed again, unable to ease his frustration.

At that moment, Peng Yeop, head of the Peng Family, spoke.

“As Sect leader Jong Ri said, it’s best to respond first. If we let them cross the river unopposed, the damage will be uncontrollable.”

Beop Jeong slowly opened his closed eyes.

‘Hegemony Lord Jang Ilso.’

There are many people in the world, but is there anyone else who can toy with others so painfully as he does?

“What is the Heavenly Friends Alliance doing now?”

“They seem inclined to follow the Hegemony Lord eastward, but they haven’t departed yet.”

“They’re dilly-dallying, uncharacteristic of them.”

Irritation colored Beop Jeong’s voice; Jong Ri-Hyung glanced at him carefully.

“I hear Mount Hua’s Sword Saint is not currently with the Heavenly Friends Alliance; might that be the reason?”

Beop Jeong bit his lip without realizing it.

What a hopeless fish. When present, they gnawed at him by existing; when gone, his absence brings its own headache.

When Mount Hua’s Sword Saint holds the reins of the Heavenly Friends Alliance, at least one can predict their actions. But with Mount Hua absent, Hyun Jong will likely seize control, and such a Heavenly Friends Alliance is hard to predict even for Beop Jeong of the world.

In the end, Beop Jeong had but one thing to say.

“Let’s wait a little longer.”

“Abbott! We don’t have that time. If they really do cross the river…”

“I know.”

The civilian casualties would inevitably be large, and Anhui would plunge into chaos in an instant.

“It’s not as if I’m at ease. But… at first, it’s not too late to move after seeing the Heavenly Friends Alliance move.”

A sigh escaped from Jong Ri-Hyung’s lips.

Beop Jeong might not be wrong, if the opponent weren’t Jang Ilso.

Why do they still not know that the moment Jang Ilso’s intention becomes clear, it’s already after irreparable events have occurred? Despite all they’ve been through!

‘There is a limit to prudence.’

Mulling words he couldn’t bring himself to say to Beop Jeong’s face, Jong Ri-Hyung clenched his lips in frustration.

“A- Abbott!”

Bang!

At that moment the door flew open and someone barreled in.

“What’s the matter?”

Sensing something had happened, Beop Jeong widened his eyes.

“T-the enemies are crossing the river…!”

“What did you say?”

He leapt up in alarm.

“Jang Ilso, who was at Mount Huang, is crossing the river? What is that supposed to mean?”

“N-no, it’s not that, it’s the Pirates! The Pirates!”

“W-what did you say?”

The messenger who had run in looked completely drained of color.

“A fleet of the Pirates is attacking this place right now!”

Beop Jeong’s wrinkled face went pale in an instant.

Chapter 1373: I Will Make It Like That. (3)

Shuaaah!

Speedboats reinforced with thick planks on their prows cut swiftly through the rough waters of the Yangtze.

Standing tall on the prow, Cheok Il, the shipmaster of the Kitefin Shark Pirates known as Yangtze’s Four Fishes, stared at the scene before him. He muttered in a bubbling voice.

“Pathetic.”

Dozens of ships were lined up along the opposite bank. But there was little sign of people aboard. They’d gathered the vessels in case the Four Sects Alliance ever crossed, but that was all; guards were lax, and the ships were probably empty.

“They must’ve thought we’d never strike first. Stupid fools.”

In fairness, it’s not wholly unreasonable. If they were still under the Black Dragon King’s orders, they wouldn’t have dared cross.

The Black Dragon King was obsessed with money made on the Yangtze and was thus extremely wary of any full-scale tension across the river. He always kept just enough tension to maximize profit.

Despite appearances, the Black Dragon King was cautious; attacking the dock where Shaolin, Kongtong Sect, and the Peng Family were stationed? That was highly unlikely.

But now it’s different. The one giving orders is no longer the Black Dragon King; it’s the Hegemony Lord.

The Hegemony Lord couldn’t care less about the Yangtze region. His sights were set on the vast Northern lands beyond the river. To seize that land, Shaolin would be nothing but an obstacle to trample.

“Captain, we’re almost in range. How shall we set speed?”

“Full speed!”

“Fu-full speed?”

“Ram them as is. Don’t give them time to prepare!”

“Ah, understood!”

The subordinate answered a bit heatedly. One of Yangtze’s Four Fishes twisted his lips as if smiling.

“If they were careless, they must pay.”

Shuaaah!

The speedboats approaching the bank didn’t slow; they accelerated. Their targets were the moored ships.

Those who spotted the enemy late watched their faces go ashen in an instant.

“They’re coming!”

“Th-those lunatics…!”

“Stand back! They’re going to ram us!”

THUDDD!

CRACKKKK!

A huge ship crashed head-on into another. The surface churned violently as splintered hull fragments shot into the air and scattered.

“Aaaaaaaah!”

Those who couldn’t jump off tumbled helplessly across the decks. But that was only the beginning.

THUDDDDDDDD!

THUDDDDDDDDDD!

The Pirates’ war boats, still at speed, rammed one moored ship after another.

Though the ships were similar in size, the Pirates’ vessels were built for naval combat, while the gathered ships were only made to look the part. The sturdier ships rammed with added speed; the outcome was obvious.

Thud!

Yangtze’s Four Fishes slammed his crescent blade to the deck and unleashed a roar so massive it made the surface tremble.

“Attack!”

“Yes!”

Emboldened Pirates leapt onto the decks and immediately boarded the half-destroyed vessels.

“Don’t be afraid! They’re just pirates!”

“We must protect the remaining ships!”

At the news, Kongtong’s disciples and those guarding the ships rushed up and, even in the chaos, drew their weapons in unison.

But half of what they said was true.

Of course, they were disciples of prestigious schools, and on solid ground they’d have no reason to fear mere pirates.

But sadly, where they stood was not solid ground.

Kwaaaang!

Just as Kongtong’s disciples raised their swords to charge, another violent shake tilted the ship.

“Ugh, what the—!”

“The ship, the ship…!”

Kongtong’s disciples hesitated in confusion. The pirates, as if expecting this, ran across the slanted deck as if it were flat.

“Dieeee!”

The pirates quickly plunged through gaps between the ill-positioned defenders. Soon a chaotic melee ensued where friend and foe were hard to tell apart—the pirates’ preferred form of battle on a ship.

Kwaaaaang!

Explosions rang out everywhere.

When ships collided, pirates who’d already dived were smashing the keels of the moored vessels from below.

Those with internal qi had pierced holes with weapons; the weaker ones stuck explosives to the exposed undersides.

Kwaaaang!

With each blast, ships tilted and began to list.

The shoreline quickly became an inferno of chaos.

If they’d faced them head-on, they wouldn’t have been defeated so quickly; but struck by this unexpected ambush, they were thrown into confusion and couldn’t tell front from back.

“Aaaaah!”

“Sajae!”

Someone struck through the side by a trident screamed. Before that wail died, a blade from behind sliced a neck.

“Th-these cowardly pirates!”

Seeing this, Kongtong’s First class disciple Seo Heum swung his sword with a roar. His deadly sword pierced a pirate’s heart, but the enemies kept pouring in faster.

Like a surging wave, pirates climbed onto the tilted ships.

“Hold on a bit! Reinforcements are coming!”

His words weren’t wrong.

“Oooooooooh!”

A tremendous battle cry came from behind the struggling men.

Figures were rushing from afar. A group in yellow robes was visible even from a distance.

“It’s Shaolin!”

“Shaolin has come to aid! Hold on a little longer!”

Though their fame wasn’t what it once was, Shaolin still held great standing in the Central Plains murim world. Knowing Shaolin was coming changed the disciples’ expressions.

Seo Heum too suppressed rising excitement and shouted calmly.

“Focus on defense! When Shaolin arrives, we’ll crush them in one push!”

“Yes!”

He bit his lip hard.

It wasn’t just Shaolin. His sworn sahyung-sajaes would be coming too; once they arrived, they’d wipe these pirate scum out without leaving one alive.

‘I’ll make them pay dearly for their filthy tricks.’

But then a question crossed Seo Heum’s eyes, still burning with hatred for the pirates.

‘What is that?’

His gaze wasn’t on the deck of battle but beyond it, toward the ship’s rail.

Pirates climbing up the deck late were carrying something black and large.

‘Jars?’

They were jars so big they had to be lifted with both hands.

Before he could think, the pirates opened the lids and tipped their contents onto the deck.

Seo Heum flinched.

‘Poison?’

But at that moment a smell wafted from the liquid.

It was a familiar scent. Seo Heum widened his eyes.

“Ah, no…!”

He saw the pirates, having poured out the jars, gloating as they pulled something from their robes and began tearing it apart.

“Flame accelerant! Get away! It’s oil!”

Flames sparked from the pirates’ fingertips onto the deck.

Flare!

The fire caught the oil and spread in an instant. The wide deck became a sea of flames.

“F-fire!”

“Aaaaah! On my back! My back is on fire!”

Kongtong’s disciples who couldn’t escape the sudden blaze screamed and ran frantically. The other ships were the same—terrifying flames engulfed them.

“Th-these madmen…”

It wasn’t just the ships; oil floating on the water had ignited, making it seem as if the entire Yangtze were on fire.

Before that overwhelmingly violent sight, Seo Heum’s legs gave out.

Flare!

The fire was impartial; both the Justice faction’s ships and even the Pirates’ war boats were swallowed by the inferno.

“Ah… ah…”

What to do? How could they possibly respond in this situation? All they could see was fire.

“Oooooooooh!”

Then Beop Gye, having reached the riverbank with his disciples, unleashed a terrifying roar.

“You wicked bastards, I will not forgive you!”

But the Yangtze Four Fishes only let out a bitter laugh upon seeing Beop Gye.

“Looks like the middle man is very angry.”

No wonder; Yangtze River Four Fishes had no intention of answering that anger.

“We’ve achieved our objective; there’s no need to linger and fight. Fall back.”

“Retreat! Retreat!”

No sooner had the Yangtze River Four Fishes given the order than the pirates fighting leapt into the burning Yangtze.

Splash! Splash!

“Hey, where are you running to!”

Frenzied Kongtong disciples, ignoring their own burning robes, slashed at the backs of retreating pirates.

But few were hit. Most pirates had already plunged deep into the Yangtze; as if the earlier onslaught never happened, they vanished without a trace.

All Pirates are skilled in aquatic techniques. Diving beneath where the flames couldn’t reach and escaping was nothing remarkable to them.

“Stupid fools.”

Yangtze River Four Fishes sneered cruelly at Beop Gye and dove into the burning Yangtze.

Kwoong!

Beop Gye burst onto the deck from the bank, his brows trembling with rage. All that remained were ships engulfed in flames and bodies scattered about. The enemies he intended to crush were gone without a trace.

“Th-this…”

Yet he couldn’t lead disciples who lacked aquatic skills into the water to pursue them. Beop Gye looked around in dismay.

Nearly thirty ships, gathered at Beop Jeong’s order with all their might, were burning.

Kwadudduk!

Ships, mangled and split by fire, began to sink one by one. Perhaps it was fortunate in a way; the flames would naturally go out once fully submerged.

“What are you doing! Put it out. Put the fire out!”

In a sudden outburst, Beop Gye, stunned, shouted like someone having a fit.

“Put out the fire at once! Save at least one ship! Now…”

“Elder.”

At that moment someone grabbed his sleeve.

Turning back with a furious face, he saw a Shaolin disciple slowly shaking his head.

“…It’s too late.”

“…”

Beop Gye again turned his gaze blankly.

Black smoke from the burning ships belched up into the sky; even those not here would know exactly what had happened.

“Am-Amitabha…”

A hollow sound escaped his lips. His voice was numb.

‘This situation… what on earth are we supposed to do about this?’

The screams of burned survivors, wails of those clutching the dead, and the shouts of those desperately trying to douse the flames.

It was truly hellish. Beop Gye squeezed his eyes shut.

Chapter 1374: I Will Make It Like That. (4)

“Huuup!”

The Yangtze River Four Fish leaped out of the water and gulped breath greedily. Wiping the water trickling down his face with a careless hand, he floated and stared at the fire raging fiercely in the distance.

“They’re burning nicely.”

A fishy smile spread across his face.

With flames like that, even the most sturdily built ships wouldn’t be able to remain functional. Of course, the fast vessels they had taken also burned to cinders, but that didn’t matter; the Pirates still had plenty of ships.

“Truly, should we call him the Alliance lord?”

Even Yangtze River Four Fish hadn’t imagined they’d guard the ships so sloppily.

No, perhaps it wasn’t originally like that; the Alliance lord’s movements must have drawn even those guarding the ships elsewhere.

In any case, the undeniable fact was that Jang Ilso’s strategy had worked perfectly.

“Has everyone returned?”

“Not all of us yet…”

Yangtze River Four Fish glanced down at the water.

“A day for Yangtze river fish to feast.”

“…”

“The mission is complete. That’s enough. We’re returning.”

“Yes!”

He began swimming to the opposite riverbank. Those trained in water martial arts cut through the Yangtze river like fish born and raised in the water.

Leaving the still-burning riverbank behind.

❀ ❀ ❀

Beop Jeong’s face was stained red by the shadows of the blazing flames. Staring at the burning ships as black smoke billowed, his expression was hardened to a grim degree.

“Th-the… filthy bastards!”

Jong Ri-Hyung couldn’t contain his wrath and spat curses in a choked voice. Those guarding the ships were none other than his disciples. Beop Jeong saw burning ships, but Jong Ri-Hyung saw the bodies of his disciples being carried out from them.

“Abbott!”

Jong Ri-Hyung cried out, his face twisted with rage.

“Those vile bastards must pay a thousand deaths over a million times!”

“Calm down, Jong Sect leader.”

“Do I look like I can calm down?!”

Peng Yeop tried to restrain him, but only provoked more of Jong Ri-Hyung’s fury. Even usually taciturn, cold Paeng Yeop flinched at the intensity.

“Th…”

Grrr-click.

The sound of his teeth grinding rang out from Jong Ri-Hyung’s mouth.

The charred bodies of the disciples, turned into lumps of coal, were still being carried out from the ships. Fortunately, they hadn’t lost many, but these were disciples raised like children—seeing them reduced to charcoal in an instant, how could Jong Ri-Hyung not be distraught?

“How could they commit such a barbaric act!”

As he exploded in anger, a short, hollow laugh escaped Paeng Yeop.

Barbaric? What is barbaric—killing people?

The Four Sects Alliance and the Nine Great Sects were at war to begin with. Though no formal declaration had been made, everyone under heaven would think so—even the Four Sects Alliance and the Nine Great Sects themselves.

Can a surprise attack during wartime truly be called barbaric?

“Abbott! Are you going to leave those Pirates be?!”

As Jong Ri-Hyung shouted again, Beop Jeong turned with a hardened face, then asked in a low voice.

“…What do you propose?”

“What a ridiculous thing to say…!”

“We’ve lost all the ships we were going to take—what method do you propose to strike them? Will you swim across? Against Pirates trained in water martial arts?”

Jong Ri-Hyung couldn’t answer and trembled.

He’d lost his reason in anger for a moment, but Beop Jeong’s words instantly snapped him back to reality.

The ships here had been desperately gathered to prevent the Four Sects Alliance from crossing. Having lost them all, those gathered here had no way to cross the Yangtze river.

“And even the minimal means to stop them from moving freely along the Yangtze river has vanished.”

Peng Yeop muttered in an emotionless voice.

“A clear blunder.”

Beop Jeong, who had been quietly listening, squeezed his eyes shut.

“We should have guarded the ships more securely.”

“Are you saying our children’s abilities were lacking?”

At Peng Yeop’s offhand remark, Jong Ri-Hyung snapped his eyes open and glared. In this tense state, such words were hard to take lightly.

This time, Peng Yeop did not back down either.

“Wasn’t that the result?”

“Watch your words! Was it reasonable to expect that number of people to guard so many ships in the first place?”

Jong Ri-Hyung pressed Peng Yeop, his tone verging on vicious.

“And if the personnel had been at normal levels, we wouldn’t have been defeated by those mere numbers! Yet more than half of the children guarding the ships were suddenly summoned to the manor hall!”

“Their force was only numerical—your men should have been able to handle them.”

“Are you finished!”

“…Let’s stop.”

A hollow sound escaped Beop Jeong.

It was Beop Jeong who, startled by Jang Ilso’s movements, ordered people from all directions to be gathered to hall to stand by.

But who could fail to react like that? The opponent was Jang Ilso.

‘We’ve been played completely.’

Jang Ilso had known.

That his mere movement would alarm Beop Jeong into mobilizing forces, thereby leaving the ships relatively undefended.

He’d been watching this place like the palm of his hand from a thousand li away.

No, perhaps the thing placed on that palm wasn’t the situation here but Beop Jeong himself.

“What good is blaming each other for what’s already happened? Peng, cease as well. It isn’t appropriate to speak to someone who lost disciples.”

“I’m sorry.”

When Lord Peng apologized, heavy silence fell again.

The three of them watched the ships with the flames still uncontained. Disciples from each sect ran about trying desperately to control the blaze, but with oil added it seemed nearly impossible.

When the fires were finally put out, how many usable ships would remain?

“Now…”

A weak voice escaped Jong Ri-Hyung. After his rage surged, he seemed drained.

“Now… what should we do?”

Peng Yeop couldn’t answer that either.

If they’d ridden the momentum of burning the ships and attacked here directly, the situation would have been clearer—you’d just fight back.

But they only burned the ships and withdrew.

They’d been properly struck but refused to fight, leaving them to hold their stinging cheeks and stare dumbly across the river.

Peng Yeop let out a deep sigh.

“What can we do? We must get ships again as quickly as possible.”

“Haven’t we already gathered all the ships we could from the Yangtze river?”

“We should at least pull in small fishing boats as a temporary measure and build new ships.”

“Easier said than done—this isn’t something you fix in a day or two.”

“…So you’ll just sit idle and watch?”

Sharp words flew between them again as if they had never quieted. A deep sigh escaped Beop Jeong.

What should be done?

Even Beop Jeong—what remedy could he have in such a situation?

Paeng Yeop was right. What was lost was lost; they needed to secure ships quickly so the Pirates couldn’t prowl the Yangtze river at will…

“A-Abbott!”

Before he could finish his thought, Jong Ri-Hyung shouted. Beop Jeong suppressed a surge of anger and spoke.

“First… yes, first, as Lord Peng said…”

“N-no! Abbott, look over there! There!”

At that, Beop Jeong opened his closed eyes.

‘There?’

What Jong Ri-Hyung pointed to were only the burning ships. They’d already been accounted for—what else was there to see?

“Even if we salvage a few…”

“That’s not it! Not the fire—look behind it!”

“Behind?”

Only then did Beop Jeong turn his gaze to the distance. Through the licking flames he caught glimpses of what lay beyond, and his eyes widened to the point of staring.

“P- Pirates! They’re moving—the Pirates are moving!”

A chill ran down Beop Jeong’s spine.

The massive fleet of the Pirates, which had long occupied the center of the Yangtze river around Plum Blossom Island, were all turning their bows at once.

Their direction was…

‘East.’

Yes. It was the very direction Jang Ilso had headed.

“Wh-why are they suddenly…?”

Jong Ri-Hyung murmured blankly, and Peng Yeop answered in a cold voice.

“They can move now, can’t they?”

“…What do you mean?”

“It’s true the Pirates had a knife at our throats, but conversely we had daggers at theirs. If their fleet pulled back, we could cross the river and strike at the Four Sects Alliance’s main camp.”

“…Yes?”

“But now that no longer matters. They’ll need ships to ferry them. Ships to move them to the nearest shore from Henan.”

Jong Ri-Hyung spoke as if he didn’t understand.

“No, we only lost our ships—we haven’t had our hands and feet cut off. Without the Pirates, it’s not that hard to swim across a river like this…”

“What’s there?”

“The Four Sects Alliance’s main camp, as you said…”

Jong Ri-Hyung stopped mid-sentence. He too understood Peng Yeop’s meaning now.

Across the river was the Four Sects Alliance’s main camp. But what did that main camp really have?

‘Nothing at all.’

The main camp was meaningless. What mattered about a few buildings hastily erected recently?

The Four Sects Alliance’s main camp only had significance because Jang Ilso was there.

Even if they burned the main camp while the bandits had fled and only civilians remained in Southern lands, what would they gain?

But the Four Sects Alliance was different. By ravaging Henan, they could extract enormous gains.

If they swam across and burned the Four Sects Alliance’s main camp, Jang Ilso would not hesitate; he’d cross to the Northern lands and push into Henan.

“Th-this…”

Jong Ri-Hyung’s face turned pale as death.

“If that’s the case, they should’ve just left with those ships in the first place…”

“Then could they have burned our ships?”

“…Are you saying that Jang Ilso showed his movement because he foresaw all of this?”

Peng Yeop didn’t answer, but even a child could see that silence meant yes.

Meanwhile, the Pirates’ ships steadily moved eastward.

“A- Abbott. How…”

“The ships…”

Beop Jeong bit his lip and then spoke with a sigh.

“Call in those fighting the fires.”

“Sorry?”

A cold murderous glint flashed in Beop Jeong’s eyes.

“This was bound to happen. Gather the disciples. We will follow them to Anhui.”

“…”

“Let this news leak to the Heavenly Friends Alliance as well.”

“A- Abbott?”

Jong Ri-Hyung flinched at the light flowing from his eyes.

“In the end, peace is unattainable without eliminating the villains.”

“…”

“We were bested by the enemy. We must admit that. But Jang Ilso must also learn one thing.”

The gentle, wise monk was nowhere to be seen; Beop Jeong’s voice was icy.

“No matter the stratagems, without strength they mean nothing. The moment they cross that river that will be Jang Ilso’s end. I will make it so.”

His prayer beads shattered in his hand and fell apart into dust.

Chapter 1375: I Will Make It Like That. (5)

“A-alliance leader! Over there!”

“I’m seeing it too.”

Hyun Jong looked at the riverbank where black smoke was billowing up with a hardened expression.

“It’s the place where the Nine Great Sects’ ships were docked.”

Hyun Jong slowly nodded at Tang Gunak’s words.

“It must be the Four Sects Alliance, right?”

“That would be so.”

Unless Beop Jeong suddenly went mad and burned his own ship, the only ones who would pull something like this are the Four Sects Alliance.

“They really caught us off guard.”

Tang Gunak slowly clenched and unclenched the hand hidden in his sleeve.

This is precisely the most terrifying part about Jang Ilso.

When everyone’s attention is fixed in one place, he strikes the most vulnerable spot that no one is paying attention to.

He even uses himself as bait.

“Alliance Leader! The Pirates are moving over there.”

“…”

Everyone clearly saw the flotilla of Pirates that had been occupying the Yangtze river change direction.

Even at a glance, the movement wasn’t abrupt.

“As if they were waiting…”

“It must have been planned already.”

A chill ran through his fingertips.

“Then…”

At that moment, Meng So, who had his arms folded behind them, spoke.

“Should we assume that the Hegemony Lord started moving east to draw attention so they could burn those ships?”

“Hmm.”

Tang Gunak exhaled softly.

There’s no denying that because Jang Ilso moved personally, the defenses across the river were left lax.

No doubt, just the fact that Jang Ilso moved away from across the river would have eased everyone’s minds somewhat.

Looking back, even Tang Gunak himself had unconsciously let his guard down; how much more so the others?

“That’s probably it. If it’s Jang Ilso.”

Meng So’s gaze sank gravely.

“He uses himself as bait to divert attention, then attacks where that attention isn’t…”

When Meng So looks at a person, he imagines an animal that matches them.

But he could not conjure any animal fitting for that Hegemony Lord.

More cunning than a fox, more ferocious than a tiger, where in the world could you find such a beast?

“What’s important is.”

At that moment, the silent Hyun Jong spoke.

“It’s not the fact that the ships burned, but that Jang Ilso is still moving east.”

“…”

“He’s moving toward where the youngsters are coming.”

Tang Gunak and Meng So looked at Hyun Jong in sudden surprise.

While the others were worrying about how Jang Ilso’s move would affect this standoff, Hyun Jong’s gaze was fixed solely on Mount Hua’s disciples.

As Tang Gunak was about to speak, Hyun Jong continued.

“Now the Nine Great Sects must know as well—that Jang Ilso’s movement isn’t mere bluff.”

“If they’re not fools, they will.”

Tang Gunak’s gaze, heavy with mixed emotions, turned toward the far bank.

‘Hegemony Lord.’

It was hard to deny.

By establishing the Four Sects Alliance’s main base across the river and holed up there, Jang Ilso had implanted a single thought in people’s minds, even in Tang Gunak’s brain.

Maybe Jang Ilso would be content merely to dominate the Southern lands?

It was absurd, yet a realistic question.

The land of Southern lands is vast. Even in the whole history of the kangho, it’s rare for the Evil Sect to have completely dominated such a huge territory.

In other words, Jang Ilso had already achieved feats that would etch his name into the annals of the martial world.

So why would he start a war against the mighty Nine Great Sects to swallow more?

If you shift your perspective a little, it’s clear: right now Jang Ilso faces no threat.

Beop Jeong might serve as a rallying point against Jang Ilso’s attack, but he lacks the influence to muster the forces to attack the Southern lands; and the Heavenly Friends Alliance, Jang Ilso’s most hostile foe, doesn’t have the power alone to pursue the Southern lands.

‘In other words, if Jang Ilso wants to maintain this balance, no one in the world can shake his position.’

Everything is guaranteed. Everything.

By holed up in that lavish main base, by taking the easy choice, Jang Ilso could have likely kept his status as the ruler of Southern lands for decades.

Maybe, given his prudence, he could have lived as the Southern land’s emperor until the moment he died.

That’s why they doubted.

Perhaps this situation had carved even a small taste of ‘stability’—a word unbefitting that ravenous beast named Jang Ilso—into him.

No matter how much of a beast driven by desire he was, abandoning all that and gambling his life away wouldn’t be an easy choice.

If he compromised even a little, he could literally gain everything.

But those raging flames were mocking all such thoughts.

Those flames spoke in Jang Ilso’s voice: he would never be satisfied with something like Southern Lands.

Even if he were consumed by those flames and turned to ash, he would forever covet this side of the river.

Feeling the chilling malice embedded in those flames, Tang Gunak pressed his lips together.

“Reporting!”

At that moment, someone rushed into manor and raised their voice.

“The Nine Great Sects are moving. It looks like they’re trying to follow the Pirates’ ships moving along the river!”

Tang Gunak nodded automatically.

In that case, the Nine Great Sects would have no other choice.

They don’t know what Jang Ilso and that pirate flotilla moving east will do, so they can only follow for now.

“Shouldn’t we move as well?”

The most anxious right now would be Hyun Jong, no matter what anyone says.

Though the remark guessed his feelings, surprisingly Hyun Jong didn’t answer and only stared at the ships on the river with a stiff gaze.

“Leader?”

When Tang Gunak asked again, Hyun Jong spoke with a hardened face.

“Family lord.”

“Yes, Alliance Leader.”

“What do you think the Hegemony Lord is aiming for?”

“…Yes?”

Aiming for what?

Isn’t the Hegemony Lord’s aim already clear? Burn the Nine Great Sects’ flotilla and seize complete control of the Yangtze river…

“Isn’t he trying to cross to the Northern lands?”

“…”

Hyun Jong quietly shook his head.

“Do you think the Hegemony Lord would have pulled this off for that one thing alone?”

“…”

Tang Gunak fell silent at those words.

Indeed, there was merit to that statement. Jang Ilso had always aimed for more than they expected.

“But… what could Jang Ilso gain from this…”

Nine Great Sects. Heavenly Friends Alliance. Four Sects Alliance.

The elites of those four powers would be gathered in one place.

‘Ah, no.’

That’s not all. If their guess is right, Chung Myung and his party moving north from the Southern lands are also likely to rendezvous there.

In other words, everyone who guides the fate of the current realm would be gathered in one place.

‘What are they trying to do?’

A cold chill ran down Tang Gunak’s spine.

If anyone would, it would be the Hegemony Lord; he would seek something from that situation, something they couldn’t even imagine.

But what on earth…?

“We must hurry.”

“…Yes, Alliance Leader.”

The Nine Great Sects showing their movement was also a statement of intent to open the way east for them.

So for now they must move east. Whatever happens, if they’re not there they can’t respond.

“We’ll sortie within the hour. Prepare.”

“Yes!”

As Tang Gunak hurried off, Hyun Jong squeezed his eyes shut.

‘Chung Myung.’

Chung Myung’s innocent, smiling face surfaced in his mind.

The face of Chung Myung who always hid his true intentions behind a smile.

‘Have you perhaps foreseen even this situation?’

He did not know.

But he believed it.

On this vast chessboard of the kangho, the only one who could sit opposite the Hegemony Lord would be Chung Myung.

Hyun Jong prayed for it… he fervently hoped so.

❀ ❀ ❀

Whooosh.

Baek Cheon, who had struck the ground, looked around with a hardened face.

The heightened senses swept the surroundings, but he sensed no sign of enemies.

‘What the hell is going on?’

A short while ago they attacked nearly thirty members of the Evil Sect and ended their lives—an action they took even though they could have avoided it.

Yet no matter how much time passed, there was no sign of heightened alert.

‘Could they not notice that nearly thirty people disappeared?’

No way.

No matter how rotten the Four Sects Alliance’s martial ranks are, thirty is not a small number anywhere.

Especially in a situation like this, where enemies are probing the interior.

Then, logically, this shouldn’t be happening…

Baek Cheon unconsciously glanced aside. In that instant, his eyes met Chung Myung’s.

Baek Cheon violently turned his head to avoid Chung Myung’s gaze, but…

“Huuuuuh.”

“…”

“Well, ain’t that strange? We beat nearly thirty of them—so why aren’t those damn guys freaking out? They should be, right?”

“…”

“It’s not like the kids’ eyes suddenly disappeared, right? Isn’t that so, Green Forest King?”

“Haha. Why do you say that?”

Im So-Byeong grinned as he picked up the remark.

“If the Taoist says that, wouldn’t the one who was foaming at the mouth and screaming that we’d all be dead for poking at the wrong guys feel extremely—eh?—awkward? A gentleman shouldn’t cling to another’s discomfort. One should also know how to let it pass magnanimously.”

“That’s how it is, right?”

“Ah yes. Of course. Since the Acting Sect Leader protested, do you think he did it out of malice and foamed at the mouth? He acted with good intentions. Even the sages said: blame malice, not ignorance. The wise Taoist should understand.”

“Tch. If you say it like that…”

“Indeed, indeed.”

Baek Cheon’s hand tremblingly held the sword.

What about the ghosts? Why haven’t they dragged those bastards away?

One bastard alone would have been infuriating, but gathering two similar ones made Baek Cheon’s insides feel like plum blossoms dancing in his belly.

“Tsk tsk. I told you so. How can a Taoist not trust people’s words like that?”

“Whom can we blame but the fact that the muriml world’s morality has fallen to the ground?”

“But Chung Myung.”

Just as Baek Cheon was about to lose it, Yoon Jong naturally spoke to change the subject.

“So the Nine Great Sects really moved?”

“Yes.”

“Why?”

“That I don’t know either.”

“…”

Yoon Jong looked at Chung Myung with an expression that said, ‘What kind of bastard is this?’

He knows they’re coming but doesn’t know why—does that make any sense?

Chung Myung let out a short chuckle at that look.

“What I know is that it’s not a reason big enough to make the bald king move.”

“Then?”

“If it’s Jang Ilso, he’ll use any means to lure the Nine Great Sects in.”

“…”

Chung Myung twisted the corner of his mouth.

Surrounded on all sides. This was the only way to break through the situation.

‘He’s got all the cards. All that’s left is to fight.’

A cold, blue gleam shone in Chung Myung’s eyes.

Chapter 1376: The Preparations Are Never Complete. (1)

“Captain. The Pirates attacked the river mouth, burned all the ships, then withdrew.”

One of the beggars discreetly tailing the lavish four-horse carriage moving east from our main unit grimaced sharply at that report.

“Ships?”

“Y-yes, sir.”

“Those bastards… It looks like they’re really trying to make a proper go of it.”

He had expected as much.

The Beggars’ Union prioritizes information coming directly from the field above all else. No matter how brilliant someone’s mind is at analyzing data, the atmosphere felt only at the scene cannot be conveyed otherwise.

The instinct of Mu Chu-Gae, a beggar seasoned in that ‘field sense,’ was shouting, those bastards’ movements were by no means mere bluster.

A sinister momentum that seemed about to explode at any moment was reaching even this distant place vividly.

“What do we do?”

“…Do what?”

“W-we’re in Southern lands, aren’t we? If a war breaks out, people like us will have our heads cut off in an instant—shouldn’t we flee immediately?”

Suha looked genuinely anxious, his face drained pale. Mu Chu-Gae’s facial muscles twitched.

“Damn it, since when did it become natural for beggar brats to value their lives and run away?”

“I-I’m not saying that, but…”

“And yet you claim to be a member of the Beggars’ Union’s Black Dirty Troops?”

At that sharp, anger-laced reprimand, the panicked beggar hung his head in shame.

“I’m sorry…”

“Tch.”

Clicking his tongue briefly in displeasure, Mu Chu-Gae turned his head and calmly watched the main body of the Four Sects Alliance as it moved.

‘Run away?’

Run away, go where? The moment they lost track of them, the Northern lands would cease to be safe ground as well.

Crossing a river doesn’t guarantee safety. Now that Jang Ilso has moved, nowhere in the realm is safe anymore.

Therefore, they must never lose sight of their trail.

Tracking their movements at the risk of life itself is the Black Dirty Troops’s duty—and the mission of its chief.

Deep in thought, Mu Chu-Gae couldn’t help but snicker.

‘Besides, do you really think they don’t know we’re tailing them?’

They probably know. Jang Ilso, who toys with the world in his palm, wouldn’t be unaware that he was being tailed. The likely reason Mu Chu-Gae and his subordinates are still alive is that Jang Ilso doesn’t intend to hide his movements.

All the more reason fleeing now would be pointless.

“Captain! They’re stopping!”

“Get down!”

When Mu Chu-Gae slightly lowered his raised hand, his followers flattened themselves to the ground to hide. Only their tense eyes peered out through the tall reeds.

‘What is it?’

Lowering his body, Mu Chu-Gae pushed his internal qi into his eyes.

They had no reason to stop now. Nothing particular had appeared.

Which meant?

Mu Chu-Gae’s gaze instinctively went to the carriage at the group’s center—the ostentatious carriage drawn by four white horses.

The carriage door, which had been tightly shut until now, finally began to open.

The moment he saw a fluttering crimson robe, Mu Chu-Gae involuntarily forgot to breathe.

‘Jang Ilso!’

Even at a distance where people looked like ants, he could recognize who it was. Though people are as numerous as grains of sand, only one could dare to wear such attire.

Hegemony Lord Jang Ilso. The leader of the Ten Thousand People Clan and the Alliance lord of the Four Sects Alliance had finally revealed himself.

As Jang Ilso stepped out, everyone moving with the carriage dropped to their knees and paid him respects in unison.

Goosebumps ran down Mu Chu-Gae’s spine.

This was a sight one would never see among the Justice faction.

Even the Abbott of Shaolin, said to be at the undisputed pinnacle of the realm, or the head of Wudang, famed as the North Star of Taoists, do not receive such deference from anyone, and they must not.

Only two people in the world can receive such homage: the emperor, ruler of all, and Jang Ilso, the sovereign of Southern lands.

Mu Chu-Gae bit his lip hard.

No matter how aloof people of the martial world were from the secular world, commoners must never dare to display such behavior. It was disloyalty to the emperor and blind worship of power, precisely the sort of thing those who titled themselves the Justice faction must beware most of.

Yet still… Mu Chu-Gae could not deny that his heart trembled at the sight. He would never admit it even under death, but the image Jang Ilso displayed now was what he had longed for even in his dreams.

“That man…”

“Shh!”

Mu Chu-Gae clamped a hand over his subordinate’s mouth before he could speak.

Even if it were not Jang Ilso himself but his grandfather, they couldn’t be sure to detect those warriors of stealth at this distance, but there was no harm in being cautious at all times.

‘What are they trying to do?’

With a tense gaze, Mu Chu-Gae watched the Four Sects Alliance’s movements.

Then Jang Ilso made a hand signal. No sooner had his gentle gesture finished than some of those kneeling sprang up and rushed off swiftly somewhere.

‘What…?’

Mu Chu-Gae’s eyes widened.

If a portion had moved during transit, Mu Chu-Gae would have sent his men to tail them too. But in this situation he couldn’t respond recklessly.

“C-captain…”

No matter how skilled they were at tailing, it was impossible to catch up belatedly with such warriors, especially if they had to follow while staying hidden.

But the situation didn’t end there.

Every time Jang Ilso gestured, another group vanished from before their eyes.

This was the very definition of being taken in plain sight. Mu Chu-Gae couldn’t contain his anger and spat out a curse.

“Damn it…”

At that moment, having finished his commands, Jang Ilso turned without hesitation.

Just as Mu Chu-Gae, drained of energy, was about to exhale the breath he’d been holding, Jang Ilso suddenly and calmly looked in their direction.

Thud!

His heart dropped. Instinctively, Mu Chu-Gae buried his head deeper, as if to hide in the earth to avoid those eyes.

‘Did he… notice us?’

He assumed they might have suspected someone was tailing them, but did they really know exactly where they were?

Impossible. No one in the world could pinpoint their trail at this distance. Absolutely impossible.

Cold sweat poured down. He breathed raggedly and thought, but couldn’t bring himself to lift his head. His body wouldn’t obey him; he burrowed into the ground like a rabbit avoiding a wolf’s gaze.

The thick scent of earth and grass, the damp, musty smell of sweat flooded his nostrils. How much time passed like that?

“Captain, they’re moving again.”

Only when a trembling voice came from behind did Mu Chu-Gae snap back to reality. Realizing the disgraceful posture he’d been in, he let go of the grass clenched in his hand and lifted his torso.

The carriage, its door firmly shut, and the escorting group continued moving as if nothing had happened.

Mu Chu-Gae was momentarily speechless. He felt thoroughly toyed with, yet oddly not angry, the sort of anger one reserves for equals.

He was the captain of the Beggars’ Union’s Black Dirty Troops.

He held a stature not to be trifled with anywhere in the murim world, but all he could do now was feel relief at having survived, shivering as the sweat trickling down his spine cooled.

“Captain, what will you do? Pursue them?”

“…Pursue?”

For a moment a laugh escaped as if the air had been let out.

“Leave them be. Report to the main unit, then tail the main force.”

“But…”

“Do you think following them will tell us anything?”

When no answer came, a tone of self-mockery touched Mu Chu-Gae’s mouth.

“Don’t act the hero. Make sure to tail them properly.”

“Yes.”

Mu Chu-Gae bit his lip. His gaze fixed on the small square window in the carriage—easy to see out from inside, but nearly impossible to see in from outside.

Jang Ilso was looking at the world through that tiny window now. But the man who saw the vast world with his own eyes could not at all match Jang Ilso’s perspective.

‘Even if I can’t see with my eyes, I will follow his steps, by any means necessary.’

A flash of killing intent briefly stained Mu Chu-Gae’s gaze as he glared at the carriage.

The dazzlingly white carriage and the scarlet pennants fluttering in all directions.

At this moment, the center of the world was clearly that tiny carriage.

❀ ❀ ❀

Clack.

The carriage door shut firmly.

“It’s really inconvenient without Ga-Myung. Even these trivial orders I have to give myself… tsk, tsk.”

Jang Ilso sighed placidly and slouched onto the soft bedding, his languid movements heavy with annoyance.

He slowly picked up the bottle and cup on the table and filled the cup.

Despite the carriage’s considerable rocking, the clear liquid arced steadily into the small cup. The strong scent of liquor spread richly through the cramped carriage.

Clack.

Setting the bottle down, Jang Ilso gazed at the cup’s rippling surface while reclining. The liquor that had shimmered with each jolt of the carriage soon lay motionless.

A gloomy aura clouded his pale eyes watching the cup. He slowly drank.

Savoring the liquor as it spread from his mouth to his stomach, Jang Ilso then turned his gaze to the carriage’s small square window.

He wiped his liquor-wet lips with a finger and murmured.

“Not difficult, is it? Yes, not a difficult thing.”

He didn’t care that the world he could see from here was small.

If he could draw those he watched into this small world, then even through a little window he would effectively see the entire world!

One by one.

All those he had been watching were gathering into this small window.

Those who crave power, those who cannot sever attachments, those who follow orders blindly, and even the pitiable souls groaning under their ideals.

“Hm.”

Music reached his ears—a splendid drumbeat loud enough to draw everyone’s attention. It was the sound announcing the start of a grand stage.

“Preparations… are now complete.”

Muttering as if speaking to someone, Jang Ilso slowly turned his head the other way—toward the carriage wall without a window.

His hollow, crystalline gaze drifted toward somewhere in the south beyond that wall. It seemed full of hatred, and yet at the same time aching with longing.

“So come on, Mount Hua’s Sword Saint. A stage doesn’t begin without its leading actor.”

A deep smile spread over his face. It was as ominous as a blood-red crescent moon.

Chapter 1377: The Preparations Are Never Complete. (2)

“W-what on earth is happening?”

The fishermen of the Yangtze River who had come to the riverside murmured.

A blaze far off was dying down. Though it now seemed to be subsiding, a short while ago the flames had roared so fiercely they looked as if they might engulf the entire river.

It was a rare spectacle to see the riverbank consumed by fire. But the flames were not the only thing drawing their attention.

“Bo-boats…!”

Huge ships began to cross the middle of the Yangtze River. Even at a glance they were nothing like ordinary merchant vessels. They were the combat ships of the Pirates, specialized in overtaking and ramming other craft.

Watching the columns of ships relentlessly steaming eastward, everyone tensed and swallowed hard.

They didn’t need to think hard to know.

That right now something enormous enough to shake the entire Yangtze River was unfolding.

“Is… is a war breaking out?”

“Hey, watch your language! War? Don’t say such ominous things!”

Voices shuddered here and there as if the words alone sent chills down their spines. Even those voices were thick with anxiety — they knew these were no idle words.

“Or could it be the Pirates would move like that? Those ships have been sitting unmoving for months.”

“They might be returning to their lair.”

“What nonsense…”

Fear crept into everyone’s eyes.

They instinctively knew the brief peace they had enjoyed was over.

“How long has the chaos been going on… There are hardly any people left around here now.”

“That’s true.”

“Now, now. Don’t worry too much. Isn’t that why the Nine Great Sects are here on the Yangtze? No matter how fearsome those scoundrels are, what could they do with those people present?”

It was a hopeful remark.

But everyone present knew otherwise. If, as rumors claimed, the Nine Great Sects were truly that formidable, they wouldn’t have allowed such villains to run rampant even on the far side of the river.

It was at that moment.

“There, over there!”

“Huh?”

At the shout, those who turned their heads widened their eyes.

Whoosh!

A group of people was running swiftly toward the riverside where they stood. They came so fast that though spotted far off, in the time it took to blink they were already upon them.

“S-Shaolin?”

They wore yellow monastic robes, and a few at the front had red cloth draped over them. Of the countless temples across the land, only Shaolin wear those yellow robes.

The murim monks of Shaolin passed them in an instant with stern faces. Behind them came groups wearing large broadswords and cold-eyed swordsmen, running in succession.

One man watching the martial fighters run with frightened eyes spoke in a dazed voice.

“Could something truly massive be about to happen?”

A warlike atmosphere began to hang over the entire Yangtze River.

❀ ❀ ❀

“Abbott! Jang Ilso has revealed himself.”

“Jang Ilso?”

“Yes! The one who had been holed up inside the carriage the whole journey came out and gave orders personally. A small number who received his orders left the main force, but reports say their exact objective is hard to determine.”

Beop Jeong gripped the prayer beads in his hand tightly.

‘Hegemony Lord.’

The mere fact that he gave orders was not the important thing.

What mattered was that orders can just as easily be issued from inside a carriage.

That Jang Ilso went out of his way to appear outside the carriage was clearly a taunt directed at Beop Jeong.

Blue veins bulged on the back of Beop Jeong’s hand.

Shaolin had clearly been struck first and yet could not even summon the nerve to counterattack. All they were doing was doggedly following in Jang Ilso’s wake.

Jang Ilso was asking Shaolin that very thing now.

If he reached the place where Jang Ilso was — across the river — what would they do then?

Did they have the courage to cross the river with the clear intent of striking at him here? To thrust a law-laden execution blade into Jang Ilso’s throat and punish him for his crimes?

‘So utterly shameless…’

A cold, bluish killing intent flowed from Beop Jeong’s eyes.

Others might dismiss it as trivial, but Beop Jeong felt Jang Ilso’s mocking clearly.

It was a sneer aimed not at just anyone, but at the Abbott of Shaolin who led the three sects beyond the river.

When had he ever been subjected to such mockery?

What made Beop Jeong angrier still was that there was no way to respond.

“Abbott, what will you do?”

Beop Gye casually called to Beop Jeong. Beop Jeong closed his eyes for a moment, then opened them and answered in a deliberately calm voice.

“We need not respond to every such provocation.”

“Yes, Abbott.”

“First, we must tail them so we can react to the enemy’s movements. We can think about countermeasures when it’s possible to implement them. No matter how good a plan is, if it’s too late it’s meaningless.”

“Yes!”

“Increase the pace!”

Having given the order, Beop Jeong drove his foot more forcefully into the ground.

Beop Gye stared at his back. The figure did not seem so large.

‘Abbott…’

What Beop Jeong said was certainly sound logic. And when dealing with Jang Ilso, it was important to adhere to that logic. Jang Ilso is someone who uses cunning like breathing; to react to every petty act of his risks missing something far greater.

But one doubt would not leave Beop Gye’s mind.

‘Is the Abbott truly so calm right now?’

Beop Gye couldn’t help but realize a certain fact anew.

From the Yangtze incident to the present, Shaolin had been entangled with the Four Sects Alliance countless times, and had often been enraged by their machinations. There was no way not to know how dangerous Jang Ilso was.

But coldly speaking, it wouldn’t be an exaggeration to say that Beop Jeong, not Shaolin, had never directly traded moves with Jang Ilso.

When the Yangtze incident occurred, it was Beop Gye, not Beop Jeong, who led Shaolin. When the Plum Blossom Island affair erupted, Shaolin only arrived belatedly after it was already too late.

This meant that Beop Jeong had never once directly confronted Jang Ilso’s schemes; he’d never been the direct target of Jang Ilso’s malice.

If that’s the case… if that is truly so…

“Didn’t I tell you to hurry?”

“Yes, yes! Abbott!”

Startled by Beop Jeong’s urging, Beop Gye snapped out of his thoughts. He motioned to those following, and the Shaolin disciples running behind gritted their teeth and quickened their pace.

Confirming the overall increase in speed, Beop Jeong glared coldly toward the far side of the river.

An indescribable unease filled Beop Gye’s eyes as he watched that back.

❀ ❀ ❀

“Alliance Leader! The distance between us and those ahead is widening.”

“Hmm.”

Hyun Jong looked ahead with a contemplative expression.

‘Beop Jeong.’

It was undoubtedly Hyun Jong who was most anxious right now. Yet the three sects, led by Shaolin and moving eastward ahead, were traveling so swiftly they would even surprise him.

Tang Gunak spoke in a low voice.

“…Indeed, the Nine Great Sects’ strength is not to be underestimated.”

“There’s nothing new about that.”

Hyun Jong nodded faintly.

Exactly so. The reason the Nine Great Sects have lost their former prestige is not because they are weak, but because, despite possessing sufficient power, they have not wielded it properly.

If Beop Jeong had truly decided to use this opportunity to wage proper war against the Four Sects Alliance, from the Heavenly Friends Alliance’s perspective it would be like gaining a powerful ally.

Though it would be a temporary ally that could not share every intention.

“What will you do? Shall we increase our speed?”

Tang Gunak asked, but Hyun Jong did not answer immediately. His gaze was grave.

Compared to the vanguard made up solely of the Nine Great Sects’ elites, the Heavenly Friends Alliance contained far too many sects. Their hearts might be aligned, but if they increased speed further it could be difficult to move as cohesively as those ahead.

In the end, Hyun Jong shook his head.

“No. We will not. Maintain our current speed.”

“But, Alliance Leader. If we do that, we may fall behind not only the Nine Great Sects but even Jang Ilso who went ahead.”

“That’s fine.”

Taken aback by the unexpected reply, Tang Gunak eyed him suspiciously.

Hyun Jong, the alliance leader and former sect leader of Mount Hua, was surely the most impatient of all — no one could deny that. Yet he said it was fine if they were late…

Then Hyun Jong spoke calmly.

“Lord Tang.”

“Yes?”

“When will the sun set?”

Tang Gunak’s face briefly went blank. What kind of strange question was that all of a sudden?

Hyun Jong said.

“The Pirates are now gathering all available vessels and heading east. Is that correct?”

“Yes, that’s what the reports say.”

“And across the river the Four Sects Alliance’s elite forces are heading east. So their troops are likely split: those guarding the main force and those following Jang Ilso.”

“Of course…”

Tang Gunak shut his mouth. He had grasped what Hyun Jong was about to say.

“C-could it be…?”

“Yes. We’ll keep a moderate speed and send our boats ahead. And when the sun sets…”

Hyun Jong declared in an even voice.

“We will lead the alliance and cross the river.”

“Alliance L-leader!”

Tang Gunak’s face went pale.

“That’s far too dangerous. If the enemy learns we’ve crossed the river, they could turn and strike us first. Then we’d be forced into a pincer with Jang Ilso and the Pirates above the river.”

“Isn’t that the situation the youngsters are in right now? Isn’t it?”

Tang Gunak fell silent.

“You need not join if it’s too dangerous. But Mount Hua will cross the river.”

Tang Gunak suddenly looked back. The eyes of Mount Hua’s disciples clinging close showed not the slightest wavering.

‘So this was his intention from the start?’

As if answering Tang Gunak’s thought, Hyun Jong said.

“It’s meaningless to merely follow at a safe distance. Doing so might mean we’ll only stand by and watch Jang Ilso harm the youngsters across the river. I have no intention of doing that.”

“Alliance Leader…”

“I finally understand why, even when I believed the youngsters were right, a doubt lingered in my heart. Why everything felt somehow wrong.”

“…”

“A sect shares its fate together.”

Tang Gunak’s expression as he watched Hyun Jong looked as if he were entranced. No wonder — Hyun Jong’s face seemed almost sculpted to embody the words ‘man of the way.’

“I will share my fate with those youngsters. I’m only now correcting what should have been done from the beginning.”

Tang Gunak slowly turned his head and watched the Yangtze River.

A crimson sunset beyond the western hills stained the Yangtze River a deep red. What might ordinarily have been beautiful now felt like an ominous calm before the storm.

“…Then we will go with you.”

“…”

“Isn’t a friend also someone who shares your fate?”

Hyun Jong quietly closed his eyes.

‘Chung Myung.’

Now he seemed to understand. What that youngster had tried to create.

‘Is this merely my delusion?’

All that had been hidden began to exert its power the moment Chung Myung was absent, like a barrier that would firmly protect Mount Hua even without him.

‘But you, lad.’

A faint smile appeared at the corner of Hyun Jong’s mouth.

“It is not you protecting Mount Hua, but Mount Hua protecting you.”

Muttering words barely audible, Hyun Jong stamped the ground resolutely and moved forward.

Chapter 1378: The Preparations Are Never Complete. (3)

“Shoot, shoot!”

Paaaaaat!

An arrow sliced through the air. Jo Gul’s face contorted.

“Damn arrows! I’m sick to death of them!”

Bang!

The moment he shoved off the ground, his sword cleaved the air like a ray of light. In the sword-shadow drawn in the air, the raining arrows bounced off like raindrops striking an eave.

“Ugh.”

Jo Gul landed after dealing with the arrows, clutching his side and groaning. Then he shouted angrily.

“Those Evil Sect bastards don’t charge with swords like men, they cowardly shoot arrows? Aren’t they ashamed!”

Baek Cheon muttered as if stunned.

“…Isn’t being cowardly why they’re called the Evil Sect?”

What on earth does that bastard picture when he thinks of the Evil Sect?

Baek Cheon was baffled, but Yoon Jong worried about Jo Gul.

“Don’t show off and fall back! You’re not even in good shape!”

“Psh, who here’s really in good shape? We’re all the same.”

“That crazy bastard…”

Jo Gul charged forward like a colt again. An incredulous sigh escaped Yoon Jong.

Of course, it’s not entirely wrong. There are fewer truly fit people here than those who aren’t.

The problem is that even the unfit come in different levels.

“He’s about as good as a walking corpse!”

Yoon Jong stamped the ground with fiery eyes. Rather than lecture Jo Gul until he understood not to show off, it would be easier to tell a three-month-old puppy to stop barking and sit quietly.

“Taap!”

In an instant, Yoon Jong surged past Jo Gul and pierced the throat of the man who’d been engaging him.

Jo Gul’s eyes went wide.

“Sa- sahyung! That was mine!”

“Shut your mouth, brat?”

Yoon Jong, uncharacteristically coarse, spat the words and immediately dropped his stance, cutting down the Evil Sect thug’s blade as it rushed in. As he prepared to counterattack, suddenly someone vaulted over him and drove a sword down—he saw that person’s back.

Kwaduk!

“Grrr!”

The Evil Sect man, his chest cleanly pierced through the center, collapsed with an expression of disbelief. The swordsman who drew his blade without hesitation and pushed the falling man shouted loudly.

“Sasuk… huh?”

Yoon Jong, who had naturally assumed it was Baek Cheon, flinched. It wasn’t the Mount Hua uniform.

“Press the attack at once! It’s nothing!”

“Yes!”

Martial artists in blue uniforms flashed past Yoon Jong to his left and right. He couldn’t help but smirk for a moment.

“I’ve seen this scene somewhere a lot before…”

Gwak Hwan-So, having felled his enemy, never looked back. The Southern Island’s disciples doggedly followed behind him.

Yoon Jong habitually twirled his sword to fling off the blood and straightened his back.

‘Is this when you use the phrase “to regard someone with fresh eyes”?’

Of course it’s still lacking. If imitating Chung Myung’s tone, he’d say, “You’re still a long way off, you rookies.”

This isn’t just Yoon Jong’s arrogance. Objectively, they still look like children holding fine swords. Especially in how they struggle to properly execute the martial skills they’ve learned.

Yet despite that, they are wielding those fine swords. It’s a world of difference from when they first trembled in fear, unaware of what was in their hands.

“…Experience really is…”

“Ah, damn it! Watch your words!”

“Huh?”

Muttering to himself, Yoon Jong turned in bafflement at the admonition that came flying to him. Jo Gul contorted his face and shouted loudly.

“I understand what sahyung is thinking, but if you say it like that, it just justifies that rotten bastard putting us through every hell!”

He was, of course, talking about Chung Myung.

“…That won’t do.”

That’s that and this is this. Ahem.

“So just keep your mouth shut!”

“…Got it.”

Yoon Jong answered humbly. But then he let out a short laugh.

It’s true the enemies before them aren’t that strong, but if the Southern Island hadn’t stepped up and fought fiercely, there wouldn’t have been time to converse comfortably with Jo Gul like now.

‘If they fight calmly, they can’t help but be a capable force.’

Even if they’re called the least of the Nine Great Sects, they’re still one of the top nine sects under heaven. There’s an enormous difference between those called part of the Nine Great Sects even by the smallest margin and those who are not.

If they keep fighting like this, they’ll definitely become reliable troops. And the moment they lose all hesitation in fighting, they might become one of the sects representing the Heavenly Friends Alliance.

Especially…

“Your sword-tip’s shaking! Don’t get excited!”

“Yes!”

Yoon Jong narrowed his eyes.

‘That person is definitely great.’

His gaze was fixed on Gwak Hwan-So’s back, who fought fiercely yet never forgot to issue precise commands.

‘Because of him, one occasionally forgets that the Sect Leader Geum is even here.’

Under normal circumstances that would be impossible.

Even a leading disciple cannot be compared to the current Sect Leader. Even if one is to become a future Sect Leader, you can’t compare realized achievements with vague possibilities, can you?

But what’s remarkable about Gwak Hwan-So is that he can sometimes make even Yoon Jong, who fully knows that fact, mistake him.

The image of Gwak Hwan-So seen at the Shaolin martial contest in the past is hardly recalled anymore. His present form is so striking that the past is all but forgotten.

A wry smile tugged at Yoon Jong’s mouth.

He finally understood the reactions of those who knew Mount Hua when they saw the current Five Swords. If someone had changed even half as much as Gwak Hwan-So, they’d naturally be shocked.

“Sahyung, front!”

“I know!”

“I’ll take the front.”

“Fall back! This is my position!”

And the Southern Island’s disciples, who took orders from Gwak Hwan-So, showed absolute trust in him.

People who overcome crises together become inseparably close. The bonds they’ve built in this short time might even surpass long periods spent together within a sect.

‘They’ll grow stronger.’

Of course, the damage the Southern Island Sect suffered isn’t easily recoverable.

They lost countless things. They had to leave the Southern Island that served as their stronghold, and many disciples departed in the process. Nearly half of the remaining disciples were sacrificed while breaking through the Southern Lands.

Frankly, Southern Island Sect might already have lost the right to be called one of the Nine Great Sects. Even if no further losses occur, it will clearly take a very long time to recover the strength they’ve lost.

Still, Yoon Jong believed the Southern Island would become stronger.

He’d already experienced that what makes a sect strong isn’t conditions. Neither territory, wealth, nor even justification is what matters.

What makes a sect strong is solely the hearts and will of those who belong to it.

And the Southern Island Sect has that now. The will they didn’t have when they left the Southern Island Sect is now present.

“Yeah. Grow stronger…”

“Don’t daydream on the battlefield, you pathetic fool!”

Baek Cheon shouted and dashed forward like a gust. Yoon Jong’s brow twisted oddly.

‘Should I try asking the Southern Island Sect to swap their leading disciple?’

Would the Southern Island Sect refuse?

Smiling at the thought, Yoon Jong gripped his sword and quickly fell in behind Baek Cheon.

“Sasuk, their numbers are increasing.”

“They’ll get more.”

Baek Cheon coolly surveyed the enemies rushing in.

‘They’re still riffraff.’

Honestly, they’re not much of a threat. Small and medium Evil Sect groups called up aren’t worth his intervention. They’re not even a match for the Southern Island Sect anymore.

The important thing isn’t how easy the enemy is to handle. It’s that the enemy has begun to identify the locations of the Heavenly Friends Alliance and the Southern Island Sect.

From now on they’ll have to cross dangerous roads again.

‘But that also means we’re almost at the Yangtze.’

Once they get past this crisis, there’s the Northern lands. A peaceful land where you can lie with your feet stretched out and no one attacks.

A hell worse than anything they’ve faced so far will await ahead, but there’s no way to reach the Northern lands without breaking through it.

“Green Forest King!”

“Yes, Acting Sect Leader.”

“How far to Nanjing?”

“If we run at full power…”

Im So-Byeong glanced up at the sky. After checking the dark sky for a moment, he answered in a calm voice.

“At most, a day.”

“A day…”

Baek Cheon’s expression darkened slightly.

‘Then our fate will be decided within a day.’

With the Yangtze ahead, there’s no option to wait for a chance to cross. The moment they reach the river they’ll have to decide: cross it, or die fighting.

‘And the latter isn’t an option.’

They must make it so.

“Let’s just deal with the men over there, then change direction!”

“Is that necessary? We’re only about a day’s distance away now.”

“It’s not the front but the rear that’s the problem! There are men chasing us.”

“Ah…”

Jo Gul quickly nodded. The ones they truly should be wary of weren’t the clumsy Evil Sect men blocking the way, but Ho Ga-Myung, who was chasing them with furious intent.

“They know we’re heading for the Yangtze, but they don’t know we’re going to Nanjing. There’s no need to give them precise direction.”

“Understood!”

“You scoundrel!”

“Yes, Acting Sect Leader!”

“Please relay this to the Sect Leader as well! And be sure to leave clear markings so our directions don’t diverge!”

“Don’t worry. I’ll check personally.”

Baek Cheon nodded.

Thanks to the Southern Island Sect’s Sect Leader Geum Yang-Baek leading the elders to move separately, their breathing room widened considerably. Defense is inherently easier when the place to stand and block is narrow. Simply dividing the group brought enormous advantages.

‘He’s an extraordinary person.’

Geum Yang-Baek doesn’t show the kind of strong leadership other sect leaders might, but he has a firm trust from others that they lack.

To entrust the command of one’s disciples to others in the midst of the enemy and personally lead a detachment is never an easy choice.

But Geum Yang-Baek volunteered for that role—to save even one more disciple and send them to the Northern lands.

“I won’t lose either.”

“Uh?”

Instead of answering, Baek Cheon grabbed his sword and stared straight ahead.

“We’re going! Follow me prepared to die!”

“Yes!”

Leading Heavenly Friends Alliance’s elite, they charged forward.

The will to definitely survive, the hope of success, the heat to overcome—it all mixed and flared.

But amid that high-spirited atmosphere, one person’s gaze remained cold and composed.

‘A day…’

Chung Myung, who had been watching everything from the very rear, glanced back.

A day. Short if it’s short, but unbearably long if it’s long, especially when spent inside enemy territory.

Chung Myung’s eyes darkened, knowing that harshness better than anyone.

Chapter 1379: The Preparations Are Never Complete. (4)

A riverbank shrouded in darkness.

A few men in tattered, shabby clothes were stamping their feet anxiously.

“Have they missed each other?”

“That can’t be! This is information from the main force!”

“No, no, didn’t Shaolin leave a long time ago? Even if they’re fast, they wouldn’t be this far apart…”

“There must be some reason.”

“Isn’t this about rescuing Mount Hua’s Sword Saint? What could be more important to the leader of the Heavenly Friends Alliance than that? Even if Mount Hua were on fire and its passages burned, they’d ignore it and go straight to save Mount Hua’s Sword Saint, so why are they being so leisurely…”

“All right, just wait a little longer!”

“Ugh.”

As the beggars craned their necks, staring upriver, it happened.

“Th-there they come, the Heavenly Friends Alliance!”

“Damn it, why are you so late!”

Spotting a group running in the distance, the beggars dashed toward them.

“Where are they?”

“Not up front… there, over there! Branch Leader!”

The moment they spotted Hong Dae-Kwang mixed in the group, the beggars shouted loudly.

“Hwa-Um Branch Leader! Here! Over here!”

At the ruckus, Hong Dae-Kwang looked around.

“What is it!”

A swarm of beggars was wildly waving their hands from the riverbank. What they shouted at Hong Dae-Kwang all at once was this:

“Branch Leader, report…!”

“I don’t have time to stop and listen — you come here, you bastards!”

But Hong Dae-Kwang cut him off irritably before he could finish. The beggars flinched and ran toward him.

“Branch Leader! Reporting the situation!”

“I know, so hurry up and speak!”

“…H-here? Here…”

The beggars showed reluctance and glanced around. The place was full of members of the Heavenly Friends Alliance who had come with Hong Dae-Kwang. If they didn’t move, they’d have no choice but to give their information where everyone could hear.

Hong Dae-Kwang was no fool; instead of understanding, he became angrier.

“What the hell is wrong with you beggar bastards being so lax! You can’t even beg properly and you’re going to starve to death! Aren’t you going to speak quickly?”

“T-the listeners…”

“If you’re not going to tell me, get lost! Don’t bother busy people.”

“Ugh… I’ll tell you!”

Reluctantly, the beggars opened their mouths as if in pain.

It would normally be impossible for the Branch Leader of a place as small as Hwa-Um village to talk back so loudly to members of the main headquarters.

But Hong Dae-Kwang is no ordinary Branch Leader. He is a key figure mentioned as a candidate for the next leader of the Beggars’ Union, and he’s the only beggar who can talk with Mount Hua, the sect currently attracting the most attention in the world.

So even those from the main headquarters had to give ground… or rather, several steps of deference.

“First, this is an order from the Leader of the Beggars’ Union: do not act together with the Heavenly Friends Alliance…”

The beggar, eyes darting uncertainly, squeezed his eyes shut and spoke.

“That… said to move to the Nine Sect side…”

He tried hard to speak so others wouldn’t hear, but that effort was completely useless.

“What? Nine Sect? They’re telling me to stick with the Nine Sect?”

“B-Branch Leader, y-your voice a bit…”

“Is the old man really senile? Sending people to spout nonsense? Enough, next!”

“It’s an order from the Leader…”

“Enough, next!”

A small sigh escaped the mouth of the reporting beggar.

Hong Dae-Kwang was never an ordinary Beggars’ Union member, but ever since he allied with Mount Hua’s Sword Saint, he seemed utterly reckless.

“…Hmph. Currently, the Pirates are heading toward Nanjing…”

“Hey, you beggar bastard!”

Hong Dae-Kwang suddenly reached out and struck the reporter hard on the back of the head.

Thunk!

“Aaah! Why—why are you hitting me…”

“Is the Yangtze split into some other branches? Is there another direction? Do those ships have another route to take?”

“…”

“Don’t waste words — tell me the important info! Before I kick you clean away.”

“Yes! Um… intelligence reports say that remnants of Black Ghost Fort, who had been stationed east of Southern Lands, are also moving toward Nanjing!”

“…Black Ghost Fort?”

“Yes.”

For a moment, Hong Dae-Kwang’s face contorted.

Once counted among the Five Terrors of the Divine Prefecture, Black Ghost Fort is now a faithful retainer of Jang Ilso.

‘Black Ghost Fort with the Pirates, and Jang Ilso personally leading the Ten Thousand People Clan to Nanjing?’

Moreover, the Ten Thousand People Clan that had headed to the Southern Island is likely moving north as well, meaning at least sixty percent of the Four Sects Alliance’s total forces could be converging at Nanjing.

By sheer numbers, this far surpasses those gathered at the Black Dragon Fort during the Yangtze Incident. In the past hundred years, there’s never been such a concentration of forces.

“That madman… is he really going to try this?”

If this happens, it won’t just be a question of whether Mount Hua’s Sword Saint can be saved.

The Justice faction, based in the north, and the Four Sects Alliance, based in the south, could come into direct conflict with the vast city of Nanjing between them.

War.

Those two words flashed through Hong Dae-Kwang’s mind.

Two powers clashing centered on the great city of Nanjing?

‘It will never end with a brief skirmish!’

Igniting a fire is the hardest part. Once lit, it spreads easily. During the Yangtze Incident, the Southern Lands Non-Invasion Pact acted like water poured on the flames, but this time no sect will accept that humiliation.

In the end, it will come down to fighting until one side is reduced to ashes.

‘Could it… could it really escalate this far?’

What on earth started this.

Was it the Gu-gang ships being attacked? Or did Jang Ilso move directly?

‘No… it was before that.’

In the end, all of this was clearly triggered by Mount Hua’s Sword Saint’s move to Southern Lands. If he hadn’t headed to the Southern Island, even if the Southern Island burned, it would have taken much longer for a war of this scale to break out.

There’s no law against a small thing snowballing into something huge, but this far exceeds that level.

“What about us? What are the main headquarters’ beggars doing?”

“The main headquarters says they’ve dispatched reinforcements to Nanjing!”

“Wait, hold on. There are beggars already at the Yangtze, aren’t there?”

“That’s not enough — they’re even sending the Red Band and the Blue Band as reinforcements.”

“…Damn, insane…”

Hong Dae-Kwang’s face went even paler.

The information held by the Beggars’ Union main troops is far greater than any single Branch Leader could possess. The Beggars’ Union uses that to understand the world.

If the main troops are sending help, it means they judge the probability of a clash between the Four Sects Alliance and the Nine Sect at Nanjing to be very high.

“Messages have flown out to all the other Nine Sect as well. Everyone’s being summoned. At the same time, smaller sects around Nanjing are being pulled in.”

“Damn it, do you think they’ll actually come because of that!”

If they were the sort to arrive just from receiving a single notice, they wouldn’t have to struggle so hard to gather forces.

Those who are distant and have always been unable to intervene in the heart of the martial world will probably be watching this situation with derision, daring the so-called center to try and stop it.

“So? Will they send support?”

“The notices probably haven’t even arrived yet. At least a day or more…”

“Hey, you damn… then even if they come it’ll be after everything’s dead! What’s the use! They should have sent them earlier!”

“We… we had no choice! The Hegemony Lord moved so suddenly…”

“Isn’t preparing for that the main headquarters’ job? What are you beggar bastards doing eating handouts?!”

At Hong Dae-Kwang’s fierce scolding, one beggar twisted his face and tried to explain.

“Well… we do have something to say, but there was nothing we could do.”

“Save your moralizing for a school! Why are you talking about morals here?!”

“No, that’s not it. Normally in situations like this, Shaolin acts as the center while Wudang and Southern Edge Sect buy time until the other sects arrive.”

“…Huh?”

“Wudang and the Southern Edge. Wudang and Southern Edge Sect! No matter how hard we try, how could we fill the void left by those two sects? Do you know the standing those two have within the Nine Sect?”

Hong Dae-Kwang clamped his mouth shut.

No matter what anyone says, Shaolin is the center of the Nine Sect, but it was Wudang and Southern Edge that perfectly supported Shaolin. And of all times, those two sects have both shut their gates now.

“Th-that’s true, but…”

Hong Dae-Kwang trailed off awkwardly.

Normally he would have cursed all day about these bastards needlessly sealing their gates and causing this situation, but Hong Dae-Kwang couldn’t say that now.
