Because it’s undeniable that Mount Hua had a tremendous influence on those two sects’ decision to seal their gates.

‘Damn it! Things are getting screwed up!’

Hong Dae-Kwang raised his voice in frustration.

“No, that doesn’t mean we should just sit and do nothing! Send people to tell them to open their gates, at least! Are those madmen just going to lock their doors and watch everyone else die?”

“It seems the main headquarters has already sent people.”

“And?”

“They couldn’t even get inside to deliver the message. They were driven out as useless intruders.”

“…This is maddening, really.”

Even sealing gates should consider the situation! What are they thinking, insisting on this now? Those damned Nine Sect ruin things out of pride. Because of that pathetic pride.

“That is the report. For the reasons I mentioned, the Master ordered the Branch Leader to join the Nine Sect…”

“I’ll handle that myself! Enough!”

Hong Dae-Kwang ground his teeth.

Even if the Leader of the Beggars personally led elites to join the Nine Sect, he had no intention of serving under them. In fact, his allegiance was to the Heavenly Friends Alliance, not the Nine Great Sects.

“You’ve heard it all, but the situation…”

“But Black Ghost Fort is coming to Nanjing from the east right now?”

But at that moment, Tang Gunak, who had been listening quietly, suddenly asked.

“Th-that’s correct.”

The beggar glanced at Hong Dae-Kwang and nodded.

“Half of the Ten Thousand People Clan led by Jang Ilso are heading to Nanjing from the west.”

“Say something obvious…”

“The other half of the Ten Thousand People Clan are moving north.”

Hong Dae-Kwang’s face slowly hardened.

“And the Yangtze is being controlled by the Pirates, effectively blocking the river?”

He understood what would come next.

Tang Gunak murmured in a bleak voice.

“Our boys are heading to that Nanjing. Into the center where the three sects of the Four Sects Alliance are gathering from all directions.”

They cannot survive.

Hong Dae-Kwang’s face went pale.

So far, Mount Hua’s Sword Saint has made a reality of countless things everyone declared impossible. Common sense does not apply to him.

But no matter how extraordinary Mount Hua’s Sword Saint is… he could never survive heading to that Nanjing.

“No, this can’t be…”

Hong Dae-Kwang’s gaze hastily turned beyond the river.

The world beyond the river was simply darkened and stood in silent stillness.

Chapter 1380: The Preparations Are Never Complete. (5)

Rustle-rustle.

The hawk perched on Ho Ga-Myung’s wrist tilted slightly.

Having flown a long way only to land on the shaking arm of someone running fast clearly didn’t please it; the hawk pecked at Ho Ga-Myung’s forearm a few times.

When Ho Ga-Myung offered the prepared raw meat, the hawk finally began to tear at it calmly.

Ho Ga-Myung lightly stroked its head with his index finger, opened the small tube tied to the hawk’s leg, and took out a letter.

His gaze dropped immediately.

Squinting at the letter as if thinking, he nodded almost imperceptibly.

He then took a small pen from inside his sleeve.

Softly.

He wrote something on a small piece of paper, held it up to dry with two shakes in the air, then quickly rolled it and slid it into the small tube on the hawk’s leg.

Click.

After sealing the lid tightly so it wouldn’t open, he smoothed the hawk’s back and lifted his arm.

The hawk, reluctant to drop the meat it was still eating and hesitating a few times, soon soared high and disappeared into the heavy darkness.

“Attention.”

“Yes!”

The aides trailing close behind brightened at his voice.

“Change the formation.”

“Yes?”

“Split the forces left and right around the central vanguard. Each unit’s formation will be extended horizontally.”

The aides exchanged puzzled glances at the unexpected order.

“Question.”

“Um… Commander. If we deploy widely across the front, our pursuit speed will slow down.”

“It doesn’t matter. They won’t be able to move quickly either.”

There was sense to that. What they’ve crossed so far has been basically unclaimed even from the Four Sects Alliance’s perspective, but from now on they are entering the Yangtze river region where forces have been concentrated.

Naturally they will collide with the Evil Sect agents spread out there, and their advance will inevitably slow.

“But wouldn’t it be better to increase our speed instead?”

At that, Ho Ga-Myung glanced back. The aide who received that indifferent look flinched and bowed his head.

“You don’t seem to understand.”

“I lack understanding…”

“Changing formation means the strategy has changed, and a change in strategy means the objective has changed.”

“…Then?”

“Once the drive is over, you spread the net.”

A chilling aura that he hadn’t shown the aides flickered in Ho Ga-Myung’s eyes as he looked ahead again.

“Now we encircle and annihilate them.”

“Yes, sir!”

A voice full of resolve rang out. The corner of Ho Ga-Myung’s eye twitched briefly.

From the moment they failed to annihilate them in the gorge, his command authority vanished. All that remained was to carry out Jang Ilso’s orders meticulously.

The order he had received from Jang Ilso was very simple.

“Attack their rear.”

But if you decide that a bit, the meaning shifts.

“Closely pursue their rear to attack and apply pressure.”

And with Ho Ga-Myung’s interpretation added, the meaning shifts slightly more.

“Keep pressing their rear so they can’t think of anything else, and force them… to the Yangtze river region.”

If anyone else heard that order they’d surely think it mad. Going to the Yangtze river is exactly what the Mount Hua scoundrels fleeing the Southern Land would desperately want, isn’t it?

Can urging someone to do what they want be called “pressure”? Can forcing them toward what they desire be called “pursuit”?

This isn’t even warcraft. The essence of strategy is to prevent the enemy from achieving what they want.

But Ho Ga-Myung didn’t think that way. More than that, those who mocked the order as nonsense would quietly lower their fingers once they learned the one who gave it is the Hegemony Lord.

Ho Ga-Myung knew.

The core is “pressure.” Or more precisely, perhaps “control.”

By tailing them closely and continuously instilling psychological pressure, the Four Sects Alliance can gain one sure thing.

Namely, preventing them from suddenly doing something insane like they have before.

“Absurd, isn’t it.”

If he judged from what he’d observed of Mount Hua’s Sword Saint so far, they might suddenly veer west toward Yunnan while heading to the Yangtze, or instead turn east, take a ship from the East Sea, and sail north to Zhejiang.

No, that fellow might even calmly reverse direction, break through the pursuing Ten Thousand People Clan, and return to the Southern lands.

Does that sound absurd? How many times has he actually done anything sensible so far?

Think about it the other way: it would be stranger if he didn’t do such things.

The Yangtze river is full of enemies. So why would Mount Hua’s Sword Saint purposely go into the enemy-clotted Yangtze?

Because even the Four Sects Alliance couldn’t block the entire long Yangtze?

No. Mount Hua’s Sword Saint would rather say: it’s dozens of times harder to blockade the sea than block the Yangtze, so why should we go the direction convenient for the enemies?

For ordinary people the idea of circling around the long Yangtze is impossible, but to Mount Hua’s Sword Saint it’s nothing.

He’s the kind of man who would choose the straight-line path from the Yangtze to the Southern Island, moving through a death-filled gauntlet to reach the Southern Island in the shortest time.

If his only goal were to seek the “safest escape route,” then skirting around the Yangtze would be the correct choice.

Given all this, the order Jang Ilso issued now and the intent behind his movements are unmistakably clear.

Namely…

In that sense, the order Jang Ilso gave and the motive behind Jang Ilso’s movement are extremely clear. Namely…

❀ ❀ ❀

“It’s about reducing the number of options.”

Inside a large carriage, Jeok Ho, sitting opposite Jang Ilso across a small low table, looked puzzled.

“…You say ‘number of options’?”

“Yes.”

Jang Ilso nodded calmly. It wasn’t a slow nod, but watching it gave the impression he always lived alongside ennui.

“What does warfare doctrine call it?”

“…I…”

Jeok Ho hesitated slightly and bit his lip.

He wasn’t ignorant of strategy either; to lead men you must know it whether you want to or not. That Jang Ilso favored followers with force but not merely forceful ones was one reason.

“I dare not discuss strategy before the Alliance lord.”

“Ha ha ha ha!”

Jang Ilso burst into laughter as if amused.

Jeok Ho knew this laugh didn’t come from satisfaction with the answer.

Generally one may disdain flattery, but no one truly hates being praised—people like to be elevated.

But that rule didn’t apply to Jang Ilso.

Being at the very top was as natural to him as breathing, and his greatness was obvious; thus he wasn’t pleased—being happy about praise for breathing would be odd.

“I find it amusing whenever I hear things like that.”

“…If I make a mistake…”

“I don’t know much about strategy. I might have learned it when needed, but I rarely needed it.”

Jeok Ho held his breath, but his eyes asked how he could so easily toy with and suppress those versed in strategy.

“After all, strategy is used by people. So as long as you understand people, it’s easy. Remember: if you want to beat the enemy, remember just two things.”

“May I ask what those are?”

“One is obvious: do only what the enemy hates.”

Jeok Ho nodded at that.

This matched Jang Ilso’s modus operandi. The intermediate steps were hard to grasp, but in the end he repeatedly did what bothered his opponents most.

“Do you know the other?”

“…I don’t.”

“Hmm. Actually this is more central, but people usually don’t understand it even when told.”

A flicker of hope crossed Jeok Ho’s face—perhaps this answer would reveal the secret behind Jang Ilso’s strategies.

But Jeok Ho’s hope was crushed miserably.

“People.”

“Yes, Alliance lord.”

“They don’t even truly know themselves.”

“…Pardon?”

Confusion appeared on Jeok Ho’s face—what was this sudden remark?

“Tsk tsk. You’re the same.”

“…It’s just that it’s so simple I can’t quite grasp it…”

“Hmm. How should I explain it more simply… Right. This might be easier. People always lie to themselves as well.”

Jang Ilso paused briefly, smiled faintly, and asked.

“It’s common. If there were the most incorruptible scholar in the world, what would you think of him?”

“I’d think… he’s remarkable.”

“Why?”

“Isn’t incorruptibility itself worthy of respect?”

Jang Ilso smiled softly.

“I see. But… do you think that scholar truly desires incorruptibility and the path of Confucian study?”

“Excuse me?”

“Saying he’s the most incorruptible implies his incorruptibility is pathological. What such a person truly desires isn’t incorruptibility per se, but an incorruptible self-image.”

“…”

“People are all the same. When someone desperately wants something, they achieve great things. So what would that incorruptible person most desperately want?”

“Well…”

Jeok Ho’s face hardened.

“Honor…”

“Exactly. Such a person craves honor excessively. But he won’t admit it—he’ll believe he’s merely upholding his principles, deceiving even himself.”

A sigh escaped Jeok Ho. Even with the answer it’s hard—doesn’t this mean one must fully fathom what’s hidden in a person’s heart?

“If that’s so, does it mean the Mount Hua’s Sword Saint leading the Southern Island Sect is hiding something in that association?”

“That’s obvious. He’s a mad child. But what you should pay attention to here is something else.”

The corner of Jang Ilso’s mouth curved up in a chilling way.

“Have I ever said that the child and I are similar?”

“…You’ve said it a few times.”

Jeok Ho was also puzzled—why would Jang Ilso, of all people, give such an assessment to such a trivial person?

“I mean it literally. The child and I are identical in one particular aspect.”

“Do you mean brilliance?”

“Brilliance? Ha ha ha ha!”

Jang Ilso laughed as if incredulous.

“No, no. It’s not that. The similarity between the child and me is…”

His eyes traced a captivating, yet chilling arc.

“…trusting no one. Not a single grain of trust in anyone in this world.”

Chapter 1381: Of Course, That Would Never Happen. (1)

Jeok Ho’s face hardened strangely.

‘Trust…’

He knew. Jang Ilso could not be wrong. Yet he could not immediately nod in agreement because the Mount Hua’s Sword Saint that he imagined did not match at all with the Mount Hua’s Sword Saint that Jang Ilso described.

‘What kind of person was the Mount Hua’s Sword Saint?’

In truth, he did not know the Mount Hua’s Sword Saint well. He had never met him, nor spoken with him. But from his deeds and the rumors, it wasn’t hard to infer what kind of man he was.

‘An absolute prodigy of martial arts.’

He dueled the Hegemony Lord and survived, and even joined forces with him to defeat the high priest of the Demonic Cult.

No matter how one underestimated him, it would be hard to deny that he was one of the world’s top masters, especially at such a young age.

‘Besides, he’s the center of the Heavenly Friends Alliance.’

Though barely beyond twenty, he stood as one pillar among giants contesting the world.

But the true strength of the Mount Hua’s Sword Saint was not in his force or his faction.

‘The most important thing is, of course, righteousness.’

Wherever the name Mount Hua’s Sword Saint rang out, those words always followed. It was the strongest power that made even his martial arts skills and the faction he’d built seem trivial.

That’s why Jang Ilso’s words were hard to understand.

The Mount Hua’s Sword Saint, regarded as the very embodiment of the code, doesn’t trust people…?

“Is that hard to believe?”

Jeok Ho shook his head.

“It’s not that it’s unbelievable, but I can’t readily understand it. The righteousness he espouses would be hard to enact without trust.”

“Hmm.”

Jang Ilso snorted softly. His eyes curved wide.

“No. That’s not it. You’ve understood it completely backwards.”

“…What do you mean by that?”

“Didn’t I say? The most impeccably upright scholar might also be the most greedy.”

Jeok Ho nodded briefly.

“It’s no different in the case of the Mount Hua’s Sword Saint.”

Jang Ilso turned his head slightly to look out the window and asked.

“What does it mean to help in the first place?”

“…What do you mean by that…?”

“Think about it. What does it mean to help? Have you ever seen a poor person help a rich person?”

Embarrassment clouded Jeok Ho’s eyes. It was absurd. Why would the poor help the wealthy?

“I’ve never seen such a thing. But isn’t that obvious? Those who have more help those who have less…”

Jeok Ho fell silent. Only after speaking did he understand what Jang Ilso was getting at.

“Do you understand now?”

Jang Ilso’s gaze shone like the jewels draped over his body.

“Helping is inherently an act bestowed upon those weaker than oneself. Of course anyone can do it. Anyone. But…”

His low, soft voice sounded chilling.

“No one tries to help the whole world. No one tries to help everything within their reach. And they don’t consider that to be natural.”

“…”

“What does that mean? It means he considers everyone weaker than himself. He may not even know it himself.”

Jeok Ho’s expression turned strange.

“But, Alliance lord. He’s not the only one to think that way, is he?”

“True. But he puts that thought into reality. He does what others only wish for. That means he evaluates himself as extraordinary.”

Jang Ilso broke off and laughed shortly as if amused. The red curl of his mouth looked particularly chilling.

“Of course, he probably doesn’t know this himself either.”

Perhaps Jang Ilso was right.

Indeed, the Mount Hua’s Sword Saint was odd. ‘Great’ alone could not explain him. His achievements were tremendous, but the acts he committed to achieve them were far from normal.

“Trust comes from recognizing others. But that fellow never recognizes others. The extent he recognizes others is only when they fall within his reach. That’s it.”

“That is…”

“Yes.”

Jang Ilso nodded slowly.

“It’s like an adult looking at babies. They can praise a child who walks better than their peers, or marvel if a toddler can recite characters. But…”

A mocking tone flickered through Jang Ilso’s words.

“Can you call such feelings ‘trust’?”

Adults do not trust their children. That’s a separate question from whether they feel affection for them.

An adult may value a child’s potential and acknowledge their greatness, but ultimately cannot look past their immaturity.

Then he must become a keeper of the code.

If the world were filled with three-year-olds, even Jeok Ho, who cannot be called a paragon of virtue, perhaps even a villain, would become a keeper of the code. He could not leave the children within his reach alone.

Right now, Jang Ilso was saying that Chung Myung’s almost-fanatical adherence to the code ultimately stemmed from distrust of people, more precisely, distrust of immaturity.

This was completely contrary to public opinion. Would the world agree with this assessment?

No, what would the Mount Hua’s Sword Saint’s reaction be if he heard this?

It certainly wouldn’t be pleasant, to say the least.

“I understand what the Alliance lord means…”

Jeok Ho, feeling slightly bewildered, looked up at Jang Ilso.

“But how does that relate to what you said earlier…?”

“Tsk tsk. After saying this much, shouldn’t you understand?”

“…My apologies.”

Jang Ilso drained the bottle and wet his mouth. After wiping the alcohol from his lip with his thumb, he answered.

“Didn’t I say people lie to themselves? The core is that the child Chung Myung firmly believes he trusts everyone.”

“…”

“Then it’s simple. All I must do is gauge what judgment his unknown true intent will make. Do you understand what I mean?”

“It’s difficult…”

Jang Ilso stared at Jeok Ho quietly. His pale eyes, devoid of any emotional shred, seemed to press down on him.

“What would you do if a child were by the water?”

“Well… I’d watch first.”

“If that child fell into the water?”

“…Depending on the circumstance, I might run to save them.”

“Even if that child had nothing to do with you?”

Jeok Ho frowned slightly.

“Of course… I wouldn’t call myself a good man, but isn’t that human nature? Saving one child hardly takes much effort anyway.”

“Yes. Right, in some cases.”

As if satisfied with the answer, Jang Ilso nodded.

“Then tell me—what if many children by the water were divided into factions and fighting each other?”

“…”

“What if, in that process, countless children could fall into the water and die? If immature toddlers, barely able to walk, fought foolishly in a dangerous place without understanding the consequences?”

Jeok Ho closed his mouth.

“You… yes, what would you do?”

Of course you’d go. Anyone would answer the same. A person must go.

A firm voice came from Jeok Ho.

“If they’re children… I’d run. Because they’re children, not adults.”

“Yes. Right. You would naturally do that.”

“…”

“Do you understand?”

Jeok Ho nodded.

“It’s not hard to draw that Mount Hua’s Sword Saint in. Just give him a grand affair. Round up inexperienced fools who can’t fend for themselves and set up a big stage, he’ll try to come somehow.”

“…”

“No need for extra effort.”

Of course the Mount Hua’s Sword Saint would surely disagree with Jang Ilso. He would arrive at his conclusions through countless reasons and calculations.

But now Jang Ilso claimed that the conclusion was already set. That it would happen as the result of the Mount Hua’s Sword Saint’s own hidden intent, which even he did not know.

‘Is that even possible?’

Can anyone foresee another’s choice better than they can themselves and plan accordingly? Is that within human capability?

Jang Ilso spoke casually, but the words sent a chill down Jeok Ho’s spine. Hearing this, he finally understood why Jang Ilso considered conventional strategy trivial.

Standardized methods are meaningless before true intent, after all.

“So… you’re saying the Mount Hua’s Sword Saint will come of his own accord?”

“Isn’t he already doing that?”

“…”

“All I have to do is moderately narrow that child’s options. Then his choice becomes obvious. And…”

Jang Ilso let out a crooked laugh.

“That child draws people by his very existence. He may not know it himself… but more will gather because of him, like kindling drawn to an open clearing.”

“…”

“Wouldn’t that be enough?”

Jeok Ho found himself staring dumbfounded.

‘Has the Alliance lord envisioned this situation since leaving the Clan?’

He predicted that such things would occur merely by showing himself? If so, was that conspicuous grand carriage part of the plan?

He was at a loss for words.

Suddenly he felt fear. Strength, persistence, and malice.

There were countless reasons the world feared Jang Ilso, but now Jeok Ho felt he had discovered the full reason to truly fear him.

Who on earth could stand against such a man?

A trembling voice slipped from Jeok Ho’s lips.

“In the end… if everything the Alliance lord says is true, then no one in the world could oppose you. No one could fathom such depths.”

“Well. I don’t know.”

“…Sorry?”

The corner of Jang Ilso’s mouth twisted oddly.

“Evaluating others is easier than you think. But knowing oneself is much harder. Even for me.”

“…”

“If someone, yes, if someone, could discover a side of me that even I don’t know. If someone could understand the true intent hidden within me…”

Jang Ilso slowly stroked his white throat. His gaze flashed.

“That person would be the one to take my head.”

“…”

“Of course, that would never happen. Hahaha.”

Jang Ilso laughed and drank. Jeok Ho trembled as dread seeped into his toes.

Jang Ilso was right.

Such a thing would never happen. No one in the world could understand this man. No one could peer into this profound abyss.

Chapter 1382: Of Course, That Would Never Happen. (2)

“Reporting! The carriage carrying Hegemony Lord Jang Ilso of the Four Sects Alliance is currently approaching Nanjing; at this rate, it is expected to reach the vicinity of Nanjing within six hours!”

“Reporting! Black Ghost Fort forces have passed the Changzhou direction and are moving rapidly toward Nanjing!”

“Estimated arrival time?”

“They are taking intermittent rests and adjusting speed, so it’s hard to predict exact arrival, but judging by the situation, it’s likely between six to ten hours.”

“Reporting! Evil Sect members near Mount Huang are gathering toward Nanjing! We are estimating their numbers now, but they’re coming from all directions, so it’s hard to be precise. It looks to be at least five thousand!”

The more reports poured in from all sides, the more the face of Beggars’ Union Elder The Kind Crow, Neung Sam, stiffened until it could no longer remain anything but rigid.

Each piece of news that came in was terrifying enough to make his heart clench. But the report that truly made him startle came after that.

“E-Elder!”

Bang!

The inner room’s door burst open violently, and a beggar with a pale face dashed in.

“It’s an E-emergency! Emergency!”

“What is it?”

“A report from the Southern Lands! Th-they’ve detected a large convoy of wagons north of Mount Huang!”

“Wagons?”

“Yes! Not confirmed exactly, but judging from the type of cargo, it’s highly likely they are carrying grain supplies!”

“Grain…”

Cold sweat trickled down Neung Sam’s back in an instant.

What would it mean, at this time, to say grain is being transported? What other reason would there be for large quantities of grain to move toward the Yangtze river?

‘Military provisions!’

Unless Jang Ilso had suddenly taken pity and decided to distribute grain to the Yangtze river refugees, the intent is painfully clear.

“Is it, i-is it true?”

Neung Sam’s fingertips trembled uncontrollably. Replenishing military provisions meant that Jang Ilso did not intend to treat this situation as a mere one-off skirmish.

While he briefly lost his words, the other beggars began to babble loudly.

“What reason is there to mobilize provisions? It’s bluster! What use would the Four Sects Alliance’s elites have for grain?”

“Stupid talk! The Four Sects Alliance scoundrels could do without food for days, but aren’t they now pulling in even the minor Evil Sects? If they plan to lead them north, provisions will absolutely be necessary!”

“I don’t think so!”

The members of the Beggars Union, clutching the flood of reports in both hands, raised their voices as if arguing.

Of course, this was what the analysts were supposed to do: to analyze the incessant stream of incoming reports in real time and find the correct response.

But Neung Sam noticed that beneath the heated voices there was fear that they couldn’t fully hide.

“Now, calm down! Bringing provisions doesn’t necessarily mean a river crossing. It could mean planning a long-term standoff centered on the Yangtze river!”

“Nonsense. Why would the Four Sects Alliance, having already secured the front at river basin, choose such an option?”

“Enough!”

In the end, the Kind Crow, Neung Sam, cut off the heated debate.

“First, send word to our main base, and immediately contact the Nine Sects forces currently moving toward Nanjing!”

“Yes!”

“Do not rush to judge what cannot be judged. The top priority is to transmit the information as it is! Analysis comes afterward!”

“Yes!”

At those words, several people hurriedly ran out.

“Elder, Heavenly Friends Alliance! What about the Heavenly Friends Alliance side?”

“…Send it to them as well.”

“Yes!”

As Neung Sam was about to finish his orders, he grabbed the one who was about to run off and shouted again.

“Ah, and check if there is any way to contact the Southern Island, which is moving north, and confirm with the Black Robbed Troops that has entered the Southern land!”

“Elder, that…”

“Confirm it!”

“Yes, understood!”

Watching the beggar run out, the Kind Crow frowned.

‘Was it really this sloppy?’

The Information room was the pride of the Beggars’ Union. They gathered and analyzed all information across the land. There was even a saying that while neighbors might not know what the next-door person ate that morning, the Beggars’ Union did.

The Kind Crow, Neung Sam, had always taken pride in the Information Room’s function. But the naked reality of the office he saw today was far different from what he had believed.

The system of the Information Room, which usually ran with pinpoint accuracy, began to creak and fall out of rhythm from here and there as the situation grew so urgent.

‘The murim world… the murim world has been far too peaceful.’

He had thought they were sufficiently prepared. He believed they could respond to any situation. But seeing this scene, anyone could tell how absurdly arrogant that belief had been.

No matter how sharp a famed blade, if it remains sheathed for decades it will dull and lose its edge. He painfully realized that truth today.

“Is there still no news from the main base?”

“Ah, not yet…”

Neung Sam bit his lip.

‘If at least the Union leader had been of sound mind…’

A young leader capable of responding swiftly to any crisis should have been leading the Beggars’ Union.

Had he foreseen this, he would have passed the Union Leader’s position on before the current Union Leader became so frail.

But now the regret came far too late. Transferring the Union Leader’s seat in this situation would only cause greater chaos.

‘Mount Hua.’

The Kind Crow clenched and unclenched his fist.

When he heard that Hyun Jong, the Mount Hua sect leader, had stepped down and placed his disciple in the sect leader’s seat, the Kind Crow thought it a grave mistake.

When he even heard that the young Mount Hua sword prodigy Baek Cheon had been seated as Acting Sect Leader, he thought Hyun Jong had lost his wits.

He was not alone; most people thought so.

It was natural. There might be countless people who disparaged Hyun Jong, but who would deny that he had resurrected the fallen Mount Hua as its sect leader?

Of course, it’s possible Hyun Jong didn’t accomplish all of it alone; perhaps he merely observed.

But for that very reason he shouldn’t have stepped down. There’s no need to change a system that’s running well. When things go smoothly, maintaining the status quo to keep the flow is more important.

But now he understood. Only after experiencing this situation did he get why Hyun Jong stepped down, why he handed the post to a younger man.

“The other sects! Has there been a reply to the Nine Sect’s request for support?”

“No reply yet!”

“Still?”

“Yes. Branch Leaders are currently consulting with the sects in their regions and waiting on their positions, but no answers have returned yet!”

“Why on earth!”

Unable to bear it, the Kind Crow burst out angrily.

“The news has been sent a long time ago — why is there still no reply! Do they think this is a fire across the river to watch idly, damn it!”

As he became furious, the beggar who had answered hastily said in a rush.

“I-I’ll send another communication!”

“You damn fools!”

Neung Sam struck the floor with the bamboo staff he held.

“What on earth is everyone thinking, what on earth!”

His shout echoed miserably outside the hall.

Qingcheng Mountain.

“They say the Four Sects Alliance are nearly at Nanjing now.”

“Hmm.”

The expression of Byeok Hyun-Ja, the Sect Leader of the Qingcheng Sect, grew grave upon receiving the elder’s report.

“Sect leader. Beggars have camped before the gate. Isn’t it time to give them an answer?”

Byeok Hyun-Ja nodded leisurely.

“We will. Tell them Qingcheng sect, once ready, will answer Shaolin’s call and march to the Yangtze river.”

“Yes, I will. Should I prepare the disciples then?”

“Yes. Have them prepared. There are watching eyes about.”

Qingcheng elder Byeok Sang’s expression shifted at those words.

“Do you not intend to march out right now?”

“Of course we must. Of course we’ll go. Qingcheng sect is one of the Nine Great Sects and leads the righteous path of the murim world — how could we not go?”

“…But if you do?”

“But there’s no need to rush, is there?”

Byeok Sang said with a stern face.

“Sect leader. But the enemy’s movements are unusual.”

“I know. It likely won’t end without bloodshed. That Hegemony Lord won’t return empty-handed.”

“But how could they…”

As Byeok Sang trailed off with a worried look, a peculiar smile curled at the corner of Byeok Hyun-Ja’s mouth.

“You are misunderstanding me. I never said we would not march. I only said we would go after thorough preparation.”

“….”

“Charging in half-heartedly will likely be of little help, so let’s be fully prepared.”

“…Yes, Sect leader.”

Byeok Sang bowed his head silently for now. He still did not look convinced, though.

Seeing him, Byeok Hyun-Ja smiled oddly.

“I’m simply curious.”

“…Yes?”

“In the long history of the martial world, Qingcheng Sect has endured many enemy attacks. At times demonic forces have run rampant, at times Evil Sects have proliferated, and at times we’ve fought lawless men from beyond the sea.”

“Isn’t that our pride at Qingcheng sect?”

“But… well. Seeing the situation like this, a thought suddenly occurred to me.”

“What kind…”

“Tell me: isn’t Nanjing closer than one might think?”

Byeok Sang fell silent.

“Henan is the same. If they chose, they could up and leave to rush support anytime. If warriors ran at full strength, it would take only a few days, wouldn’t it?”

“That may be so, but…”

“Yet… looking back, our ancestors always fought without proper support from Henan, relying only on their own strength.”

Byeok Sang’s face hardened, understanding where Byeok Hyun-Ja was going with his words.

“So I’m curious. When we bled, were those calling for help truly so distant that they couldn’t arrive in time?”

“Sect leader…”

“Isn’t it laughable?”

Byeok Hyun-Ja gazed out the window.

“Seated in the safest places in the realm, watching the distant flames, they must always have felt this way.”

Though he called it laughable, his gaze was solemn.

“So let’s wait a bit. I must see with my own eyes what kind of hearts watched Qingcheng Sect’s Mountain burn. Do not worry. Do you think I want Shaolin to fall? I will go, not too late. Not too late…”

But never, in a hurry.

Byeok Sang, who knew the meaning hidden behind the veiled words, bowed his head without comment.

“What do you think? Do you think I’m being emotional right now?”

After a moment, Byeok Sang slowly lifted his head at Byeok Hyun-Ja’s question. Meeting his gaze, he replied calmly.

“Harboring emotions over past events would not be proper.”

Byeok Hyun-Ja’s brow twitched slightly. A faint smile blossomed at the corner of Byeok Sang’s mouth.

“But patience is a warrior’s virtue. If enduring some bitterness yields greater gain, ought we not to encourage it?”

“Ha… hahahahaha!”

Byeok Hyun-Ja, staring blankly at Byeok Sang as if incredulous, burst out laughing.

Those words were exactly the answer he had been waiting for.

“Tell the disciples to prepare. They must know that.”

“Yes, Sect leader.”

Watching Byeok Sang leave, Byeok Hyun-Ja gave a wry smile.

“…Beop Jeong. Really, why did you pretend to be so grand?”

Byeok Hyun-Ja wondered what expression Beop Jeong would be wearing at this moment.

But more than that, he was curious about the look Beop Jeong, now weakened by the war with the Four Sects Alliance, would have when facing him.

Would he still be able to wear that same arrogant expression as before?

“…Don’t die, Abbott. I want to see you just as you are again. Haha.”

The laugh he let out spread slowly and chillingly throughout the main hall.

Chapter 1383: Of Course, That Would Never Happen. (3)

“Argh! Damn it! Why are there so many of them?”

Jo Gul sliced through the chest of a charging enemy and snapped irritably.

“Shut your mouth, you damned bastard!”

“No! This is just too much, isn’t it?”

Jo Gul contorted his face and complained.

Break through Nanjing in one go without meeting the enemy until the right position?

‘We haven’t even reached that so-called “right position” yet!’

It felt as if every Evil Sect under heaven had gathered here. Cross one grove and Evil Sect members swarmed; cross one stream and they swarmed again. The problem was….

“W-what is this!”

“Enemies!”

“No! Why have you all been surprised from the start?!”

Jo Gul shouted angrily.

What was maddening was that it should be the Heavenly Friends Alliance and the Southern Island Sect who were startled by meeting the Evil Sect, yet those bastards were even more shocked, like they’d been suddenly attacked by the Demonic Cult in their own neighborhood.

“I’m really going to die…”

Paaaah!

Jo Gul was about to keep whining when suddenly someone leapt over his shoulder and darted forward.

Pararararak!

With a sound like dragonflies taking flight, the sword spread its wings in all directions.

“Aah!”

“Aaaaah!”

Evil Sect members, cut in an instant, clutched their wounds and writhed about.

“Sago!”

“…Fall back. If you’re going to keep whining.”

“Ah, no, that’s not it…”

Jo Gul, with a sulky face, began to defend himself but then shut up.

He too was seriously wounded, but Yu Yiseol’s injuries were worse. For her to fight without complaint while he kept whining made him look utterly pathetic….

“Jo Gul, you’re already pathetic.”

“Don’t read my mind! Have you mastered telepathy or something?”

Baek Cheon, who had spoken casually, followed behind the advancing Yu Yiseol.

“Samae! Are you okay?”

“Yes, sahyung.”

Yu Yiseol nodded calmly.

“I can fight now.”

“Don’t overdo it. Your wounds are severe.”

“Yes.”

Though told not to overexert herself, Yu Yiseol’s swordplay was no different from usual—still lively, clean, and swift.

And watching that sword, Baek Cheon felt something inside him melt away—relief.

He had been worried inside. He feared that Yu Yiseol’s deep wounds might make her lose her sword. Even leaving aside whether she could be at full strength right away, her sword was a treasure Mount Hua could never afford to lose.

But looking at that sword, he could see his worries had been needless.

‘Still, no matter how I think about it, it’s strange.’

Baek Cheon’s gaze turned to the Evil Sect members Yu Yiseol was cutting down.

“Rotate the vanguard and rearguard. Those who are tired, replenish your strength!”

“Yes!”

Gwak Hwan-So’s voice rang out. Not only Yu Yiseol but the Southern Island Sect were pressing the enemies relentlessly—so much so that they even had the luxury to show mercy by only wounding foes rather than killing them.

So strong that the enemy couldn’t match them? Because they’d grown that much?

‘No. It’s that the other side is too weak.’

The Evil Sect force they were encountering now was far below the level of those who had hampered them in Guangdong.

That’s why it was strange.

Whether Ho Ga-Myung or Jang Ilso had deployed the forces, they wouldn’t have sent such lackluster men if their aim was to capture them. Those two couldn’t be ignorant that such people would be useless.

Even a rotten fish is still a fish—would those small fry be a match for the core of the Heavenly Friends Alliance, bolstered around the Southern Island sect?

But if so, why? Jang Ilso or Ho Ga-Myung wouldn’t engage in such pointless attrition.

“Acting Sect Leader.”

At that moment, Namgung Do-Wei, who had run up beside him, started to speak but then looked at Baek Cheon’s face.

“…You already noticed, it seems.”

“Yes.”

“These people did not come to face us. Leaving their strength aside, didn’t it seem like they didn’t even know we existed?”

“Indeed.”

Baek Cheon, who had been about to turn toward where Chung Myung and the Green Forest King stood, flinched and kept his head fixed.

‘Don’t rely on them!’

Having allies with deep insight is a blessing. But if you entrust all analysis and judgment to them, that blessing becomes a shackle.

If he himself lacks the ability to think and judge, it’s meaningless. There’s no guarantee those people will be by his side every time he must make a crucial decision.

‘Where could this Evil Sect force be useful?’

They certainly wouldn’t be effective against the core of the Nine Great sects. Then?

“They’re gathering these not to face us but to face others.”

“I think the same.”

“If that’s the case…”

A chill rose along the back of Baek Cheon’s hand.

Even troops unable to face the core of the Nine Great Sects could readily handle medium and small Justice faction sects. Just because they seemed riffraff to their eyes didn’t mean they truly were.

The problem was, as long as this Evil Sect force was in Southern lands, they would never be tasked with confronting the medium and small sects in Northern lands.

“Could it be… and I know it sounds absurd, but did Shaolin somehow rally forces in the Northern lands in the meantime?”

“…Could that be possible?”

“It could be.”

Namgung Do-Wei, who had spoken up, laughed as if his own words were ridiculous.

In the past, if Shaolin raised its banner and shouted, countless sects would gather under it. But now that’s impossible.

Everyone had clearly seen what transpired at Plum Blossom Island. They realized they could be abandoned by Shaolin the moment they were isolated—who in their right mind would follow Shaolin’s orders after that?

That seemingly trivial choice opened the path of survival for each by themselves among many Justice faction sects.

‘Exactly, why be so foolish….’

A cause often appears foolish if judged only by short-term results. But the reason it’s a cause is that it ultimately leads to the right outcome.

If Shaolin had rushed to rescue Namgung at that time instead of following their principles, the power behind Shaolin’s voice would have been incomparable to now.

In any case, with the reputation and power Shaolin currently had, a full-scale assault on Southern lands was impossible. They might lead a few sects that followed them in an attack, but gathering all the medium and small sects would be difficult.

Then only one possibility remained.

“…They’re going to cross the river? Leading these Evil Sect forces?”

“Could they? These aren’t fighters skilled enough to cross the vast Yangtze river barehanded, are they? Even if what we see is all there is, many flotillas would have to make several trips back and forth. The Nine Great Sects wouldn’t stand by and watch that, would they?”

“Something must have happened in the meantime. Something that makes that crossing impossible to stop.”

“I see….”

Namgung Do-Wei’s face grew grimmer. Summing up the conversation so far, the conclusion was clear.

A northward advance. It was certain Jang Ilso had drawn his sword.

‘Now?’

Namgung Do-Wei swallowed dryly without realizing it.

No, it’s possible. Anytime. Jang Ilso could cross the river and start a war at any moment. Ordinary people seek stability and certain victory, but what he desires is chaos and a dramatic victory using it.

Even knowing all that, the reason they trembled at this fact was simple.

‘We all assumed it wouldn’t happen now—even I did.’

War can break out at any time. Everyone here knew that.

But at the same time, everyone thought the timing wouldn’t be now. It would be later, when some time had passed.

Jang Ilso openly mocked their certainty and hopes.

“Why of all times is it now….”

“If you think in reverse, isn’t now the only time?”

“Huh?”

“The world is lived by the masses, but it’s moved by a few. Aren’t the gazes of that few completely focused on the Southern Island right now?”

“….”

“Jang Ilso would certainly exploit such a situation. He will try to steer events as he wishes. Even if the time isn’t ripe, if the conditions fit, he’ll disregard that.”

“Then….”

Namgung Do-Wei’s voice trembled slightly. The meaning of those words hit him painfully clearly.

“Are you saying war is breaking out because of us… because of none other than us?”

Baek Cheon was silent for a moment. Then, having organized his thoughts, he spoke again.

“The war would have occurred sooner or later. Just because the timing was slightly hastened doesn’t make it entirely our fault.”

“But….”

Namgung Do-Wei still couldn’t hide his discomfort and hesitated. Baek Cheon looked him squarely in the eye and asked.

“Then, knowing this outcome, would you have stayed in the Northern lands to avoid the war?”

Namgung Do-Wei could not answer that.

“The opponent is Jang Ilso. Had we not moved, he would have used the fall of Southern Island Sect to spin another scheme. That’s the sort of man he is.”

Namgung Do-Wei gave a faint nod.

“So for now, let’s only think about breaking through this situation.”

“Exactly the right answer!”

The two turned sharply at a voice behind them. Im So-Byeong was twirling the fan in his hand and bobbing his head.

“You two seem to have grown in your own ways. This strategist is quite pleased.”

“That makes me feel filthy.”

“Isn’t praise from an Evil Sect bastard just an insult?”

The two bristled for no reason. Then Im So-Byeong’s gaze suddenly darkened as he spoke.

“More importantly, focus on something else.”

“Yes?”

“If Jang Ilso truly intends to wage war… if he plans to advance north, one advantage we’ve enjoyed all this time will vanish completely.”

“An advantage?”

The two turned cold. What advantage could they possibly have gotten thanks to that Jang Ilso? If anything, they’d suffered losses.

Namgung Do-Wei, who had lost half his family because of Jang Ilso, glared at Im So-Byeong with murderous eyes. Im So-Byeong shrugged and spoke decisively.

“To be precise: there will no longer be a need to maintain balance.”

“Balance?”

“The existence of the Heavenly Friends Alliance.”

Only then did the two fall silent, understanding Im So-Byeong’s words.

“Until now, Jang Ilso had aimed for fractures among the Justice faction by leaving the Heavenly Friends Alliance alone. But if war breaks out, the story changes. The Heavenly Friends Alliance is the faction most likely to fight most fiercely on the war’s front lines.”

“Indeed….”

Even if they didn’t want it, it would happen. If one man loses his mind and ramps up his brutality.

And that meant….

“Until now the Alliance was kept alive because they were useful, but now they’ll be an obstacle to be killed, by any means necessary.”

Their gazes turned back at once—toward the person trailing behind, Chung Myung.

“That….”

Baek Cheon, about to add something while looking at that expressionless face, flinched.

His gaze instead moved a bit further back, toward the grove they’d just passed. Tall, jutting trees rustled and swayed.

“Al-already….”

“They’re coming.”

Im So-Byeong folded his fan and gripped it tightly.

“Steel yourselves. This will be different from what we’ve faced so far.”

Kwaaaang!

Then large trees crashed down one after another, revealing all-too-familiar figures. The Ten Thousand People Clan uniforms, now a wearisome sight.

Ho Ga-Myung, standing in their midst, growled with cold eyes.

“Show them how vicious the Evil Sect’s vengeance can be.”

Baek Cheon bit his lip until it bled.

Chapter 1384: Of Course, That Would Never Happen. (4)

“Damn it, he’s even going to show up in my dreams now! That cursed bastard!”

Jo Gul spat out harsh words. Now, just seeing Ho Ga-Myung’s face made his legs go numb. If asked to name the person he hated most in the world, he would have answered Jang Ilso without a second’s hesitation… no, Beop Jeong? Uh….

Anyway, if someone asked, he’d pick Jang Ilso or Beop Jeong, but if asked who the most terrifying person in the world was, he was now sure he could shout Ho Ga-Myung’s name without hesitation.

Even Chung Myung, who barged into his quarters in the dead of night, wasn’t as loathsome as that man.

And though he made sobbing noises with his mouth, the situation was far more serious than his cries suggested. A real enemy, not the riffraff blocking their way, had reappeared.

‘Already?’

Baek Cheon’s situation was the same.

They had expected those bastards to follow them eventually, but they hadn’t thought it would be this fast. He had assumed it would be at least after they’d almost reached Nanjing.

But there was no time now to retrace where his prediction had gone wrong and analyze it.

“Break through at full speed!”

“Yes!”

This place was adjacent to the Yangtze River, a wide plain. They wouldn’t be forced into unfavorable terrain as before, but conversely, there was nowhere to hide.

So the only option left was a straight force-on-force breakthrough.

“Hae…”

Baek Cheon urgently tried to call Hae Yeon and Namgung Do-Wei, but they had already thrown themselves backward. They seemed to know what needed to be done without orders.

Somewhat relieved, Baek Cheon set about doing what he had to do.

Kwahng!

With force that nearly crushed the ground, Baek Cheon charged past Gwak Hwan-So and surged forward.

“Yiseol, Jo Gul, Soso! Cover the rear!”

“Yes!”

“Acting Sect Leader! We’ll help too!”

“Follow us!”

Without looking back, Baek Cheon shouted at Gwak Hwan-So and shot red sword qi from his sword in torrents.

“Aaaaaaah!”

The violently released Plum Blossom surged at the Evil Sect members ahead like flood-swollen stream water. The Evil Sect fighters turned green with fear.

“Ge-get out of the way…”

Before they could even finish screaming, the Evil Sect members were swept away by the incoming sword qi. Baek Cheon cried out.

“It’s a short run to the Yangtze River. Let’s go in one push!”

“Yes!”

Ho Ga-Myung’s mouth twitched slightly as he watched.

“Not too late, then.”

Logically, a few with greater martial prowess shouldn’t be catchable by many with lower skill. Yet that impossible thing was possible because of the stupid choices they had made.

Ho Ga-Myung narrowed his eyes; he could clearly see the injured people they had carried.

‘If they’re carrying the wounded, no matter how fast they are, they can’t reach their full speed.’

And having to hide now and then to avoid detection would mean they couldn’t even manage half their normal speed. Catching up to them wasn’t that difficult.

“Stupid bastards.”

If it were him, he’d have mercilessly abandoned the wounded. Even if they could become fighters later, no one should risk total annihilation for those who weren’t combat effective at the moment.

But Ho Ga-Myung knew—and so did they—that they could never make that choice. The two characters ‘benevolence and righteousness’ they’d shouted countless times had shackled them; no matter how they struggled, they couldn’t cast them off.

“Every choice has a price. Execute them. Leave not a single one of them alive.”

The Ten Thousand People Clan members surged forward without answering.

A vicious malice filled their eyes.

Ho Ga-Myung wasn’t the only one who bore hatred for those Justice faction bastards. Even if comradeship among the Evil Sect was thin, to be toyed with by much fewer and weaker people and not bear a grudge would make them no martial men at all.

Moreover, they were members of the Ten Thousand People Clan, those who might forget favors but never forget grudges. They poured out murderous intent like predators who’d rediscovered the prey that had bitten them and escaped.

“Ahhaaaat!”

Of course, the ones who stood in the path of their red wave were the gatekeepers of the Heavenly Friends Alliance.

“Monk!”

“I know, Taoist!”

Hae Yeon and Namgung Do-Wei, who stood before the onrushing men, exchanged looks.

One was the top prodigy of the millennium Shaolin, likely representing Shaolin, leader of the Nine Great Sects. The other was the Young lord who had inherited the Namgung name, thought to walk only the path of glory as the heir of the world’s foremost clan.

Those who should have walked the most blessed paths in the world stood side by side in one of the most dangerous and weighty places in the current central plains.

But there was not a hint of hesitation in their eyes. They stood there by their own will, not by anyone else’s.

“Oooooooo!”

“Taaaaaaap!”

The power and sword qi they unleashed blasted ferociously at the charging enemies.

Kwahaaang!

Shaolin’s flaming brilliance and Namgung’s sword qi exploded like bombs, lighting up the world for a moment. It was a truly powerful strike. The charging Ten Thousand People Clan members were literally shattered by a single blow.

‘Brutal.’

Ho Ga-Myung watched the scene without blinking and unconsciously bit his lip.

Logically, they should have been exhausted by now. Their power should have weakened and their swords dulled.

But what they showed now was sharper than before. Even to Ho Ga-Myung, whose martial strength wasn’t particularly high, it was clearly visible.

‘They’re still growing even now?’

He understood it intellectually.

They were the most promising talents in the Central Plains, and they’d had experiences no one else could.

He could guarantee that nowhere in the Justice faction’s murim world would there be people who’d endured harsher real combat than they had.

But what he knew in his head and what he felt watching such talents grow before his eyes were completely different sensations.

Perhaps what Ho Ga-Myung felt now was fear, the fear of watching a predator at arm’s reach grow day by day, something he couldn’t directly control.

Ho Ga-Myung lightly clenched and unclenched his fist. Yet he now had an opportunity to cut off the throat of the predator that hadn’t fully grown.

By taking their lives?

No.

Ho Ga-Myung’s gaze turned to one side, the true lifeline of the Heavenly Friends Alliance’s predator, and the source they were nurturing.

’ Mount Hua’s Sword Saint. No… Plum Blossom Sword Ghost.’

He met eyes with Chung Myung, who glared at him coldly from the group’s center. Murderous intent rippled through Ho Ga-Myung’s gaze.

“Hear this. This is not my order. It is the order of the Alliance Lord—carry it out with your lives!”

“Yes!”

“Whatever sacrifices it takes.”

The moment those words were spoken, the subordinates’ faces hardened as much as they could.

“Do not let the Mount Hua’s Sword Saint escape. Kill him without fail! Even if it costs the lives of everyone else!”

“We obey!”

Hearing the erupting responses, Ho Ga-Myung slowly nodded.

He had suffered a crushing defeat. The mistakes he’d made would probably be irredeemable in his lifetime.

But he could say proudly that it wasn’t a meaningless defeat.

Because that defeat led the Alliance Lord to resolve to take the Plum Blossom Sword Ghost’s head, it wouldn’t be entirely meaningless.

“You will never live, Plum Blossom Sword Ghost.”

Ho Ga-Myung murmured as if making a vow to himself.

“They’re pressing in from both sides!”

“You’ve faced this before, respond!”

Southern Island Sect was no longer the Southern Island Sect of the past. Even without direct orders, he moved on his own to counter. Swift and precise, there was no need to speak.

However….

“This isn’t good.”

It didn’t seem so to Im So-Byeong. He glanced at Chung Myung beside him and asked.

“Wasn’t it earlier than expected?”

“…”

“Then there’s an unexpected variable… In times like this, I usually think of one thing first.”

“There won’t be.”

“You mean it’s not betrayal.”

“No, that won’t happen.”

A strange light crossed Im So-Byeong’s face. He sensed that a lot was contained in that answer.

Chung Myung hadn’t said there would be no traitor. But he wasn’t considering the possibility of one.

Because in this situation, hunting for a traitor would be pointless.

He judged it better to trust each other and move forward, even if that risked throwing them into an abyss.

“You’re still extreme with your thoughts.”

Im So-Byeong nodded with a slightly calm expression.

“But what will you do? You’ve already used up all useful moves, and the enemies are on our tail. Even though the Yangtze River is near, if we fight these and reach the river, only about ten people will remain.”

Around ten people.

Chung Myung knew why Im So-Byeong mentioned the number. He was quietly urging them to abandon the Southern Island Sect.

“Wasn’t that decided from the start?”

He had said it himself—Chung Myung.

If they were ever in an inescapable crisis, he’d abandon the Southern Island Sect. The members of the Heavenly Friends Alliance mattered many times more than they did.

If, in trying to save them, those who came with the Heavenly Friends Alliance lost their lives, Chung Myung himself could never forgive himself.

“Or is the Taoist really more of an exemplary gentleman than I thought?”

Chung Myung sneered, twisting the corner of his mouth.

“I’ve said it every time… I’ve never once thought of myself as a wandering Taoist on the right side. I don’t want to be one, either.”

It was a voice that gave off a strange feeling. Im So-Byeong’s eyes flickered as he looked at Chung Myung for a moment, then nodded.

There was one reason he’d regained composure after exiting the gorge. No matter the failures, unless the enemy blocked their path, the option to cut Southern Island Sect loose and for the Heavenly Friends Alliance to survive by themselves always existed.

“Good answer. Then the conclusion is decided, isn’t it?”

“Of course we’ll do that. But…”

“…Yes?”

A chilling voice whispered in Im So-Byeong’s ear.

“This isn’t a crisis of that level.”

“…Taoist?”

Chung Myung’s eyes were seething.

It was unlike him; recently he’d shown only dark, calm eyes. Im So-Byeong’s heart dropped. He sensed it: Chung Myung was finally letting go of something he’d been suppressing.

“You’re going to come out like this?”

Chung Myung murmured and turned to walk forward.

Im So-Byeong, sensing what he intended, hurriedly reached out to grab his sleeve, but his hand found only empty air. Chung Myung was already running.

“Taoist! Taoooist!”

Im So-Byeong yelled until his throat strained. His eyes reddened. He didn’t know why he was so furious, but it felt like his insides would burst.

Chapter 1385: Of Course, That Would Never Happen. (5)

“Ta- Taoist?”

The fleeing disciples of Southern Island Sect were startled when they saw Chung Myung running toward them from across.

“Keep going!”

“Yes!”

But at Chung Myung’s shout they soon regained their composure. Besides, this wasn’t the first time Chung Myung had acted unpredictably.

What actually unsettled them was not Chung Myung but Im So-Byeong standing like a stone behind him, staring at him.

Green Forest King Im So-Byeong.

He was hardly someone the disciples of Southern Island Sect would feel close to. Had they not come together under the name of the Heavenly Friends Alliance, they wouldn’t even have had cause to meet him.

That made it all the more disconcerting. Im So-Byeong, who always looked at everyone with a cynical air, was standing like that.

“…No, Green For…”

Grit.

At someone’s voice, Im So-Byeong bit his lower lip for a moment. The fan he usually held casually in his hand bent as if about to break in his veiny grip.

Im So-Byeong’s gaze, which had been glaring at Chung Myung’s retreating back as if to devour him, turned to the Southern Island Sect disciples who had frozen in front of him. He said coldly.

“What are you doing?”

“…Yes?”

“Don’t hesitate, fall in and follow ahead! Now!”

“Ah, yes! Yes!”

The disciples of Southern Island Sect charged forward without knowing why. Glancing at them, Im So-Byeong bit his lip, glared at Chung Myung’s back, then turned sharply.

‘Damn it.’

No matter how he thought about it, he couldn’t understand.

They could just be abandoned, couldn’t they? What meaning does a bunch like Southern Island Sect have? Comrades who shared life and death? People who might help someday?

Ridiculous!

Comrades, the future—first I have to live for any of it to mean anything, isn’t that so? You can make new comrades; you can train those who will become strength. Nothing is more important than my life in the moment. No, it can’t be.

Then why should I invite danger for such things? What on earth is in that lunatic’s head?

At that moment, Yoon Jong, having noticed the Green Forest King, asked with a puzzled look.

“Green Forest King. Chung Myung…?”

Im So-Byeong barely forced back an expression that was about to contort and answered.

“He’ll cover the rear. Don’t worry about him and move forward!”

“Cover the rear? Two people already…?”

“There’s no time! Break through ahead! If you don’t want to see the people left behind killed!”

“Ah, understood!”

Yoon Jong quickly nodded and stomped the ground.

The Green Forest King bit his lip hard. He wasn’t lying. It was true—they had to force their way ahead urgently.

If they sensed the Mount Hua’s Sword Saint’s intent and acted against it, it would be ruin. Im So-Byeong knew better than anyone that plans failing to commit to a clear course end in destruction.

He looked at Chung Myung one last time. He had already run far toward the rear.

Creak.

In the end, Im So-Byeong snapped the fan in his hand and turned his head coldly, unaware that his bitten lip was bleeding.

“Monk!”

“Understood!”

Boom!

As the hearty stamp of stance echoed, dozens of currents of power burst from Hae Yeon’s fists. It was too flashy to be the typically solemn, weighty Shaolin martial art, and thus appeared all the more menacing.

Those who had been surging in an unstoppable tide faltered before that power. Hae Yeon’s fists were desperately suppressing a terrifying blaze that looked ready to ignite.

‘But it won’t last long!’

Hae Yeon knew as well: that he and Namgung Do-Wei alone had their limits in holding them back. For now they were merely buying time so those ahead could get a bit farther.

“Woooaaaah!”

Namgung Do-Wei’s sword emitted countless white rays.

His sword was not much different from Hae Yeon’s fists: flashy and vast, but lacking substance—a sword meant only to intimidate and push enemies back.

“Guh!”

The onslaught of sword qi made the Ten Thousand People Clan warriors hesitate. They knew not to underestimate how much inner force those two attacks carried. But the cost was fatal.

Crack!

“Ugh!”

A bloody blade jutted out, having pierced the neck of one who had staggered back. The eyes of the man who had driven the blade into his subordinate’s throat were bloodshot and bulging as if about to burst. He let out a howl.

“Those who retreat will die by my hand! Choose! Will you die by those bastards, or by me?”

No one could step back after hearing that. All gritted their teeth.

“Charge them, now!”

Instead of an answer, beast-like roars burst forth.

Hae Yeon expelled his power once more to try to sap the enemies’ momentum.

“Amitābha!”

A majestic blaze burst forth with a mighty roar, battering and crushing the chargers like demonic foes. Yet the attackers did not stop advancing even as their skin split under the pressure.

“Diiieeee!”

Before Hae Yeon, a Ten Thousand People Clan warrior had crawled forward, matted in blood. At the moment Hae Yeon grit his teeth—

Fwoosh!

A red sword qi flew instantly and mercilessly sliced the Ten Thousand People Clan warrior’s throat.

Startled, Hae Yeon turned his gaze and saw Chung Myung surging in like a divine bird.

“What are you doing, you fools!”

“Taoist!”

Chung Myung wasted no time and passed them, plunging into the heart of the enemy.

Whish!

Even as he ran forward, plum blossom petals streamed from the tip of his sword in a steady line. It was as if a trail of petals opened behind Chung Myung as he ran.

‘That’s…!’

Hae Yeon opened his eyes wide. He recognized it. What to others might seem merely wondrous contained a sublime pinnacle of martial art.

“Plum Blossom Sword Ghost!”

“Kill him! Don’t fall back, kill him!”

The Ten Thousand People Clan warriors who had hesitated surged forward with even more explosive momentum. Their arch-enemy of the Ten Thousand People Clan had appeared before their eyes—of course they reacted.

“Kill him! Whoever severs the Plum Blossom Sword Ghost’s neck will enjoy riches and glory beyond imagination for life! Kill him by any means!”

There was no way out anyway. The moment they retreated they’d die at their allies’ hands. So they could only risk everything and drive their blades into that fiend’s body.

The Ten Thousand People Clan warriors accelerated in a moment, their charge like rapids turning into a waterfall.

Amid that brutal onslaught, Chung Myung’s sword moved calmly. Small, then smaller. From the neatly drawn circle at the tip, plum blossom petals curled forth.

The plum petals trailing the sword tip began, at some point, to spread in all directions. At first they were so slow their spreading was barely noticeable, but they gained speed and soon fully enveloped the surroundings. The sight of the area covered was spectacular.

The Ten Thousand People Clan warriors widened their eyes at a sight they could hardly believe.

“Wh- what…?”

The trembling forest and the people before them were all covered in plum blossoms. The whole scene turned crimson.

“Th- that’s!”

At that moment a breath escaped from Namgung Do-Wei’s mouth.

“Plum blossom… fragrance….”

It couldn’t possibly be, yet it felt as if he had smelled plum blossoms. The sight before him, each petal, was so vivid it created that illusion.

Plum Blossom Sword Formation, Plum Blossom Ten Thousand Mile Fragrance.

The pinnacle of Mount Hua sect’s swordsmanship. At this moment a line of the Plum Blossom Sword Formation bloomed as if reenacting a scene from a hundred years past.

A truly dreamlike spectacle.

Even those who had charged with all their might momentarily forgot what they were doing. The fluttering plum petals gently settled on their bodies.

Shing!

A sharp pain awakened their hazy consciousness. Those who came to saw a cascade of petals around their bodies, like a faint scent seeping in.

“Ah, no…!”

Fwoooosh!

The fluttering plum petals spread like a blaze and covered everything: hostile intent, the people bearing it, and even their screams.

The petals, shattered and scattered, soon vanished like an illusion—present yet leaving nothing behind, like a scent.

“Uh….”

As the plum petals vanished right before him, the ashen-faced man raised his head and looked ahead.

The world had stopped.

Those who had screamed and rushed at the Plum Blossom Sword Ghost, and the Plum Blossom Sword Ghost facing them, were all frozen in place as if time had stopped.

It was a scene that bred doubt. Had they perhaps already died? Would they now gaze upon this sight for eternity?

And then.

Thud! Thud! Thud!

The Ten Thousand People Clan warriors, frozen like statues, all collapsed at once, as if the stopped time had begun to flow again.

“Uh….”

Those overwhelmed forgot their positions and looked at Chung Myung with dazed eyes.

Sss.

It was felt distinctly.

The movement of the sleeve tips swaying in the wind, the residual tremor on the sword tip not yet steadied, and the movement of the foot beginning to step toward them—all were felt.

Had they ever been so mesmerized by someone in their lives? Even with all their concentration?

This was not their will. That sword was bewitching everyone. That sword was forcibly drawing them in, as if being sucked into its tip were natural.

Then, to those drifting somewhere between reality and illusion, Chung Myung charged in.

Fwoom!

The sword he swept in a sudden rush instantly cut down the neck of a Ten Thousand People Clan warrior who still hadn’t come to his senses.

Three necks shot into the air at once, and red blood spouted like fountains.

“That….”

Before the smell of blood could spread, Chung Myung’s voice, laced with mockery, was heard first.

“You little brats… how dare you think of killing someone?”

Ho Ga-Myung clenched his fist tightly. He clearly knew the man standing in the distance and staring coldly was looking at him. Standing before that gaze made his insides boil. Rage and venom seemed about to burst from his mouth.

Chung Myung shook his head slightly.

“You still don’t know your place. You and that idiot Jang Ilso are the same.”

Then he scanned the surroundings.

Even the Ten Thousand People Clan warriors surrounding Chung Myung had stopped. Not missing the moment, Southern Island Sect’s main force was steadily putting distance between this place and themselves.

Glancing at the situation, Chung Myung smiled, baring stark white teeth in contrast to Ho Ga-Myung.

“Kill him!”

Ho Ga-Myung shouted loudly. Somehow his voice sounded like a scream.

“Make sure to kill him! By any means necessary that bastard must die! Kill him! Kiiiilllll!”

As if infected by him, the Ten Thousand People Clan fighters who had survived among the plum petals rolled their eyes back and charged again.

The Ten Thousand People Clan’s sprint toward a single person. At its culmination, Chung Myung gripped his sword so hard it seemed it might snap.

Chapter 1386: Underestimating People. (1)

“Ooooooh!”

The Ten Thousand People Clan swordsman at the front, eyes bloodshot, brought his blade down toward Chung Myung.

Kaaaang!

The qi-enhanced single strike lost its force the moment it struck the slender, rising sword; it felt as if the blade had hit an iron wall.

But there was no time to shudder. The sword, gliding obliquely along the blade, pierced his throat.

Sploosh!

“Gurgle……”

The Ten Thousand People Clan swordsman, horror in his eyes, reflexively tried to grab the sword lodged in his neck, but before his hand even touched it the blade twisted. With a horrific sound his life was brutally cut off.

“Huaaaaagh!”

The sight of blood only excited the Ten Thousand People Clan swordsmen more; they surged at Chung Myung with increased ferocity.

Paaaat!

And what greeted those Ten Thousand People Clan attackers was a swift sword like a ray of light. Dozens of afterimages of the blade split and shot toward them, like what Jo Gul had shown—but far more precise and much faster!

Kwaduk! Kwaduk! Kwaduk!

Holes like wind tunnels appeared in the bodies of those charging. Their flesh collapsed as if shedding a husk, before they could even scream.

A wide clearing formed around Chung Myung for an instant, but it was quickly filled again by attackers pouring in, like water rushing to fill a scooped-out pool.

“Taoistt!”

From behind, Namgung Do-Wei, seeing Chung Myung, shouted loudly. The Ten Thousand People Clan pressed at Chung Myung with terrifying momentum—nearly a thousand of them focusing solely on him, swirling into the hole in the ground like a vortex.

It was a terrifying sight never seen anywhere else; merely watching made the chest constrict.

“No, don’t……!”

Suddenly!

Someone grabbed Namgung Do-Wei’s shoulder as he reflexively lunged forward. Turning, he saw Hae Yeon standing with a stern face.

“Do not act rashly!”

“But the Taoist is right now……”

“Do not forget your duty, young lord of Namgung!”

Namgung Do-Wei flinched. Hae Yeon was right. His duty was not to rescue Chung Myung but to delay their pursuit as much as possible.

And Chung Myung would be thinking the same—he was doing that very thing now. Trying to assist him here would be foolish. He must hold the line and quickly rejoin the main force once they pull away.

“Kahaaaa!”

And, looking coldly at the reality, helping Chung Myung was impossible—forces left even after surrounding him now charged at Namgung Do-Wei and Hae Yeon with ferocity.

“Haap!”

Namgung Do-Wei flicked his sword and released sword qi. A white blade qi imbued with powerful internal qi poured out, crushing the charging Ten Thousand People Clan into pulp.

But the space created, stained with blood, was soon filled by other Ten Thousand People Clan attackers—perhaps even more forcing their way in.

Hae Yeon shouted decisively.

“Withdraw!”

“M-Monk! Right now……”

Before Namgung Do-Wei could reply, Hae Yeon’s grip on his shoulder conveyed strong force. A dizzying vertigo hit them as their bodies were already pushed back nearly ten paces.

Hae Yeon’s Diamond-Static Divine Method was at its peak. But the enemies, unconcerned by that near-miracle, only poured out cold killing intent and kept chasing.

Wooooooong!

Hae Yeon swept both hands in all directions. Golden-tinted palm shapes formed one after another in the air along his fingertips. Those hands, like the Thousand-Hand Avalokiteshvara, blocked the relentless attacks from all sides.

‘Thousand Buddha Hands!’

It was a Shaolin technique famed across the land. The Ten Thousand People Clan hesitated before the wall of force; indeed, the power radiating from each palm-shadow pushed them back before they could approach.

“Eek…!”

At that moment, the retreating Ten Thousand People Clan widened their eyes. The phantom palm-shadows vanished in dazzling light—and a strong white sword qi descended upon them.

Kwaaaarrrng!

Namgung Do-Wei’s sword qi surged through the gap Hae Yeon had opened. Their combo flowed as if they had sparred together for decades—perfectly linked! Yet the faces of those who landed the sequence were not cheerful.

Because as much space as they created, other Ten Thousand People Clan pressed in. Over a thousand of them circled around Chung Myung, spinning and spinning—malice and obsession to never let him go were palpable.

And then, at that moment.

Fwaaahhh!

A splendid plum blossom bloomed in the midst of the black-and-red whirl. Like a plum tree that roots and blooms even in filthy mud.

“Taoist! What on earth……!”

Namgung Do-Wei cried out in shock.

It was brilliant. Overwhelmingly powerful—sword qi that could only inspire awe in people. The idea that a person could create such a scene with a sword sent chills down the spine.

But everything has a cost. That blade must be consuming enormous internal qi. It wasn’t the kind of technique Chung Myung would use in his current impaired state.

“Taoist!”

Hae Yeon also, startled, stared at the blossoming plum.

Chung Myung normally deployed a blade so efficient it could be called extreme—he never wasted energy needlessly. But the sword Chung Myung was displaying now was excessively ornate, almost ostentatious.

It seemed as if he wanted to fix everyone’s gaze on the tip of that blade.

They assumed Chung Myung would slip away by now, so this was all the more bewildering.

“It’s too late! Taoist, what on earth are you thinking!”

A roar burst from Hae Yeon’s mouth.

They had spent a long time together. Hae Yeon trusted Chung Myung utterly—more than Namgung Do-Wei could even imagine.

But Chung Myung’s current actions were so strange they shook even that great trust.

Then Namgung Do-Wei shouted like a scream.

“Monk! There!”

A black line of a sword rose in the air, slicing through the blooming plum. The line was Chung Myung, fiercely deploying his divine method. The two men felt a momentary relief.

There would be no suicidal “jade smash.” The Chung Myung they knew would never do such a reckless thing.

He was simply a bit later than expected—he was surely extracting himself from within. Hae Yeon shouted.

“Let us withdraw too!”

“Hold a bit longer and we can join him!”

Hae Yeon fell silent at Namgung Do-Wei’s words. It made sense: if they helped Chung Myung and pulled out together……

But at that moment Hae Yeon’s gaze faltered.

The black line in the air was moving in a somewhat odd direction. If he were chasing the main force, it should head toward their location. Even to sow confusion for the pursuers, it shouldn’t stray far north.

But the black line was clearly moving away from them.

“What!”

Before he could assess or give chase, the Ten Thousand People Clan filled Hae Yeon’s vision.

“Oh, ooooo!”

Hae Yeon desperately released his power. In the chaos it contained barely half his usual force, and it was impossible to subdue all the Ten Thousand People Clan at once with that.

“Dieeee!”

The legs of those struck by Hae Yeon’s palm were crushed, yet they did not lose consciousness. Even as they fell they clawed and slashed wildly.

Kakak! Kakak!

Blades rained down on Hae Yeon, who had drawn his internal force to its peak. Shaolin body martial arts, arguably unrivaled in external technique, protected him, but blades still sliced skin and blood spurted.

“Ugh!”

Hae Yeon did not collapse; he rooted himself and unleashed a lion’s roar.

The supreme great ability he unleashed in an instant pushed the rushing enemies away like a torrent.

But only for a moment—the enemies began to charge again.

“Back, back!”

In an instant the situation turned. One misstep and they’d be surrounded and torn apart alive.

“Guh!”

Hae Yeon and Namgung Do-Wei were pushed back again and again like a tiny boat on a raging current, not by choice, but because they could not withstand the enemy onslaught.

And what caught their eyes was the Ten Thousand People Clan moving with the fluidity of black water.

“Taoist!”

A thunderous shout erupted from Hae Yeon.

The troops pushing them back were all turning and being channeled to one side. What awaited at the end of that current was obvious without looking.

Chung Myung.

He was drawing the enemies away, heading in a completely different direction from where Mount Hua and the Southern Island Sect were fleeing.

“Damn it! That damned man!”

A rare curse escaped from Hae Yeon’s lips.

They tried to turn the situation around somehow, but it was already impossible. Just avoiding being swept up by the pressing attackers was exhausting—a limit had been reached.

Kwoong!

A punch, released like a scream, momentarily rebounded the barrier in front. With that brief clarity, Hae Yeon saw clearly: Chung Myung, flanked by elite Ten Thousand People Clan in red garments, running east—completely away from where they needed to go.

“Taoissssstttttttt!”

A cry so desperate it could only be called tragic burst out, too distant to reach Chung Myung.

Ho Ga-Myung’s gaze, watching the situation, was chillingly calm.

“Commander, the enemy has split directions! What shall we do?”

“We will split our forces into two as well.”

“Yes? Our objective is……”

“The moment the main force is secure, that Plum Blossom Sword Ghost will try by any means to shake us off and escape. But if he thinks the main force is still in danger, he’ll try to hold as many of us as possible, despite knowing that’s the more dangerous option.”

“……”

“It’s the predetermined sequence. Do as ordered.”

“Yes!”

Ho Ga-Myung bit his lip lightly.

‘We should have done this sooner.’

Seeing Mount Hua and Chung Myung as a single unit was the mistake. They could have been separated this easily.

‘This is why I pledge such loyalty to the alliance lord.’

Because Jang Ilso is someone who can paint the picture he wants so easily even from afar.

“When cornered, humans look for an escape. But if there are only two choices, they will pursue whichever they think is the better path.”

A phrase heard countless times before……

Ho Ga-Myung was curious.

If only one of two outcomes could be achieved, would that Plum Blossom Sword Ghost choose his own life or the lives of those few sahyung disciples?

“Either way, you will fall by this, Plum Blossom Sword Ghost.”

Chapter 1387: Underestimating People. (2)

“Faster!”

“Yes!”

The reply came from ahead, not beside him. Before his words were even finished, no, before he could even open his mouth to convey his intent, the sahyung-sajaes were already moving according to his will.

Baek Cheon unknowingly clenched his fist.

It might be strange to say now, but he had never felt especially in sync with those guys.

That made sense; Mount Hua’s disciples each had such strong personalities it was hard for them to blend. Without Chung Myung as their focal point, they might have spent their whole lives treating each other like strangers.

But now they moved like limbs. Not merely following orders; everyone was thinking the same way centered on Baek Cheon.

If the front seemed a little loose, Jo Gul struck like lightning to fill the gap. Before a thought that Jo Gul might be struggling could form, Yoon Jong rushed in.

Worrying that Yu Yiseol might be overexerting herself, he turned and found Tang Soso already clinging to her like a spirit. The tip of her sword, swung so passionately, carried a strong will not to burden Yu Yiseol.

Just like Baek Cheon’s own heart now.

Baek Cheon lashed his sword like lightning. The red sword qi sliced through the enemy rushing at Yoon Jong in an instant.

“Thank you, sasuk!”

“Don’t worry about me, just watch the front!”

“Yes!”

Baek Cheon’s eyes hardened slightly at the sight. The enemies weren’t particularly formidable, but with a time limit, it became an unbearable burden.

But fortunately, they were not alone here anymore.

“Haaaah!”

Tang Pae leaped up and shouted, swinging his hand. Dozens of beams burst from its tip and soon struck the retreating enemies right on the crown of their heads.

Jo Gul was momentarily shocked at what he saw.

“What, pine needles…?”

“They won’t even need a needle! It won’t kill, but it will help!”

“…Even the assassination technique is worth learning.”

A successor of the Tang Family backed him, and Seol So-Baek was desperately swinging the sword in his still-unfledged hand.

“Don’t leave it all to Mount Hua! We must open the path for our own survival!”

“Yes!”

And the Southern Island Sect disciples—who had always waited only for Baek Cheon’s command—were desperately trying to get ahead of the Heavenly Friends Alliance to slaughter the enemies.

“Let’s go, Ja-Yang!”

“Yes, sahyung!”

Gwak Hwan-So and Ja-Yang surged forward like a flash, cutting down the stunned Evil Sect members at once. The water-colored sword qi from their tips summoned the Southern Island sea he had once seen to this place.

A strange feeling rose on Baek Cheon’s face.

Where else could one see such a sight again? No, who could have imagined such a spectacle could exist?
