Like the Tang Family’s Young lord stepping on a Mount Hua disciple’s shoulder to leap, or a Southern Island’s sect leader throwing his body at the Ice Palace lord to strike down his way.

It’s hard to label them under the Justice faction. Among those here are people you couldn’t call the Justice faction.

Different backgrounds, different regions, different lives lived. But at least in this moment no one cared about any of that. They only tried to protect the person beside them and open the path together.

“Perhaps…”

Baek Cheon’s lips twitched. He couldn’t explain it in words; he couldn’t be sure what exactly this emotion he felt was.

But such a thought came to him.

All the things Chung Myung had done: binding the divided Mount Hua, tying together sects that seemed impossible to connect with threads of fate, rescuing even those everyone said must be abandoned.

Could it be that all the deeds Chung Myung had committed were meant to create this very scene?

He would never have Chung Myung tell him directly, and asking would only earn him ridicule, but somehow he felt that way.

But strangely…

“Sasuk!”

“Yes!”

Baek Cheon snapped back to focus and shook his sword.

‘I’m still far from it.’

To have such idle thoughts while needing to concentrate all his mind to open the path, he was far from worthy as a Sect leader. He chastised himself and steadied his mind.

‘How much distance have we opened?’

The situation was far from good. Even though Chung Myung, Hae Yeon, and Namgung Do-Wei were buying time, they couldn’t hold out for long by themselves.

No matter how strong they were, if sheer skill alone could overcome numbers, then no strong warrior in the world would bother forming factions.

Eventually the enemy would pursue. Baek Cheon had to lead them all out of the enemy’s grasp.

He wasn’t certain. Could it really be done?

They were the bastards who had doggedly followed them all the way from Guangdong. Was it really possible to shake them off? Especially with wounded among them?

But Baek Cheon’s certainty didn’t matter. It wasn’t a question of whether it was possible. It had to be done. If not, only annihilation remained.

Then.

“Sasuk, behind…!”

Baek Cheon hurriedly looked back. Hae Yeon and Namgung Do-Wei were running with all their strength. The blood droplets that sprang up with each footfall hinted at how fiercely they’d struggled.

The two leapt over the Southern Island Sect’s disciples in a single bound and ran to where Baek Cheon stood.

“Acting Sect Leader!”

“You two have endured…”

Baek Cheon’s mouth froze. He had seen their expressions—wild as if driven by rage, yet terrified.

In that moment Baek Cheon realized one fact.

“Se-Sect…”

“Chung Myung is—”

“…”

“Where is Chung Myung? Is he still behind?”

At Baek Cheon’s question Hae Yeon bit his lip. Namgung Do-Wei, on the other hand, cried out with a desperate face.

“The Taoist…! The Taoist didn’t join us and instead changed direction and disappeared!”

“…What?”

As if struck by a sudden blow, Baek Cheon’s face went blank for a moment. Namgung Do-Wei bit his lip tightly and spat out the words.

“The Taoist who had broken into the enemy lines changed direction… damn it! How do I put this! Ah, east! He went east! Most of them chased the Taoist instead of us. No, maybe more than half! The… the numbers aren’t that different, but still…”

Namgung Do-Wei, who was usually composed, wouldn’t have babbled so incoherently. He was clearly flustered and desperate.

Even from that messy speech, Baek Cheon understood the situation immediately.

“They followed him?”

“Yes, Acting Sect Leader! We must… we must do something.”

Baek Cheon’s gaze wavered.

It wasn’t normal. For so many to be chasing a single person made no sense. It was logically impossible…

At that moment Baek Cheon’s gaze snapped sharply to one side.

“Green Forest King!”

At the screamed cry, Im So-Byeong—who had been following from behind—met his gaze. He showed no surprise, only a cold look.

Only then did Baek Cheon realize: Im So-Byeong, who would usually stay by his side offering advice, hadn’t said a word for some time.

“Ugh…”

Baek Cheon ground his teeth and clenched his fist.

There was no need to ask why this had happened; he already understood. He understood what had occurred, why Chung Myung had turned his direction, and why the Green Forest King was silent.

“That idiot bastard!”

A curse finally escaped Baek Cheon’s lips.

He knew it too. Despite his relentless efforts, the situation remained desperate. That’s why Chung Myung hadn’t joined them. If he became bait to draw the rear, those here could survive.

As Baek Cheon had seen moments ago, the abilities of those here had grown unimaginably compared to before. If they avoided facing the enemy’s main force, crossing the river alive should be possible. But if that happened…

“Then what about Chung Myung?”

Baek Cheon flinched.

Jo Gul had already leapt to his side, his eyes flickering with intense emotion.

“Then what will happen to Chung Myung?”

Only silence returned. No words were needed—they had already seen what had become of Chung Myung, who stayed behind alone to hold off the enemy.

Even using the advantage of the gorge, he had been driven to the brink of death had it not been for Jo Gul and Yu Yiseol.

But in this wide open field, could he face more enemies without anyone’s help?

“Where is he?!”

An instant reaction burst from Jo Gul’s mouth.

“East? You said east? Sasuk! Then should we change direction too…”

“No!”

But Im So-Byeong cut him off with a blade-like voice.

“That’s not what Chung Myung, the Taoist, wants.”

“What nonsense are you spouting!”

“Then choose.”

“…What?”

“There is only one way to survive facing the enemy’s main force: abandon the Southern Island Sect and have only the able ones escape.”

Jo Gul fell silent.

“There is no other way. The Yangtze river is not a place you can break through while dragging baggage.”

They all knew that reaching the Yangtze river was the true beginning. That the real crisis would come from then on. They had only been turning a blind eye.

“We’ll deal with that when we get there…”

“If you’re going to speak of miracles, don’t bother speaking at all. Our opponent is Jang Ilso. There are no miracles for us.”

Those who heard it shivered. These were precisely the people who best understood the weight of the name Jang Ilso.

“If you want to save the Southern Island Sect, there’s only one method: avoid facing the enemy’s main force. To do that, someone must lure them away.”

“You mean Chung Myung?”

“Yes.”

Im So-Byeong continued in a low voice.

“Fortunately, it seems they don’t particularly care about us. That Jang Ilso seems intent on killing that one at all costs, even if it means losing the Southern Island Sect and us. He didn’t seem to intend that when the Southern land march began, but it’s clear he changed his mind midway.”

“…”

“So leave him and go. That’s what Chung Myung, the Taoist, wants us to do. Then we can live.”

“Shut up with that bullshit!”

A murderous look flared in Jo Gul’s eyes.

“Who said we should sacrifice that bastard and survive? Speak sense. Mount Hua is not that kind of place!”

“Then will you go to die leading everyone?”

“Of course…”

“You should’ve known this!”

Im So-Byeong, unlike himself, shouted in a rage.

“That pact you always yammer about—it’s nothing but throwing away your life for things that aren’t truly important! If you were willing to permit that, it means you must accept anyone else doing the same, not just the self!”

For a moment the surroundings grew so silent they felt chilling. Im So-Byeong muttered with a bitter face.

“You should have known that, you foolish people.”

Jo Gul glared at him silently. But then he noticed—Im So-Byeong’s hand, half-hidden in his wide sleeve, was trembling.

So Jo Gul’s gaze naturally turned to Baek Cheon.

“Sasuk. Give the command.”

“…”

“We have to go save him!”

“Listen.”

At that moment Baek Cheon spoke in a cold voice.

“As the Acting Sect Leader of Mount Hua, I command this. Dissent will not be tolerated. Especially, any Mount Hua member who disobeys this order will be punished by expulsion from the sect.”

“…Sasuk?”

With a strange foreboding, Jo Gul looked at Baek Cheon. His chiseled face showed no hint of emotion.

“…Ignore Chung Myung’s situation.”

“Sa-sasuk!”

Jo Gul reflexively reached out hurriedly, as if to stop whatever Baek Cheon would say next. But before his hand could touch him, Baek Cheon’s cold command flowed out.

“Everyone, go to the Yangtze river. Open the path with all your strength. No objections will be tolerated.”

It was like a cold dagger slicing through their chests.

Chapter 1388: Underestimating People. (3)

“Th—!”

Jo Gul, eyes full of bloodshot fury, opened his mouth to shout something. Before he could, someone grabbed his shoulder so hard it might have broken it.

When he turned, Yoon Jong was shaking his head with a stern expression. Seeing that, Jo Gul bit his lip hard.

This was neither a plea nor a negotiable suggestion. It was an order issued in the name of the Acting Sect Leader. Now that ‘expulsion from the sect’ had even been mentioned, there was no room for compromise. Jo Gul knew that. He did.

But…

“Do we really have to do this?”

Still unable to hold back, the question slipped out. Baek Cheon nodded firmly once again.

“Do not confuse what you want to do with what you must do. What we must do now is clear.”

Jo Gul’s gaze darkened.

“Then at least let me…”

“I already said no.”

Baek Cheon did not listen further. His face was harsh.

“No objections. If you are a disciple of Mount Hua, follow my order. If you insist on going, hand over the Plum Blossom engraved on your chest and go.”

At that icy voice, Jo Gul trembled with rage and clenched his fist.

“Sa—”

“I accept the Acting Sect Leader’s order.”

But before Jo Gul could resist further, Yoon Jong spoke as if to pre-empt him.

“Jo Gul. Step forward and open the way.”

“…”

“Now!”

Jo Gul glanced between Yoon Jong and Baek Cheon. Without a word he spun around and moved forward. Yu Yiseol and Tang Soso, who had been staring at them silently, followed him without a word.

“Out of the way! You bastards!”

Shouting a furious yell, Jo Gul slaughtered those blocking the way in an instant. The group, which had hesitated for a moment, began moving forward rapidly again.

Pushing off the ground, Baek Cheon looked back at Yoon Jong beside him.

“Help Jo Gul.”

“Yes.”

“…Thank you for calming him down.”

“There’s no need to say that. Following the Acting Sect Leader’s order is only natural.”

“…”

“And I do not think the sasuk’s decision is wrong. The moment intentions diverge, everyone perishes. Even if the direction is wrong, you must see it through resolutely to open even the faintest path.”

Baek Cheon nodded. What Yoon Jong said precisely captured the basis of his judgment.

And in truth above all, Chung Myung would not want them to go rescue him.

Even if they dropped everything and rushed in, what they’d face would only be his despairing face. Baek Cheon did not want to see that.

“However…”

At that moment, Yoon Jong’s voice came a bit belatedly. Baek Cheon turned and looked at him.

“The heart wishes to follow the way, but the body is merely human and finds it hard. I now understand those words.”

“…”

“I know the sasuk’s decision is right, and I understand it’s the best choice we got, but…”

Yoon Jong hesitated slightly, then continued.

“If by any chance Chung Myung dies and we walk safely into the Northern lands, I will never forgive the sasuk for the rest of my life.”

“…”

“Even if it turns out to be the wrong thing to feel towards sasuk.”

It was a remark half like a curse. But Baek Cheon’s expression did not change. Yoon Jong left behind words that sounded like admiration—or perhaps derision—and ran forward.

“You’ve become the sect leader, sasuk.”

Left alone, Baek Cheon closed his eyes for a moment. His lashes trembled faintly. When he opened them again, there was not a hint of hesitation on his face.

“You two take the left and right.”

At that calm command, Hae Yeon and Namgung Do-Wei exchanged looks of disbelief. After a moment and a sigh, they nodded and split left and right.

Now remaining were Baek Cheon and one other person.

“A good decision.”

“…”

“I worried the Acting Sect Leader might make a reckless decision, but it seems my fears were unfounded.”

“As for the Green Forest King…”

“Yes, I will watch the situation from the rear. Farewell.”

Im So-Byeong slipped back and muttered just barely audible.

“However, I still thought that if it were the Acting Sect Leader, he might make a different choice.”

Baek Cheon did not answer. He simply suppressed the outrage that seemed ready to burst out. He breathed in a long, deep breath so others wouldn’t notice and bit the inside of his cheek.

‘Damn bastard…’

Blood seeped from his tightly clenched fist. His fingernails dug into the flesh of his palm.

But Baek Cheon hid that hand inside his sleeve. By his expression, no one could tell what mood he was in.

“Hurry. Push straight to the Yangtze river! Faster, every moment counts!”

Chung Myung glanced up and scanned the enemies pouring in from all sides.

“Hmm. No need to harass them.”

Judging by how the enemies swarmed, it was clear they hadn’t considered the other side at all. There was nothing to worry about.

He had secretly worried, a one-in-a-thousand fear, that Baek Cheon might lose control and make a suicidal decision, but luckily he wasn’t that stupid.

If he had been the sort to make that choice, he wouldn’t have been left to become Acting Sect Leader. All of Chung Myung’s actions stemmed, when examined, from complete trust in Baek Cheon.

Without saying it outright, he was certain Baek Cheon would never make a foolish choice.

Chung Myung chuckled softly.

‘How long has it been?’

He realized how long it had been since he’d trusted someone not for their brilliance but for who they were, believing they’d know his intent and act accordingly without explanation.

A face came to his mind.

The face of the one who always scolded and nagged him, yet always believed in him.

“Ah, what nonsense. No chance.”

Smiling, Chung Myung raised his sword. It was bravado; the situation hardly allowed for leisure. Baek Cheon’s correct choice meant that those damned Ten Thousand People Clan would have no obstacle in targeting just him.

No matter who Chung Myung was, overcoming this situation wouldn’t be easy.

“Well, still…”

“Dieeee!”

He struck aside a Ten Thousand People Clan warrior’s blade with one blow and severed his neck in an instant. Hot blood spurted from the cut carotid. Leaping, Chung Myung deflected the vicious blades of enemies aiming for his vitals.

“That doesn’t mean I can’t say it’s not hard!”

If being surrounded meant death, or if the enemy focusing on him meant he should throw away his life, his past self would have needed twenty lives and still come up short.

Kraaaang!

He slammed the ground hard and red-clad foes rained down over his head like a storm.

“Plum Blossom Sword Ghost, don’t let him escape!”

“Sword Ghost?”

Crack.

Gripping his sword hilt until it nearly broke, Chung Myung unleashed his sword skyward. In an instant he swept aside the incoming spearpoints and tore through the exposed torsos of those before him.

“Call me Sword Saint, you fools. Don’t attach such disrespectful words to an elder. You’re dead in my hands. Pft!”

Spitting the blood filling his mouth, Chung Myung glanced back.

‘Not yet.’

A little more. He had to wait just a bit longer.

His goal was neither to escape from here nor to run away.

If he fled too quickly, the enemy forces would split. Then those left behind would choose to chase the main force rather than pursue him, whose capture was unlikely.

To prevent that, he had to lure as many as possible deep inward so they could never take their eyes off his back.

Then, in that gap, Mount Hua and Southern Island could cross the river. Across the river lay Shaolin and other sects tangled in a complex web, making pursuit difficult.

“Ha-hah…”

It was somewhat amusing.

To sacrifice the desire to save one more for the sake of many, that was the choice Chung Mun once forced on him. Chung Myung never forgave Chung Mun for making him choose, even to his dying day.

Though he still respected and cherished him, he could never untie that knot. Chung Mun probably lamented that fact until his last breath.

But now it was Chung Myung forcing that damned choice on Baek Cheon, just as Chung Mun had once forced on him.

‘If I meet sahyung in the afterlife, I’ll apologize.’

He had intended to complain first if they met again. Had he known it would come to this, he would have at least told him he understood when he went to cut off the Heavenly Demon’s head. Even saying that would have sufficed.

Would Baek Cheon resent him? As he had once resented Chung Mun?

Perhaps.

But he must endure. And he could endure. He was more than fit to become Mount Hua’s sect leader. He would have to make such choices countless times in the future.

Just like the great Chung Mun.

“Then what remains is…”

Chung Myung watched the enemies surge like a tide.

“Chase him! He’s alone! Give him no time to rest!”

“…You underestimate people.”

What’s so great about being alone.

Tang Bo is no longer at his back, but Chung Myung is firmer than his former self.

Chung Mun, who had tried to protect him, was gone, but he had become someone who could protect others on his own.

His past self could never grow up, but now he understood what mattered most.

“So there’s nothing surprising about it!”

Chung Myung’s sword sliced the air like an apparition. Drawing a Plum Blossom with its tip, he shredded the attackers in an instant and then surged forward like a snake slithering through the brush.

“Oooooo!”

Paaaah!

Black poisonous powder exploded before him.

Holding his breath for a moment, Chung Myung crouched lower and pushed through the poison.

No sooner had he cut through the black smoke than dozens of chained sickles flew in from all directions, but Chung Myung sliced them all with a single stroke.

For a moment, he felt the sensations of the past return to his body. He felt he could do anything, pierce anything. So this much…!

“So you were here, you idiot! Who let you fight alone?”

Eyes wide, Chung Myung snapped his head around.

But there was nothing where he turned.

Jo Gul charging with a cry, Baek Cheon glaring as if to kill, expressionless Yu Yiseol, tearful Tang Soso… none of them were there.

They were not there.

Chung Myung snorted a laugh.

Swoosh.

Kicking off the ground, Chung Myung shot forward at speed.

A single leaf hanging at the tip of a branch trembled in the slow breeze. Even as the wind died and the world settled back into quiet, the lone leaf continued to sway and sway.

Chapter 1389: Underestimating People. (4)

A terrible, throbbing pain pulsed in his thigh. The strike he had allowed a moment ago must have run deeper than he had thought.

Dizzy spells washed over him from time to time. He couldn’t tell whether it was from losing too much blood or the poison seeping into his wound, eating at his body. His breaths grew shallower.

“Huff! Huff! Huff!”

But he couldn’t stop now.

“Chase him!”

A harsh voice sounded not far away. The pursuers were merciless and cruel. If they caught him, he would suffer so much he’d beg them to just kill him.

It wasn’t the pain he feared. What he truly feared was ending his life like this, dying in a place like this, accomplishing nothing, holding nothing, crawling on the ground like a worm. He could not accept that.

Screeeeeeetch!

At that moment a stabbing pain shot through his calf.

“Ugh!”

The boy, losing his balance, tumbled to the ground with the momentum of his run. After bouncing and rolling several times, his body lay sprawled out.

Whoosh!

With a short tearing sound, several armed men landed in front of the boy.

“You bastard…”

Clad in blue, they glared at the boy with vicious, cruel eyes. Though he lay wounded all over and could have evoked pity, there was not a trace of it in their gazes. Their hatred boiled so hot it made even mortal enemies’ looks seem gentle by comparison.

But those revealing hatred toward the boy had their reasons.

“How dare you, not only steal the clan’s precious treasure without fear, but assassinate the clan leader while fleeing?”

“We’ll tear you apart a thousand ways to repay the sect master’s grudge.”

The boy before them looked at first like a heedless, weak child, but in truth he was a vicious demon to rival snake and scorpion.

He had ambushed the clan leader, who had especially favored him as a successor and bestowed boundless favor, while the clan leader was traveling, then fled after stealing the clan’s true relic. The number of people he killed while escaping reportedly reached into the tens.

“Even a mute dog knows what gratitude is. You’re worse than a dog! The clan leader cherished you so much!”

“Heh heh heh heh.”

The boy lying there chuckled in a suppressed voice, then slowly sat up.

“Gratitude…”

Between his blood-matted bangs, pale eyes shone—an eerie madness emanating from them.

“Does such a thing even exist in this world?”

“…What did you say?”

“That old man only raised me to make me his instrument. If I had no talent, would that ‘grace’ ever have come to me?”

“This bastard…”

“People are strange. They live without thinking of such things most of the time, but when it’s convenient to preach, they heap them up like they’re holy.”

The boy clenched his hand as if seizing the ground and slowly pushed himself fully upright.

“Fool. Even if that was the clan leader’s intention, if you’d calmly inherited the leader’s legacy you could’ve become one of the few top warriors. You threw that chance away yourself. And for that you’ll die miserably.”

“A top warrior… by becoming that old man’s disciple?”

The boy laughed brightly as if incredulous. Sharp mockery and overt self-mockery mingled in it—hardly a laugh one would expect from a child.

A ‘top warrior.’ What would change by making a name in some backwater like this?

A pitiful life, a pitiful reputation.

How worthless is a life spent scrambling for such things?

Some might be satisfied with that. But not the boy. A life that cannot ascend was worse than a miserable death.

“Not just the clan leader! The whole clan had pinned their hopes on you. If it was you…”

“Hopes? For what, exactly?”

The boy sneered as he looked at the man.

“If I become great, will your lives change?”

“Y-you bastard!”

“Would that change your worth? You’re useless lives anyway.”

The man foamed at the mouth with rage. Then the boy’s voice, sharp, slipped out.

“The clan? Hopes? It’s because you lean on such things that you live like that. The only thing you can trust is yourself! If you want to change, if you want to obtain, the only thing you must train and abuse is yourself. If the unqualified grasp what’s beyond them, all that’s left is misery.”

“…”

“I won’t live like that. If I want something, I’ll take it with my own hands, and I’ll bear the sacrifices that come with it myself.”

Childish bravado or the arrogance of someone naive of the world… could such words describe the boy?

There was a venom in the child’s voice that even those older than him would find hard to endure. Its source was beyond guessing.

“…So it was you.”

Then a man’s voice came out with a hoarse sound, as if he’d finally realized something.

“The one who killed Caoling Clan’s young clan leader and stole their secret technique… the one who killed Evil Tryant Clan’s maiden and took their sacred relic while fleeing… it was all you, wasn’t it?”

At those words everyone was shocked and looked at the man who spoke.

“Th-then all the incidents that occurred in the various sects over the past few years…?”

“…Impossible. There’s no way they were that stupid, right?”

“Were we fools who fell for it?”

Those words shut everyone up.

No, they weren’t stupid. They could never be stupid.

Enlightened Way clan was a clan known in the north area as if it were a reaper; if its leaders had truly been foolish, how could they have attained such stature?

“We’re not foolish. That bastard, he’s simply too cunning. He deceived everyone in such a short time without a trace.”

The men bit their lips. Someone muttered with a face of contempt.

“Worse than a dog.”

“Heh heh…”

The boy lazily wiped the blood trailing down his face. The red smeared along his mouth like exaggerated stage makeup. At a glance he looked like a madman grinning wide.

That alone changed his impression completely. The previously innocent-looking face had turned into that of a depraved demon.

“Is that really my fault?”

“What other nonsense are you going to spew…!”

“Do you have any idea? What I’m thinking now? With what thoughts I’m looking at you?”

The man’s face contorted—he couldn’t grasp what that cunning bastard was saying.

“You don’t know. Of course you don’t. One cannot fathom another’s heart or intentions. Standing just a pace away, glaring as if in hatred, you still couldn’t know.”

The men’s eyes twitched. The boy smiled.

“But what nerve did you have to trust a person whose inner heart you couldn’t even know? And why do you rage that you’ve been betrayed? Is the trust you gave something that must be returned? If not, does that grant you the right to blame and curse?”

“What sophistry…”

“If that’s the case, it’s utterly shallow.”

Perhaps this wasn’t mere mockery but a genuine question.

The boy could not understand.

Those who relied on others, who pinned hopes on others, even those who believed others would act as they wished.

To the boy, it was like throwing oneself into a pitch-black well. A madman’s wager, betting everything on groundless optimism and faith without knowing what monsters or abysses lurked within.

They might deem him a madman, but to the boy the world was madder, filled only with the unhinged.

To the boy, watching such a world was both revolting and indescribably beautiful. It glittered.

So he wanted to clutch it in his hand, the world full of things he could never permanently possess.

“Say whatever you like. We will exact the price for what you’ve done.”

“You lot?”

“I’ll give you courage. But kid, you know nothing of the world. And thanks to you everyone will learn how cruel and thorough Enlightened Way Clan’s vengeance can be!”

Hearing that, the boy scoffed.

Of course his body was in the worst state. Other pursuers were coming after him in the distance. Yet the boy felt no loneliness or isolation.

“This is why the dull-minded…”

“What did you say?”

“You blurted it yourself. The one who stole Caoling Clan’s secret technique scroll and the one who stole Evil Tyrant Clan’s treasure, both were me, you said.”

Those who heard it flinched.

“Then what do you think I did with them? Did you think I sold them off for petty coin?”

“S-seize him at once…”

“Now do you understand? I didn’t flee because I feared you. I fled because I didn’t want my name to spread here. You should’ve known!”

A perverse madness burst from the boy’s eyes.

A crazed shout echoed from somewhere.

A killing intent felt even from afar, like something slicing the skin.

It felt as if the whole world spun violently and screamed to seize and kill that one thing.

But it didn’t matter. The world had been like this from the start. He did not want it to be different either.

The moment he trampled this filthy world beneath his feet, he would finally find the reason he was born into it.

Even if he were torn to pieces along that path, leaving not a scrap of flesh behind, he would have no regrets.

He would simply laugh once and go to hell.

“Strike!”

Watching his attackers rush in, the boy, Jang Ilso, grinned with narrowed eyes.

‘I will seize it.’

All things he could not obtain.

Jang Ilso slowly opened his eyes and, with a languid face, gazed out the window.

‘Rare occurrence.’

He rarely had dreams, especially not that dream of rolling in misery.

Lost in thought, Jang Ilso suddenly wanted to ask himself a question.

Had he obtained what he sought?

No. Not yet. He hadn’t grasped a single thing.

The power others envied, the soaring fame, the wealth that could never be exhausted even in a lifetime—these remained trivial to him.

None of them were what he truly wanted to obtain.

But Jang Ilso was certain. If he saw this path through, everything would soon fall into his hands. And after that…

“…Who knows.”

Jang Ilso murmured with a languid face.

“What comes after is none of my concern. Isn’t that right?”

Most people wouldn’t understand.

For some, it’s not the life they will gain by achieving a goal but the very fact of achieving the goal that matters more.

Perhaps the one who would understand is writhing now in a hellish situation, just as the past Jang Ilso once did.

And they would probably feel exactly what past Jang Ilso felt.

Jang Ilso survived by abandoning everything. But could he do that as well?

“Either way is fine.”

A seemingly benevolent smile flickered across his face. Yet his red lips twisted, exuding a chilling aura.

“It wouldn’t be bad if you came this way.”

Then perhaps Jang Ilso might gain it.

The one and only person in the world who could understand him.

Chapter 1390: Underestimating People. (5)

His gaze, following his slowly bowed head, fell upon his own hand.

The hand holding the sword. That hand trembled.

‘Has my stamina run out?’

No. It cannot be.

He had rested enough. No matter how endlessly enemies surged, his endurance wouldn’t be drained so easily.

The purest internal qi he had gathered gave him greater stamina than in the past.

Yet the limit he felt was probably not of the body but of the mind wearing thin and collapsing.

Why?

That was the part he couldn’t understand.

In the past, Chung Myung never broke. Even after facing worse situations many times, he always prevailed and always overcame them.

In that process he had met the body’s limits many times, but he had never once confronted the limits of his spirit.

So why now?

“There!”

Enemies sprang out of the bushes by the dozens. With his gaze cooling like a blade in an instant, Chung Myung actually charged at the attackers.

Stabbing a fleeing man’s back is easiest of all. If you truly want to face an enemy, do something they cannot predict.

Just like now.

“Aaaaaagh!”

Perhaps they never expected Chung Myung to charge them; the floundering attackers screamed and fell. Blood spurted red, and there were harrowing screams.

Those two things would draw more enemies to this place.

Or perhaps they already had.

Chung Myung’s blade-like gaze turned to his rear. He felt enemy forces flanking and encircling his back, more instinct than sense.

Fwoooom!

Chung Myung pushed off the ground and moved toward the rear like lightning.

“Dieeeeee!”

The enemies, as if waiting, covered his entire sight and surged forward.

In the time it took for him to throw off a few slashes, those who could seize his back were predictably skilled.

Razor-sharp attacks flew at his front, intent on slicing his flesh, breaking bones, and slaughtering his soul.

Clang-clang-clang!

Chung Myung’s sword, like lightning, knocked away each incoming weapon. The sparks from countless metal clashes lit the dark forest for a moment.

In that light cut out of the dark, the death-delivering sword sank into an enemy’s neck.

Screee! Screee! Screee!

Chung Myung, who had severed three necks at once, plunged into the fallen without even checking if their foes were dead.

The sword that cloaked Chung Myung’s body swung as if exploding, drawing a blood-red arc around him.

Fwoooaaah!

With an ear-splitting tearing sound, those who had rushed him collapsed to the ground as bodies gutted at the waist.

“Huk!”

A ragged breath escaped Chung Myung’s lips.

But there was no time to catch his breath. He pushed off the ground and surged forward. Spears that had been hurled at where he stood stuck into the earth in quick succession.

You have acquired everything far too easily. Things that were so difficult for others must have been so simple for you.

Something flashed from all sides.

Swords and spears. Heavy broadswords and even scythes hung with long chains.

Those countless weapons flew in, aiming only at Chung Myung.

People regard you as remarkable and also fear you. Perhaps you feel the same.

Chung Myung bit his lip until it bled.

“Aaaaaaah!”

Plum Blossoms burst like an explosion around his body. The red plum blossoms spreading in an instant resembled droplets of blood erupting from wounds more than petals.

But Chung Myung, you did not earn these yourself. Perhaps all of it was simply given. Held in your hands regardless of your will.

‘So?’

Chung Myung’s eyes burned with fury.

What of it? So what am I supposed to do? What does that matter?

Kwaaang!

He slammed the ground as if to break it and surged forward. There was only one life-and-death way: forward. The place that blocked him most thickly.

Without an inch of hesitation, Chung Myung rushed the enemies and brought his sword down with an indescribable fury.

Kaaang!

The heavy broadsword that blocked his strike was severed at once. Before the wielder could fully open his eyes, Chung Myung’s Dark Fragrant Plum Blossom Sword sliced his body vertically.

The body split in two and sprayed blood over Chung Myung. A scorching heat as if flesh were burning. The screams of severed life. All of it piled upon the tip of his sword.

That is why I want you to know the weight of it. The value of something earned through one’s own effort and paid-for price.

“Me!”

I did not take it without doing anything!

It may look that way to you! But I— at least I!

But Chung Myung.

At that moment, Chung Myung’s sword trembled.

On the other hand, I am afraid. One day, when you gain something truly valuable, and understand the meaning of something not given freely but forged by your own hands.

His sword sliced the air.

His sword that cut throats and pierced hearts remained unchanged in a single way.

Just…

Whether you can remain as you are now. Whether you can keep living as you do now.

Kwaduk!

The edge of a broken broadsword embedded in his shoulder. The vivid sensation of thick steel cutting into flesh. In that chilling pain, Chung Myung took a step.

‘Sect leader sahyung.’

Chung Myung bit his lip.

Chung Mun was always like that. Chung Myung liked him. But he couldn’t quite grasp what Chung Mun’s words truly meant.

Because they looked toward very different places.

‘You truly wanted me to…’

Paaaah!

Chung Myung’s sword spun like a top and stabbed in all directions.

“Kraaaaagh!”

“Aaaaagh!”

Those charging found wind-holes blown through their bodies and tumbled. Amid blood bursting like fireworks, Chung Myung absentmindedly looked up at the sky.

‘You wanted me to grow up?’

He seemed to understand. Only now did he understand.

Chung Mun had always told him to see more. To understand more, and to stand where those unlike him stood.

Always. Yes, always.

But only standing there now could Chung Myung know Chung Mun’s true heart.

He always told Chung Myung to understand others, but perhaps he wished that Chung Myung would never stand among them.

Because only Chung Mun knew what burdens someone like Chung Myung would carry if he truly understood others.

Tao is the cutting away of oneself.

Because Chung Mun had walked that path before anyone else and knew how harsh and painful it was, perhaps he wished that Chung Myung would never walk the same path as he did.

Even if that went against the duty of a Taoist.

“Huh…”

A life that puts others before oneself.

Chung Mun walked that path his whole life. Others came before him; he prioritized others over his own sect.

If sacrifice was needed, he sacrificed himself first; if he had to make sacrifices, he sacrificed those he cherished most.

‘With what kind of heart… did you endure that? Sahyung?’

He could not know.

But he felt he might.

“There!”

The entire forest was suddenly covered with murderous intent. Facing that onslaught, Chung Myung’s hand trembled once more.

‘Ah…’

In that moment, Chung Myung understood.

Why his hands kept shaking. Why he faced a limit he hadn’t felt before.

‘Right now I…’

Chung Myung’s gaze cooled, colder than ever.

‘I’m afraid.’

He had never thought himself afraid of death.

He believed that even if he were to die, doing what must be done was enough. Life was not something he held as supremely valuable.

What he feared was not death itself but an unfinished, half-done end, nothing more.

But now, for the first time, Chung Myung keenly felt the fear of dying.

He might never meet them again.

Those he knew.

He couldn’t return to places that were so familiar, couldn’t chuckle at silly jokes. Turning his head, the faces that should have been there would be gone.

A place where everything is unchanged.

Except Chung Myung himself no longer exists there.

Chung Myung clamped his mouth shut.

Suddenly nausea surged. Even though there was nothing in his stomach, his body kept demanding to vomit.

Why did he try to protect everyone?

Yes. He was afraid. Afraid that a void would open in the precious bonds he had created.

The name Baek Cheon, the name Yu Yiseol, the name Yoon Jong and the name Jo Gul, the name Tang Soso, and the name Hae Yeon disappearing from their world.

He was more terrified than anything that, upon suddenly turning his head, an emptiness where they did not exist would face him.

No one could feel more keenly how terrifying the absence of those who once existed could be.

But….

Is that really all right?

Does their existence make everything good? If Chung Myung isn’t there, what meaning would that have?

The moment Chung Myung disappears, it’s as if everything disappears. Shouldn’t he survive for that reason?

Wouldn’t it be preferable that he lose some rather than lose everything himself?

It’s not too late now.

With his current condition, he could break out of this encirclement. If he ran forward without looking back, he could survive.

Even if, as a consequence, the majority here chased after Mount Hua and the Southern Island sect, even if the Southern Island Sect were annihilated and some faces he knew were never seen again, he himself would live…

“Uweeeeeeek!”

Rivulets of red blood streamed from Chung Myung’s mouth.

Chung Myung looked with blurred eyes at the blood smeared on the hand that covered his mouth and let out a short, low laugh.

“…The heart demon has come, and it’s come filthy.”

Yes. He’s afraid.

He is terribly afraid of dying.

What terrifies him more is dying alone again with no hand to reach out, feeling once more the vivid loneliness of dying alone on the Ten-Thousand Great Mountains.

Enemies surged at him like a black wave.

“Hey, sahyung. Are you watching?”

Chung Myung looked up toward the sky.

“Come to think of it, I’ve never given you what you wanted, sahyung.”

Chung Myung gripped his sword.

His feet dug into the ground, as if to bind the foot that would naturally fall.

“So… don’t expect anything. That’s how I am by nature.”

The enemies closed in.

His trembling hand remained unsteady, and his heart still howled.

Nevertheless, Chung Myung held his ground.

The deep regret still lodged within him would link to what he protected. It mattered not even if he did not exist where past and future met.

Will is not something stitched together by life alone.

Fwoooaaah!

The cold-bloodedness honed and honed again became a sword and pierced the air.

It was the sword of a Sword Ghost and also the sword of a Sword Saint.

Chapter 1391: He Will Be Waiting For Us. (1)

“Faster!”

Baek Cheon kept barking orders without pause.

Even though speed alone wouldn’t solve their situation, Baek Cheon kept shouting as if enemies were poised to stab them in the back.

“More!”

But the group’s pace would not quicken.

“Strike them in one blow!”

The enemies they ran into along the way? Of course those slowed them down.

“Sasuk! Enemies are on our tail!”

“Damn it, I’ll go myself!”

The Ten Thousand People Clan that clung back the moment they thought they’d shaken them off? Naturally they were impacting things. In combat their speed inevitably slowed, especially for those running with the resolve to allow not a single person to fall behind.

But those who understood knew the lack of speed wasn’t only due to that.

“Done now! Move!”

After instantly annihilating the enemies with Namgung Do-Wei, Baek Cheon shouted in a thundering voice. But Tang Pae, hearing it, did not surge forward—he instead turned back.

Behind them there was nothing. The place they had already passed.

“What are you doing! Young lord.”

“…I’m sorry.”

Tang Pae gritted his teeth and looked forward. It was as if a creak sounded in his throat, like forcibly snapping something that could not be returned to.

His gaze kept drifting backward.

As if he had left behind someone he should never have left.

‘Why hasn’t he joined us yet?’

The closer they got to the Yangtze river, the more real it became: the distance between them and Chung Myung was widening drastically.

And they sensed it.

He wasn’t an idiot either, so he understood what it meant that they hadn’t seen the enemy main force or Chung Myung at all.

They were getting to safety.

This hellish journey south had finally reached its end. The Yangtze was within reach; if they could just avoid falling into the enemy’s trap in that short span, they would reach the river without major trouble.

But that fact made Tang Pae more uneasy.

“Green Forest King.”

“…”

Tang Pae, who had struck the ground, turned his head to look at Im So-Byeong, whose face was expressionless as if armored.

“Is there no one blocking the front?”

“…”

“Isn’t it strange? If they’ve driven us here they’d surely try to encircle us. Aren’t more than half of the Ten Thousand People Clan’s forces still by the Yangtze? What are they doing now…?”

“You have quite the temper.”

Im So-Byeong’s indifferent voice came out—an odd mismatch with his grave expression, which made the words ring even clearer.

“Why ask about what you already know? Are you trying to confirm it through my mouth because you’re unsure, or because you want to claim you didn’t know?”

The insult was full of venom, yet Tang Pae stayed silent, feeling the cold edge in those words wasn’t necessarily aimed at him.

Seeing Tang Pae’s reaction, Im So-Byeong lightly bit his lip.

“Where do you think they went?”

“…”

“Let’s not ask the obvious. There’s nothing we can do.”

What Tang Pae felt now was a weight hard to describe.

But perhaps Im So-Byeong felt beyond heaviness—he might have felt despair.

A soldier’s role is to find the best option within limited circumstances. The moment he fails to do that, a soldier becomes utterly worthless.

In that statement that there was nothing they could do, Im So-Byeong’s despair was fully contained.

“…They went to the Taoist, then.”

“…”

A touch of irritation crossed Im So-Byeong’s face. What would saying that out loud change?

Having seen Im So-Byeong’s expression, Tang Pae turned forward. He saw Mount Hua’s disciples fiercely cutting through the enemies.

‘Wouldn’t they know too?’

Why they were heading so easily to the Yangtze, and where the enemies were gathering.

How could they fight without ever looking back?

Even he, who did not share a roof with Mount Hua’s Sword Saint, felt as if someone kept tugging him from behind, forcing him to look back despite himself. What state of mind must they be holding their swords with?

He couldn’t even bring himself to guess.

“Tang Young lord.”

At that moment someone called Tang Pae. Turning, he saw Gwak Hwan-So had come up beside him, looking at him with hard eyes.

“Is it true that Mount Hua’s Sword Saint is luring the enemies?”

“…I’m not certain.”

Tang Pae couldn’t bring himself to answer that way.

Any answer would be painful from the Southern Island Sect’s perspective. These were not shameless or foolish people; they would feel keenly that Chung Myung was taking excessive risks.

How could he drive a knife into the faces of such people?

“Soon… probably soon they’ll join. Chung Myung Taoist has always been like that. It’s hard to judge him by a common person’s measure.”

Tang Pae said that much and turned his gaze away.

Because continuing would have been impossible.

“…”

Gwak Hwan-So seemed to sense Tang Pae was lying. He parted his lips as if to speak, then closed them.

“Hurry up!”

Baek Cheon’s strong voice rang at the right time, wiping away their lingering thoughts.

“Let’s go.”

“Yes.”

Now was not the time to let such thoughts slow their steps.

Even with worries and doubts, they had to bury them and follow Baek Cheon’s orders.

Because that was Chung Myung’s will—staying behind in danger to save them.

‘But…’

Tang Pae shifted his gaze and slowly scanned the surroundings.

Putting aside the faces of Mount Hua’s disciples, whose backs were all that could be seen, even Namgung Do-Wei, Hae Yeon, and Southern Island Sect’s disciples had faces set unyieldingly hard.

‘Taoist. Is this really what you wanted?’

Perhaps all here might survive without sacrifice.

But even if they survived, could they accept that survival honorably? Could they simply affirm the lives gained at the cost of sacrificing the one who should never have died?

If Chung Myung truly thought that way, Tang Pae would hate him.

If he had thought Chung Myung was a person with even a shred of decency, he couldn’t make that judgment. None of them would want to save their lives at the price of Chung Myung’s sacrifice.

‘Then why…’

Tang Pae bit his lip.

He was asking, but he knew. In the end there would be no choice.

Between the path that endangered himself and the path that endangered others—if those were the only two options, Chung Myung would naturally choose the one that put himself at risk.

He had always made that choice.

That’s why everyone followed him. That’s why everyone liked him.

But the way Chung Myung had always led them was now producing the worst result. As if it were a price he—and they—would inevitably have to pay someday.

So they could only resent him.

At least no one wanted this worst outcome. No one.

And then.

“Ah…”

A sight like a lie entered Tang Pae’s eyes—the end of a road that seemed endless, the end of a vast forest that seemed to stretch forever.

Over the low ridge the land unfolded, and its far edge was dyed a darker, deep black than the sky.

Those who recognized the black line’s nature stopped one by one, unable to contain the tremor running through them.

A voice, as if in a trance, quietly flowed through them.

“Yangtze… it’s the Yangtze.”

Yangtze river.

The great river dividing the Central Plains’ south and north—and the place they had so longed to see.

The Yangtze lay spread before their eyes.

Their legs went weak. Even Tang Pae, who hadn’t expected to falter, had to steady himself.

How to name this feeling?

Relief? Collapse? Or just plain joy?

It was impossible to define that complex swirl of emotion in a single word.

In that deep feeling, Tang Pae and the others around him only trembled.

“Ugh.”

At that moment, in front of Tang Pae, Lee Ja-Yang lowered his head.

“…Ja-Yang.”

Gwak Hwan-So put a hand on Ja-Yang’s shoulder. Ja-Yang opened his mouth in a choking voice.

“Honestly…”

Ja-Yang swallowed down something boiling inside, and a squeezed voice escaped.

“I honestly didn’t think… I’d live to see this sight… alive.”

Those words voiced the feelings of all the Southern Island Sect’s disciples.

Only ten of them.

Only ten had come to rescue them.

Who could dare think to break through the south trusting just ten? They had stepped into the southern lands determined they would not die a pitiful death even if it cost them their lives.

Yet after countless sacrifices and a hellish path, they had finally reached this place.

Of course it wasn’t over yet.

Crossing that river would not be easy either; in the process they might all lose their lives.

But even knowing that, the emotion of seeing the Yangtze with their own eyes was irresistible.

“…Now it’s truly one step away.”

“Yes, sahyung.”

Ja-Yang nodded at Gwak Hwan-So’s words.

“Now it’s really soon.”

Without dwelling on the dangers remaining by the river, Gwak Hwan-So and Ja-Yang steadied each other’s spirits. Hearing that, Southern Island Sect’s disciples set their faces firmly.

And at the same time, Baek Cheon, who had only looked ahead until now, turned his head.

With an icy expression, his face as hard as if encased in iron, Baek Cheon looked at Gwak Hwan-So.

“Warrior Gwak.”

“Yes, Acting Sect Leader.”

Gwak Hwan-So met Baek Cheon’s gaze squarely.

If Baek Cheon told him to leap into the flames, he would. If asked to give his life, he would. Baek Cheon deserved that.

“There is the Yangtze. Once we cross it lies Northern lands. When we reach the Northern lands, whether the Heavenly Friends Alliance or the Nine Great Sects, someone will come to meet Southern Island sect.”

Gwak Hwan-So’s eyebrow twitched slightly.

It wasn’t displeasure—just curiosity about Baek Cheon’s intention. The Baek Cheon he knew didn’t bother stating the obvious.

“By Mount Hua’s grace…”

“Warrior.”

Baek Cheon folded his hands in a formal salute and bowed deeply. Startled by the sudden courtesy, Gwak Hwan-So blinked and looked at him.

“Mount Hua’s help to you ends here.”

“…Sorry?”

Gwak Hwan-So echoed with a stunned expression.

What on earth did that mean?

“The Yangtze’s defenses won’t be as strong as you think. Cross the river before dawn. That should keep the Southern Island Sect safe.”

“W-wait. Acting Sect Leader. What do you mean? Cross the Yangtze? Then what about Mount Hua?”

Baek Cheon lifted his head and met Gwak Hwan-So’s eyes.

That gaze.

The unwavering look told Gwak Hwan-So immediately what Baek Cheon meant.

“You’re asking the obvious.”

Mount Hua’s disciples stepped up beside Baek Cheon. Their eyes matched his without a flicker.

“We are going to fetch that foolish sajil.”

“Now, Acting Sect…”

“I’m sure he’ll be waiting for us too.”

Baek Cheon’s voice came out resolutely.

“He will be waiting for us.”

Chapter 1392: He Will Be Waiting For Us. (2)

His breath caught.

“They’re going to rescue them?”

Mount Hua’s Sword Saint?

No, thinking about it, it’s not a surprising choice. If you consider who they are,

it might be the most natural thing. But………..

“Acting Sect Leader.”

Gwak Hwan-So forced his trembling voice under control and forced himself to speak.

“As you know…….. our rear is currently overflowing with the Ten Thousand People Clan chasing us. Going now to rescue Chung Myung’s Taoist is not much different from saying you’ll break through them.”

Baek Cheon silently looked at Gwak Hwan-So.

“I know Mount Hua is strong. It can’t be compared to us. But…..”

Gwak Hwan-So trailed off. But everyone could predict what would follow.

This is nothing less than a suicide mission.

They had done everything they could so far to escape the clutches of the Ten Thousand People Clan.

Tormented by the fear that one day those men might stab them in the back, they finally made it here.

And now they were going to throw themselves back into the arms of those they had been fleeing from? To save only one person?

‘They’re insane.’

Can this really be dismissed as chivalry or courage?

A truly brave person might risk their life and run into a burning house to save a crying child.

That first act can be called courage. But if someone who already lost an arm then runs back into the flames to save another child, can you still call that courage?

Even though they’re running into a place that has become many times more dangerous?

That’s not courage.

Those who do such things are simply people with something broken inside them.

However, among Mount Hua’s disciples discussing that insane action, not a single face showed hesitation or fear.

Their faces treated that most natural thing as natural. That sight stirred indescribable emotions in Gwak Hwan-So.

“I truly apologize to the people of the Southern Island Sect.”

“Wait, Acting Sect Leader.”

“But as I said, by now Mount Hua has fulfilled its duty to you.”

Gwak Hwan-So unknowingly nodded.

In truth, ‘this much’ is an understatement. What Mount Hua had given them was excessive to the point of overflowing.

Even if they had abandoned them halfway to the Southern Island, Gwak Hwan-So could not have blamed them.

“Expecting more from Mount Hua would be beyond shameless; it would be inhuman. But Acting Sect Leader…… are you really alright with this?”

He already knew the answer.

Because the response from someone with that look was obvious.

But the answer he heard began a little differently than expected.

“Well, I don’t know. In truth, as Mount Hua’s Acting Sect Leader, my duty ends here.”

“Huh?”

“What remains is not me as Mount Hua’s Acting Sect Leader, but Baek Cheon, the sect’s great disciple. So I will simply do what I want to do.”

“Oh, no, then the others……”

“They’ll do as they see fit. I don’t intend to force anyone.”

Gwak Hwan-So opened his mouth without meaning to. Baek Cheon’s mood was somehow different from before.

There was a strange expression on that obedient face.

“Those who want to follow will follow, and those who remain will remain. That’s not something I should be worrying about, is it?”

“Ah, no……”

At that moment, Jo Gul spoke up, sounding irritated.

“Do you have that much time to speak? There’s no time.”

Gwak Hwan-So turned to Jo Gul with a look of incredulity, and Yoon Jong rebuked Jo Gul for his words.

“Doesn’t he have things to do as the Acting Sect Leader?”

“Then will you wait here for him?”

“No, so just leave him and go.”

“Wow…. I hadn’t thought of that.”

No sooner had he finished than Yu Yiseol strode over and stood beside Yoon Jong.

“Arrogant.”

“Guh, Sago, that’s not what I meant……..”

“I can’t. I’ll lead them.”

“………..Yes.”

Tang Soso also stood beside Yu Yiseol with a stern face, and the silent Hae Yeon naturally stood at Jo Gul’s side.

As if to show that they were going to Chung Myung not because of Baek Cheon’s orders.

‘What the hell……..’

Gwak Hwan-So was left speechless by that absurd sight, and Baek Cheon spoke with a nonchalant face.

“See. They’re not the kind to listen to whatever I say. We should be grateful they’ve come this far quietly.”

“If only it weren’t for the sasuk.”

“If only it weren’t for the Acting Sect Leader.”

“If only it weren’t for Dong-Ryong.”

“Who just said that, you bastard?”

When Baek Cheon shouted, everyone pretended not to hear as they turned away.

A hollow laugh escaped Gwak Hwan-So’s lips.

They were now about to head into a battlefield that was like hell.

A place where the chance of death was many times higher than survival. Yet there was no look of burden or tension on them.

No, they couldn’t lack burden and tension. But something more important than that existed.

‘So were they planning this from the start?’

Is that why they fought so fiercely and sped up? To lead them to the safest possible place and then turn back?

“If you have nothing more to say, let’s depart.”

Gwak Hwan-So turned his head toward the voice.

Namgung Family’s young lord, Namgung Do-Wei. He too, as if it were the natural choice, had already turned his body.

With Tang Family’s young lord Tang Pae.

The ones remaining were…….

“I’ll make it clear in advance.”

Green Forest King Im So-Byeong said to them in a cold tone.

“This goes against the will of Taoist Chung Myung.”

At those words, everyone’s expression hardened slightly.

“Taoist Chung Myung would not want you to do this madness. Even if a miraculous event happens and you break through hell to meet again, you will only be met with curses, and it’s more likely the ending will be you holding hands and dying together.”

“……….”

“Will you go anyway?”

At that, Baek Cheon scratched at his ear.

With an unusually rough-looking face, he replied indifferently.

“What are you gonna do about it?”

“……Huh?”

The Green Forest King blinked. He truly had not expected that answer even a bit.

“So what are you going to do about it?”

“…………”

“It seems the Green Forest King doesn’t quite understand. Take this opportunity to learn.”

“What are you saying?”

“I’m the sasuk, I am!”

“……Huh?”

“But why should I listen to that bastard? That lowborn bastard with no standing! It’s not that I have to obey him; that bastard should obey me! Do you understand?”

“That’s right.”

“Exactly! I’m the sahyung!”

“I agree. I’m the sajae, though.”

Im So-Byeong’s mouth trembled.

“Do you all think the same?”

Namgung Do-Wei and Tang Pae shrugged at that look.

“We know it’s unrealistic. If you think about gains and losses, it would be right to give up cleanly.”

“A reasonable statement.”

“But isn’t friendship a relationship that doesn’t weigh those things?”

Namgung Do-Wei said firmly.

“That’s why it’s the Heavenly Friends Alliance. If we were to discuss gains and losses, would I even be here? We would have died on Plum Blossom Island. Yet Taoist Chung Myung came to save Namgung family. At that moment we had already become friends. So now it’s my turn to risk my life for him.”

“………..”

“Tang Young lord, do you feel the same?”

“If I abandon Taoist Chung Myung and go back, I’ll die at my father’s hand anyway.”

“……….”

“This route will likely be the slightly less painful way to die.”

At those words, several nodded. The thought of an angry Tang Gunak is frightening as if from a nightmare.

“Madmen.”

“So, Green Forest King, what will you do?”

“Tsk tsk, such pitiful talk.”

Im So-Byeong took a fan from his robes.

Seeing that, some whispered.

‘How many fans does he carry around?’

‘Seems like each has its own purpose?’

Im So-Byeong opened the fan wide, half-covering his face, and spoke.

“If that man dies, Green Forest will certainly be left in the cold, so it’s better business to have a debt wiped off while we have the chance.”

“……I see why Green Forest is poor.”

“You can’t calculate. Calculate.”

“Quiet!”

Baek Cheon snorted at Im So-Byeong’s annoyed reaction and turned toward Gwak Hwan-So.

“Please understand.”

As Baek Cheon made his plea, Gwak Hwan-So hesitated for a moment and then spoke in a suppressed voice.

“Acting Sect Leader. If that’s truly the case……..”

“No.”

Baek Cheon cut him off as if he could guess what Gwak Hwan-So was about to say.

“That won’t do. Returning now to lead Southern Island Sect back would make all we’ve done up to now look like foolishness.”

“…….”

“It’s not something numbers alone can fix. So don’t worry and go. And make sure you survive. Only then will everything we’ve done not be for nothing.”

Im So-Byeong nodded.

“The Southern Island Sect heading straight for the Yangtze river to draw their attention will be more helpful.”

“See?”

The tension drained from Gwak Hwan-So’s shoulders.

They were right. True, but………

“Then at least let me…..”

Baek Cheon smiled faintly and shook his head.

“That won’t do either. Warrior Gwak must lead the Southern Island Sect. You know that, don’t you?”

The difference between Southern Island Sect with Gwak Hwan-So and without him would be vast. So even if they understood his feelings, they couldn’t take him along.

Unable to do anything, Gwak Hwan-So clenched his fist, and Baek Cheon laughed and patted his shoulder.

“Don’t worry. When we meet again in the Northern Land, we’ll make you pay back with interest.”

At that, Gwak Hwan-So finally shook his head.

These guys were fools. Incorrigible fools.

He couldn’t tell. Whether fools unusually gathered around the man named Chung Myung, or that man turned those around him into fools.

But now Gwak Hwan-So found himself wanting to be one of those fools.

“Then.”

Baek Cheon turned his body away from the Southern Island Sect. Leaving not a shred of regret.

His gaze landed not on his companions, but on the path they had to break through.

No, on someone who would be at the end of that path.

“Ready?”

“What do you mean, suddenly.”

“Yeah.”

Baek Cheon snorted. It was sudden indeed.

Sssshhh.

Baek Cheon drew his sword from its sheath and gripped the Plum Blossom Sword tightly in his hand.

“Those who fall behind will be left.”

“Sasuk, don’t get tired.”

“Damn bastards.”

Gritting his teeth, Baek Cheon ran forward without another word.

Around him, comrades like shafts of light followed.

“Let’s go! At that damned bastard!”

“Yes!”

The members of the Heavenly Friends Alliance charged forward with all their might.

And Gwak Hwan-So, staring vacantly at their backs, gritted his teeth.

He wanted to be there with them. But he couldn’t go. He had people he had to protect.

So…….

“Sahyung, I’ll go and come back.”

Gwak Hwan-So turned his head.

“Ja-Yang?”

Lee Ja-Yang said bluntly with a nonchalant face.

“The Southern Island Sect has its pride. At the very least, we mustn’t have the story spread that only they returned alive while we left our benefactor behind.”

“……..”

“Even if I die, it’s better that I die than you, Sahyung. So there.”

“J-Ja-Yang!”

Lee Ja-Yang didn’t wait for Gwak Hwan-So’s permission and ran after those who had gone ahead.

Gwak Hwan-So, who had reached out toward that retreating figure, slowly lowered the awkward fist that had only grasped air.

“……..Damn it.”

A hollow voice resonated with a deep sigh.

Chapter 1393: He Will Be Waiting For Us. (3)

“Do not slow down! Anyone who falls behind dies by my hand!”

Ho Ga-Myung’s aide, Jong Gwak, shouted until his throat gave out, but their speed barely increased.

Jong Gwak glanced around with a slightly anxious face.

Could his orders be going unheeded?

No, that wasn’t it. They too were running with every last ounce of strength, but their legs could no longer bear them.

‘It’s only natural.’

It was to be expected. From Guangdong to here they had run without proper rest.

Even the finest of warhorses would collapse under such relentless marching; how could human bodies not be exhausted?

It’s natural that those pursued by the Southern Island Sect men felt pressure, but the pursuers were equally desperate.

Is it because they are more numerous?

Being more numerous isn’t necessarily an advantage for the hunters. These were disciples of the Nine Great Sects, whose average skill made comparison with the Ten Thousand People Clan’s ordinary disciples meaningless.

If both sides had been at full strength, no matter how hard they tried they couldn’t have kept up. It was only because the pursuers had injured men and were exhausted from continuous fighting that they could barely follow.

Jong Gwak bit his lip slightly.

‘No whining. If I am tired, they’re more tired!’

Moreover, Jong Gwak’s mission wasn’t to chase them down and slaughter them. It was merely to pressure them so that the commander could get what he wanted.

He couldn’t afford to complain that even that was too difficult.

“We must cut them off before they reach the Yangtze river!”

Jong Gwak shouted at the top of his lungs.

Hearing that, the men’s eyes blazed. What drove them now was half hatred; a grudge whose origin none could even recall.

Jong Gwak clenched and unclenched his fist.

‘They’re the enemy, but…’

He could never have imagined they’d break that encirclement and reach this place. If they had been the incompetent Nine Great Sects disciples he knew until now, it would’ve been impossible.

But they had done it. Even if they hadn’t yet breached the Yangtze river, the fact they’d reached here while conserving half their strength left no doubt about their ability.

But even that was about to end.

The one who made such an impossible miracle real the fate of the Plum Blossom Sword Ghost was about to reach its end.

Even if they all escaped alive to Northern lands, if they could strike down the Plum Blossom Sword Ghost, the battle would be theirs.

‘I never thought I’d value a single head more than an entire sect of the Nine Great Sects.’

A month ago it would’ve been unthinkable. Now no one in the Ten Thousand People Clan would question it Plum Blossom Sword Ghost, Chung Myung had proven it himself.

‘For now, I must carry out my mission…’

It was at that moment.

“Lieutenant Commander! There’s someone ahead!”

“Hmm?”

Jong Gwak narrowed his eyes slightly.

Did they catch the enemy’s rear? That’s faster than he had expected.

But the subsequent shouts shattered Jong Gwak’s thoughts.

“They’re approaching! A group is moving this way!”

“What? Approaching us?”

The unexpected words made Jong Gwak’s pupils widen.

“Isn’t there some mistake…”

“They’re coming!”

At that moment, around a dozen figures shot out of the forest at tremendous speed.

“Ooooooo!”

A surge of white sword qi shot high into the sky.

Before anyone could show surprise or give a warning to dodge, the risen sword qi rained down upon them.

Kraaaaaang!

“Aaaaargh!”

“Waaaah!”

With a wail, the vanguard was smashed to pieces and flung aside.

And Jong Gwak saw it: the formation they’d set to pursue the enemy rippled and distorted the moment the vanguard collapsed.

“N-no…”

“Move!”

“A-MI-TA-BUL!”

Woooooooom!

Before they could catch their breath, a majestic golden light rose where the white sword qi had subsided, ripping open the gap the white blades had created.

“They’re being pushed back!”

“Aaaaargh!”

A heaviness like an entire mountain rolling over them. The Ten Thousand People Clan disciples, unable to resist, were driven sideways, and a clear straight path opened between the ranks spread to left and right.

And through that path those all too familiar figures flew in like wind.

“Mo-Mount Hua!”

“They’re Mount Hua people! Avoid them!”

Swordsmen dressed in Mount Hua’s emblematic black uniforms charged toward the opened path.

And what came next was something the Ten Thousand People Clan disciples could all too predict.

Fwoooooosh!

Crimson Plum Blossoms burst forth in succession. Those who knew how deadly and vicious those beautiful blooms could be turned pale and rolled away in every direction.

“Stop dawdling and run, you bastards!”

“Ugh, seriously!”

About a dozen men began sprinting along the path they had cut and twisted open.

“Lieutenant Commander! Orders!”

“Th-they’ve broken through the center!”

Jong Gwak’s gaze flitted about, searching for where to look.

“Stop… wait. No! Why are they attacking?”

Why would those men suddenly attack them? They hadn’t gone mad, had they?

“Lieutenant Commander!”

“I know, shut your mouth!”

Jong Gwak barked and snapped his head forward.

‘What about the main force then?’

If the enemy had suddenly ambushed, it was likely the main force would attack toward the center! A clumsy change of direction and they’d be stabbed in the back, suffering unbearable losses.

But no matter how they strained to sense it, there was no sign of enemy momentum following.

‘Just those fellows?’

Impossible. It couldn’t be.

If they hadn’t gone insane, a mere ten or so men wouldn’t dare face them head-on.

“They’re breaking through! Lieutenant Commander!”

At that moment Jong Gwak clearly saw the group that had charged pierce their encirclement like an awl and run toward the rear.

In literally the blink of an eye, the formation he’d set was breached as easily as flipping a child’s palm.

“Th-this…”

Jong Gwak stared dumbfounded at the scene. Those who had broken through were already receding.

“Shouldn’t we pursue them from behind?”

“…”

Jong Gwak couldn’t answer and bit his lip hard.

Just because the men here were breached didn’t mean the Ten Thousand People Clan had been broken. Many more were in pursuit behind. If they chased that way they’d only be swept up and die in the wave of pursuers.

Normally they’d simply ignore them and chase Southern Island Sect’s main force. That was his mission, after all.

What made Jong Gwak hesitate was the identity of that small group.

‘Heavenly Friends Alliance.’

If they were the Heavenly Friends Alliance without Southern Island Sect with them, if they’d chosen to split from Southern Island Sect to come here, wasn’t their reckless counter-move’s purpose obvious?

“Are they trying to save the Plum Blossom Sword Ghost? With just about ten men?”

An absurdly reckless act.

“…Send a messenger to the commander and report the situation.”

“Yes!”

Having regained his composure, Jong Gwak looked toward where the Heavenly Friends Alliance had vanished with a mocking gaze.

“That’s not courage.”

Jong Gwak let out a slow sneer and turned his head with cold eyes.

❀ ❀ ❀

“Aaaaargh!”

Swaaash!

With a chilling tearing sound, the swung sword sliced deeply across the enemy’s chest.

Shreeek!

But strangely, no blood flowed from the wound. The blood that should have spilled had frozen solid. The chill that pierced the body stopped hearts, and men collapsed.

Bang!

“Move aside! You bastards!”

“Palace Lord! Don’t overdo it!”

Yoon Jong shouted to calm Seol So-Baek, but Seol So-Baek seemed not to hear him and swung his sword even more fiercely.

He poured every bit of his cold qi into the tip of that sword.

Shreeeek!

Wherever his sword grazed, translucent ice crystals blossomed in the air.

“That brat really doesn’t listen for shit!”

“You idiot! He’s the Lord of the Ice Palace!”

“Who doesn’t know that?”

Jo Gul replied gruffly.

When the situation to rescue Chung Myung unfolded, the one who ran wild most was not Jo Gul, Yu Yiseol, or even Baek Cheon, but Seol So-Baek.

Seol So-Baek, who had barely shown a presence during the journey to and from Southern Island Sect, was now swinging his sword as if possessed.

“Aah! It’s cold!”

“Do you think this is the time for jokes?”

“No! I’m serious, it’s freezing! What on earth did that brat eat in the meantime?”

“…Because he’s the Palace Lord.”

“As if being born common makes you immune!”

“What are you saying? You rich brat?”

Jo Gul flinched at Yoon Jong’s fiercely angry voice.

Even so, Seol So-Baek, as if not hearing a word, launched ruthless sword strikes one after another at the foes blocking their way.

‘Taoist!’

Seol So-Baek’s eyes blazed.

To others his sudden fury might seem strange, but to him it was only natural.

He agreed they needed to save Southern Island Sect, but he found it hard to accept sacrificing oneself for them.

Though Southern Island Sect lamented their hardships, he couldn’t sympathize that their plight was worse than what the Ice Palace had faced.

Above all, he saw no reason to risk his life for those who could abandon their homes and leave.

He supported what Chung Myung did. He tried because Chung Myung decided. But truly empathizing with the meaning and purpose was still difficult for someone so young.

But not now. The one he was going to save now was Chung Myung himself benefactor of the Ice Palace and his personal benefactor.

He would never let him die here. He had not yet repaid Chung Myung for anything.

At that moment, Ten Thousand People Clan disciples charged at Seol So-Baek with shouts.

“How dare you!”

Blue light of fury shot from Seol So-Baek’s eyes as his left hand drew back and then thrust forward in an instant. From his right hand white tensile force erupted like a small avalanche of snow.

“Ice Hundred Divine Form?”

“My goodness!”

Mount Hua’s disciples cried out in shock at the sight.

The Ice Palace’s technique they’d witnessed in the North Sea was now being recreated by Seol So-Baek’s hand.

The charging Ten Thousand People Clan disciples froze in an instant. Seol So-Baek kicked the hardened men away and without the slightest delay slammed into the ground.

“Hurry!”

“Yes, sir!”

“Understood! Palace Lord!”

Seol So-Baek ran without looking back, biting his lip until it bled.

Chung Myung’s life hung in the balance. Right now.

‘Taoist! I’m coming now!’

The boy Chung Myung had saved.

That boy had now become a swordsman and was running to save Chung Myung.

Chapter 1394: He Will Be Waiting For Us. (4)

It’s dark and damp. A musty stench of something dried up stung the nose. Even that awful smell only felt dulled.

Chung Myung was used to it.

The smell of the dead. It filled the surroundings, as if half a step already taken between life and death.

It wasn’t surprising. For some time now this smell hadn’t left him, like the scent of blood that won’t wash away no matter how much you scrub.

“Are you still alive?”

At the voice, Chung Myung turned his head slightly. Seeing who had entered the cave, he nodded slowly.

“People’s lives can be stubborn things, huh.”

Hearing that, Chung Myung leaned his head against the cave wall with an indifferent expression and asked.

“And them?”

“They don’t seem to have figured out where we are yet… They’ll be forming a cordon, no doubt. Have we not been through this before?”

“Probably.”

Tang Bo clicked his tongue, checked Chung Myung’s condition, and frowned.

“Let’s see. It’s opened up again, it seems.”

“Leave it. I won’t die.”

“Those about to die always say that and then die. Move your arm a bit.”

Chung Myung frowned slightly, but Tang Bo ignored it and roughly pushed his arm aside. A low groan escaped Chung Myung’s lips.

“Ugh, damn it…”

“Well, looks like it hurts.”

Tang Bo rolled up his sleeve and his face contorted. A long gash ran from the armpit to the lower belly. It had been treated once before, but it had reopened and rotten blood and pus poured out.

“Does your mouth not know the word ‘treatment’, hyung? Or is your head so empty that the concept couldn’t fit? Did you even learn the basics?”

“Shut your mouth. You’re irritating.”

“You’re like a madman.”

Tang Bo grumbled as he skillfully cut the sutures mending Chung Myung’s wound. Even as he sliced through the caked wound, Chung Myung remained silent.

After a moment of silence, Chung Myung asked.

“Is there any alcohol left?”

“Not enough to even disinfect.”

“Give it.”

“No, you fool. How many times do I have to say it? There’s not enough to disinfect.”

“I said I know. Give it.”

“Ha…”

Tang Bo looked at Chung Myung in disbelief, then shook his head. He unfastened a bottle of strong alcohol from his waist and tossed it toward Chung Myung’s face.

“Drink it and die fast.”

Tap.

Chung Myung snatched the bottle in front of his face and deftly opened the cap with one hand. He poured the liquor into his mouth at once, closed his eyes, and rested his head against the cave wall. Tang Bo muttered like a sigh.

“Is he possessed by a ghost that can’t die from alcohol?”

“The other way around. He drinks before he becomes a ghost, he won’t be able to drink once he’s dead.”

“Ha…”

Tang Bo shook his head as if he didn’t even want to deal with it and examined Chung Myung’s wound again. He squeezed out the pus and cut away the dead flesh. Such treatment would knock out most people, but for a warrior like Chung Myung, he could endure it.

“There’s no clean cloth here, you damn man! You need to disinfect with alcohol.”

But Chung Myung ignored him and kept drinking. Tang Bo rolled his eyes and swallowed his curse. It was his own mistake to try to argue with this man.

Tang Bo applied the last of the hemostatic salve over Chung Myung’s wound as if covering it, then plopped down beside him.

“Give it here.”

“There’s barely anything to drink…”

“I’m going to drink some, too, so hand it over!”

Chung Myung snorted and finally handed the bottle to Tang Bo. Tang Bo snatched it irritably and gulped it down.

“Drink it sparingly.”

“Better I drink it than have the patient swallow it all.”

Chung Myung chuckled. The remark wasn’t entirely wrong.

Tang Bo put the bottle down and looked out of the cave. Outside, Demonic Cult warriors swarmed like ants. It wouldn’t be long before this place was discovered.

“Okay, Taoist hyung.”

“What?”

“Can we make it back alive?”

A nonchalant answer came back. Tang Bo sighed.

“I told you to just let that damn high priest go. Is it sensible to come all the way here just to chop off his head? If we die like this, it’s ‘natural causes,’ huh! Natural causes!”

“But we killed who needed killing.”

“We’re the ones likely to die here, I’m telling you!”

“Then it can’t be helped.”

Tang Bo silently tore through his hair once and muttered words that made little sense. They were curses about Chung Myung, vicious enough that anyone could tell without translating.

‘Likely to die…’

A strange smile formed at the corner of Chung Myung’s mouth.

So what if that was the case?

There were countless bodies rotting in this damned land. They all turned into maggot-ridden corpses and rotted away; what difference would adding Chung Myung’s corpse make?

While alive, you do what’s to be done. If the price is death, that doesn’t stop you from doing it.

“Sometimes, when I look at you, Taoist hyung…”

“…Hm?”

“You seem to have a screw loose somewhere.”

“…”

“No, really. Among those called absolute strong warriors, how many are sane, anyway?”

“You’re one of them, too.”

Tang Bo, who’d briefly been at a loss for words, laughed.

“Fair enough. If I were sane, I wouldn’t be stuck by the likes of you.”

“Good, then.”

Chung Myung snatched the bottle from Tang Bo’s hand and drank. Then he firmly pressed the cap closed.

Slowly rising to his feet, he clenched and unclenched his fist lightly, nodded, and handed the bottle back to Tang Bo.

“Tie it on so it won’t break.”

“Huh?”

“Because we’ll finish it when we get back.”

“…You’re going out now?”

“The cordon will only tighten over time and get harder to break. Let’s move.”

Tang Bo stared at Chung Myung in astonishment.

Of course Chung Myung wasn’t wrong. The longer they delayed, the thicker the cordon and the greater the danger; the chances of coming back alive shrank.

But isn’t that something you say after checking people’s conditions? Could they break the cordon in Chung Myung’s current state?

Tang Bo bit the inside of his cheek and said.

“Hyung… if we wait longer, reinforcements might come. Elder Chung Mun’s forces…”

But there was no answer. Tang Bo sighed deeply.

He knew the truth: the main force couldn’t spare aid for the two of them. In the first place, wasn’t Chung Myung’s reckless attempt on the high priest’s neck meant to buy time for the ambushed main force?

In a situation where just containing the chaos was a struggle, sending a relief team would be impossible.

“Enough whining, get up.”

“Who’s whining…”

Reluctantly grumbling, Tang Bo got to his feet. His eyes quickly scanned Chung Myung’s body again.

“Can you fight?”

“Stronger than you, at least.”

“…I guarantee, Taoist hyung, you’ll fall into hell.”

“You make it sound novel.”

Chung Myung strode out of the cave without hesitation.

The sword in his hand felt heavy. With every step, his wavering body had long since slipped beyond his control.

The stench of death stung more strongly now. Somewhere on the mountain before him could become his humble grave. He murmured softly.

“Don’t fall behind.”

“Who is that supposed to be for?”

Chung Myung snorted. Exactly.

Bang!

He kicked off the ground and plunged into the woods, where Demonic Cult members in black garments appeared before his eyes. They charged at once, their eyes bloodshot.

Chung Myung swung his sword casually and immediately severed the cult members’ necks.

Hot blood splattered his face. The warmth of what had been living breath moments before would soon disperse and quickly cool, like this blood already losing heat.

“They’re coming!”

Tang Bo’s thrown blade cut the air like lightning. Chung Myung spat the blood from his mouth; the corner of his eye twitched.

‘Should’ve had one more swig before coming out?’

The thick smell of blood chased away the lingering scent of alcohol in his mouth.

‘If we get back, I’ll drink until I’m drunk.’

If they couldn’t return?

Well. He’d probably just regret the alcohol he didn’t get to drink. Nothing more.

Swoosh!
