He deflected the incoming blade. But perhaps due to his wound his reaction was a touch slow, and he couldn’t prevent it grazing his flank.

Pushing the nauseating pain out of his awareness, Chung Myung swung his sword. Another life was cut down mercilessly.

‘No one really wants to die.’

But there are people for whom life isn’t desperately cherished; those who find breathing dull and tiresome.

“Tang Bo.”

“Now of all times?”

Tang Bo moved quickly and asked, incredulous. Chung Myung impaled a cult member’s heart with the tip of his sword and said calmly.

“When the war’s over, will you go back to the Tang Family?”

“Or what, come to Mount Hua? Is there anywhere else for me to go?”

Chung Myung chuckled. Hearing it, the question sounded stupid.

Swoosh!

At that moment Tang Bo hurled the retrieved throwing blade again and said.

“Would you like to tour around with me?”

“Tour…”

“If you’ve nothing better to do, you might as well do that.”

“…Not bad.”

Mulling over Tang Bo’s words in his ear, Chung Myung murmured.

Rather than an empty Mount Hua, better than watching the surviving Chung Mun rot away in self-reproach.

“Then stop whining and hold your sword properly. First we have to survive; only then can we tour or whatever!”

“Right. When we return, first thing’s to drink.”

“Fine, so watch the front properly!”

Chung Myung smiled.

A tour wasn’t bad. Following that man to the Tang Family and living as a guest might not be so bad. Perhaps returning to Mount Hua might be better than he thought.

Even living as an empty shell would be better than rotting away.

Chung Myung adjusted his grip on the sword.

But even then, the thought that one more sip might have been okay didn’t disappear.

Demonic Cultists surged in a dark wave. Chung Myung’s mouth twisted with a predatory light.

“No matter what…”

Baring his teeth, Chung Myung emitted a fierce killing intent.

“I won’t die until I cut off that Heavenly Demon’s head.”

Chung Myung kicked off the ground with all his might; the Sword Master stepped forward.

❀ ❀ ❀

He had no strength in his fingertips.

His head, resting against a tree trunk, kept slipping to the side. If he relaxed even a little he’d lose consciousness. The two eyes glimpsed through blood-soaked hair looked perilously close to going out.

Where was this again?

A burning thirst surged. It was unbearable. In the lethargy that enveloped his whole body, only that thirst felt whole.

Crack.

The moment his mouth parted slightly, his parched lips split and bled.

“Alcohol…”

His head tilted back.

He looked toward the sky, where daylight brightened in the distance. Even glimpsed through the dense trees, it was cruelly beautiful.

“…I should’ve brought some.”

A thin laugh, about to break, slipped from his parted lips.

“This way!”

The peace lasted only a moment before the surroundings erupted into noise.

Snap.

Chung Myung reflexively grabbed the Dark Fragrant Plum Blossom Sword.

“They don’t give a moment’s rest… damned bastards.”

Chung Myung laughed softly and turned his head to glare at those rushing toward him.

‘How did I get back alive then?’

He couldn’t remember clearly.

Maybe it was because he’d been through such things too many times.

Maybe the memory was just too old.

And maybe…

Bang.

Chung Myung launched himself with all his might. Behind him, members of the Ten Thousand People Clan followed, their eyes flashing with killing intent.

Chapter 1395: He Will Be Waiting For Us. (5)

“Commander! The Jeong unit has completed the encirclement! The Mu and Gi units are moving to their designated positions!”

“Eul unit is currently engaged with the Plum Blossom Sword Ghost! There’s a high chance the encirclement will be broken through!”

Ho Ga-Myung’s gaze grew progressively chillier as he listened to the flood of reports.

The Plum Blossom Sword Ghost, Chung Myung’s speed at breaking the encirclement was slowing down. Or, to be precise, after breaking through once, the distance he fled each time was rapidly shortening.

If this were before, one would have interpreted it as him deliberately stalling to buy time for the main force to retreat…

‘Considering all circumstances, this is different.’

Ho Ga-Myung’s eyes sank with sober gravity.

The longer time dragged on, the denser the encirclement around him became. He wasn’t someone unaware of that situation.

Knowing it yet being unable to escape meant he lacked the strength to break the encirclement.

That monstrous fellow had finally shown his limits.

Ho Ga-Myung glanced around. The aides, as if sensing the same thing, had flushed faces.

A sigh-like words escaped Ho Ga-Myung’s mouth.

“In the end… you were human after all.”

The aides fell silent and looked at Ho Ga-Myung. His voice carried a wealth of emotion.

Resentment and hatred. A relief that at last the opponent had been forced into the net. Joy that he could finally carry out the Alliance lord’s orders.

But anyone who heard that voice directly could tell there was also a subtle tinge of pity within it.

As soldiers of the Ten Thousand People Clan—and further as forces of the Four Sects Alliance—such feelings must never be shown. Depending on the situation, it could even risk their fall from grace.

But the aides did not bother to point that out.

Not because they feared the damage from criticizing Ho Ga-Myung’s lapse, but because they too could understand the feelings that had finally escaped him.

“…No need to rush.”

Ho Ga-Myung said, as if steadying himself.

“Even if slow, we’ll sever his lifeline for sure.”

“Yes, Commander!”

Just as Ho Ga-Myung, bolstered with certainty, prepared to move, it happened.

Screeeeeech!

A piercing hawk cry rang overhead. Ho Ga-Myung narrowed his eyes and frowned as he looked up.

‘Emergent dispatch?’

A powerful hawk swooped down and landed with a flutter on Ho Ga-Myung’s outstretched arm as if snatching prey. Ho Ga-Myung withdrew a dispatch from the tube attached to the hawk’s talons and read it without delay.

“…Heavenly Friends Alliance?”

A hollow laugh escaped his mouth.

“I knew recklessness was the Heavenly Friends Alliance’s forte… but this just crosses the line.”

The aides stiffened as they saw the dispatch Ho Ga-Myung held out.

“Commander, this is…”

“Don’t be alarmed. They still won’t be able to do anything. But… we must prepare.”

Ho Ga-Myung adjusted the positions of the encirclement around Chung Myung bit by bit, gave orders, and then said calmly.

“We go to where he is.”

“Co-Commander! That’s dangerous!”

“Dangerous?”

Voices of objection rose around him, but Ho Ga-Myung’s face showed no change.

“Is there any greater danger to us than him escaping alive right now?”

The aide momentarily gaped, looked at Ho Ga-Myung, and then bowed his head deeply.

“Lead the way.”

In the end, the aides nodded.

“This way!”

Ho Ga-Myung set off after the aide, his eyes cooling to steel.

‘I will not allow a single variable.’

Chung Myung’s absolute death. In this entire campaign, Ho Ga-Myung’s sole objective was that alone.

❀ ❀ ❀

“W-wait!”

Namgung Do-Wei shouted urgently. His specialty was a crushing sword qi rooted in robust inner strength. By the nature of his swordsmanship, his attacks inevitably had a wide range.

The problem was that every time he swung his sword, someone kept cutting in front of him.

“T-Taoist! Just a moment!”

“What ‘just a moment,’ damn it!”

Jo Gul snarled and lunged before Namgung Do-Wei. The explosively emitted red sword qi tore through the charging Ten Thousand People Clan warriors in an instant. Even after felling more than three with a single stroke, unsatisfied, he crouched nearly to the ground and surged forward.

“Aaaaaargh!”

But Seol So-Baek’s attack, who had leapt up before Jo Gul, was faster.

Slaaash!

When Seol So-Baek’s sword sliced the enemies like lightning, a white frost quickly blanketed their bodies.

The two breaking the path seemed almost to be competing.

No—perhaps the phrase ‘breaking a path’ doesn’t suit this scene. It’s hard to call it that when they plunged between enemies without even confirming whether all were down or properly finished.

“Get out of the way! You damned bastards!”

The two shouted and swung their swords relentlessly.

At that perilous sight, Namgung Do-Wei hurriedly shifted his gaze—he was searching for Baek Cheon.

“Now then, Acting Sect Leader. First, let’s get things under control…”

Because the only one here who could calm these lunatics was Baek Cheon.

But the Baek Cheon he desperately sought was already gone from his side.

“Anyone who blocks me dies!”

A red sword qi shot up sharply ahead. Sword qi that hadn’t even become a Plum Blossom. Discarding the finesse essential to Mount Hua’s sword, it carried only swift force and swept the enemies roughly and violently.

‘Uh, when did they get over there…?’

The system they had maintained from the Southern Island to here had long since become meaningless. Everyone was charging out as if competing to make a path. Namgung Do-Wei couldn’t help but be bewildered by such a sight.

“Oooooooo!”

At that moment, a brilliant golden radiance burst forth behind him.

“Hey, Yiiik…!”

Namgung Do-Wei violently lunged to the side. The radiance that narrowly brushed his former spot crushed the retreating Ten Thousand People Clan warriors in an instant.

Kraaaash!

The following explosion of qi showed not an iota of compassionate mercy. Startled, Namgung Do-Wei turned sharply to look at Hae Yeon.

“M-Monk. It’s dangerous!”

“What’s that supposed to mean?”

“N-no, not just me—Acting Sect Leader nearly got swept away just now…”

“He can dodge on his own! If he can’t, tell him to get out of the way!”

“…”

“So slow!”

Hae Yeon’s shout was followed by his figure vanishing from that spot as if snuffed out.

‘Body Displacement?’

Appearing again at the front as if by sorcery, Hae Yeon unleashed a thunderous lion’s roar and thrust his palm. An excessive Arhat Fist unfolded, his limbs seeming to multiply as they jabbed in all directions.

Namgung Do-Wei was speechless as he watched the scene.

Yoon Jong, the calmest at Mount Hua, was rampaging like a yaksha, and Mount Hua’s youngest, Tang Soso, was trampling over her own sahyung as she broke through.

Odd, isn’t it?

No, not really. Come to think of it, the Heavenly Friends Alliance was always like that. Didn’t Namgung Do-Wei know?

Still, there’s one reason this scene feels awkward now.

‘Everyone has been holding back until now.’

From the Southern Island sect to here, one could see from this sight how much they had borne and repressed themselves. Now, with nothing left to bear and only the single purpose of saving Chung Myung, they revealed themselves without restraint.

They were already utterly exhausted.

Yet they pooled every ounce of strength and relentlessly pressed the enemy, to reach Chung Myung in danger as quickly as possible.

‘I—I will too!’

Just as Namgung Do-Wei grit his teeth and pushed to advance, a sudden heavy weight settled on his sword.

‘Huh?’

Turning in surprise, he saw someone—Yu Yiseol. She was pressing her sword gently against Namgung Do-Wei’s blade.

“Taoist Yiseol?”

Namgung Do-Wei was momentarily puzzled. Was it not his turn to step forward?

But her following words were indifferent and clear.

“Send me flying.”

“…Yes?”

“Send me flying.”

“…”

“Quickly!”

Namgung Do-Wei, caught off guard, stomped the ground and swung his sword with all his might. Yu Yiseol, lightly riding that blade, shot into the air like a soaring bird and then slammed down over the heads of the enemies blocking the front.

Fwoooom!

Yu Yiseol’s Plum Blossom Sword traced a graceful semicircle through the air. The necks of those caught in the arc popped up one after another.

“Run faster, you sluggish worms!”

“Ah, I know!”

Baek Cheon gritted his teeth and thrust his sword into the retreating enemy bodies. Without waiting for them to collapse, he vaulted on their chests and scattered countless sword qís.

‘Faster!’

The more urgent his mind, the more the veins on his jaw bulged.

No matter how many he felled, these ant-like foes seemed endless. No matter how he fought, it felt as if the enemy would be replenished forever.

The reason Baek Cheon felt urgency now wasn’t because dealing with them was too hard; it was because Chung Myung had been facing them alone for quite some time.

He could tell that the level of enemies standing against the Heavenly Friends Alliance entourage wasn’t that impressive. Considering them being Ten Thousand People Clan, that should never be the case.

That meant only one thing: the stronger ones among the Ten Thousand People Clan had all been deployed to chase Chung Myung.

Could he hold out?

He was certainly monstrous, but could that body truly shake off their pursuit?

“Aaargh! Damn it!”

Baek Cheon unleashed sword qi with a mad light in his eyes.

He could escape—of course he could. But Baek Cheon also knew the enemy would never flee in this situation. He would stall until the limit, and only at the very end, when cornered, try to open his path.

Even if that ending turned out badly.

‘Who the hell gave you permission, you fucking bastard!’

A murderous light flared in Baek Cheon’s eyes.

“Sasuk! Over there!”

At that moment, Baek Cheon snapped his head up at a shout erupting nearby.

Sure enough, a group came into view. They were looking not toward the Heavenly Friends Alliance entourage, but the opposite direction.

Baek Cheon intuited it.

He’s there. If they can just break through those bastards, Chung Myung will be right behind!

That wall-like encirclement couldn’t be spread over a wide area!

Baek Cheon sprinted with all his strength and brandished his sword.

Kaaang!

But unlike before, his sword was abruptly blocked by a blade that flew in an instant. Those with killing intent in their eyes growled and glared at Baek Cheon.

“You brat!”

Intense, blue blade qi brimming with killing intent poured out continuously.

Teeth clenched, Baek Cheon parried those attacks and drove his sword into the throat of a Ten Thousand People Clan blade in front.

One, then another. Slowly he cuts them down. Slowly he breaks through.

But…

‘Not enough!’

This won’t do. It must be stronger. Faster. Only then can he break through this path in time.

“Not enough, damn it!”

In the end his cry burst out. His face twisted with rage and desperation.

He felt powerless. If only he had been a bit stronger, he would have swept these bastards away in an instant. If he weren’t the one saving Chung Myung but the other way around, Chung Myung would have reached his position long ago.

He could become stronger. Even if it took a long time, he’d become stronger.

That vow he once made felt hollow at this moment. The times he needed to be strong always arrived earlier than the times he grew strong. Hadn’t he grown weary of being frustrated by that wall?

An image he dared not imagine kept forming in his mind.

Chung Myung gutted by the enemy’s blade. And him arriving too late.

‘No!’

He would die before seeing that sight.

In an instant, a great surge of energy burst from Baek Cheon.

“Sasuk!”

“Hey, you madman!”

An imperfect Purple Cloud Divine Cultivation. Baek Cheon drew it up as it was.

Cultivation technique not yet fully mastered eats away at the user’s body. It was a perilous act that could easily lead to heart demon at any moment. Especially in a situation lacking reserves like this, it was tantamount to suicide.

“Get out of the way!”

Aghast, Yoon Jong and Jo Gul rushed to grab him, but Baek Cheon shook them off without hesitation and surged toward the enemy. The red sword qi pouring from his blade spread like a sunset dyeing the west.

‘Please, somehow just stay alive!’

I will definitely save you. No matter the price to be paid.

Chapter 1396: To The Place You Belong. (1)

Ho Ga-Myung’s expression, as he kicked off the ground, darkened more and more.

He normally didn’t show emotion on his face, but before the carnage in front of him, even he couldn’t help it.

All he could see were corpses everywhere.

The mangled corpses and the ground soaked in dark red blood; only the word “blood path” could describe the scene.

‘Could all of this really be the work of one person…’

Awe came before anger. This was not a sight that strength alone could produce.

Who else in the world could create such a scene besides that Plum Blossom Sword Ghost? Even against the Ten Thousand People Clan.

Seeing this, he finally understood why killing just one person had become so slow.

Even if it weren’t Ho Ga-Myung, anyone in this situation would reach the same conclusion. The scenes he witnessed were that shocking.

How many people had to be slashed, how many sword swings, for one person to create this devastation?

‘But in return, that bastard won’t be unscathed either.’

As long as he’s human… no, even if he’s a monster, he can’t commit this and remain whole.

“Hurry.”

“Yes!”

Ho Ga-Myung increased his pace. The bastard must also stand at the brink of life and death. This time he had to cut that throat for good.

❀ ❀ ❀

“Surround him! Don’t let him escape!”

Screech!

He had never felt regret.

Loss over forfeited power? Compared to all the things he had to lose despite not wanting to, the strength he once carried was trivial.

“Encircle him! Give him no opening to slip out!”

Screech!

But if he still had that power, he could have stopped the blade that cut his shoulder. He could have avoided the sword that pierced his side. He could have laughed calmly while facing all these enemies.

If he’d focused solely on regaining his strength after returning… or at least taken better care of himself, he could still be a Sword Saint here—aloof and strong.

So does he feel regret now?

Perhaps…

Shing!

A sword shot out like lightning and pierced an enemy’s throat without mercy. An indescribable expression crossed the enemy’s face, unable to accept death. Then red blood gushed from his mouth.

“Gurgle…”

A heavy weight settled on the tip of the sword that pierced him—the full weight of a person. Normally it would feel almost weightless, but at this moment it felt crushing.

Was it fatigue? Or was the weight of life he felt simply not what it used to be?

“This wretched…”

Chung Myung seemed on the verge of collapse but wouldn’t fall, and a terrified enemy hesitated for a moment. That gave Chung Myung a breath; he forced his bowed head up.

The world blurred and sharpened repeatedly.

How many had fallen? He didn’t know. He’d long since given up counting.

‘It’s hot…’

His body felt like a ball of fire. No, at times it felt like endless cold ice. Maybe both.

His internal qi had long since run out, and crushed wounds were eating away at his body.

The price for killing so many wasn’t exactly steep, but its aftermath pushed Chung Myung toward a single clear outcome.

His eyelids felt unbearably heavy. With half-closed eyes he scanned around. All he saw were enemies in red clothes.

Once such scenes were perfectly familiar; breathing within them felt natural. Now they felt strange—has Chung Myung grown weak? Or…

Ssshh.

The dead body’s weight slid off the sword. Though the burden was gone, the blade still felt heavy—too heavy to lift.

The world kept losing its edges.

It felt like nothing remained in the world but enemies. Yet why couldn’t he turn away, why couldn’t he let go and find rest?

Was there still attachment?

Those never truly understood by others end up mocking everything—even themselves. It’s a habit. Better to receive an enemy’s small, comprehensible gaze than to face stares that see you as something strange. It’s a different kind of understanding.

He tried to twist his mouth into a smirk, but his face didn’t move. His mouth no longer held ease or menace.

‘Even the bluff is gone…’

A laugh slipped out—not to threaten, but a self-mocking hollow chuckle.

“What are you doing! Kill him! He’s at his limit!”

“Eeaaah!”

Someone thrust a spear in a frenzy. Instinctively he swung his sword to block the incoming spear.

Crunch!

A sharp metallic sound and the noise of a blade slicing flesh rang almost together—the chilling sound when a keen edge cuts through skin and transmits into the skull.

When they clashed, the sword that ricocheted was the Tang Family’s spirit that had cut down countless foes—the blade that had never once betrayed Chung Myung’s will.

The spear strike itself wasn’t remarkable, but his weakened sword couldn’t even stop an ordinary attack.

“Ugh…”

Shing!

Chung Myung gritted his teeth and swung like lightning. The Ten Thousand People Clan sword, which had hesitated in surprise at his handiwork, had its head torn from its body.

Chung Myung’s knees buckled.

The blade was utterly powerless, his reactions ever slower. Everything he’d piled up seemed to backflow and spill out of him.

“Cough…”

Each cough brought blood. Yet the body that nearly toppled somehow steadied itself again.

“Cough! Cough!”

A strange fervor began to ripple in the eyes of enemies who dared not come closer but stared at Chung Myung.

The Plum Blossom Sword Ghost, who had seemed endless, now showed weakness before them. Perhaps… maybe they could accomplish the feat of cutting that beast’s throat with their own hands.

It was dangerously tempting and lethal. There was no one here who wouldn’t waver before it.

Those who swallowed dryly took cautious steps toward Chung Myung. The circle around him began to close, little by little.

Grip.

Chung Myung grabbed the spear lodged above his heart with his left hand. He pulled, but the hooked blade was embedded in flesh and wouldn’t come free.

Crack.

He finally snapped the spear shaft without hesitation and spat the blood pooled in his mouth.

In his narrowed vision there were only enemies. The whole world was full of foes. Foe, foe, foe, and more foe.

‘…Just cut them down.’

Cut and topple. And open a path. As he had done countless times before. As he had always done. Then a way opens.

‘And after that?’

There will be more enemies. Even if he felled them, new foes would appear behind them.

Cut, cut, cut again. Cut once more…

What remains at the end of it all?

“Huh…”

A sound that might be laughter, a groan, or a sob slipped from his lips. Nothing would change. It had always been this way.

He had never been arrogant about his strength. He hadn’t recklessly assumed he’d survive every battle. He had simply managed to survive one deadly fight after another—without particularly yearning to live.

It wouldn’t be strange if his luck ran out here.

The ending that everyone met. The end Chung Myung too should have met had come for him—terribly slow, terribly clear.

Propped up by his sword, Chung Myung forced his collapsing body upright again and again.

“D-Die!”

Before the enemy could extend his blade, Chung Myung’s sword moved a step faster. Sword qi from the tip sank straight into the foe’s chest.

“Gurgle…”

The injured enemy staggered briefly, but that was it. He grabbed the wound and stood his ground instead of falling.

Even the sword spirit could no longer exert its strength. It couldn’t fully pierce a human’s soft flesh.

Any regrets?

No. There was nothing to regret.

In the past he saved the world, but he lost all the things he shouldn’t have.

But this time it’s different.

‘…I protected them.’

Long ago… yes, it should have been this way long ago.

He never wanted to again be the lone survivor looking at the dead. He didn’t want to suffer the pain of collapsing alone in an empty world.

Even if everyone pointed and called it foolish and stupid, this had been his only path from the start.

‘Sahyung…’

You wouldn’t know. How far the mission you trusted me with pushed me.

You couldn’t understand. The dead can know nothing.

You’ll never know what it felt like to be the lone one left after everyone’s death.

Now that it’s come to this… I wasn’t as great or strong as you thought.

Shing!

Dozens of blades flew toward him.

Before his consciousness could react, Chung Myung’s sword cleaved the air. The instinct imprinted in his body didn’t abandon him until the very end.

Klang!

The blades shattered. The shards pierced enemies and also lodged into Chung Myung’s exhausted body.

“Cough!”

A metallic clump of blood kept spurting out. It felt as if every drop inside him had already dried—strangely so.

He lifted his head with effort as dawn was breaking. The red twilight sky gazed down on him, like the unforgettable sky of that day.

Like Mount Hua’s final day as he remembered it.

That day, if his sahyung and sajaes’ greatest wish as they died was for Mount Hua to bloom Plum Blossoms again, and if what he wished most then was that his death might save just one more life…

Hasn’t Chung Myung now fulfilled that role?

What he set out to do was done. What his sahyung-sajaes wished for was achieved. They may be unreliable, pitiful, stupid as can be—but they’re good men.

Isn’t that enough?

They will prevail—even without him.

No matter how cold the wind, they will endure and carry it on. They will continue what those fallen before guarded and add to that will.

‘Will they resent me?’

They will. They’re that sort of people. They’ll be angry, curse, and… cry some more.

But they’ll forget soon enough.

Just as he forgot Chung Mun and built a life with new people. Just as they laughed and made noise at a new Mount Hua instead of the old one.

His absence will sadden everyone for a while, but in the end they’ll forget. New flesh will grow over the wound, and grass will one day sprout on the parched earth.

That makes him a bit… just a little… resentful.

“What are you doing! Kill him! Kill him at once! He’s at his limit!”

The enemies surged forward.

His vision blurred, his arms would not move. Sensation dulled. He was too pathetic and small to overcome anyone now.

Screech!

Blood flowing down the Dark Fragrant Plum Blossom Sword stained his fingertips.

He had cut somewhere on an enemy, but he couldn’t tell. His legs wouldn’t obey.

But he had to cut down at least one more.

Each life he cut off would make those who survive even a little safer. So one more! Even just one more person…

Screech.

‘Chung Mun sahyung.’

Would he smile? Would he praise Chung Myung for what he accomplished upon returning?

Maybe he’d scold him. He always did. He might be angry that he went so far when it wasn’t necessary.

He hoped so.

‘I did my best, Chung Mun sahyung.’

So… please… just think of me a little… just a little bit with pity.

His knees finally gave out. The ground seemed to rise. The world spun so dizzy he couldn’t tell up from down.

A different death from that time was coming for him.

It’s all right. He had left it behind.

Somewhere in Shaanxi, somewhere on Mount Hua where the Plum Blossoms bloom, in the hearts of those who will wail, on the tips of swords not yet fully tempered—he had left enough.

So even if this is the last, there are no more regrets. Yet…

“Finish him off!”

“He’s exhausted! Sever his neck!”

But even so… even so…

Kaaaaang!

A flying blade suddenly struck the sword that shot upward.

Kicking off the ground, Chung Myung leapt into the midst of his enemies. The ferocious sword qi that poured forth split the foes in an instant.

“Ah…”

Chung Myung’s mouth opened, and a beast-like scream tore out.

“Ah… Aaaaaaah!”

He wanted to live.

The squeezed-out sword qi cleaved those filled only with malice. Muscles tore, bones crushed, but he swung and swung again.

He wanted to live.

“Aaaaaaaaaah!”

He wanted to live and laugh again. He wanted to return alive to Mount Hua.

The place he left. The place he had to return to.

The place where everyone awaited him.

“Aaaaaaaaaah!”

Chung Myung’s scream as he leapt between raised blades thundered through the forest.

I want to live.

Chapter 1397: To The Place You Belong. (2)

Fwooooosh!

Chung Myung’s sword was swung. It was a sword charged with all his strength.

Kakagak!

But the sword, failing to pierce the blade, simply bounced off.

“Aaaaaaaah!”

Grasping the rebounding sword with both hands, Chung Myung let out a scream and brought his blade down on the enemy, again and again.

“Y-you madman!”

Seeing Chung Myung repeatedly strike the chest of the already fallen man, the Ten Thousand People Clan members recoiled in horror.

“Die!”

One member of the Ten Thousand People Clan swung a blade at Chung Myung’s back.

Kwadeuk!

The flying blade tore into Chung Myung’s back. Thick blood beaded and splattered up.

Chung Myung thought.

Was there a place where he belonged?

Fwooooosh!

Ten Thousand People Clan members surrounding him leaped from all sides and brought their blades down on Chung Myung. Without hesitation he rolled on the ground to dodge the incoming blades.

Screech! Screech! Screech!

The blades that brushed his body left shallow cuts across Chung Myung. Grasping at the dirt with his hand, he rose and swung his sword convulsively. Screams erupted all around.

“Aaaargh!”

“M-my leg!”

“Gurgle!”

Shaking violently as he rose, Chung Myung coughed blood incessantly.

Was he there?

Who are you peeping into someone else’s room? Are you a thief?

Chung Myung always thought no for that. This was not where he was meant to be. He was someone who had already lost the place he ought to be.

He knew. He knew from the beginning the end he would one day face. From the very start…

He was a specter who should not have been left behind. The fate awaiting one who should not exist was predetermined.

So he thought it enough if he could do what remained undone before that end came. But…

What’s your name?

I am Yoon Jong!

To confess, even Chung Myung sometimes forgot that fact.

Thud!

The leg Chung Myung used to stand momentarily gave out. His body lost balance and collapsed forward.

“This is the opening!”

Hungry jackals would not leave a wounded beast alone. Seeing Chung Myung’s weakness, they charged fiercely.

Kaang!

Placing a hand on the ground to brace himself, Chung Myung barely moved his sword with one hand and struck spears and blades aside one after another.

Kwadeuk! Kwaduk!

But he couldn’t defend against the spear that flew from behind; the blade plunged into his back without mercy. Chung Myung’s body couldn’t withstand the impact and staggered forward, collapsing.

‘Opportunity!’

To cut off his neck! That would be an undeniable kill. When the giant fell, a witness, face consumed by desire, hurled himself at Chung Myung. But before he could even swing his dao fully, Chung Myung’s suddenly sprung hand seized his throat.

“Gahk!”

Chung Myung and his foe became entangled and fell forward together.

“Ggrrk… L-let go…”

Clenching his teeth, Chung Myung twisted his body as they fell. The man whose throat he held was forced onto Chung Myung’s upper body. What met his eyes then were countless blades raining down aimed at him.

Screech! Screech! Screech! Screech!

With eyes wide open, the man’s body was mercilessly slashed by the blades.

“G-ggrr…”

In his dimming vision he saw something flying that darkened the sky.

Kwaduk! Kwadeuk!

Weapons rained down like a storm, piercing the corpse that had already stopped breathing.

“Gulp!”

Even though the body above had not yet entirely lost its life force, the fierce swords and blades pierced mercilessly down into Chung Myung beneath.

“Ugh…”

Chung Myung, like a lightning strike, swung his sword and cut out the blade embedded in his body. Without a moment’s hesitation he rolled violently on the ground.

Kwaaaaang!

Tangible qi poured down where he had just been, unleashing a tremendous explosion.

Unable to withstand the blast, Chung Myung’s body was flung lifelessly like a leaf caught in a typhoon.

Who are you?

But now… yes, now he thought he knew.

There had never been a place he was meant to belong. There was only the place he wanted to be.

He realized it a little late, though.

Kuuuuung!

Chung Myung’s body plummeted. A massive shock washed over him, enough to snatch his consciousness away. Yet he somehow clung to it. The moment he let go, everything would end.

Swaaaaak!

Chung Myung kicked the ground and rolled his body once more.

Kwagak! Kwagagak!

The blades plunged one after another, gouging the earth like mud. In the chaos, a blade that he couldn’t avoid sliced across Chung Myung’s neck.

Blood gushed from the deeply cut throat. But with no time to feel the pain, he raised his sword and aimed at the approaching enemy.

In his ears his heartbeat and ragged breathing echoed.

‘Plum Blossom…’

Plum Blossoms bloomed countless times. But the tip of his sword could no longer bloom a Plum Blossom. Half-drawn, it faded and fell into futility.

‘Live…’

Just as a Plum Blossom withers in winter, his sword too was wilting.

“Aaaaah!”

Not missing the opportunity, the enemies leaped onto Chung Myung and brought their blades down.

Dodging those strikes by launching his body away was pure instinct, nothing more. Pushing off the ground, he barely escaped the blades. With no time to catch his breath, countless weapons poured toward him.

Screech! Screech!

He rolled and rolled desperately to avoid the attacks. But clear, definite wounds were now accumulating on his body.

“P-please die…!”

Screech!

Chung Myung’s sword pierced the chest of a Ten Thousand People Clan member who had charged with desperate intensity.

Kwaang!

But the sword, failing to strike the heart precisely, left room for a counterattack. Before the man collapsed, the blow he threw struck Chung Myung’s chest.

Chung Myung spat blood from his mouth and tumbled backward.

Thud! Thud!

Bouncing off the ground, Chung Myung rose then fell flat again. Groaning deeply, he tried one last time to lift himself when—

Kwadeuk!

A metal spear sliced through the air and pierced Chung Myung’s hamstring, pinning into the ground. His body, which had tried to spring up, collapsed forward.

His face was shoved into the dirt—into a spot already darkly stained with someone’s blood.

Only a faint groan, as if it might cease at any moment, barely escaped him.

‘I am…’

Isn’t this enough now?

Can he finally let go?

How much more pain must he endure? How much longer this hell?

He could let go. Letting go would bring relief. Hadn’t he done enough?

‘To Mount Hua…’

Chung Myung struggled to lift his head. Thick blood ran down his face and trailed to the ground.

His head, lifeless as if it might break at any moment, refused and turned stubbornly backward. Trembling, he stretched out a hand and painfully gripped the spear that had pierced his knee.

“Ugh…”

But despite all his strength, he couldn’t pull the spear free. His blood-slick hand kept slipping; nothing moved as he willed.

In the end Chung Myung summoned all his strength and hauled the spear toward himself. Gripping the twisted shaft as if embracing it while it ripped and tore flesh, he bit down hard on it.

With no strength left in his hands, he was pulling it out by biting.

Crack. Gggrk. Grk.

The spear, stained red by the blood dripping from his mouth, creaked and was slowly wrenched out of his knee. Even the Ten Thousand People Clan members who had rushed forward halted at the sight.

It was brutal. Though they were enemies, they couldn’t bear to watch.

Thud.

At last the pulled-out spear fell to the ground. Chung Myung, mouth still agape, slowly bowed his head.

Who is the sahyung-sajae? I don’t recognize the face from Hwa-Um village.

His fingertips were cold. Should he feel relieved that he still had enough sensation to feel the chill?

He wanted comfort. He wanted to let go.

But equally… he wanted to live.

Chung Myung warrior! Stand right there!

What did I mean to you?

Did you see it in me, in the past I so desperately wanted to emulate?

Chung Myung’s hand, which had not moved even slightly, twitched.

Everyone watched, holding their breath, as that barely moving hand fumbled and then gripped his own sword.

Someone’s groan escaped like a sigh.

His eyes half-closed. A body ruined with not a single intact part. Even in a situation where he should have died many times over, the man still strove to fight.

Complex emotions surfaced on the faces of the Ten Thousand People Clan members.

Are they afraid? No, it’s not that. What reason would they have to fear someone in that state?

Yet their feet would not move.

After all, they too are warriors. Even if they are pointed at by the Evil Sect, they are people who staked their lives on martial learning.

At the very least, anyone who has lived as a warrior could not help but feel awe at this sight.

If they could, they’d turn away and avoid this place entirely.

Even though they knew he had killed their comrades countless times and was an enemy that must be killed.

A suffocating silence spread.

Only Chung Myung’s faint breath, as if it might stop at any second, quietly echoed in the stillness.

“What are you doing?”

Then, breaking the dry silence, a cold voice rang out.

Startled, everyone turned to find Ho Ga-Myung standing with an expressionless face. He, who had been in the rear, had somehow appeared here.

“C-Commander!”

“I asked what you are doing.”

The Ten Thousand People Clan members couldn’t bring themselves to answer and avoided Ho Ga-Myung’s gaze. After a quick glance at them, Ho Ga-Myung turned his cold stare to Chung Myung.

The Ten Thousand People Clan members hesitated and stepped aside, clearing a path between him and Chung Myung. But Ho Ga-Myung did not bother to walk it. He simply stared at Chung Myung’s figure.

It was a pitiful sight, almost too tragic to believe it was the Sword Ghost who had tormented him and the Ten Thousand People Clan so mercilessly.

Yet Ho Ga-Myung did not let down even an ounce of his vigilance.

“…Kill him.”

A heavy voice escaped.

Even though the command from the commander fell like an abstraction, the Ten Thousand People Clan members couldn’t move easily.

Ho Ga-Myung squinted and clicked his tongue inwardly.

Why don’t they know?

That hesitation only prolongs this man’s suffering.

That the greatest respect they could show him now was to end his pain as quickly as possible.

No matter how much hatred had passed between them, no one would want to see such a being groan in pain and die miserably.

Even Ho Ga-Myung, who hated Chung Myung most in the world, felt the same.

“Shib Bi.”

“Yes, Commander.”

“Bring me the Sword Ghost’s head.”

“Understood!”

The man standing behind Ho Ga-Myung walked coldly toward Chung Myung. A dagger emerged from his sleeve, glinting with a chilling light.

A chilling killing intent approached.

The letters ‘death’ took on a clearer shape as they approached Chung Myung, shining with an almost pale coldness.

Yet even feeling the approaching death, Chung Myung could not resist.

No matter how he tried, his body no longer responded. Even the last fragment of his soul seemed to have faded to ash.

‘I…’

I want to live. I want to live and set foot on Mount Hua’s soil again.

Amid the Plum Blossom scent that filled Mount Hua, he wanted to laugh and chatter drunkenly with his sahyung sajaes.

That was all he wished for. As always.

‘…Chung Jin.’

He thought he knew what Chung Jin, who had died alone, must have thought in his final moments.

‘You must have felt the same.’

Only now could he understand. What remained for Chung Myung were not resentment, doubt, or worry. He simply wanted, just once more, to see those foolish bastards again.

Fwoooosh!

At that moment a fierce qi shot toward Chung Myung. A great shock, as if the world had gone dark, struck his whole body and it grew heavy as waterlogged cotton.

The consciousness he’d clung to slowly drifted away.

It was a sensation he’d felt before. Surrendering to it, Chung Myung fell into an endlessly cold place.

An endless float and a biting chill.

You’re a bit late.

Within it, something warm wrapped around Chung Myung’s shoulder.

That awkward warmth opened eyes that seemed tightly closed.

In the hazy world, something took shape. What he met was the face he’d longed for.

‘…Sa… hyung?’

Chung Mun. He smiled gently at Chung Myung, as if simply pleased.

An immense, indescribable emotion welled up.

‘Sahyung, I…’

Perhaps there were too many things he wanted to say. He tried to speak, but nothing formed.

Chung Mun simply waited for him with a warm smile.

Words of resentment, words of longing—things that couldn’t be expressed in words…

As he reached to bring them out, the face of Chung Mun before him grew blurry again.

From a familiar face to a strange one, yet strange and somehow familiar.

‘Sahyung?’

At first he didn’t recognize him.

Because the bloodied face looked a bit odd. But when that face took full form, a word slipped from Chung Myung’s cracked lips before he knew it.

“…Sa…suk?”

It was a face so battered he could hardly look, yet the moist, reddened eyes were undeniably those he’d seen countless times.

A vision seen before death?

Even if it were a hallucination… this was too cruel.

At that moment, a familiar voice flowed into his ear.

“…You waited, didn’t you?”

The blood that ran down Baek Cheon’s hand transmitted warmth to Chung Myung’s shoulder.

“Sorry I’m late.”

The tremor in his shoulder told him this was no illusion.

Speechless, Chung Myung stared blankly at Baek Cheon.

But only for a moment; Chung Myung’s body, exhausted of all strength, slowly collapsed forward.

Baek Cheon gently caught the falling Chung Myung.

“…Let’s go back, Chung Myung. To the place… to the place where you belong.”

Chapter 1398: To The Place You Belong. (3)

Gripping the shoulder of the unconscious Chung Myung, Baek Cheon’s hands trembled slightly.

Is he still alive? Is he still breathing?

He was even afraid to check. The faint warmth he felt at his fingertips seemed like it might cool at any moment.

‘Chung Myung…’

To him, Chung Myung was like Mount Tai.

A mountain that never wavered, never showed weakness in any situation, someone others could neither understand nor should try to understand.

But now that mountain-like man lay unconscious with wounds severe enough that it wouldn’t be surprising if his breath stopped at any moment.

Why did he forget sometimes?

That this man was still human—wounded like the others, feeling pain, worrying like any other person.

Baek Cheon placed his hand on Chung Myung’s abdomen and breathed energy into his body.

“Just wait a little. I’ll get you to a safe place soon. Just… just hold on until then.”

Baek Cheon whispered to the already unconscious Chung Myung and pulled him closer.

Baek Cheon’s gaze, as if suppressing something boiling, shifted away from Chung Myung. There stood Ho Ga-Myung, watching the scene with an expression like a cracked, cold mask.

What flickered across Ho Ga-Myung’s face was unmistakably bewilderment.

‘How…’

That presence had clearly not registered in his senses until a moment ago. Yet it appeared in an instant as if it had cleaved through the space itself. And moreover…

Ho Ga-Myung’s gaze dropped slightly.

There lay the corpse of Shib Bi, felled with his neck severed. Even if his specialty was escorting and assassination, Shib Bi was a murim warrior whose power would be formidable anywhere in the murim world.

And he had been cut down with a single stroke.

Of course, Ho Ga-Myung wasn’t the highest calibre of warrior, so deceiving his attention might not be that hard. Striking the neck of a distracted Shib Bi in one blow might not be terribly difficult either.

But the problem was that, by the measurements Ho Ga-Myung had made of Baek Cheon’s martial power, such a thing was impossible.

Was he to believe that that man’s power had surged dramatically in such a short time?

Ho Ga-Myung met Baek Cheon’s intense gaze and muttered.

“Those Mount Hua bastards… each and every one of them has a knack for startling people.”

Even Ho Ga-Myung couldn’t clearly tell whether the tone carried admiration or mockery.

‘Even so, nothing will change.’

Ho Ga-Myung coldly glared at Baek Cheon.

Baek Cheon’s body was covered in wounds. Blood had soaked his clothes through and dripped in drops. Chung Myung’s injuries were severe, but Baek Cheon’s were hardly less so.

He had probably run here without tending to his own body.

Thanks to that, he’d managed to prevent Chung Myung’s head from being severed, but with his injuries it would be impossible for him to escape this place carrying the unconscious Plum Blossom Sword Ghost.

“You planning to die with him?”

“…”

“Foolish to call it loyalty, and too stupid to call it strategy.”

It was provocation aimed at rousing Baek Cheon, who must have lost his senses upon seeing Chung Myung’s condition.

But Baek Cheon showed no reaction at all. He only stared at Ho Ga-Myung with an unmoving look—an emotionless gaze devoid of even the smallest shred of feeling. Even Ho Ga-Myung felt a faint chill before it.

As his stomach twisted, Ho Ga-Myung opened his mouth, but Baek Cheon spoke first.

“…Die?”

“…”

“Who?”

The voice carried no emotion, but beneath that feigned coldness a fierce anger slowly revealed itself.

“Do you lot dare claim you’ve killed someone?”

Hearing that, Ho Ga-Myung wore a faintly bitter smile, as if somewhat relieved to confirm the anger.

“Do you think he’ll make it back alive?”

“Not with me, probably impossible.”

“I know that…”

“But.”

Baek Cheon spat out the words.

“With us, it’s different.”

At that moment something flew over their heads. As Ho Ga-Myung hastily looked up, the Ten Thousand People Clan members around quickly formed a protective circle around him.

Swoooosh!

With a sound like a fine sword slicing silk, the heads of two Ten Thousand People Clan members near Baek Cheon were sent spinning into the air.

Thud.

The one who had turned the two into lost spirits with a single stroke gently landed and first checked on Chung Myung.

The usual indifference that always shaded her face cracked for a moment. Yu Yiseol, lips bitten and face contorted, looked at Chung Myung for a moment before turning her gaze to Baek Cheon.

Before any question could be asked, Baek Cheon answered briefly.

“…He’s alive.”

That was the question she’d wanted answered. Sensing her thought, Baek Cheon’s reply made Yu Yiseol give a faint nod. Then she slowly turned and glared at the Ten Thousand People Clan members surrounding them.

Another person dropped down from above in succession.

Boom!

The strong landing kicked up clouds of dust in all directions. When the dust settled, those present saw the back of a figure whose red monk’s robe fluttered.

Silence fell over the area.

The monk who had landed stared at Chung Myung as if he were a statue, motionless. After a long, frozen moment, he turned his head so slowly it was suffocating.

Flinch.

The Ten Thousand People Clan members who met that gaze involuntarily took a step back.

Those eyes were not what one would expect of a monk in robes; the hatred burning in them oppressed everyone.

Grit.

Hae Yeon bit down hard on her lower lip.

Her anger turned into killing intent. Hae Yeon, half-forgetting she was a devout, veins bulged blue on her fist.

But before that ferocity could fully burst forth, several shadows swooped in one after another.

“Chung Myuuuuung!”

Jo Gul, who had landed, sprinted with all his strength toward Chung Myung. His outstretched hand stopped just in front of him.

He couldn’t bring himself to touch him—there wasn’t a single spot without injury.

“Ugh…”

Jo Gul’s hand trembled uncontrollably. It wasn’t from anger—it was the fear of possibly losing something that mustn’t be lost.

“S-sahyung. Sahyung… Chung Myung is…”

Beside him, Yoon Jong stared intently at Chung Myung without saying a word, as if he intended to etch every wound on that body into his memory.

“Move!”

Pushing past the two, Tang Soso ran up and clung to Chung Myung. She jabbed a few needles in like lightning and then reached back, shouting.

“Great Rejuvenating Pill, quickly!”

“Here!”

Taking the vial handed by Tang Pae, Tang Soso pried open his mouth as if breaking the entrance and poured it in. With trembling hands she quickly found a few pressure points and pushed the medicine down into his throat.

“Th-this spot… ah… and here…”

She was at a loss where to begin.

There wasn’t a single wound that wasn’t serious, not a single place that wasn’t grave. It was a miracle he was alive with such injuries, and his old wounds had worsened, leaving his condition at its worst.

Though Chung Myung had survived countless injuries before, this time it was not a situation to be optimistic about.

“Please, sahyung… please!”

Tears streamed endlessly from Tang Soso’s eyes.

She’d expected him to be at death’s door, but not to this extent. Just seeing the layers of wounds carved into his body made her vividly feel how much pain he had endured.

“Ah…”

Seol So-Baek’s face looked almost spiritless. Shock, sorrow, fear, and anger seemed to overwhelm him at once; his whole body trembled like a leaf.

But no one here tried to comfort or reassure Seol So-Baek.

Everyone’s attention was focused on Chung Myung. There was not a single person with the capacity to spare concern for Seol So-Baek right now.

“…Taoist.”

Namgung Do-Wei’s eyes shimmered with cold killing intent. Even Im So-Byeong twisted his face and bared his teeth.

Uncontrollable wrath. A hatred that seemed to burn the body.

Had they arrived any later, Chung Myung would not be of this world. Or perhaps he would no longer even be considered among the living.

Thus they could only be enraged. They could only hate.

The life path they had walked, the comparatively easier road, had been sustained by Chung Myung’s blood and suffering.

“Sahyung… sahyung, please…”

Tang Soso frantically staunched Chung Myung’s wounds and applied medicine. It was doubtful whether such flustered actions could truly heal him, yet her hands did not stop for a moment.

Then Baek Cheon’s cool voice reached Tang Soso’s ear.

“…Can he live?”

Tang Soso visibly flinched. She couldn’t bring herself to answer with certainty and simply tended to Chung Myung’s wounds.

“Soso.”

“…”

“Soso!”

At last Tang Soso glared at Baek Cheon with eyes full of grief and anger; a venomous gleam was in her eyes.

But Baek Cheon didn’t flinch and pressed her again.

“Answer me.”

“…”

“Now.”

Tang Soso bit her lip until it bled.

“As… as it stands…”

At those words Baek Cheon’s eyes closed involuntarily.

A hellish silence descended. At the end of that silence, when no one could speak, Baek Cheon opened his eyes again.

“At this rate, he’ll die…”

“…”

“So that means he can live only if things aren’t left like this. What’s the way?”

Tang Soso’s pupils trembled violently for a moment, as if Baek Cheon had pointed out something she hadn’t even thought of.

“F-father.”

“Father?”

“Father! We must go to the Tang Family Lord! If anyone can—”

“Understood.”

Baek Cheon nodded at once without hesitation.

Breaking through them to reach Tang Gunak would certainly be difficult. But it would be a thousand times easier than standing by and watching Chung Myung die.

The others seemed to think the same. The moment Tang Gunak’s name was spoken, everyone prepared to move.

Red killing intent flowed from the tip of Yu Yiseol’s sword—anger more vivid than any words or expression.

A heavy killing intent poured from everyone. A firm resolve stood tall in their eyes without the slightest wavering.

Baek Cheon tightened his hold on Chung Myung’s shoulder.

‘You’ll probably be angry.’

He would surely call it foolish. But everyone here shared the same thought.

Even if it costs them their lives, they would save Chung Myung.

‘I’ll take all the anger you want to give. So… so please never… never!’

Baek Cheon rose, cradling Chung Myung, and spoke softly. Even now he continued to pour his qi into Chung Myung without stop.

“You know what needs to be done, don’t you?”

There was no reply—none was needed. Now was a time for action, not meaningless words.

There was only one thing Baek Cheon had to say at that moment.

“Let’s go.”

Different sects, different origins, different stances.

All of that was set aside for a single purpose. The ones who had become one seamed line of lightning and shot toward the way they must go.

Chapter 1399: To The Place You Belong. (4)

“St- stop…”

Fwoooosh!

The extremely swift sword was shoved into the mouth of the one about to scream. The sword, ending one life in an instant like a venomous serpent, sank into the next enemy’s heart.

It was literally a ray of light. Even if one knew, they could not stop it; even if they saw it, they could not react.

“It’s Jo Gul!”

“O- One-Sword Splitting Light!”

“It’s the Plum Blossom Five Swords!”

Those who recognized the one at the front shouted. Before that cry even ended, another flying blade mercilessly turned someone into a wandering soul.

“B- back off! Create distance!”

The Ten Thousand People Clan also prided themselves on never being outmatched in battle. They could not be ignorant of how to counter a swift blade.

But what greeted those who quickly widened the distance and dodged the blades was a golden power cutting through the air.

Kraaaaaaaaaang!

An utterly ferocious power detonated amid those retreating. Their bodies, smashed into pulp by the blast, flew in all directions like a ball kicked by a child.

Into the space thus opened, one person descended forcefully.

Sshhhhhh!

What came first?

The sound of silk being cut by a fine sword? Or the momentary flash of a crimson blade? Or perhaps a severed head soaring into the air?

Though it was hard to order events, who had created that sight was painfully clear. Yu Yiseol, fierce as a divine falcon, sprang off the ground and surged forward.

Lowering her stance like a flying swallow, she emitted semicircular streams of sword qi one after another.

“Aaaaaaah!”

Those cut by the sword qi screamed in agonizing despair.

What truly plunged them into terror was neither their arms flying off nor their guts spilling out.

It was the face as if carved from hardened cold iron and a killing intent like boiling ice. An indescribable discord emanated from the swordswoman before them. That instilled an inexpressible terror in those who faced her.

Screech!

Yu Yiseol, having cut down another enemy, briefly turned back.

Her gaze moved beyond those following to Baek Cheon in the rear. More precisely, to Chung Myung whom Baek Cheon held.

After checking his condition once more, she fixed her stare ahead with eyes full of determination.

Fwoooosh!

A fierce sword qi, as if condensed with killing intent, erupted.

Originally her sword sought the extreme of efficiency. But now her blade abandoned everything she had pursued and wrought nothing but raw power, to carve a path even a moment faster.

Before Yu Yiseol’s sword qi even faded, Namgung Do-Wei’s sword detonated, and Tang Pae’s dagger embroidered over it.

Before assaults that threw everything away, the Ten Thousand People Clan’s fortress-like encirclement began to crumble like a sandcastle.

“Get out of the way, you bastards!”

Kraaaaaaang!

Just then, the power Hae Yeon hurled caused a huge explosion in the path they were opening.

“I- it’s breaking through! They can’t stop it! Commander!”

Ho Ga-Myung silently watched the unfolding scene with a hardened face.

Kraaaaaaang!

Every time the power exploded, those who stood in the way were scattered in all directions. The once-tight encirclement was forcibly twisted, and cracks opened in the solid iron wall.

Ho Ga-Myung thought that even in such exhausted states they were forcefully wrenching open and tearing apart the encirclement.

‘…It’s infuriating.’

How many more times must he endure this sight?

Since he joined the Ten Thousand People Clan following Jang Ilso, no one had rendered him so helpless.

Of course, he hadn’t always succeeded or always beaten his opponents.

But this experience differed in trajectory from anything he’d faced before. Up until now… yes, until now the reason had been clear.

Plum Blossom Sword Ghost.

This was not a being Ho Ga-Myung could handle. As long as they existed, all of Ho Ga-Myung’s stratagems were rendered meaningless and tactics lost their worth.

So they had relentlessly cornered that one alone, willing to endure sacrifices that others might scorn as violating military principles.

The strategy clearly fit neatly and drove that devil into a life-or-death line.

So it should have been over now. The moment the Plum Blossom Sword Ghost lost consciousness, this brutal fight should have finally ended.

Yet… why was this happening again? They should have eliminated every possible variable.

Was there a miscalculation? No, he did not make miscalculations. He had never once underestimated an opponent.

Ho Ga-Myung had even considered the possibility that the enemies might break through the encirclement and enter.

There was only one thing he hadn’t predicted.

That those who should have exhausted all their strength just by breaking the encirclement were shattering it with force far beyond his expectations. It was as if…

Grit.

Ho Ga-Myung ground his teeth.

‘This is impossible.’

They could not be the Plum Blossom Sword Ghost. There could not be many like that in the world.

“Commander!”

At the urgent call from his officer, Ho Ga-Myung took several deep breaths and clenched his trembling fingertips tightly.

“…Keep calm.”

“But right now…!”

“Didn’t I tell you to stay calm?”

The officers’ commotion subsided. Ho Ga-Myung coldly analyzed the situation. Finally, he spoke softly.

“Open the front.”

“What? What do you mean…!”

The officers’ gazes focused on Ho Ga-Myung as if unable to believe it. To open the front meant to lift the encirclement.

Judging the momentum, it would be difficult to encircle them like this again once they were let through. Letting them go in this situation would render all the sacrifices the Ten Thousand People Clan had made meaningless!

“C- Commander! You must stay calm!”

“Do I look like I’m excited?”

The aides watched Ho Ga-Myung with trembling eyes. In the middle of those stares, he spoke in an icy voice.

“Blocking their front now does nothing but raise their momentum. What we must aim for now is rather…”

Ho Ga-Myung’s gaze fixed on a single spot.

“The rear.”

His blade-like stare locked precisely on Chung Myung, whom Baek Cheon was holding.

He admitted it. They had exceeded his predictions at this moment. He didn’t know by what principle it was possible, but denying the phenomenon unfolding was pointless.

But that didn’t make them omnipotent. Their desperation only highlighted their weaknesses.

“Ignore the others and focus thoroughly on the Plum Blossom Sword Ghost alone.”

The aides glanced at one another.

“Immediately!”

“Yes!”

The officers issued orders crisply in all directions. Amid the bustle, Ho Ga-Myung’s dark gaze remained fixed on the unconscious Chung Myung.

‘Is this the future you saw, Plum Blossom Sword Ghost?’

Could they even be willing to die for that? Did they believe one day they’d replace everything he had done?

Truly… it’s a ludicrous overconfidence that makes one want to laugh bitterly.

There cannot be two Jang Ilso’s in the world. Likewise, there cannot be two Chung Myung’s. Such people are not raised by others; they are bestowed by heaven alone.

But no scoff came.

There was nothing to mock. Ho Ga-Myung himself now saw fragments of the future the Plum Blossom Sword Ghost had dreamed of.

Ho Ga-Myung bit his lower lip.

‘It’s over now anyway.’

That future would not come. No, even the fact that such a possibility existed would vanish today, right here and now.

He could feel it.

Fwoooosh!

The sensation of cutting enemies was felt not at his fingertips but at the sword’s tip. It’s a strange way to put it, but nothing else could describe the feeling.

Swishhh!

Blades surging like lightning pierced the enemy’s skin, tore flesh and vessels, and finally split bone—an intense sensation vivid at the sword’s tip.

It felt as if the boundary between sword and hand had vanished.

It was an overwhelming unity that made one feel they must forcibly distinguish the sword’s tip from the hand’s.

Everything he had known was collapsing. The boundaries that divided the world blurred. Within it existed only blade and self. No, even the blade disappeared; only he remained.

The unity he had only imagined arrived in a transcendental way. If he plunged into it, he would surely take one more step forward.

But Jo Gul forcibly pushed away the realm that had come to him… no, the realm he had clearly already stepped into.

‘Strange! This is strange…!’

The enemy no longer felt strong. It seemed he could trample anyone if he willed it.

But that sense of fulfillment instead sent a warning to Jo Gul’s senses.

The conflict in his mind grew progressively deeper and muddled.

Couldn’t he go a little further?

If he could feel this sensation a bit longer, he’d undoubtedly become incomparably stronger. So why push such a possibility away himself?

‘What about me?’

I am a swordsman. I want to grow stronger. I want more…

“Chung Myung!”

KWHANG!

At that moment, the hazy world enveloping Jo Gul shattered like a mirror. Spinning to look, the current situation became clear.

Enemies who should have been in front were gathered behind him.

‘What? Why?’

For an instant he couldn’t understand the situation. Had he struck out too quickly?

No, that couldn’t be. If that were the case, the enemies would have blocked him more fiercely in front. So why was the situation like this…

At that moment, Jo Gul saw a scene.

One Ten Thousand People Clan member, eyes full of killing intent, ignored the sword flying at him and threw his body toward Baek Cheon.

Though swords were lodged all over his body, his path aimed solely at the unconscious Chung Myung.

Kaaang!

Before Jo Gul could finish a scream, Baek Cheon’s blade struck and cut down the path.

“Hyaaaaaa!”

But that was only the beginning.

The enemies began to rush toward Chung Myung. Relentless attacks poured in.

“Th- these bastards…!”

Jo Gul immediately drove his foot into the ground toward the rear.

Those who had been fiercely breaking the encirclement were now already surrounding Baek Cheon, who held Chung Myung, and fending off the incoming assaults.

‘No!’

Urgency flashed across Jo Gul’s eyes.

Chung Myung was unconscious now. That meant he couldn’t even offer the slightest reflex to twist away from incoming attacks. It also meant a blind blade could end his life.

No, there was no need to strike a vital point. Chung Myung’s body couldn’t even withstand a simple impact now.

Wooooooong!

At the sound, Jo Gul’s gaze snapped forcefully backward. He clearly saw someone gathering qi. The power concentrated in their raised hand was enormous at a glance. Jo Gul reflexively hurled himself to block in front of Baek Cheon and Chung Myung.

“Aaaaargh!”

Screech!

With all his strength he thrust his sword and cut the qi-filled hand. It was razor-close.

Hearing the agonizing scream erupt, Jo Gul clenched his teeth until his jaw muscles bulged.

‘Who do you think I am?’

His shout, filled with his spirit, burst out explosively.

“You won’t touch Chung Myung! You bastards!”

Chapter 1400: To The Place You Belong. (5)

“They’re coming from the right!”

No sooner had Baek Cheon’s voice burst out than Namgung Do-Wei flew through the air and moved to Baek Cheon’s right.

“Ooooooh!”

A blinding white radiance flared forth.

Kwaaaaargh!

The sword qi, pouring down like a waterfall, crushed the attackers. It was a sight that made the spine chill just to watch, yet the Ten Thousand People Clan members, even as their comrades were crushed beside them, pressed on relentlessly without hesitation.

“E-exeek!”

A look of bewilderment crossed Namgung Do-Wei’s face. The enemies who charged without regard for themselves seemed not to see him as they passed and lunged sword attacks toward Chung Myung, whom Baek Cheon was holding.

“You bastards!”

Namgung Do-Wei’s enraged sword sliced the air again. But even as their waists were severed, the Ten Thousand People Clan members did not stop swinging their blades.

The moment Namgung Do-Wei widened his eyes, Seol So-Baek intercepted an attack flying toward Chung Myung.

“Gyaaaah!”

Seol So-Baek’s sword, imbued with a cold qi, pierced an enemy’s throat. The Ten Thousand People Clan member’s body, corroded by the cold, was quickly covered in white frost.

“Young Palace Lord!”

“I-I’ll stop them! In the front!”

Seol So-Baek grit his teeth and glanced at Namgung Do-Wei. Seeing the desperation in his eyes, Namgung Do-Wei nodded and turned his body.

The Ten Thousand People Clan members were charging madly only toward Chung Myung.

It was a familiar sight, but their momentum felt different from before. They seemed less like fanatics and more like terrified people.

He felt he understood.

‘They’re afraid too.’

They were terrified at the thought of letting Chung Myung go from here, terrified that he might regain consciousness, raise his sword, and fight back.

What on earth had they seen in Chung Myung that made them fear his survival more than their own deaths?

“Hoooaah!”

With beastlike roars, enemies surged from the opposite side.

Ssshhh! Waiting for them was Yu Yiseol, who had already turned and was furiously swinging her sword.

Shick! Shick! Shick!

Wherever her blade grazed, red blood blossomed like flowers. But those with half-severed necks or entire thighs cut away did not stop advancing, as if they felt no pain.

Kaaaarang!

A Ten Thousand People Clan member’s sword, swung as his entrails spilled out, bounced away before it could even touch Chung Myung.

Ugh.

The shock contorted Baek Cheon’s face horribly.

Of course he had blocked it perfectly. But Baek Cheon could not stop his own body from trembling.

Right now, Chung Myung could die from the slightest impact.

“Block harder, damn it! Don’t even let them swing their weapons!”

There was no answer. No answer was needed.

Everyone already felt the same. Even if it cost them their lives, they’d save Chung Myung. From the moment they turned back from leaving the Southern lands, their purpose had been one thing.

“Oooooooh!”

Hae Yeon’s force flared wide, mercilessly pushing the attackers back. Tang Pae’s throwing dagger leapt forward and sliced the air like a shaft of light.

“Yoon Jong!”

“I know, sasuk!”

Yoon Jong leapt into the air and dove between enemies falling from above. His sword painted a massive plum-blossom shadow in the air.

‘Chung Myung!’

Even in the brief moment he cut foes and descended, Yoon Jong turned to check Chung Myung’s condition. Dried blood covered his face, and the glimpsed complexion was corpse-pale.

Gnash.

Yoon Jong bit his lip and spun his body. Sword qi streamed out in succession.

The enemies still came without ceasing. The Five Swords’ advance had long since been halted. Just pushing back the foes charging Chung Myung was exhausting every ounce of strength here.

“This…”

For a moment, a hollow laugh escaped Im So-Byeong’s lips.

“We’re really screwed.”

He looked around. He was searching for Ho Ga-Myung hidden among them. But Ho Ga-Myung was buried in the crowd and not visible.

If the Ten Thousand People Clan members had tried to stop them from the front, he might have broken through. But if they were aiming for Chung Myung, there was no way to stop them. Protecting one person is harder than killing a hundred.

Even amid that chaos, they identified weaknesses and targeted them thoroughly. That’s why Ho Ga-Myung was terrifying.

‘I must escape.’

Realistically, survival came first. Even if it meant abandoning Chung Myung, he had to leave this place. If that couldn’t be done, then he’d have to dash out even if Chung Myung took some wounds.

Yes, that’s right. As a warrior, that’s the opinion one would give.

But as he was about to speak, Baek Cheon’s back caught his eye—steadfast, without a hint of hesitation.

His mouth involuntarily closed.

‘He must know.’

Yes. Even without Im So-Byeong, everyone would know what to do to survive.

Mad waves of attackers; successive strikes poured toward the unconscious Chung Myung.

If even one of them turned away, pretended not to see the attacks, they could be free. They could save their own life.

But no one hesitated. As if they could not bear to see Chung Myung die before their own lives ended, they drew out every last drop of their strength.

What could be said to people like this?

Im So-Byeong’s gaze went to Chung Myung in Baek Cheon’s arms. The one who had repeatedly done foolish and reckless things—who, though he knew it was wrong, had never once hesitated.

A short, rueful laugh escaped Im So-Byeong.

“…Commander this and that…”

If they wanted to discuss strategy, coming this far had been a mistake from the start.

‘That’s why fools like me are useless in the end.’

Im So-Byeong stamped the ground and swept the attackers away with his fan.

‘Right. You win, Ho Ga-Myung.’

Defeat is always bitter. But this defeat didn’t sting as before. Unpleasant as it was to admit, there seemed to be defeats better than victory.

“Kill me first! You filthy Evil Sect bastards!”

Im So-Byeong howled and swung his fan, sending forth a spray of deep-blue qi.

Watching his actions, Baek Cheon slowly scanned the surroundings.

It felt as if all around, the entire world was filled with enemies.

But Baek Cheon felt no fear. All he felt was pity.

‘So this is the spectacle you saw.’

Fighting alone in a place like this—what state of mind did he swing his sword with? What feelings kept him holding on?

“You always make people annoyed.”

As Baek Cheon snorted, a large explosion occurred above his head. Black poisonous vapor began to spread. Bloodshot fury flared in Baek Cheon’s eyes.

“Block it!”

“Oooooooooh!”

Hae Yeon leapt straight into the air. His hands multiplied into hundreds, thousands. The sky being covered in golden hands was a sight beyond words.

But the successive bursts of poisonous vapor even penetrated Hae Yeon’s force.

Kwaaaargh!

Namgung Do-Wei, who had leaped in an instant, unleashed a torrent of sword qi. The immense sword qi wrapped the poison and shot it up into the air.

Once! Again! Once more!

Sweat poured like rain, but Namgung Do-Wei didn’t stop and swung his sword until his dantian felt like it would burst.

‘It’s a life already indebted!’

His life had never truly been his. There was nothing to save, nothing to regret.

Paaaah!

Under the sword qi he released, Mount Hua’s red plum blossoms fell like rain.

Even on land where plum blossoms shouldn’t bloom, as if on some barren mountain in far-off Shanxi.

Kwaaang!

“Ugh!”

“Young palace lord!”

“Don’t look back, fight! Look ahead!”

At the worried shout, Seol So-Baek cried out like he had a seizure. He had used his body to block an incoming dagger that his sword had been too late to intercept, his chest split open and bleeding.

But Seol So-Baek made no effort to staunch the wound and struck the incoming dagger again.

‘I won’t die from this.’

Wounds like this won’t kill him even if he took ten more. But Chung Myung was different—now a single small wound on him could cost him his life. Yet there was no time to complain.

“Dieeeeeee!”

But when the wound slowed him even a little, an attacker leapt over Seol So-Baek without missing the opening and tried to thrust a dagger into Chung Myung. Seol So-Baek’s eyes widened.

In that instant, someone’s arm blocked the space in front of Chung Myung.

Kwaduk!

With the sickening sound of a dagger driving into bone, blood gushed out.

“Taoist Yu!”

Seol So-Baek roared and swung his sword, cutting the Ten Thousand People Clan member’s throat. But Yu Yiseol didn’t spare a glance that way and continued cutting down others.

The qi wrung out to the limit and focus pulled to the brink was now exhausted. Yet despite clearly feeling the limits, no one lost their resolve.

“Kuhk!”

A dagger charged with blue blade qi fell fiercely toward Tang Pae, whose shoulder had been pierced. Just as he squeezed his eyes shut, a steel fan that had leapt from behind fiercely struck the falling dagger.

“Pull yourself together!”

“Green Forest King!”

Im So-Byeong gritted his teeth and swung his fan. The neat cap he always wore was gone, and his face was a wreck.

Still, he fought. Until the very end.

“Dieeeee!”

Sensing their momentum falter, the Ten Thousand People Clan increased their assault. From distances where the daggers couldn’t even reach, blue dao-qi streamed toward Chung Myung.

“No chance! You sons of bitches!”

Kwaang!

With the impact, Jo Gul spat up a rush of blood.

But he, showing no sign of pain, struck away every incoming blade qi without leaving one. Each time blade met blade qi, his insides lurched and his consciousness swam away, yet still the sword never stopped.

Kwaaaaang!

Then a blast of force someone had sent crashed onto Jo Gul. His body was flung backward like a shot projectile.

“Gul!”

G-grrgrrk!

Jo Gul drove his sword into the ground and managed not to fall, forcing his body to a stop. Spitting more blood, he painfully turned his head. He saw the fallen who had knocked Chung Myung unconscious.

“You sleep well, you little…”

Jo Gul gave a grim smile.

“Gul! Back off for now…”

“Waaah!”

Ignoring Baek Cheon’s protests, Jo Gul charged forward again. Baek Cheon bit his lip and struck a spear flying at Chung Myung with all his strength.

Kaang!

At the same time Baek Cheon spun. Two hidden daggers that had flown in under the spear stabbed mercilessly into his back. Excruciating pain spread. Yet even then Baek Cheon poured more qi into Chung Myung’s body.

Outmatched.

Suddenly a laugh slipped from Baek Cheon’s lips.

How would the world speak of them?

Would they be called heroes who kept their honor to the end? Or comrades who perished opposing the Four Sects Alliance for the sake of agreement?

‘I don’t care either way.’

Because they’re neither.

Those here were just fools who couldn’t restrain themselves, who ran here and died. They were idiots who couldn’t protect the world, their sect, or anything, and threw away their lives.

But…

“Being idiots is fine.”

A laugh kept escaping.

If he’d only sought reason and theoretical righteousness, he wouldn’t have taken up a sword. At least Baek Cheon thought so. Now he was where he had wanted, meeting the end he had chosen.

So how could there be regret?

He hadn’t looked into others’ hearts, but perhaps they felt the same.

Kwaduk!

The dagger that had been stuck at Yoon Jong’s hip slid free. But Yoon Jong made no sound and drove his sword into the back of the one trying to pass him. Only desperation shone in his eyes.

Baek Cheon quietly called out within himself.

‘Yoon Jong.’

“Aaaaaah!”

Tang Soso, covered in blood no matter where she’d been cut, screamed and stabbed at enemies. Every time her wild hair whipped, her eyes flashed an icy blue.

‘Soso.’

Kwaduk! Kwaduk!

Jo Gul, his leg cut, rolled on the ground and kept driving his sword into the tops of enemies’ feet, even as blade qi detonated along his back.

‘Jo Gul.’

Kwaang!

Yu Yiseol, who had blocked the incoming force, was pushed back. The blood she gagged up poured over her chest. The arm not holding the sword twitched and then fell limp—perhaps it no longer obeyed her.

‘Yiseol.’

They were not alone.

Kuuuuung!

“Monk!”

“Ooooooooh!”

Hae Yeon raged like the asura. The man who had so resisted opening his killing intent now let himself go and poured every remaining ounce of strength into holding the enemy.

‘Hae Yeon.’

Kwaaaang!

Namgung Do-Wei fell, spattering blood. Seol So-Baek hurled himself and blocked the blade qi raining onto Chung Myung with his back.

Tang Pae, his leg pierced by a spear, still sent out assassination weapons; Im So-Byeong, who had thrown away his broken fan, now spewed force with both hands like a madman.

‘Everyone…’

Baek Cheon’s face was so pale it was almost blue. Black blood trickled from his lips. The backlash of the force he’d forced himself to raise was coming. A sharp, cutting pain rose persistently from his dantian. Still, Baek Cheon did not stop pouring his internal energy into Chung Myung’s body.

“…How are you, you damned bastard?”

Baek Cheon smiled faintly, his blood-soaked lips twisting.

“Everyone’s doing this to protect you alone. So… don’t go acting lonely and pitiful again. Don’t try to show off and do things alone.”

They couldn’t be fully protected.

Their strength was insufficient. They couldn’t hold him safe.

But…

“Don’t worry.”

His hand gripping Chung Myung’s shoulder tightened.

“Because we can die with you.”

Baek Cheon chuckled and shoved every last scrap of qi into Chung Myung. Those shoved back by the enemy rolled and bled in front of him.

Baek Cheon looked down at the battered fighters. Perhaps sensing their limits, the enemies slowed their pace and cautiously tightened the encirclement.

“I’m sorry.”

For some reason, the words slipped out.

“Bullshit.”

Jo Gul gritted his teeth.

“Don’t apologize, sasuk. This was my choice.”

“It wasn’t even the sasuk who ordered this from the start.”

“So arrogant.”

“I always thought he was… kind of an asshole.”

The blood-soaked Mount Hua disciples each threw out a word.

Baek Cheon laughed lightly instead of getting angry.

“Fine. Take your apologies… from this bastard.”

“We should take that.”

“I’ll beat him up. I’ll beat him till he dies.”

“…Go to hell.”

“Still, to a Taoist…”

Even those who weren’t Mount Hua disciples spat curses. Yet there was no trace of regret in their eyes—only the will to fight to the death.

‘Not bad.’

Baek Cheon laughed.

It wasn’t the death he had imagined. Still… this wasn’t bad. Far better, hundreds or even thousands of times better than dying a cowardly long life. At least he could be proud of himself.

There was only one regret…

Baek Cheon closed his eyes briefly, burying the lingering remorse, then snapped them open and shouted resolutely.

“Until the end, we are Mount Hua’s disciples. Die with pride!”

“Yes!”

As if his words were a signal, the enemies howled and surged forward.

“Oooooooooh!”

The Ten Thousand People Clan’s blade qi poured toward them like a waterfall. An unstoppable onslaught. Baek Cheon widened his eyes.

He would watch closely. To the last person’s end. That was his final remaining duty.

But in that moment, what he saw was a green flash of light.

Kwa-dududududduk!

A sound like eardrums being torn rang out and the charging enemies were flung away like being struck by a runaway cart. The terrifying torrents of blade qi were shredded and crushed.

‘…What?’

Startled, Baek Cheon watched dumbfounded. What on earth…

“…Looks like they’re not completely too late.”

Everyone, including Baek Cheon, turned their gazes to the same place.

Far away, a familiar figure appeared.

“Ah…”

Why was that person there? Was it a dream?

Standing composed and steady, his cold, taciturn face bore anger like a chill.

And then another person appeared beside him.

“Ah…”

“Aaah…”

The Mount Hua disciples who saw them collapsed on the spot.

White hair thick with age, black martial robe, a plum blossom engraved on the chest, and… an austere prayer-like aura, with refined rage.

His gaze turned to Baek Cheon at the center. The moment their eyes met, Baek Cheon’s entire body went limp.

It was neither dream nor illusion.

“I command Mount Hua’s disciples!”

“Sect Leaderrrrr!!”

A scream like a wail tore from Baek Cheon’s mouth.

“Annihilate the enemy!”

Hyun Jong’s sword, which had risen high into the sky, fixed itself firmly ahead.

“Save Mount Hua’s disciples!”

“We take the order!”

Un Geom and Hyun Sang surged forward at the lead and leapt. Behind them, Mount Hua’s disciples in black robes ran with barely contained fury.

On the dry land of the Southern lands, red plum blossoms bloomed like a dream.
